- **Already known as** Named as a risk in the architecture map ("Sweep against a real wallet-rpc") and covered obliquely by BL-11 ("no stagenet run"). Both understate it: BL-11 reads as "untested", whereas the monero source shows the failure is near-certain, and the code's error handling turns a recoverable condition into a permanent one. No BL number covers the terminal-on-any-error behaviour.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/HIGH/reproduced: The half that matters and that the finder could actually run — that ANY SweepAll error is terminal and nothing ever retries — I reproduced independently with my own test rather than theirs. swap.go:562-568 sets Sweep.Unknown and calls finish("sweep_unknown"), which sets Done=true; the next pass find

### ZEC-2 — An upper-case hash on the board makes the DERO claim impossible while the Zcash claim still works — the same theft, by a different asymmetry

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `3175401` (the Zcash side refuses a hash that is not 64 lower-case hex) and `cffdd29` (`swap.bas` Lock and `board.bas` Post refuse it too).
- **Where** `contracts/swap.bas:28` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** swap.bas Claim line 28 compares HEX(SHA256(raw)) — the DVM's HEX() is lower-case (vendor/derohe/dvm/dvm_functions.go dvm_hex) — against the stored h_<id> as a string, and board.bas Post line 24 / swap.bas Lock line 22 store the hash as any 64 characters. The Zcash side holds H as bytes, so case is invisible to it. The maker Posts and Locks under H rendered in upper-case hex; the taker's tool decodes it (tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go:51 uses hex.DecodeString, which accepts either case) and produces exactly the script and P2SH address the honest lower-case form would produce; the taker funds; the maker claims the ZEC with the correct 32-byte s; the taker's swap.bas Claim compares a lower-case digest with the upper-case stored string and is refused forever; T_dero passes and the maker Refunds the DERO.
- **Preconditions** The maker posts the offer. The taker does not compare h_<id> character-for-character against the lower-case hex of the bytes it fed into the script — which no code in the repository does, and there is no ZEC-swap screen in app/ at all.
- **Impact** Total loss of the taker's ZEC leg, same as ZEC-1. Less severe only because a taker who thought to check could see the upper-case hash on the board before funding.
- **Exploitability** Trivial. Mixed case works as well as upper case, so it also survives a naive "is it all upper case" check; only a strict equality against the lower-case rendering catches it.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzswap_preimage_test.go, second half, on the real DVM:   tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestSwapPreimageAsymmetry' -v  → PASS It Locks under strings.ToUpper(hex(SHA256(raw))), asserts c.S(scid,"h_1") is the upper-case string, asserts Claim with the correct 64-hex 32-byte preimage is refused, and asserts the maker's Refund then succeeds. The tool's complicity, run directly:   cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap script -hash ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF0123456789 -claim 751e76e8199196d454941c45d1b3a323f1433bd6 -refund 751e76e8199196d454941c45d1b3a323f1433bd6 -cltv 3000000 -net mainnet   redeem script 63a820abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcd
- **Fix** Two local changes, neither touching a contract. (1) tools/zecswap: add an exported ParseSwapHash(s string) ([32]byte, error) that refuses anything but 64 characters of lower-case hex, and use it in cmd/zecswap for -hash; every consumer of a board h_<id> must go through it. (2) The taker's companion (and the app screen when it exists, BL-43) must refuse to fund any offer whose h_<id> is not equal, character for character, to hex.EncodeToString of the bytes it put in the script — that single equality closes it. Optionally also state the rule in docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §3 step 4, which today says only "checks every field".
- **Regression test** Add tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestSwapHashMustBeLowerCaseHex once ParseSwapHash exists (assert upper-case, mixed-case, 63- and 65-character and non-hex inputs are all refused with a reason). tests/zzswap_preimage_test.go already pins the DVM half.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/script.go, tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go, tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** Partly known: the architecture map's DVM reader lists "swap.bas/htlc.bas check hash length only (132; 322): an uppercase or non-hex hash can never match HEX(SHA256()) (lowercase), making Claim impossible" and calls companion validation the only guard. It understates it in two ways: no BL number exists, and it frames the outcome as an unusable escrow rather than as the maker keeping both legs; and it does not note that tools/zecswap's own CLI builds the working script from an upper-case hash without complaint.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-3 — cltv is bounded by nobody: an already-passed or Unix-time CLTV leaves the taker's ZEC unlocked and the maker holding a revocable sale

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `3175401`.
- **Where** `tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go:54` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** board.bas Accept line 25 requires only `cltv != 0`. The CLI accepts any value in 1..0x7fffffff (main.go:54). Nothing compares cltv with the Zcash chain tip, and nothing enforces Zcash's LOCKTIME_THRESHOLD of 500,000,000, above which BIP 65 and nLockTime are a Unix time rather than a height. Two consequences. (a) A taker Accepts with a cltv already below the Zcash tip (or any value ≥ 500,000,000, all of which are timestamps already in the past). The maker's companion, having no such check, Locks the DERO; the taker funds the P2SH; but the refund branch is spendable immediately, so the taker can pull the ZEC back at any moment before the maker's claim confirms — including the moment the maker's claim appears in the mempool and reveals s, if the taker's conflicting spend wins. The maker has locked capital for 8 hours against a sale the counterparty can cancel at will, which is the exact inverse of the risk allocation docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §2 says it built the whole role assignment to achieve. (b) Symmetrically, a taker who is merely careless writes a cltv far in the future (up to 2,147,483,647) and locks their own ZEC for centuries; the board records it and the app would show it as a normal swap.
- **Preconditions** One Accept transaction on board.bas. For (a) the taker must also be willing to pay a second Zcash fee; for (b) no adversary is needed at all.
- **Impact** (a) The Zcash leg's timelock — the thing that makes the escrow an escrow — is void; the maker's capital is exposed for T_dero against nothing. (b) The taker's ZEC is stranded for an arbitrary time with no recourse.
- **Exploitability** One field in one transaction, no tooling required. board.bas cannot fix it (the DVM cannot see the Zcash height), so every guard must live in the companion and the app, and neither exists.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap script -hash 00…00 -claim 751e76e8199196d454941c45d1b3a323f1433bd6 -refund 751e76e8199196d454941c45d1b3a323f1433bd6 -cltv 1 -net mainnet   redeem script 63a820…88ac670101b17576a914…88ac68 (92 bytes)   address       t3LReDh45RLyJ9HDkhKr5K5HK9sqUYz8DtG (mainnet)   nLockTime     1 (the refund transaction's, at least) and with -cltv 1600000000 (a Unix time in 2020, i.e. already satisfiable through median-time-past):   redeem script 63a820…88ac670400105e5fb17576a914…88ac68 (95 bytes)   nLockTime     1600000000 Both accepted without a word. contracts/board.bas:25 read directly: `IF STRLEN(refundPkh) != 40 || cltv == 0 THEN GOTO 900`.
- **Fix** In tools/zecswap: add CheckCLTV(cltv uint32, zcashTip uint32) error refusing cltv >= 500_000_000 (LOCKTIME_THRESHOLD — a height and a Unix time are not interchangeable and the board's cl_<i> is documented as a height), refusing cltv <= zcashTip, and refusing cltv outside the policy window of docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §5 (tip+96 with a stated tolerance). Wire it into the CLI's -cltv (which must then take the tip, or refuse to run without -tip) and, when the companion exists, into the taker's Accept path and the maker's pre-Lock check — the note already says the maker "refuses to lock unless cltv and T_dero satisfy the margin"; make that a function, not a sentence. The bound cannot go into board.bas.
- **Regression test** tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestCheckCLTV: table of (cltv, tip) pairs — 1, tip-1, tip, 500_000_000, 1_600_000_000, 0x7fffffff all refused; tip+96 accepted; tip+96±tolerance boundary asserted.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/script.go, tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go, tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** New. board.bas's weak validation is noted by the architecture map ("cltv is a bare number with no chain binding … no minimum expiry distance") but only as a bulletin-hygiene point; nothing records that it makes the escrow's Zcash leg non-binding, and no BL covers it. docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §5 states the policy (cltv = Zcash tip + 96) and §3 step 2 says "cltv must lie inside policy … or the maker will not lock" — no code performs that check.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-4 — Consensus branch ids are three network upgrades stale, there is no node to read them from, and the documented field is the wrong one

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `3175401`.
- **Where** `tools/zecswap/sighash.go:25` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** sighash.go defines BranchNU5 = 0xC2D6D0B4 and BranchNU6 = 0xC8E71055 and nothing else, and Tx.txPerson() personalizes every ZIP 244 digest with "ZcashTxHash_" + the branch id, so the branch id is signed over. Mainnet has since activated NU6.1 (0x4DEC4DF0, mainnet 3,146,400 — ZIP 255), NU6.2 (0x5437F330, mainnet 3,364,600 — ZIP 257) and NU6.3 (0x37A5165B, mainnet 3,428,143 — ZIP 258). A signature made under BranchNU6 today is invalid everywhere. There is no node client in the package at all, so the mitigation both docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §7 and the code comment claim — "read from the node (getblockchaininfo)" — does not exist. Worse, the code comment at sighash.go:23 names `consensus.chaintip`; the transaction will be mined in a future block, so the correct field is `consensus.nextblock`, and the two differ exactly at an activation boundary — the one moment the choice matters. The consequence is not confined to "invalid, never a loss" as the note claims: the claim branch of the redeem script has no time limit, so if the taker cannot build and broadcast a refund at cltv, the maker can come back after T_dero has expired — by which time the maker has already Refunded the DERO — and take the ZEC with s for free.
- **Preconditions** Any real use of the package against mainnet, or any Zcash network upgrade between a swap's funding and its refund.
- **Impact** Every signature is rejected, so nothing can be claimed or refunded on Zcash. Chained with the missing builder (ZEC-7) it becomes loss of the taker's ZEC rather than delay.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack — a certainty. It fires on the first real transaction.
- **Reproduced** yes — tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestBranchIDsAreNotStale, added:   cd tools && go test -count=1 -run TestBranchIDsAreNotStale ./zecswap   --- FAIL: TestBranchIDsAreNotStale (0.00s)       hazards_test.go:57: sighash.go does not know consensus branch id 0x4DEC4DF0; every signature it makes for mainnet today is invalid       hazards_test.go:57: sighash.go does not know consensus branch id 0x5437F330; …       hazards_test.go:57: sighash.go does not know consensus branch id 0x37A5165B; … All ten ZIP 244 vectors in testdata carry branch c2d6d0b4 (NU5), which I confirmed with a throwaway probe test, so BranchNU6's value has never been exercised by any test — it is asserted by the constant alone. Bran
- **Fix** Add the three missing constants and a table mapping (network, height) → branch id, so a stale binary can at least detect that it does not know the tip's epoch and fail closed rather than sign under a guess. Then make the branch id an input read from the node's getblockchaininfo `consensus.nextblock` (not chaintip) when the node client lands, and refuse to sign when nextblock differs from chaintip and the caller has not said which it wants. Correct the comment at sighash.go:23 and docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §7 to say nextblock, and drop "never a loss" — say instead that a refund that cannot be broadcast becomes a loss once T_dero passes, because the claim branch never expires.
- **Regression test** tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestBranchIDsAreNotStale (added; RED until the constants land). Add TestBranchIDForHeight once the table exists, pinning the four mainnet activation heights and the boundary block on each side.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/sighash.go, tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** BL-47 asks to "Confirm CLTV and P2SH remain in the transparent consensus rules" but says nothing about branch ids; BL-40b lists "the watcher, the ZIP 317 fee and broadcast" as what remains and does not mention the branch-id read. The backlog understates: as shipped, tools/zecswap cannot produce a signature valid on Zcash mainnet at all.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### DVM-1 — An unregistered payee that is not the LAST transfer half-commits: nfa.bas settles the sale, pays the creator, and destroys the seller's whole remainder plus the protocol fee

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired in nfa.bas** in `47cdd4c`, with LIV-01. The general rule's static test across every contract is DVM-3, still open.

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:345` (dimension: DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17))
- **Attack path** dvm.SanityCheckExternalTransfers (vendor/derohe/dvm/simulator.go:227-238) is the only registry check on a contract's transfer list, and its loop body is `if _, err = balance_tree.Get(addr); err == nil || xerrors.Is(err, graviton.ErrNotFound) { /* everything is okay */ } else { err = ...; return }`. An UNREGISTERED destination takes the "okay" branch, and the named return `err` is left holding whatever the LAST Get returned. A bad destination therefore fails the call only when it is the last transfer; anywhere earlier, err comes back nil. process_transaction_sc (vendor/derohe/blockchain/transaction_execute.go:391-408) then runs dvm.ProcessExternal, which commits EVERY state entry first (simulator.go:307-326) and only then walks the transfers in emission order (simulator.go:328, order fixed by TX_Storage.SendExternal, dvm/dvm_store.go:138-144), panicking on the first unregistered one (simulator.go:356-359). process_transaction_sc recovers from that panic (transaction_execute.go:277-282) and the block commits the trees regardless (blockchain/blockchain.go:971-1004, 1010-1012). SanityCheck has ALREADY debited the contract's own asset balance by the FULL total (simulator.go:212-224), so the dropped transfers are refunded to nobody: that money stops existing.  nfa.bas processDEROFinalPayment (contracts/nfa.bas:329-353) emits up to five transfers in one fixed order: royalty->creatorAddr (337), artificerFee->artificerAddr (341), charity->charityDonateAddr (345), protoFee->treasury (349), remainder->owner (352). Two of those five destinations are never checked against the registry: (a) charityDonateAddr is a string the SELLER hands to Start(); nfa.bas:140 and :144 check only that it decompresses to a curve point, and app/nfas.js:95 does the same format-only check with V.canonAddress. A charity that has never transacted on DERO — exactly the kind of address a donation recipient publishes — is unregistered. (b) artificerAddr is HARD-CODED at nfa.bas:60 to Civilware's mainnet address and paid whenever a creator sets artificerFee > 0 (app/forms.js:206-208 offers the field, 0 to 100). protoFee is 50 bps on every DMZ NFA, so there is always at least one transfer after both of them, which is what turns the revert into a half-commit.
- **Preconditions** An NFA sale or auction close whose saleAmt is large enough that protoPaymt > 0 (>= 200 atomic units at 50 bps), and either (a) charityDonatePerc > 0 with a well-formed but unregistered charityDonateAddr, or (b) artificerFee > 0 on a network where dero1qy0khp9s9yw2h0eu20xmy9lth3zp5cacmx3rwt6k45l568d2mmcf6qgcsevzx is unregistered. Case (c) also exists: an NFA installed outside the app with an unregistered `treasury`, which destroys only the owner's remainder. Both (a) and (b) are one form field away in the shipped app. The two NFAs currently on the simulator have artificerFee 0 and charityDonatePerc 0, so nothing deployed today is exposed.
- **Impact** A completed, irreversible sale in which the seller is paid nothing. Reproduced: on a 1,000,000-unit sale with royalty 10%, charity 20%, protoFee 50 bps, the buyer receives the NFA, the creator receives the 100,000 royalty, and the 200,000 charity share, the 5,000 protocol fee and the seller's 695,000 remainder are credited to nobody — 900,000 of 1,000,000 destroyed — while the contract's own quote balance reads 0, i.e. its books say it paid everything out. Identical for a DERO-quoted and a wXMR-quoted NFA (so it destroys wrapped XMR/ZEC, permanently breaking the vault invariant reserve >= sup for that amount: units left sup on the seller's side of the book but never reached a wallet). The artificer variant needs no bad input from anyone and applies to every NFA on the affected network: 845,000 of 1,000,000 destroyed in the reproduction. Nothing can undo it; the transaction is a success from the chain's point of view and the app shows the ownership change.
- **Exploitability** No attacker is required — the loss is inflicted by an ordinary, well-intentioned input the app accepts. There is no profit path for a third party (the destroyed money reaches nobody), so this is fund destruction rather than theft. Trigger difficulty is low: type a charity address that has never received DERO, or set the artificer share above 0. Silent: the wallet reports success, the app shows the new owner, and no error is surfaced anywhere.
- **Reproduced** yes — Written and run by me: tests/zzsplitpay_test.go (new), plus one additive helper Chain.AccountRecord in tests/harness/chain.go.  $ cd <repo> $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestNFASaleToAnUnregisteredCharity|TestNFASaleToARegisteredCharity|TestUnregisteredArtificerAddress' -v  === RUN   TestNFASaleToAnUnregisteredCharityStripsTheSellerAndTheTreasury/dero     ProcessExternal panicked exactly as the chain does, and the chain recovers: sending to non registered account acc 245486ae... err leaf not found ...     treasury balance record unchanged: the 5000-unit protocol fee was never delivered     LOST: sale of 1000000 settled. buyer holds the NFA, creator took
- **Fix** Two parts.  CONTRACT (needs a redeploy and a new template hash — NOT safe now). In contracts/nfa.bas processDEROFinalPayment, move the charity transfer so it is the LAST statement of the function, after the owner's remainder: royalty (337), artificerFee (341), protoFee (349), owner (352), charity. Then the one destination the seller controls is the last transfer, SanityCheckExternalTransfers returns its ErrNotFound, and the call reverts cleanly with the buyer refunded — fail closed instead of destroy. This changes the line numbering of a file whose whole design point is being ART-NFA-MS1 verbatim, so it is an operator decision: it requires regenerating app/templates/nfa.bas, the sha256 in app/config.js and deploy/networks.json, and a redeploy of every listed NFA (existing ones cannot be fixed — nfa.bas has no UpdateCode). The alternative contract fix, checking registration on chain, does not exist: the DVM exposes no primitive that reads the balance tree.  APP (safe now, and it closes both live triggers). app/nfas.js:95-101: before Start, resolve the typed charity address against the node and refuse it when the account is unregistered — DERO.GetEncryptedBalance for an unregistered address returns an error, which is the same test ProcessExternal applies; say in the sheet that an unregistered charity would destroy the sale proceeds. app/forms.js:206-208: refuse artificerFee > 0 unless the artificer address resolves on the configured network, or fix the field at 0 until operator decision D-4 is taken. Both are format-only checks today (V.canonAddress).  OPERATOR: settle D-4 with artificerFee pinned at 0 for the mainnet template, and confirm the artificer address is registered on mainnet before allowing anything else.
- **Regression test** tests/zzsplitpay_test.go, already written and passing: TestNFASaleToAnUnregisteredCharityStripsTheSellerAndTheTreasury (DERO and wrapped quote), TestNFASaleToARegisteredCharityPaysEveryone (the control), TestUnregisteredArtificerAddressDestroysEverySaleOnThatNetwork. After the reordering fix, the first and third must flip to a clean revert: r.OK() == false, owner unchanged, scBalance still 1, and the buyer refunded — invert the assertions rather than deleting them, so the fix is what the test proves. Add the static invariant of DVM-3 as well.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/nfas.js, app/forms.js, app/templates/nfa.bas, app/config.js, deploy/networks.json, tests/zzsplitpay_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md and not in any prior audit as a DMZ finding. The asset-system reader's map raises it as reasoned-only, NOT PERFORMED ("check that first"), and states it wrongly: it says the sanity check "only returns the LAST transfer's Get() error" but concludes the whole call is lost. It is worse and sharper than that — the call SUCCEEDS and only the tail of the payment list is destroyed. tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go L2 (another finder, this session) covers the SINGLE-transfer case in vault.bas, which behaves completely differently (clean revert, re-runnable). Upstream civilware/artificer-nfa-standard's README admits "there is currently no internal SC catch for this" for the charity address, but does not say the sale still completes and the money is destroyed. The backlog understates it: nothing records it at all.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-1 — The legacy template list keeps a launch contract with a known total-drain defect as a green "Verified" listing

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired in part** in `099a4b1`: a market whose code matches an older template is marked, and this app will not send the `...To` entrypoint the known drain needs on one. Revoking the two legacy launch hashes de-lists a live market, which is the operator's decision.
- **Where** `app/config.js:23 (templates.launch.legacy) + app/feed.js:161-165 (verify) + tools/simdeploy/main.go:147-169 (unconditional demotion):23` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** tools/simdeploy records every outgoing template hash into networks.json `legacy` unconditionally (main.go:153-155, comment: "kept as legacy so markets already deployed from it still verify"). genconfig copies that into app/config.js. app/feed.js verify() maps ANY hash in {sha256} union legacy to a kind (feed.js:151-153, 161-165) and never reads the contract's own `v` key (absent from NUMKEYS/STRKEYS, feed.js:7-8). So a market installed from the pre-BC-1 launch template verifies identically to one on the current template: ui.js verified() falls through to the plain green shield (ui.js:104) and token.js renders Buy/Sell. A DMZ user buys into a curve whose entire raise any anonymous caller can take with one SwapDeroForAssetTo: the pre-fix buyto sends the overshoot refund at line 59 and only then checks min_out at line 60; the ...To wrapper turns the resulting RETURN 1 into refundto()+RETURN 0, and the DVM never decrements state.Assets on a send, so both transfers commit and refundto pays out the WHOLE deposit again. Repeatable until `real` is empty.
- **Preconditions** A market listed on the factory whose on-chain code hashes to a legacy launch hash carrying both (a) the send-before-min_out ordering and (b) the ...To wrapper with refundto. No wallet, no key, no privilege: the attacker calls SwapDeroForAssetTo directly. The victim is any DMZ user who trusted the badge.
- **Impact** Total loss of a launch's raised quote for everyone holding its token, on a market the app affirmatively certified. The app's own first-run promise (app.js:269, "Every listing is verified - The app checks each contract's code against the shipped templates before it shows it") is what routes the user there. Verified live: 7 of 20 listed launches on the simulator carry the vulnerable ordering; 2 of them (hashes 3fc1d05b..., 09cb53d2...) have the full BC-1 shape. scid 9f5d93affef8217a... holds real = 145,336 atomic DERO today.
- **Exploitability** Trivial for the drain itself (one contract call, no privilege). The frontend contribution is the certification: it costs the attacker nothing and there is no in-app signal distinguishing the defective code from the fixed code.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ cd <repo> # every listed launch, hashed and checked for the pre-fix ordering $ node -e '<walk factory launch_<i>, DERO.GetSC code:true, sha256, grep /SEND_(ASSET|DERO)_TO_ADDRESS\(s, d - used/>' bd954bf435165bd3... 60a30a9b BRW  real=153794 | SEND_DERO_TO_ADDRESS(s, d - used) b7fed4dbc185f700... bc05b8e3 BRW  real=103665 | SEND_DERO_TO_ADDRESS(s, d - used) b76e15e804169258... bc05b8e3 BRW  real=78000 d8178aa7a9794874... bc05b8e3 BRW  real=174081 fed57846f826bb4e... bc05b8e3 BRW  real=158861 9f5d93affef8217a... 3fc1d05b BRW  real=145336 | SEND_DERO_TO_ADDRESS(s, d - used) cfbe49b17a41bde9... 09cb53d2 WXMR real=0     | SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(s, d - used, q) markets: 7  TO
- **Fix** Two parts. (a) SAFE NOW, app-side: make verify() return the tier as well as the kind - a hash that is not templates[k].sha256 is `legacy` - and (i) render a distinct badge in ui.js verified() ("Older template - not the current code") instead of the plain shield, (ii) add a row to H.risks so the trade sheet says it before the button, (iii) have app.tx refuse the ...To (ring-16) entrypoints on any non-current hash, which is the exact call BC-1 needs and the only one the app itself can fire accidentally. (b) OPERATOR DECISION, not safe to do unilaterally: remove the BC-1 hashes (3fc1d05b..., 09cb53d2..., and any mainnet equivalent) from templates.launch.legacy, which de-lists those markets - the trade-off the operator made in d1c5a89 and must re-make with the security context. Separately, tools/simdeploy/main.go:153 must stop demoting silently: a template change that fixes a defect needs an explicit legacy entry with a reason, not an automatic carry-forward.
- **Regression test** tests/js - assert that every hash in config.templates.launch.legacy either (1) is absent from the set of hashes whose Canon source contains a SEND_* before the min_out check, or (2) is rendered by ui.js verified() with a non-plain badge. Plus a DVM test running TestNoExternalSendBeforeAFailureExit against each shipped legacy template source, not only the current one.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/feed.js, app/ui.js, app/home.js, app/app.js, app/config.js, tools/simdeploy/main.go
- **Already known as** New. The drain itself is BC-1, fixed in contracts/launch.bas at d1c5a89. No BL covers the fact that the app still badges the pre-fix code Verified; the FOLLOWUP and BACKLOG treat the legacy list purely as a de-listing convenience.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-2 — One permissionless factory registration permanently bricks Home, Markets and Trade for every client

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/feed.js:341-345 (the pool_/launch_ pmap loop) + app/feed.js:272-286 (loadNFA) + app/stats.js:44-57 (flow):344` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** factory.bas reg() (lines 101-108) checks only STRLEN(scid)==32 and DEROVALUE() >= regfee, and the registry can never be pruned. feed.js refresh() walks ids = [...reg.launches, ...reg.pools] and does `if (s && !s.untrusted) next.set(id, s)` with NO kind check - unlike the two loops immediately after it, which both filter (`s.kind === "nft"`, `s.kind === "nfa"`). loadContract() dispatches on the verified hash, not on which list the id came from, so an NFA scid registered with RegisterPool returns loadNFA()'s record - which has no `points` and no `trades` (feed.js:277 builds the object without them, unlike loadContract's `trades: [], points: []` at feed.js:224). decorate() then reaches S.enrich(s) -> flow(s) -> `pts = s.points; ... pts.length` -> TypeError. decorate() aborts, so `this.lastRefresh = Date.now()` on the next line never runs and every market after the poisoned one in map order never gets s.m. Home/Markets/Trade sort on b.m.hot and throw; the router paints an errbox.
- **Preconditions** Anyone with regfee (10000 atomic = 0.1 DERO in the shipped config) and the scid of any contract whose code hashes to templates.nfa. Two such NFAs are already deployed and listed on the simulator, so the attacker deploys nothing. Permanent: factory.bas has no delete.
- **Impact** Every DMZ client, everywhere, loses Home, Markets, the Trade hub, the token page and the portfolio, forever, for 0.1 DERO. D.lastRefresh stays 0 so screens that branch on it sit in the "Reading the registry..." state rather than reporting an error. The Wrap screen and the notice survive (their own router handlers do not read D.assets), so users can still burn - but they cannot reach a market. No funds move; availability loss is total and irreversible.
- **Exploitability** One transaction, no privilege, no timing, permanent. The only cost is the registration fee.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ cd <repo> && node --test tests/js/registry.test.mjs ok 1 - stats.enrich throws on a record with no price history (what loadNFA returns) ok 2 - one NFA in the pool_/launch_ list aborts decorate() and breaks every ranking ok 3 - the pool_/launch_ loop has no kind guard while the nft_ and nfa_ loops do  The second test loads app/{config,rpc,amm,stats,feed}.js in Node with a four-function ui shim, puts one loadNFA-shaped record and two healthy pool records into D.assets, and asserts:   decorate() THREW: TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')   pool got metrics? false        (a healthy market after it never gets s.m)   lastRefresh: 0              
- **Fix** app/feed.js:344 - require the kind, exactly as the two loops below already do: `const s = await this.loadContract(id, cached); if (s && !s.untrusted && (s.kind === "pool" || s.kind === "launch")) next.set(id, s);`. Belt and braces: give loadNFA's record `points: []` and `trades: []`, and make stats.flow()/published()/priceAt() treat a missing history as empty rather than throwing. Both are local, and tests/js/registry.test.mjs turns on the first change.
- **Regression test** tests/js/registry.test.mjs (added in this pass) - test 3 asserts today's missing guard and must be inverted when fixed; tests 1 and 2 assert the crash and must both become "no throw" once stats.js is hardened.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/feed.js, app/stats.js
- **Already known as** New. BL-105 / PROD-P7-04 note that the factory has no dedup or prune; neither says a single registration crashes the data layer. The backlog understates it: this is not a spam-cost problem, it is a permanent global denial of service.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-3 — Every per-user read on the Wrap screen is keyed wrongly, so the deposit address never appears and Reclaim can never be reached

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:53-77 (myVault) and app/wrap.js:80-92 (vaultWithdrawals) against contracts/vault.bas:264-284 (Register) and 375-402 (Burn):55` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** Not an attack - a defect that removes the vault's only permissionless exit from the interface. vault.bas is the one contract in the tree that keys per-address records by the RAW signer: Register() does `LET s = SIGNER()` then `STORE("uc_" + s, ...)` / `STORE("ul_" + s + "_" + c, j)` (lines 20, 31-32), and Burn() does `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)` then `STORE("w_" + j + "_by", s)` (lines 20, 44). SIGNER() is Chain_inputs.Signer, the 33-byte compressed key (vendor/derohe/dvm/dvm_functions.go:307-309). founders.bas, launch.bas, nft.bas and social.bas all take ADDRESS_STRING(s) first, so the app's convention is right everywhere else. app/wrap.js builds `"uc_" + addr` and `"ul_" + addr + "_" + n` from the canonical dero1 string and compares `hexToStr(w_<j>_by) === addr` - its own comment at wrap.js:80 states the wrong convention ("w_<j>_by is the burner as ADDRESS_STRING"), which is how it survived review. Every one of those keys answers NOT AVAILABLE.
- **Preconditions** None. Any connected holder, on any vault, always.
- **Impact** (1) `registered` is always false, so step 1 of the deposit walkthrough always offers "Register a deposit index" and steps 2-5 stay greyed; the user never sees da_<j>, the foreign address they must pay. Registering again is accepted by the contract whenever the previous index was attested, so each visit burns another regfee, consumes another index, and forces k more Attest votes from every signer - the user drives an unbounded work amplification on the federation without knowing it. (2) vaultWithdrawals() always returns [], so the "Your withdrawals" section and, with it, the Reclaim button (wrap.js:441) can never render. Reclaim is the vault's liveness-by-construction exit - the one path that does not need a live quorum. Through the app it does not exist. A holder whose federation has gone quiet has no in-app way to get their units back.
- **Exploitability** Not attacker-triggered; it is the default state for every user. Severity is HIGH because it defeats a stated invariant ("any state that needs a human to leave it is a defect") and because the deposit walkthrough dead-ends in front of an irreversible foreign-chain transfer.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:20000/json_rpc -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \     -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":"1","method":"DERO.GetSC","params":{"scid":"f23403b0741852161cd796249bdb1d32b796fa2b646f60041043f6f03bf686fb","variables":true}}'   ... "uc_\r\xa6\xf5pX\xe0c\x83\xfb\xc2\xd0\x135b\xcdV}\xf1_\xa8\xf9\x9d\x03\xea,=\x9cRE\xbb\xa6\xca\x01": "1"   ... "ua_0": "0da6f57058e06383fbc2d0133562cd567df15fa8f99d03ea2c3d9c5245bba6ca01"  # that raw key's canonical address, via the app's own rawToAddress():   dero1qyx6datstrsx8qlmctgpxdtze4t8mu2l4rue6ql29s7ec5j9hwnv5qgnajyz8  # ask for it the way app/wrap.js does: $ curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:20000/json_rpc -H 'Content-Type: applicatio
- **Fix** The app cannot fix this alone: keysstring is a JSON array, and a byte above 0x7f becomes its UTF-8 encoding in the Go string the daemon builds, so a raw 33-byte key cannot be requested by exact key at all (asserted in tests/js/vaultkeys.test.mjs). Two options, and the choice is the operator's. (a) Preferred: change contracts/vault.bas to key uc_/ul_/w_<j>_by by ADDRESS_STRING(s), matching every other contract - free to do now because no mainnet vault exists, but it invalidates the simulator vaults and needs a redeploy. ua_<j> must stay raw: it is a SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS target. (b) Stopgap while (a) is pending: have myVault()/vaultWithdrawals() read the vault with `variables: true` once per refresh and match on the raw key bytes; local and testable, but a whole-tree read that grows with the user count. Either way, delete the false comment at wrap.js:80.
- **Regression test** tests/js/vaultkeys.test.mjs (added) pins the contract-side and app-side spellings and the fact that raw bytes cannot survive a JSON keysstring round-trip; invert its assertions when the fix lands. Add a UI-pass step that registers from a funded wallet and asserts "Index 0" and a deposit address appear on #wrap.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, app/wrap.js, tests/js/vaultkeys.test.mjs
- **Already known as** Reported by this audit's vault reader as CONFIRMED and independently reproduced here against the live simulator vault. No BL number; BL-83 speaks about Reclaim's semantics, not about it being unreachable from the app.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-02 — Any stranger can take a concluded NFA auction away from its winner and strand the winning payment in the contract with no exit the winner can take

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`, with NFA-1: BuyItNow on an ended auction settles it to the highest bidder. Liveness test L3.

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:183` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** When an auction's endBlockTime has passed, checkActive returns 111 for any listType. BuyItNow line 30 computes checkActive("sale"), which is 111 for an ended AUCTION too; line 40 falls to 500, 510 (nfa.bas:183) sends the unit back to LOAD("owner") at 520, zeroes scBalance and calls resetVars(1). resetVars clears startBlockTime, endBlockTime, bidCount, startPrice, currBidPrice and listType — but NOT currBidAddr or currBidAmt, which only outbidReturns and processHighestBidder ever delete. The caller need attach nothing (line 920 with ASSETVALUE 0 just returns) and need only be a non-owner. Afterwards scBalance is 0 and listType is "", so checkActive returns 999 or 900 for every entrypoint: the winner's Bid, BuyItNow, CloseListing and CancelListing all refuse or no-op, and CloseListing/CancelListing are owner-only anyway. The winner's payment sits in the contract until the OWNER voluntarily starts a fresh auction and someone bids (outbidReturns refunds it) or an empty Bid settles an expired new auction to the stale bidder at the stale price. The owner has the asset back and no incentive to do either.
- **Preconditions** A live NFA auction with at least one bid, and one block after endBlockTime. The attacker needs a registered DERO address, one ring-2 transaction and gas. No stake, no relationship to the asset.
- **Impact** The winning bidder's payment — denominated in the market's quote, i.e. wXMR or wZEC on a wrapped-quote NFA — is locked in the contract indefinitely and released only by an action of a party who benefits from not taking it. Reproduced with 5,000,000 quote units. The asset returns to the seller, so the seller can then sell it to a third party and hold the first bidder's money hostage: the only remaining release is a NEW auction, and whoever owns the unit by then controls whether it ever happens. This is a state that needs a specific human to leave it, which directive §5 forbids by construction.
- **Exploitability** Trivial and cheap: one contract call with no value attached, mempool-visible timing, repeatable against every auction on the platform. Griefing rather than theft (the attacker gains nothing), but the seller is a natural beneficiary and can have an accomplice make the call.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestAStrangerCanStrandAnAuctionWinnersPayment (wrapped quote, royalty 10, protoFee 250). $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'StrangerCanStrand' -v === RUN   TestAStrangerCanStrandAnAuctionWinnersPayment     nfa.bas install (wrapped=true): 13859 bytes of code, storage gas 14861     STRANDED: 5000000 of the quote asset held by the NFA, owed to alice, with the seller holding the unit again. The only release is the owner starting a NEW auction and someone bidding (outbidReturns), or an empty Bid on an expired new auction, which would hand alice the asset at her old price. --- PASS The test asserts: the unit went to the seller, alice was refunded
- **Fix** Contract (needs a redeploy policy — nfa.bas is a hashed shipped template, so changing it rotates app/config.js templates.nfa and the legacy list): the activeFlag==111 branch of BuyItNow must call outbidReturns() before line 520, exactly as CancelListing does at nfa.bas:250, so any standing bid is returned in the same call; or make that branch call processHighestBidder() when bidCount>0, which is what CloseListing does and what the caller's intent implies. Safe now without a redeploy: app/nfas.js should stop offering an ended auction's 'Buy' action to non-owners and should warn a bidder that an ended auction they lead can be closed to the seller by anyone. Note this branch is inherited verbatim from ART-NFA-MS1 v1.1.1, so the fix is a deliberate divergence from the standard and should be recorded in docs/NFA.md alongside the quote/protoFee divergences.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go TestAStrangerCanStrandAnAuctionWinnersPayment — invert its assertions once fixed: after the stranger's call, r.Sent(quote, alice) must equal the bid and SCBalance(nfa, quote) must be 0.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/nfas.js, docs/NFA.md, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** docs/NFA.md §7 R4 (open) and docs/BACKLOG.md BL-68. The backlog UNDERSTATES it: it frames the case as 'the bidder settles' (an empty Bid closes the auction to the leader), which is the benign branch. It does not record that a THIRD PARTY can force the other branch — BuyItNow's activeFlag==111 path — which returns the unit to the seller and leaves currBidAmt/currBidAddr set, after which the bidder has no call at all that reaches outbidReturns or processHighestBidder.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-03 — One signer's Paying mark permanently destroys the vault's only permissionless exit, so a withdrawal in flight when the federation drops below quorum is stuck forever

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:444` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** Paying (vault.bas:438-448) requires one active signer, no vote, no DEROVALUE, no w_<j>_rel check and no halt check. Line 444 makes it one-shot: once w_<j>_p exists the function returns early, so the mark can never be refreshed — and never cleared, because nothing writes or deletes that key anywhere else in the file. Reclaim (vault.bas:479) refuses for ever when the key exists. From then on the only exits are Released and Veto, both k-of-n. Slash (vault.bas:551) refuses when na < k+1, so a federation can be walked down to na == k (by legitimate slashes, or attrition) and then never remove or replace anyone; AddSigner2 also needs k votes. At na == k, the loss of ONE key makes every k-of-n vote unreachable for the life of the contract. Every marked withdrawal is then frozen at st 0 permanently, its units in the contract and still counted in sup — so the foreign reserve stays committed against a claim nobody can settle. A single compromised or buggy signer key can mark every open burn and every new one as it appears, at gas cost only, converting the whole vault to 'k live signers required, for ever'.
- **Preconditions** A sealed vault. One active signer key (honest daemon behaviour is sufficient — vaultsigner marks before paying). Then any later reduction of live signers below k: two lost keys in a 2-of-3, or one lost key after the set has been slashed down to na == k.
- **Impact** Permanent loss of the burner's wrapped units with no recovery by anyone: no owner exists post-Seal, no timeout applies, no third party can act. sup is never reduced, so the federation's reserve stays encumbered by the dead claim and honest signers' reserve>=sup checks keep counting it. This is the exact failure the vault's design comment says cannot happen, and it defeats the directive's requirement that the operator be able to walk away.
- **Exploitability** The freeze half is free and unilateral for any one signer. The permanent-loss half needs the federation to fall below quorum, which the vault has no mechanism to prevent or recover from — Slash's na>=k+1 floor guarantees a k-of-n vault reaches an unrecoverable configuration after enough attrition.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestPayingMarkStrandsABurnForeverWhenTheQuorumIsLost. 2-of-3 vault, sup 2000, burn 500. $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'PayingMarkStrands' -v === RUN   TestPayingMarkStrandsABurnForeverWhenTheQuorumIsLost     STUCK: burn 0 st=0 (open) at height 1020001 with 1 live signer and k=2; Reclaim is refused for ever by the Paying mark and Veto/Released can never reach quorum. 500 units are in the contract and sup is still 2000. --- PASS Asserted along the way: Paying succeeds in the burn's own block (w_0_p == burn height, long before w_0_rel); Slash is refused once na == k; Reclaim is refused at w_0_rel + 20000 + 1,000,000 blocks; single-signer V
- **Fix** Needs a redeploy policy (vault.bas is installed by tools, not hash-verified by the app, but a live vault cannot be changed). Give the mark an expiry instead of making it eternal: Reclaim should refuse only while BLOCK_HEIGHT() < w_<j>_p + N (the same 2000-block window Veto already uses at vault.bas:459, or a longer one), so a marked-but-unsettled withdrawal returns to the permissionless path once the window the mark was meant to protect has passed. That preserves the double-pay protection the comment at vault.bas:429-437 argues for — the window exists precisely because a payment takes at most that long — while restoring the liveness the header claims. Also reconsider Slash's na>=k+1 floor: a vault that reaches na == k is permanently unable to heal. Safe now without touching the contract: audit tools/vaultsigner so it does not mark speculatively (only immediately before submitting the foreign payment), and make deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md and app/wrap.js state plainly that a marked withdrawal has no automatic exit and depends on k signers remaining alive.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go TestPayingMarkStrandsABurnForeverWhenTheQuorumIsLost — after a fix it must be inverted: Reclaim at w_0_p + N must succeed and pay w_0_by. Keep the existing tests/vault_paying_test.go cases so the fresh-mark protection is not lost.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, tools/vaultsigner/main.go, app/wrap.js, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** Related to SEC·RT-09 (residual) and docs/BACKLOG.md BL-83. Both UNDERSTATE it. BL-83 records that 'Reclaim returns unmarked burns'; it does not record that tools/vaultsigner marks EVERY withdrawal before paying, so in normal operation no burn ever retains the permissionless exit. tests/vault_paying_test.go TestAMarkedWithdrawalThatIsNeverPaidUnfreezesItself asserts the recovery path only while k signers are still live, which is the assumption in question. The contract's own header (vault.bas:4-9) states 'no state needs a human to leave it' and 'A burn that no signer marks as being paid returns its units by itself' — the second sentence is true and the first is false.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-04 — A vault Burn naming a valid-form but unregistered refund address can never be returned by any route

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired in part** in `099a4b1`: this app always names the connected wallet as the refund address. The contract still accepts a burn to an address the chain has never seen; refusing it changes what a withdrawal is worth, which is the operator's decision (`tests/vault_repair_test.go` records why).
- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:378` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** Burn (vault.bas:378-379) accepts `refund` after ADDRESS_RAW + IS_ADDRESS_VALID, which decompress the point and say nothing about whether the account exists on the DERO chain, and stores the raw bytes in w_<j>_by (line 395). Both routes that give the units back address their single transfer to that key: Veto at vault.bas:465 and Reclaim at :482. Because each emits exactly one external transfer, SanityCheckExternalTransfers's final lookup error is what the function returns (vendor/derohe/dvm/simulator.go:229-237), so the whole call is refused and reverted every time, at every height, for every caller and for any quorum. The withdrawal stays at st 0 for ever. The only entrypoint that still functions is Released, which reduces sup and pays the burner nothing.
- **Preconditions** A Burn whose `refund` argument is a well-formed DERO address that has never registered on chain. Burn is callable at any ring size and never reads SIGNER(), so the refund address is freely chosen and need not be the caller's. app/wrap.js:483 always passes me() (the connected, therefore registered, wallet), so the app path is safe; any foreign tool, a hand-built call, or a user naming a freshly generated wallet that has not yet registered hits it.
- **Impact** Permanent loss of the burned wrapped units. Reproduced with 500 units on a sup of 2000. The units stay in the contract, sup still counts them, and no party — burner, stranger, quorum, treasury — has a call that can move them. Unlike LIV-01 this fails closed rather than half-applying, so nothing is mis-accounted; it is a pure, unrecoverable lock.
- **Exploitability** Self-inflicted, not an attack on a third party (Burn's refund is chosen by whoever burns). Severity comes from the silence: the contract accepts the address, the burn succeeds, the withdrawal looks normal on chain for wdelay + 20000 blocks, and only the eventual Reclaim reveals it.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestBurnRefundToAnUnregisteredAddressCanNeverBeReturned. $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'BurnRefundToAnUnregistered' -v === RUN   TestBurnRefundToAnUnregisteredAddressCanNeverBeReturned     Reclaim refused by the chain, not by the contract: leaf not found: collision, keyhash fa64ebc4... not found ...: leaf not found     STUCK: burn 0 st=0 for ever. 500 units sit in the contract, sup is still 2000, and the only exit the contract still permits (Released) hands the burner nothing. --- PASS Asserted: Burn accepted the ghost address and stored it raw in w_0_by; Reclaim at w_0_rel+20000+1 fails; a full 2-signer Veto fails; st is still 0; a str
- **Fix** The DVM exposes no primitive by which a contract can test registration, so the contract cannot check this itself — the guard must be in the callers, which makes it SAFE NOW. app/wrap.js should resolve the refund address against the node (an account lookup on the canonical address) before building the Burn and refuse an unregistered one; today it hard-codes me(), so add the check as a guard and never let the burn form accept a typed refund address. contracts/README.md (vault section) and deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md must state that `refund` has to be an address that has already transacted on DERO, and tools/vaultsigner should surface a burn whose w_<j>_by is unregistered as unpayable so the federation knows to Release rather than wait. A contract-side change — defaulting the refund to SIGNER() at ring 2 — would need a redeploy and would defeat the deliberate ring-agnostic privacy design at vault.bas:372-374, so it is an operator decision, not a safe fix.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go TestBurnRefundToAnUnregisteredAddressCanNeverBeReturned pins the contract behaviour. Add a JS test under tests/js that app/wrap.js refuses to build a Burn when the refund lookup comes back unregistered.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/wrap.js, contracts/README.md, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, tools/vaultsigner/main.go, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** Listed as a risk by the vault architecture reader ('Burn validates refund only as a curve point (379); an unregistered DERO address makes every Veto/Reclaim fail') but reasoned only, and NOT in docs/BACKLOG.md nor in any prior audit. The reader's mechanism is also slightly wrong: it attributes the failure to SanityCheckExternalTransfers, which in fact only rejects it because this call emits exactly one transfer and the check returns the LAST lookup's error (simulator.go:230, named return). That accident is what makes this fail closed rather than half-apply like LIV-01.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-01 — Padding the shared htlc.bas contract permanently blinds every companion's chain reads, and converts into theft of the maker's DERO by Punish

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/dero.go:411` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** Every chain read the companion makes goes through DeroRPC.getSC (dero.go:409-411), which is DERO.GetSC with `variables:true` — the WHOLE contract — and call() reads at most 8 MB (dero.go:137) before json.Unmarshal, so an oversized answer is truncated and every read fails. htlc.bas Lock is permissionless: any address may Lock one atomic unit of DERO for any taker, and each lock writes ten keys (m_/t_/ha_/hk_/hb_/a_/v_/t1_/t2_/st_) that nothing ever deletes — the contract has no prune, no owner and no admin. Measured on the wire, one lock is 672 bytes of GetSC, so ≈12,500 locks pass the 8 MB cap and every companion on that SCID is blinded for ever. The second half is the theft: Bob's refund path reads the lock before it refunds (swap.go:810-813 `l, err := s.dero.ReadLock(...); if err != nil { return err }`), so a blinded maker never sends Refund; cmd/xmrswap/main.go's loop only logs the error and sleeps. At t2 the taker takes the whole locked amount with Punish, which needs no secret, no companion and no XMR — only the lock id and her own wallet.
- **Preconditions** htlc.bas deployed once and shared (the design: docs/XMR_SWAP.md; simhtlc installs a fresh one per run today, so this bites at the moment the contract becomes a fixed SCID in config). Attacker needs ~12,500 Lock transactions. Measured cost on the simulator this session: one Lock moved Bob's balance by 2,858 atomic units of which 2,000 was the burn, so ≈859 units of fee per junk lock ⇒ ≈107 DERO one-off for the whole attack, reusable against every maker thereafter. For the theft the maker must not hand-refund inside [t1, t2) — at mainnet policy an 8-hour window, i.e. exactly the unattended case the companion exists for.
- **Impact** (1) Availability: the only trustless DERO↔XMR contract is permanently unusable by the shipped companion — the state is unleavable without redeploying under a new SCID and changing every config, which is precisely the 'state that needs a human to leave it' the directive forbids. (2) Funds: a taker who has deposited nothing extracts the maker's whole locked DERO via Punish, breaking requirement 4 (nobody extracts more than was deposited). (3) Even with no attacker, ordinary use has a hard ceiling: at 672 bytes per lock the contract dies at ~12,500 lifetime swaps.
- **Exploitability** Cheap, permissionless, permanent and repeatable; requires no protocol knowledge and no counterparty cooperation. The companion fails open on the money path (it retries a read error for ever) rather than closed.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo>/tools && go test ./xmrswap -run 'TestWholeContractReadsBreakOnceTheContractIsPadded|TestAPaddedContractLetsTheTakerPunishAMakerWhoCannotRefund' -v  === RUN   TestWholeContractReadsBreakOnceTheContractIsPadded     adversary_test.go:278: one lock is 672 bytes of DERO.GetSC; 24966 locks pass 8 MB     adversary_test.go:284: with 24967 locks ReadLock says: DERO.GetSC: decode: unexpected end of JSON input     adversary_test.go:289: Lock says: DERO.GetSC: decode: unexpected end of JSON input --- PASS === RUN   TestAPaddedContractLetsTheTakerPunishAMakerWhoCannotRefund     adversary_test.go:335: bob locked 5000 atomic units, t1 1053 t2 1063, height 1007     adversary_t
- **Fix** Stop reading the whole contract. derohe's DERO.GetSC accepts `keysstring` (vendor/derohe/rpc/daemon_rpc.go:238): ReadLock(id) should request exactly the ten keys of that id, and FindLock should read `n` and then walk ids downward with per-id keysstring reads under a bounded budget (the maker only ever needs the last few). Separately, make Bob's refund path fail closed: when ReadLock errors inside [t1, t2) the companion should still submit Refund — the contract refuses it harmlessly if the lock has already moved — rather than returning the read error and doing nothing while the window closes. Raise the 8 MB body cap only as a secondary measure; it buys time, not safety.
- **Regression test** Keep TestWholeContractReadsBreakOnceTheContractIsPadded, inverted: with the contract padded past any cap, ReadLock(0)/FindLock/Refund must still work. Keep TestAPaddedContractLetsTheTakerPunishAMakerWhoCannotRefund, inverted: with a blind Dero, Bob's lock must reach LockRefunded before t2.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/dero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/dero_test.go, tools/xmrswap/adversary_test.go
- **Already known as** NOT in docs/BACKLOG.md. BL-19 ("one swap = one HTLC id") is the nearest entry and does not mention contract growth or whole-contract reads at all; the backlog therefore does not merely understate this, it omits it. tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go (another agent, this session) proves the second half independently: past t2 only the taker can move the funds.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-02 — The maker locks twice when the first Lock is still in the mempool, and the taker claims both with one s_a and one k_b

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/dero.go:577` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** DeroRPC.Lock's idempotency is FindLock (dero.go:556) over DERO.GetSC, which shows executed contract state only — a transaction sitting in the mempool is invisible to it. Lock submits, waitTx times out after Timeout (3 min default), and Lock returns an error. swap.go:658-679 `case "dero_locking"` returns that error without saving anything, so the next Step (5 s later, cmd/xmrswap/main.go's loop) re-enters dero_locking; the LockSettleBlocks wait at swap.go:659-669 guards only `resumedPhase == "dero_locking"`, i.e. a restart, not an in-process retry. FindLock still sees nothing, a second Lock goes out with the same ha/hk/hb/taker/t1/t2/amount, and htlc.bas has no uniqueness rule, so both mine as separate ids. st.Lock.ID records only one. The taker is the named taker on both, both are open, both are before t1, and the same s_a and k_b satisfy both — she claims both.
- **Preconditions** A Lock transaction that does not mine within DeroRPC.Timeout: congestion, a fee below what miners take, a wallet or daemon stall, or a lying/slow node. No counterparty cooperation needed; the taker only has to notice a second lock carrying her hashes, which is a public read.
- **Impact** The maker pays 2x the agreed DERO for one Monero payment of the agreed size. His companion tracks one id and never refunds the other; the taker sweeps both. Direct violation of requirement 4.
- **Exploitability** Not attacker-triggerable end to end by the taker alone, but a hostile or merely broken node the maker names can hold his transaction and force the double at will; the taker's half is trivial once it happens.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo>/tools && go test ./xmrswap -run 'TestLockIsDoubledWhenTheFirstTransactionIsStillInThePool|TestTheDoubledLockIsClaimedTwiceWithOneSecret' -v  === RUN   TestLockIsDoubledWhenTheFirstTransactionIsStillInThePool     first Lock: tx 94b0b5d8... not mined within 150ms     second Lock recorded id 0     lock 0: ha 19c3173f taker 1f4e4d3b amount 5000 t1 1050 st 0     lock 1: ha 19c3173f taker 1f4e4d3b amount 5000 t1 1050 st 0 --- PASS === RUN   TestTheDoubledLockIsClaimedTwiceWithOneSecret     one s_a and one k_b claimed both locks: the taker took 10000 atomic units for one Monero payment of the agreed size --- PASS  The test's poolDaemon parks the first transfer in the 
- **Fix** Three layers, all local: (1) record the submitted txid durably before waitTx (a `Lock.Tx` set at submit time, saved by the caller) and, on a not-mined error, have the next attempt wait for that txid rather than re-submitting; (2) make FindLock's miss non-authoritative when a txid is outstanding — poll DERO.GetTransaction for the recorded txid until it is mined or provably gone from the pool; (3) apply the existing LockSettleBlocks wait to the in-process retry, not only to `resumedPhase == "dero_locking"`. A contract-side uniqueness rule (refuse a Lock whose (maker, ha, hk, hb) already exists) would be belt and braces but needs a redeploy, so it is not the fix to make now.
- **Regression test** TestLockIsDoubledWhenTheFirstTransactionIsStillInThePool inverted: after the pooled first attempt and the retry, exactly one lock exists on the chain and Lock returns its id. Add a fakes_test memDero variant with a pool so the crash fuzz can actually reach this branch.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/dero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/state.go, tools/xmrswap/adversary_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. The crash fuzz cannot see it: memDero.Lock (fakes_test.go) dedupes against c.locks atomically, i.e. the fake models every transaction as instantly executed, so the fuzz's own `len(chain.locks) != 1` assertion (swap_test.go check()) is satisfied by construction.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-03 — The maker's timelocks have a floor and no ceiling: the taker's companion locks her XMR against a t1 up to ~208 days away

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:898` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** Alice's offer check (swap.go:979) tests only `got.T1Delta < s.cfg.T1Min`, and CheckLock (swap.go:898-916) tests only `l.T1-now < e.T1Min`; neither has an upper bound, and the second sight at swap.go:1063-1069 only halves the same floor. htlc.bas:33 admits `t2 <= h + 1000000`. So Bob offers T1Delta = 900,000, Alice's companion accepts the offer, verifies the lock, waits StableBlocks and sends her XMR to the joint address. From that moment she has no exit of her own before t1: she cannot claim without k_b, she cannot Refund (maker only), and Punish is only at t2. Bob may release k_b at any moment before t1 — taking her XMR at the price fixed months earlier — or simply refund at t1 and hand her back the same XMR. That is a free American call on her coins for as long as he chose, at zero cost and with no bond.
- **Preconditions** Bob runs a companion with -t1 set high (the flag is accepted; the only floor check is `cfg.T1Delta < cfg.T1Min` at swap.go:145). Alice runs the shipped defaults. No node, chain or timing anomaly is needed.
- **Impact** The taker's XMR is immobilised for up to ~208 days at 18 s blocks while only the counterparty decides whether the trade happens, and he decides with hindsight. Economic loss equal to the option's value, borne entirely by the taker. It also breaks the docs' own trust statement, which frames the maker as the party with time at risk.
- **Exploitability** Trivial: one flag on the maker's side. The taker's companion prints the lock's t1 but refuses nothing.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo>/tools && go test ./xmrswap -run TestTakerLocksXmrAgainstAnUnboundedT1 -v  === RUN   TestTakerLocksXmrAgainstAnUnboundedT1     adversary_test.go:82: alice locked 1000000000000 piconero against a lock whose t1 is 899993 blocks away and t2 900003 blocks away: her XMR is immobile until then and only bob decides --- PASS
- **Fix** Add T1Max/T2Max to Config (defaults: the honest numbers are the ones §1 argues, e.g. 1,200 and 1,200 blocks) and test them in both places: the offer check at swap.go:979 (`got.T1Delta > cfg.T1Max` → refuse) and CheckLock (`l.T1-now > e.T1Max` → refuse), at first and second sight. Print the window in hours before the XMR goes out.
- **Regression test** TestTakerLocksXmrAgainstAnUnboundedT1 inverted: with T1Delta = 900000 the taker must end in phase "refused" with zero Monero transfers. Plus a table test on CheckLock over t1 at the floor, inside the band, and above the ceiling.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, tools/xmrswap/adversary_test.go
- **Already known as** Partly BL-18 ("Rate and slippage… a price move between setup and t1"), which assumes t1 is the policy's 400 blocks (~2 h). BL-18 badly understates it: the window is bounded only by htlc.bas's 1,000,000 blocks. BL-25 is the mirror case (Bob's DERO idle until t1) and does not cover this. docs/XMR_SWAP.md §3 and §6 analyse only the taker's free option, not the maker's.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-01 — One signer key permanently removes the vault's permissionless Reclaim exit for every withdrawal, present and future

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:438` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** Paying(j) (vault.bas:438-448) is gated only by issigner(SIGNER()) and w_<j>_st == 0. There is no vote (no votek call), no cost beyond gas, no check against w_<j>_rel, and no halt check. Reclaim (vault.bas:475-485) refuses forever once w_<j>_p EXISTS (line 479), and Paying can never clear or refresh it (line 24 returns early). So a single compromised, coerced or buggy signer daemon iterates j = 0 .. wn-1 calling Paying, and repeats for each new burn as it appears. From then on every withdrawal can only leave via Released or Veto, both of which need k signer votes.
- **Preconditions** One signer private key (1 of n; k not required). The vault sealed. Gas only. Works while halted.
- **Impact** The contract header (vault.bas:4-9) states "LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION: after Seal no privileged party exists or is needed, and no state needs a human to leave it" and "A burn that no signer marks as being paid returns its units by itself". One key falsifies both. After the mark, if the federation ever falls below k live signers — key loss, a slash war down to na == k, jurisdictional pressure, or a quorum that stops answering — every marked burn is stuck permanently: the units sit in the contract, sup never falls, and the foreign reserve stays locked behind them. The user has burned their wXMR/wZEC and can get neither coins nor units back.
- **Exploitability** Trivial and cheap. A signer host compromise is the only requirement, and the daemon already marks every withdrawal on its normal path (tools/vaultsigner/main.go processWithdrawal calls markPaying before every payment), so in practice EVERY withdrawal is already marked and the permissionless exit is dead in normal operation, not only under attack.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestOneSignerKeyPermanentlyRemovesTheReclaimExit.    cd <repo>   tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestOneSignerKeyPermanentlyRemovesTheReclaimExit' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestOneSignerKeyPermanentlyRemovesTheReclaimExit       zz_adversary_priv_test.go:74: ADV-1 CONFIRMED: one signer marked every open withdrawal at height 1; at height 5025001 Reclaim is still refused for both and only k votes can settle them   --- PASS: TestOneSignerKeyPermanentlyRemovesTheReclaimExit (0.04s)   ok  github.com/deroproject/derohe/veil/tests 0.940s  The test: 2-of-3 vault, two burns, signer sg[0] alone calls Paying on both in the burn's own
- **Fix** Two options, both needing an operator ruling because they change the safety/liveness trade-off the contract was designed around. (a) Bound the mark: store w_<j>_p only when BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= w_<j>_rel, and let Reclaim succeed at w_<j>_p + N (N > the 2000-block Veto window) rather than refusing forever — the exit is then delayed by one window per mark instead of deleted. (b) Require two signers to mark (votek with a threshold of 2), so one key cannot do it. Either way the header's liveness claim and wrap.js's withdrawal copy must be re-read against the new rule. This is a change to an immutable contract, so it is a redeploy decision, not a patch.
- **Regression test** tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestOneSignerKeyPermanentlyRemovesTheReclaimExit already pins today's behaviour and will fail the moment either fix lands — at which point it should be inverted to assert that Reclaim does come back.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, app/wrap.js, tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go
- **Already known as** Related to SEC·RT-09 and BL-83. Both understate it: BL-83 says "Reclaim returns unmarked burns" and RT-09 was closed as "Paying is one-shot by design". Neither records that the mark needs no vote, no fee, no delay and never expires, so one key deletes the exit for the life of the vault.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-02 — factory.bas ownership cannot be revoked: the mainnet revocation ceremony BL-241/BL-147/D-10 plans is impossible, and attempting it leaves the old key in full control behind a misleading on-chain 'pending transfer'

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`. `Renounce()` deletes `owner` and records `renounced`. After it, SetTreasury and SetRegFee refuse every caller. TransferOwnership and ClaimOwnership are removed. `owner` is deleted rather than overwritten, because a signer above ring 2 is 33 zero bytes (`contracts/factory.bas`). `TestFactoryRenounce` calls every setter afterwards, as the old owner, a stranger and an anonymous caller, and each is refused.

- **Where** `contracts/factory.bas:45` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** TransferOwnership (factory.bas:45-53) only writes tmpowner; the transfer completes when the NEW key signs ClaimOwnership (factory.bas:55-62, `IF SIGNER() != LOAD("tmpowner") THEN GOTO 900`). A burn address cannot sign. So the ceremony leaves owner unchanged with SetTreasury and SetRegFee live for ever, while the chain now shows a tmpowner key that a reader may take for a completed handover. The old owner can also overwrite tmpowner at will, so even the pending state is not a commitment.
- **Preconditions** None beyond the operator following their own documented mainnet runbook.
- **Impact** The directive's central premise — "the operator revokes their own access after deployment" — is unattainable for the factory. Whoever holds that key keeps, for ever: SetTreasury, which repoints the address the app compares every market against (feed.js reads only `tstr`), delisting every existing market from the app in one call and redirecting all future regfees; and SetRegFee, which is unbounded (see SEC-K-06). Post-'revocation' the only real revocation is destroying the key, which nobody can verify on chain and which the app never shows.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack — a structural impossibility that the launch plan currently treats as a scheduled step. The exploit case is a stolen or retained key after the operator believes access was given up.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go:TestFactoryOwnershipCannotBeRevoked.    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestFactoryOwnershipCannotBeRevoked' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestFactoryOwnershipCannotBeRevoked       zz_adversary_owner_test.go:45: ADV-5 CONFIRMED: a transfer to an unclaimable address leaves the old key in full control; only a transfer to a LIVE key ever removes it, so no no-owner state exists (BL-241 / BL-147 / D-10 plan a ceremony the code cannot perform)   --- PASS: TestFactoryOwnershipCannotBeRevoked (0.02s)  The test transfers to an address it never signs with, then successfully calls SetTreasury and SetRegFee from the old owner, then re-arms tmpowner to a dif
- **Fix** Two parts. (1) Docs, safe now: rewrite BL-241, BL-147, D-9 and D-10 to say that factory ownership has no renounce and that 'revocation' means destroying a key, unverifiable on chain — matching app/notice.js:67 — and remove 'the documented no-owner state' from deploy/README.md's mainnet runbook. (2) Contract, redeploy decision: add `Renounce()` that stores owner to a value SIGNER() can never equal (e.g. the 33 zero bytes, since IS_ADDRESS_VALID rejects them and ring>2 already fails closed), and have the app read and display factory `owner`. Part 2 changes an immutable contract, so it is a redeploy policy call.
- **Regression test** tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go:TestFactoryOwnershipCannotBeRevoked pins the current behaviour; invert it once a Renounce path exists.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/factory.bas, docs/BACKLOG.md, deploy/README.md, app/screens.js, app/feed.js, tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go
- **Already known as** BL-241 (A), BL-147 (C), D-9, D-10, BL-93. All four understate it: BL-147 says "the pending owner can be a burn address (the revocation path)" and BL-241 plans "TransferOwnership on every owned contract to the documented no-owner state". The code has no such state. app/notice.js:67 is the only honest statement in the tree ("There is no renounce, only a transfer to another key") and it contradicts the backlog.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-03 — The TELA INDEX publisher key can republish the whole app for ever, and the only version indicator the app shows the user is a hand-edited constant no tool writes and nothing checks against the chain

- **Where** `tools/telainstall/main.go:249` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** telainstall installs the INDEX with tela.Installer(w, 2, idx) — ring size 2 is hard-coded (main.go:249, and :259 for updates). TELA-INDEX-1.bas:31-37 stores owner = ADDRESS_STRING(SIGNER()), which is a real address at ring 2 and the literal string "anon" only above ring 2; UpdateCode (:52-62) calls UPDATE_SC_CODE and is refused only when owner == "anon". There is no renounce entrypoint. So whoever holds the deployer wallet can replace every app file for every user, for ever, and the app builds every transaction the user's wallet is asked to sign. Meanwhile the version the user sees comes from `<meta name="veil-commit" content="14">` (app/index.html:9), read by app/app.js:54 and app/screens.js:239 — a repository constant. Grep confirms no tool writes it, and the app never reads the INDEX's on-chain `commit` or `owner`, nor factory `owner` (feed.js registry_ reads only pc, lc, nc, regfee, tstr). A substituted bundle therefore displays whatever commit number it likes.
- **Preconditions** For substitution: the deployer wallet key. For the verification gap: none — it is the shipped state.
- **Impact** The single largest privilege in the system, and the one users have no way to check. notice.js:70 discloses it honestly ("whoever holds that key can publish a new version of this app until the key is thrown away. Settings shows the commit you are running") — but the commit Settings shows is not evidence of anything. Both facts the user most needs (is the publisher key gone? am I on the version that was audited?) are on chain and free to read with one DERO.GetSC, and the app reads neither.
- **Exploitability** The substitution needs one key. The verification gap is live today and demonstrable: deploy/tela-deployment.json records the simulator INDEX at commit 14 and deploy/tela-testnet.json records the testnet INDEX f20511a8… at commit 0, while a single constant "14" ships to both — so the stamp is already wrong for at least one live network.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -n 'tela.Installer(w, 2' tools/telainstall/main.go      -> :232 (DOC), :249 (INDEX)   sed -n '31,37p;52,62p' vendor/tela/TELA-INDEX-1/TELA-INDEX-1.bas   -> owner = address(), "anon" above ring 2; UpdateCode owner-only; no renounce   grep -rn 'veil-commit' --include=*.go --include=*.sh --include=*.mjs --include=*.js . | grep -v vendor     -> app/app.js:54, app/screens.js:239, and the dist/ copies. READ ONLY — no writer.   grep -n '"commit"\|"index"' deploy/tela-deployment.json deploy/tela-testnet.json     -> simulator index ece54602… commit 14; testnet index f20511a8… commit 0   grep -n 'const keys = ' app/feed.js  -> :124  ["pc","lc","nc","regfee","tstr"]  (no "owner") Not performed: 
- **Fix** Two parts. (1) Safe now, app-side: in Settings, read the INDEX SCID from config, fetch its `commit` and `owner` with DERO.GetSC, and show both — "TELA commit N (from chain)" and "Publisher: <address> / none" — instead of the meta tag; likewise show factory `owner`/`tmpowner`. Delete the veil-commit meta so a stale constant cannot be shown. (2) Operator decision: give telainstall a -ring flag and install the FINAL mainnet INDEX above ring 2 so owner is "anon" and UpdateCode is dead, or document explicitly that the key survives and is only ever destroyed off chain. Part 2 is a deployment-policy call.
- **Regression test** A JS test under tests/js that renders the Settings security section against a stubbed node and asserts the displayed commit equals the value the node returned for the INDEX (not a DOM constant); plus a grep test in tests/run.sh that fails if `veil-commit` reappears in app/.
