- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: app/screens.js, app/feed.js, app/app.js, app/index.html, tools/telainstall/main.go, deploy/README.md
- **Already known as** REQ·A8-10 (hand-edited commit) and RISKS R-3 (the app is the trust anchor) are open. Both understate it: A8-10 is filed as build hygiene, not as "the sole anti-tamper indicator is attacker-controlled", and no backlog item records that telainstall offers no ring>2 (renounceable) install path at all.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-01 — Any third party ends an NFA auction with an empty BuyItNow: the unit goes back to the owner and the winner's bid is stranded in the contract

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`, with NFA-1: the empty BuyItNow now settles the ended auction to its winner.

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:184` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** checkActive (nfa.bas:288, line 40 precedes line 50) returns 111 for ANY ended listing before it compares listType. BuyItNow therefore takes the 111 branch (nfa.bas:182-186 → 510 → 520-540) on an ended AUCTION: SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(LOAD("owner"), scBalance, SCID()), scBalance=0, resetVars(1). processHighestBidder is never called, and resetVars (nfa.bas:355-368) clears startBlockTime/endBlockTime/bidCount/active/startPrice/currBidPrice/listType/charity — but not currBidAddr or currBidAmt. The winner's quote stays in the contract balance. BuyItNow line 20 turns away only the recorded owner, and the 111 branch is reached before the ASSETVALUE >= startPrice check at line 60, so the call needs nothing attached. An owner who dislikes the winning price makes it from a second wallet the first second after the end.
- **Preconditions** An NFA auction that has ended with at least one bid. Any address other than the recorded owner, one transaction, nothing attached. No special timing beyond "after endBlockTime".
- **Impact** The auction winner receives neither the asset nor their money. The owner keeps the asset. The bid sits in the contract with no path out that the bidder controls: Bid() returns 999 (scBalance is 0), CloseListing and CancelListing both refuse. Recovery requires the OWNER to Start a new listing and a third party to bid into it (outbidReturns), or the owner to Start and CancelListing within 300 s — i.e. the party who profited from the strand is the only one who can undo it. On a wXMR-quoted NFA the stranded amount is federation-backed wrapped Monero. Alternatively, if the owner later Starts a new auction and anyone calls an empty Bid() after its end, processHighestBidder settles to the STALE currBidAddr at the STALE currBidAmt — the original bidder is forced to buy at a price from a listing that no longer exists.
- **Exploitability** Trivial and free: one ring-2 call with no value attached, from any address, in a window that opens automatically and never closes. No race, no capital, no privileged key.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestNFAEndedAuctionBuyItNowStrandsTheWinningBid and ran:    $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestNFAEndedAuction' -v   === RUN   TestNFAEndedAuctionBuyItNowStrandsTheWinningBid       zzadv_market_test.go:21: nfa.bas install (wrapped=true): 13859 bytes of code, storage gas 14860, compute gas 1051600       zzadv_market_test.go:69: stranded 1000000000 of the quote; recovered only because the owner relisted and a third party bid   --- PASS: TestNFAEndedAuctionBuyItNowStrandsTheWinningBid (0.02s)  The test asserts, in order: creator Starts a 1-hour auction at 1,000,000,000 of the wrapped quote; alice bids exactly that and becomes currBidAddr; the cl
- **Fix** Contract change, and it is a deliberate divergence from "kept exactly" (docs/NFA_BUILD.md §1), so it needs the operator's ruling — NFA.md R4 already puts that choice to them. Minimal form: in checkActive, compare listType BEFORE the ended test (swap lines 40 and 50), so BuyItNow on an ended auction returns 999 and refunds rather than taking the 111 branch; or, in BuyItNow's 111 branch, call processHighestBidder() when LOAD("listType") == "auction" and bidCount > 0 instead of returning the unit to the owner. Until that ships, the app must not present an auction as settled-by-anyone: nfas.js should show the held bid, name who can release it, and refuse to render a "Settle" action that a bidder cannot in fact perform.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestNFAEndedAuctionBuyItNowStrandsTheWinningBid, as written — it fails the moment the strand is closed, and its failure message says so ("the strand is fixed, update the report"). Convert the assertions to the desired behaviour when the fix lands.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/templates/nfa.bas, app/config.js, app/nfas.js, tests/zzadv_market_test.go
- **Already known as** NFA.md §7 R4 (second half) and BL-68. NFA.md states it correctly, including "which the winner cannot recover alone". BL-68 UNDERSTATES it: it says the bid is stranded "until the bidder settles" and files it as priority (B) with an app-side "Held bid" row — the bidder cannot settle, because after resetVars listType is "" and scBalance is 0, so checkActive returns 999 for every entrypoint the bidder can reach.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)


## MEDIUM (49)

### PRIV-03 — Vault Burn is sent at ring 2 with the refund address hard-coded to the connected wallet, and the burn form states that exposure as something the contract requires — which is false

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:387` (dimension: Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares)
- **Attack path** contracts/vault.bas Burn (lines 375-402) never calls SIGNER(): line 20 is `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)`, and the comment above it says so — "refund is where a Veto returns the units, named rather than taken from SIGNER() ... Naming it also lets a burn stay private." The contract was deliberately written so a withdrawal can go out above ring 2. app/wrap.js doBurn (lines 473-486) passes no `ring`, so app/app.js:116 pins it to 2 and app/app.js:134 additionally sends `signer: W.address` in the gas estimate; and it hard-codes `["refund", "S", me()]`, so w_<j>_by is always the connected wallet. One withdrawal therefore publishes the same identity three times over: as the extractable ring-2 signer, as w_<j>_by in contract storage, and to the node in the estimate — next to w_<j>_to, the foreign destination, stored in the clear. The form then tells the user this was unavoidable: "Burn is a ring-size-2 call: the contract must record who burned, so this address is public in that transaction."
- **Preconditions** Any withdrawal made through the app.
- **Impact** An avoidable, permanent DERO-address-to-foreign-address pair on a privacy product's withdrawal path, plus a copy line that stops the user learning there was a choice. A ring-16 burn naming a fresh refund address would have published a foreign destination and an unrelated DERO address, and nothing linking either to the user's trading wallet.
- **Exploitability** Passive: read w_<j>_by and w_<j>_to from the vault, and DERO.GetTransaction on the burn transaction returns the signer (demonstrated for ring-2 calls in PRIV-01).
- **Reproduced** no — Read, not run against a vault — no wallet range was assigned to me, so I sent no transaction.   contracts/vault.bas:375-402 — Burn; line 20 `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)`; SIGNER() appears nowhere in the function; lines 41,44 STORE w_<j>_to and w_<j>_by side by side.   app/wrap.js:473-486 doBurn — args [["dest","S",dst],["refund","S",me()]], no `ring` key (contrast doReclaim at wrap.js:466-472, which does pass ring:16 and explains why in its comment).   app/app.js:116 `const ring = opts.ring === 16 ? 16 : 2;`   app/app.js:134 `... ring === 2 ? { signer: W.address } : {} ...`   app/wrap.js:387-388 the quoted sentence. That sentence is false about this contract, and the contract's own comment a
- **Fix** Split it. Safe now, and do it regardless of the rest: correct app/wrap.js:387 to "This app sends the burn at ring size 2, so your address is public in that transaction. The contract does not require that — it pays the refund address you name — but a ring-16 burn is not safe until vault.bas has a BurnTo wrapper." And add a refund-address field defaulting to the connected wallet, the way the trade sheet offers "receive at" (app/token.js:239-242), so a user can name a fresh address today even at ring 2 — that alone breaks the link to their trading wallet and needs no contract change. NOT safe now, and the reason: setting `ring: 16` on the current Burn would destroy funds. Burn exits via `RETURN 1` on every precondition failure (lines 21-26) and above ring 2 there is no signer for derohe to credit the attached units back to (vendor/derohe/blockchain/transaction_execute.go:396-405), so a below-minimum or over-supply burn at ring 16 loses the user's wrapped asset. Ring 16 requires a `BurnTo` wrapper in vault.bas on the pool.bas:144-152 pattern (refund to the named address on the failure path, RETURN 0) — a contract change and a vault redeploy, an operator decision. Note also that D.vaultWithdrawals() matches w_<j>_by against the connected address, so a non-default refund address needs that list re-keyed or the withdrawal vanishes from the user's own screen.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs: assert the burn form no longer contains "the contract must record who burned", and that a refund-address input is present and prefilled with the connected address. In tests/: a DVM test asserting Burn succeeds with a `refund` that is not the caller and that w_<j>_by equals that named address — pinning the property the app is failing to use. When BurnTo lands, a test that a failing BurnTo above ring 2 returns the units to the named address (the sendorder_test.go discipline).
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: app/wrap.js, contracts/vault.bas, app/feed.js
- **Already known as** SEC·RT-08 (docs/audit/DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:191, detail at FINDINGS.md:1327-1343), P2, CONFIRMED. Re-verified today: still open, unchanged. It has no BL number; DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md §5 item 5 correctly notes the ring-16 fix needs a BurnTo wrapper first. The prior finding is accurate and does not understate.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real, and the false sentence is the load-bearing part. contracts/vault.bas Burn (lines 375-402) never calls SIGNER(): line 20 is `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)`, and the comment two lines above says the naming was deliberate — "Naming it also lets a burn stay private." app/wrap.js doBurn (473-486) har || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Every code claim checks out, but this is SEC·RT-08 (FINDINGS.md:1327-1343, P2, CONFIRMED) re-filed, and MEDIUM overstates the incremental harm. Verified: contracts/vault.bas Burn line 20 is `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)`, SIGNER() appears nowhere in the function, and lines 41/44 store w_<j>_to and w_

### PRIV-04 — The node is told the user's DERO address on ordinary screen loads and before every ring-2 transaction — including trades the user then cancels — undoing the decoy signer the code goes out of its way to use

- **Where** `app/app.js:134` (dimension: Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares)
- **Attack path** Two channels, neither disclosed. (1) Reads: D.myKeys(scid, addr, prefixes) (app/feed.js:364) builds key strings by concatenating the user's bech32 address onto a prefix and sends them to the node — `st_<addr>`/`sd_<addr>` on every staking sheet (app/sheets.js:115), `lk_/lh_/lm_/mt_<addr>` on every holder-NFT page (app/nft.js:15), `uc_<addr>`/`ul_<addr>_<n>` on the Wrap screen (app/wrap.js:56,60), `hu_/hd_<addr>` on Founders, `fc_<me>` on social. Merely opening a screen with a wallet connected tells the node who is looking at what, with no transaction and no chain record. (2) Writes: app/sheets.js:33-47 fetches a random address from the node and uses it as the estimate signer, with an explicit comment — "asking 'what would this cost' must not tell the node who is about to act" — and then app/app.js:134 re-estimates the identical call moments later with `signer: W.address`. The second estimate happens BEFORE the wallet prompt, so a user who fills in a trade sheet and cancels at the wallet dialog has already told the node their address, the market, the entrypoint and the amount for a transaction that never exists and leaves no trace on any chain. Two further details: the decoy at sheets.js:36 is cached in `gasAddr` for the whole session, so it is itself a stable per-session identifier handed to the node; and app/forms.js:221 sends `signer: W.address` too.
- **Preconditions** A wallet connected and any node the user did not personally run — which the app permits (a confirmed wss:// endpoint, or silently the endpoint the wallet reports, app/app.js:128).
- **Impact** A remote node builds a profile the chain does not contain: which markets, NFTs, vaults and Founders positions an address inspects and when, plus every trade the address contemplated and abandoned. On a product sold on privacy this is the surveillance surface the chain was supposed to remove, and the Settings "Security & privacy" list (app/screens.js:257-265) covers only the node's ability to MISREPORT ("a node you do not run can misreport all three") and never its ability to OBSERVE.
- **Exploitability** Passive by whoever runs the node. No collusion. The IP already identifies the browser; this adds the DERO identity and the intent.
- **Reproduced** no — Traced through code, not instrumented in a browser (I ran no UI pass).   app/feed.js:364 `async myKeys(scid, addr, prefixes) { ... this.fetchKeys(scid, prefixes.map((p) => p + addr)) ... }`   app/feed.js:145-149 fetchKeys -> `V.node.getSC(scid, { keys: part })` — the address is inside `keys`.   Call sites: app/sheets.js:115 `D.myKeys(s.scid, me(), ["st_", "sd_"])`; app/nft.js:15 `D.myKeys(scid, me(), ["lk_","lh_","lm_","mt_"])`; app/wrap.js:56 `this.fetchKeys(w.scid, ["uc_" + addr])`.   app/sheets.js:33-36 the decoy and its comment; app/sheets.js:46 `signer: gasAddr`.   app/app.js:132-134 the re-estimate with `signer: W.address` at ring 2 (correctly omitted at ring 16).   app/forms.js:221 `r
- **Fix** The disclosure is local, testable and safe now, and it is the half that must not wait: add a line to the Settings "Security & privacy" list at app/screens.js:257-265 — "Your node sees which contracts and which keys this app reads. Once a wallet is connected, several of those keys contain your address, and the fee estimate for a transaction names you before you approve it — so a node you do not run learns who you are, what you are looking at, and what you were about to do even if you cancel." — and name the node as a party in app/notice.js next to the factory owner and the federation. The estimator change is a separate, smaller change I would not bundle with copy: have app.tx reuse the estimate Sh.gas already computed with the decoy rather than re-asking with the real address, or drop `signer` from the app.tx estimate entirely and confirm the returned gasstorage is unchanged. That needs a test proving the displayed fee and the charged fee still agree (they are currently computed from two different estimates, which is ARCH·G1's other unresolved half), so it should not ride along on a copy fix. Also stop caching `gasAddr` for the session (app/sheets.js:36) — draw a fresh decoy per estimate.
- **Regression test** A JS test under tests/js that loads app/app.js and app/forms.js as text and fails on any `signer: W.address` inside a GetGasEstimate payload, once the estimator change lands; and a uitest assertion that the Settings security list contains the words "your node" and "even if you cancel". For the fee-equivalence half: a test that calls Sh.gas and the app.tx estimate path against the harness for the same swap and asserts equal gasstorage.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/screens.js, app/notice.js, app/sheets.js, app/app.js
- **Already known as** Partly ARCH·G1 (DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:394, FINDINGS.md:1658-1667), filed P3 as "duplicated logic" after the verifier judged the privacy framing collapsed because a ring-2 commit names the signer anyway. That judgement misses the abandoned-transaction case and the read path entirely. BL-130 and BL-202 both say the node sees "which contracts, which keys" — neither notices that the key strings CONTAIN the user's address, nor that the estimate names the payer. Both understate.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Both channels are real and I demonstrated both against the live daemon rather than only reading them. Read path: D.myKeys (app/feed.js:364) concatenates the connected address onto a key prefix and hands the result to V.node.getSC as a `keys` entry (feed.js:145-149), so the address travels inside the || REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real, correctly located, and MEDIUM holds — and one of my checks strengthens it rather than weakening it. Every cited line verified: app/feed.js:364 myKeys concatenates the user's bech32 address onto a prefix and app/feed.js:145-149 fetchKeys puts those strings in `keys` sent to the node; call sites

### UX-04 — The withdraw form blames the contract for publishing the burner's address — vault.bas Burn never reads SIGNER(), so the disclosure is false and it forecloses the private option it should be offering

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/wrap.js:387` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/wrap.js:387-389 renders, above the "Burn and withdraw" button: "Burn is a ring-size-2 call: the contract must record who burned, so this address is public in that transaction. The destination you type is stored on DERO in the clear — anyone can read it. Use an address you are willing to publish." 2. contracts/vault.bas Burn(dest, refund) contains no SIGNER() call at all. Its only address handling is `LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)` / `IF IS_ADDRESS_VALID(s) == 0 THEN GOTO 900` and `STORE("w_"+j+"_by", s)`. The contract's own header comment says why: "refund is where a Veto returns the units, named rather than taken from SIGNER(): above ring 2 SIGNER() is blank, and a revert there cannot address its refund. Naming it also lets a burn stay private." 3. app/wrap.js:483 sends the burn at ring 2 with refund = the connected wallet's canonical address. That is the app's choice — made so a reverted burn refunds the attached asset — not a contract requirement. 4. The consequence: on every withdrawal the burner's DERO address (w_<j>_by) and the foreign destination (w_<j>_to) are published together, in the clear, permanently linked. The user is told this is unavoidable.
- **Preconditions** Any withdrawal. Universal.
- **Impact** On a product whose entire proposition is Monero and Zcash privacy, the single screen where a DERO identity is joined to a foreign-chain address tells the user that join is forced by the protocol. It is not. The contract supports naming a refund address the user does not otherwise use, which would break the link between the DERO wallet that holds the wrapped units and the identity attached to the withdrawal record. Because the app hardcodes refund = me() and explains it as a contract rule, no user will ever look for the alternative. This is the highest-value false statement in the app: it is wrong about a privacy property, on a privacy protocol, in the direction that costs the user privacy.
- **Exploitability** Passive. Anyone reading the vault contract's storage gets a permanent map of (DERO address → foreign address) for every withdrawal ever made. That map is the deanonymisation surface a Monero user came here to avoid.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '370,402p' contracts/vault.bas   → Burn(dest String, refund String); lines 20-26 are ADDRESS_RAW(refund), IS_ADDRESS_VALID, sealed, ASSETVALUE(SCID()), minburn/DEROVALUE, STRLEN(dest), sup. No SIGNER() anywhere in the function.   grep -c 'SIGNER' on the Burn function body → 0.   sed -n '384,392p' app/wrap.js   → the quoted note.   grep -n 'refund' app/wrap.js   → line 483, doBurn sends args [dest, refund=me()] at ring 2.  Rendered: .sim/ui-audit-ux2/wrap.txt — "Burn is a ring-size-2 call: the contract must record who burned, so this address is public in that transaction." Also visible in .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-wrap.png in the WITHDRAW block.
- **Fix** app/wrap.js:387-389, replace the false clause with the true one and offer the option the contract already supports:    "The destination you type is stored on DERO in the clear — anyone can read it. So is the address the units come back to if this withdrawal is vetoed or reclaimed. This app sends the burn at ring size 2 and names your connected wallet as that return address, so a burn that reverts refunds you — which also publishes your wallet next to the destination, permanently. The contract does not require it: it never reads who signed. Use a destination and a return address you are willing to see linked."  And — the substantive half — add an optional "Return address" field to burnForm, defaulted to the connected wallet, with one line: "If a vetoed or reclaimed withdrawal should come back somewhere else, name it here. That address is published instead of this wallet." Keep ring 2 (the revert-refund property is worth having and is the follow-up's own ruling); the privacy gain comes from decoupling the *published* address from the wallet holding the units.  Copy plus one optional input in a form that already validates addresses (doBurn already parses `dest`; `refund` would use V.isAddress, already imported). wrap.js has 3.4 KB of DOC headroom. No contract change, no operator decision.
- **Regression test** tests/js: assert app/wrap.js contains no string matching /contract must record who burned/. In the DVM suite, tests/vault_test.go: add TestBurnNeverReadsSigner — burn from a signer-less context (harness signer==nil, the ring>2 emulation) with a valid `refund`, and assert the burn is recorded with w_<j>_by == ADDRESS_RAW(refund) and succeeds. That test pins the contract property the copy denies, so the two can never drift again.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\wrap.js, tests\vault_test.go
- **Already known as** SEC·RT-08, recorded as open. Prior audits note the mismatch between the copy and the contract; what is not recorded is that the false sentence actively removes the user's private exit — the contract was deliberately built to allow a burn at ring 16 with a named refund address, and the app tells the user that is impossible.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: The false sentence is real and already recorded: SEC·RT-08, P2, CONFIRMED in docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:1327 and in DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:191. contracts/vault.bas Burn (375-402) contains no SIGNER() — line 378 is `20 LET s = ADDRESS_RAW(refund)` — so 'the contract must record who burned' is false a || REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: The sentence is false and I proved it twice on the DVM rather than reading it. But the finder's impact reasoning is partly wrong and their fix is wrong on its substantive half, so this verdict corrects as much as it confirms. (a) TRUE: contracts/vault.bas Burn contains no SIGNER() call; my TestRefut

### UX-05 — "Signers have bonded 0.6 DERO, slashable by a quorum of the others" is shown with a green tick, and is false for the deployed 2-of-3 shape: the quorum that can steal the reserve is the only quorum that could slash it

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/wrap.js:341` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/wrap.js:341-343: `trustLine(w.bondtot > Z, "Signers have bonded " + A.fmtDero(w.bondtot, 2) + " DERO, slashable by a quorum of the others.")` — trustLine renders a green check whenever the first argument is true, i.e. whenever any bond exists at all. 2. contracts/vault.bas Slash: line 23 `IF t == s || issigner(t) == 0 THEN GOTO 900` (cannot slash yourself), line 24-25 `LET na = LOAD("na") / IF na < LOAD("k") + 1 THEN GOTO 900`, line 31 `votek("s", kb, t, s)` requires k votes. 3. The deployed simulator vaults are k=2, na=3. Take signers {A,B,C} with A and B colluding. To slash A, votes must come from signers other than A: only B and C can vote, and k=2 votes are needed. B will not vote. C alone cannot reach the quorum. A and B are permanently unslashable. 4. Symmetrically, A and B *can* slash C (na=3 >= k+1=3). After that na=2 < k+1=3 and Slash is refused for everyone forever. The colluding pair ends holding the whole reserve, unslashable, with their own bonds stranded. 5. Meanwhile the same screen's first line correctly says "2 of the 3 signers can move the whole foreign reserve" — with a red cross. The green tick two lines below claims the counterweight to exactly that, and the counterweight does not exist at n = k+1.  Second, smaller falsehood in the same line: 0.6 DERO is presented as an assurance with no statement of what it backs. The vault's cap is 100 XMR. The screen never puts the two numbers in the same sentence.
- **Preconditions** Any vault sealed with n < 2k. vault.bas Seal (line 248) requires only n >= k+1, so this is a permitted and — on both live simulator vaults — an actual configuration. deploy/federation.md, the artefact that would fix the deployment rule, does not exist.
- **Impact** A depositor is shown a green safeguard that does not hold. The bond is the only thing standing between "a named federation holds your Monero" and "a named federation holds your Monero and loses nothing if it keeps it", and the screen affirms it in the one configuration where it is void. Compounding it, a slashed bond goes to the treasury (vault.bas:49) and never to the depositor who lost coins — so even where slashing works it is not restitution, which the line's phrasing ("slashable") invites the reader to assume.
- **Exploitability** Requires k colluding signers — the same threshold as taking the reserve outright, which is the point: the display claims a deterrent against exactly the actor who is immune to it. Zero additional cost.
- **Reproduced** yes — Read on the rendered screen and traced in source; not driven on chain (I hold read-only wallets and slashing requires signer signatures).    .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-wrap.png, WHAT YOU ARE TRUSTING block — green tick, "Signers have bonded 0.6 DERO, slashable by a quorum of the others." Directly above it, red cross, "2 of the 3 signers can move the whole foreign reserve."   .sim/ui-audit-ux2/wrap.txt reproduces both lines verbatim.   sed -n '325,350p' app/wrap.js → the trustLine call at 341.   sed -n '543,571p' contracts/vault.bas → Slash lines 23, 24-25, 31 as quoted.   Live vault f23403b0…: k=2, na=3, bondtot=60000 atomic (0.6 DERO), cap=1e14 (100 XMR) — read via DERO.GetSC on 127.0.0.1:20000 an
- **Fix** app/wrap.js:341-343. Make the tick conditional on the property actually holding, and put the two numbers in one sentence:    const slashable = w.na >= 2n * w.k;   // a quorum of non-colluders must still exist   trustLine(w.bondtot > Z && slashable,     w.bondtot === Z ? "No signer bond is at stake."     : slashable ? "Signers have bonded " + A.fmtDero(w.bondtot, 2) + " DERO against a mint cap of " + amt(w.cap, 4) + ". A quorum of the others can slash a signer's bond to the treasury — not to you."     : "Signers have bonded " + A.fmtDero(w.bondtot, 2) + " DERO against a mint cap of " + amt(w.cap, 4) + ", but with " + w.na + " signers and a quorum of " + w.k + " the bond is not a deterrent: the " + w.k + " signers who could take the reserve are the same " + w.k + " needed to slash anyone, so they cannot be slashed. A slashed bond goes to the treasury, never to you.")  Copy plus one comparison on values the screen already loads. No contract change and no operator decision — whether to *deploy* n < 2k is the operator's call; whether to tell the user which shape they are looking at is not.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs wrap step: assert that when `window.Veil.data.vault.na < 2n * window.Veil.data.vault.k` the trust-panel line containing "bonded" carries the down/cross class rather than the up/check class. On the current simulator vaults (2-of-3) this fails today. Add the mirror assertion for a stubbed 3-of-6 vault.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\wrap.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** PROTO·BC-4 / PROD·P7-10 / BL-84 record that n>=2k is a deployment rule the contract does not enforce and that slashed bonds go to the treasury. What is not recorded — the architecture map says so explicitly ("the app does not display the na<2k disclosure the follow-up said is shippable") — is that the app renders the unenforced property as an affirmative green check. The backlog understates it: a known contract limitation is being displayed to users as a satisfied safeguard.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real, and I confirmed the precondition on the live chain rather than taking it on trust. DERO.GetSC f23403b0… returns k=2, na=3, bondtot=60000, cap=100000000000000, capratio=2000000000, sealed=1. na=3 < 2k=4, so the vault is in exactly the shape where the bond is not a deterrent. The argument holds  || REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Reproduced end to end, including the part that decides it — the icon class, which the finder asserted from a PNG and I read from the DOM. The bond line renders `svgi up` (green check) while the reserve line two lines above renders `svgi down` (red cross). The arithmetic holds: Slash refuses a self-v

### UX-06 — The chain name is a config sentence used as an inline noun and truncated mid-word: the Wrap screen renders seven broken, unclosed-parenthesis fragments, including the label and placeholder of the field where the user types where their Monero goes

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/wrap.js:203` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/config.js vaults[0].chain = "Monero — simulated on this network (12 decimals, as Monero)" — 55 characters, a whole descriptive clause. 2. app/wrap.js:203 does `chain = U.clean(a.chain || "its own chain", 40)` and then splices `chain` into running prose in eleven places, on the assumption that it is a short chain noun: lines 254 ("Send Monero on " + chain + " to your own deposit address"), 256 ("pays you back on " + chain), 274, 295, 308, 312, 313, 339-340, 404, 431, 435-436, 451. app/wrap.js:360 and :384 use it as the withdraw destination field's placeholder and label with the same 40-char clean. app/screens.js:67 does the same at 44 chars on Portfolio. 3. clean() cuts at 40 characters, landing inside "(12 decimals", producing "Monero — simulated on this network (12 d" — an unclosed parenthesis and a severed word. 4. The result is prose that reads as broken software: "Send Monero on Monero — simulated on this network (12 d to your own deposit address." · "This app cannot see Monero — simulated on this network (12 d, so it cannot show your payment arriving." · field label "DESTINATION ON MONERO — SIMULATED ON THIS NETWORK (12 D" · placeholder "Your address on Monero — simulated on this network (12 d". Portfolio cuts at a different point: "a claim on Monero — simulated on this network (12 decim, held one for one by a k-of-n federation."
- **Preconditions** Any vault whose config `chain` string exceeds 40 characters. Every vault in the shipped config does (all four are 55-70 characters). The simulator strings are simulator-specific, but nothing in the app, in tools/genconfig, or in tools/netcfg constrains the field's length or shape, so a mainnet config that writes "Monero mainnet — 12 decimals" would break the same way at a different cut.
- **Impact** §47 directly: the screen on which a user commits irreversible Monero reads as unfinished software. The two places it is worst are the settlement row in the hero and the withdrawal destination field — the exact places a user checks that they are sending to the right chain. A user who cannot trust that the interface was finished has no basis for trusting the custody statement three lines below it. It also degrades the honesty work everywhere else on that screen, which is otherwise unusually careful.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack, but note the shape: a creator-controlled or deployment-controlled string being spliced into prose with only a length clamp is the same pattern that produces the raw-SCID leak in tools/simnfa/main.go:113 ("priced in quote f23403b0 on the DMZ simulator", rendered on the NFA page). Both are the app trusting a config/tool string to be a noun.
- **Reproduced** yes —   tools/withlock.sh ui env PW_DIR=<playwright> node tools/uitest/ui.mjs http://127.0.0.1:8082/index.html .sim/ui-audit-ux   → UI TEST OK, and the step line itself shows the break: "• wrap: Deposit & withdraw XMR XMR ZEC WRAPPED-ASSET VAULT OPEN XM MONERO — SIMULATED ON THIS NETWORK (12 D → DR DERO"    grep -c 'simulated on this network' .sim/ui-audit-ux2/wrap.txt → 7   grep -o 'Monero — simulated on this network ([^)]*' .sim/ui-audit-ux2/wrap.txt → every occurrence ends "(12 d", none closes the parenthesis.    Screens: .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-wrap.png (seven occurrences incl. the destination field), .sim/ui-audit-ux2/c2-wrap-loaded.png, .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-portfolio.png ("a cla
- **Fix** Two halves, both local.  (1) Give the app a short noun and keep the long string for the one place it belongs. In app/wrap.js:203, derive the inline name from the vault record's `name` ("Monero", "Zcash" — already short and already in config) rather than `chain`: `const chain = U.clean(a.name || "its own chain", 24)`. Use the full `a.chain` string once, unspliced, as the caption under the settlement row, where a clause reads correctly. Same change at app/screens.js:67. This alone fixes all eight renders.  (2) Stop the class of bug rather than this instance: make U.clean's truncation visible instead of silent — when it cuts, append "…" — so any future over-long config string reads as elided rather than as a typo. One line in app/ui.js:23.  Neither touches a contract, a parameter or a config value; if the operator later wants a different mainnet `chain` string, the app no longer cares how long it is.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs wrap step: assert that the rendered #screen text contains no unbalanced '(' — count '(' and ')' in the wrap screen's innerText and require them equal. That single assertion catches this and any future splice of a clause-shaped config value, and it fails today (seven unclosed). Add a netcfg-side check in tools/genconfig that warns when any vaults[].name exceeds 24 characters.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\wrap.js, app\screens.js, app\ui.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog and not in any prior audit. Adjacent to nothing recorded.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: The breakage is real — `grep -o 'simulated on this network' .sim/ui-audit-ux2/wrap.txt | wc -l` returns 7, every occurrence ends '(12 d' with the parenthesis unclosed, and two of them are the withdraw destination field's label and placeholder (app/wrap.js:360, :384). app/config.js:12-15 confirms all || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced exactly and then some — my own render counts 7 truncated occurrences and 10 open parentheses against 0 closing ones in the wrap screen's innerText, and every occurrence severs 'decimals' at '(12 d'. The mechanism is as described: app/config.js gives `chain` a 55-character descriptive clau

### UX-07 — The product's front door leads with DERO: Home's single largest number is "0 DERO", and the app can only create DERO-quoted pools — so the market surface is structurally DERO-first, against §0

- **Where** `app/home.js:123` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a structural product finding, and it is the one the directive's §0 exists to catch.  1. app/home.js:122-123 computes `flows = flowsOf(act)` sorted by volume and takes `flows[0]` as the hero's headline figure and symbol. The comment says "the largest quote leads because that is the market this deployment actually is". The same rule drives the Trade hub's "Flow 24h" read-out (home.js:216) and the Markets summary line (markets.js). 2. The market surface is structurally DERO-quoted, so that rule will keep choosing DERO:    - app/forms.js:154 — Create a pool hard-codes the quote to the zero hash. There is no chip, no field, and no explanation; a user cannot create a wXMR- or wZEC-quoted pool from the app at all.    - app/sheets.js:59 — `const canAdd = !s.quote`. Add-liquidity is refused on every wrapped-quote pool, so even a graduated wXMR AMM cannot be deepened from the app.    - app/forms.js:43-44 — the launch form's quote chips are `[DERO].concat(D.vaults())` with `quote = quotes[0]`, so DERO is first and is the default. (By contrast forms.js:191 puts DERO *last* for NFAs and defaults to XMR — which is the correct shape, and shows the team knows it.) 3. The rendered result on the live deployment: Home's hero is the word "0" at display size followed by "DERO", with "0 XMR-old  0 XMR" as a small second line. 20 of 27 markets are DERO-quoted clones of one token. The Trade hub repeats "FLOW 24H · 0 DERO".  §0 says DERO is the cheap execution and hosting layer, NOT the primary trading currency, and that the product must read that way; standing requirement 1 is XMR/ZEC first and obvious on every screen. The largest, first, and most prominent number on the first screen is denominated in the infrastructure asset.
- **Preconditions** Any deployment where DERO-quoted volume exceeds wrapped-quote volume — which the creation paths above make the default outcome.
- **Impact** §47: a visitor's first impression is a generic DERO memecoin venue with a wrapping feature bolted on, not a Monero and Zcash exchange. This is not cosmetic — it determines who the product attracts and what liquidity forms, and the creation paths make it self-reinforcing: DERO-quoted markets are the only ones the app lets you build and deepen, so DERO-quoted volume dominates, so DERO leads the hero.  Secondary, same root: the hero and the Markets summary list "XMR-old" — a superseded, withdraw-only vault — as a first-class quote alongside XMR, because the ordering is by volume with no vault-status filter.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes — Rendered on the live simulator:   .sim/ui-audit-ux/01-home.png — hero reads "FLOW · LAST 24 HOURS" / "0 DERO" at display size / "0 XMR-old  0 XMR" beneath. Trending, Movers and Launch pipeline are entirely DERO-quoted BRW markets.   .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-trade.png — "FLOW 24H  0 DERO" with "0 XMR-old · 0 XMR" beneath; all eleven listed markets DERO-quoted.   .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-markets.png — "27 markets · 24h flow 0 XMR-old · 0 XMR · 0 DERO · 1 split pair"; rows 1-20 DERO-quoted BRW.   .sim/ui-audit-ux2/markets.json — of the 27 loaded markets, exactly 2 carry a `quote` (one of them the superseded XMR-old vault).  Source:   sed -n '148,156p' app/forms.js → pool quote hard-coded "0000…0000".   s
- **Fix** Three parts, and the third needs the operator.  (1) Local and safe now — order the hero by product, not by volume. In app/home.js:122-123 and the Trade hub at :216, put the configured wrapped quotes first in a fixed order (D.vaults() order, which is XMR then ZEC) and DERO last, regardless of magnitude; keep the volume ordering only *within* the wrapped group. A zero next to XMR on day one is the honest statement of what this venue is for; a large DERO number in its place is not.  (2) Local and safe now — exclude legacy vaults from the hero and the Markets summary line (D.vaults() rather than the volume map's raw keys), so "XMR-old" stops appearing as a currency the product offers. See UX-08 for the rest of the legacy handling.  (3) Needs an operator decision, so not safe to change unilaterally: whether the pool form should offer wrapped quotes. Creating a wXMR-quoted pool is supported by pool.bas (InitializePrivate takes a quote SCID) but its first liquidity cannot be added from the browser, because app.tx carries one asset plus DERO and pool.bas:42 rejects stray DERO — so shipping the chip without solving two-asset AddLiquidity would create markets nobody can fund. The decision is: solve two-asset add (a second transfer entry in app.tx, which is a real change to the tx pipeline), or state on the pool form why wrapped quotes are unavailable and that launch graduation is the only route. Either is defensible; silently hard-coding DERO with no explanation is not.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs: on the home step, assert the hero's leading symbol is one of the configured non-legacy vault symbols (currently XMR or ZEC), not "DERO" and not a legacy symbol. Fails today. For part (3), a comment-free assertion is not possible; add a note to docs/BACKLOG.md instead.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: app\home.js, app\markets.js, app\forms.js, app\sheets.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. BL-13 (no swap screen) and the wrapped-quote liquidity gap are recorded separately; the composition of them into "the app renders as a DERO token venue" is not.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real and well-evidenced. I read .sim/ui-audit-ux/01-home.png myself: the hero is the numeral 0 at display size followed by 'DERO', with '0 XMR-old  0 XMR' as a small second line. app/home.js:123 takes flows[0] and home.js:77 sorts flowsOf by volume. The structural argument is the part that makes it  || REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Reproduced. Home's largest number is '0' followed by 'DERO', with '0 XMR-old  0 XMR' as a small second line — the infrastructure asset leading the front door of a Monero/Zcash exchange, against standing requirement 1. I pushed back on the ordering half and it survives only because of the structural 

### UX-08 — Markets quoted in a superseded, withdraw-only vault carry no warning — the app computes the flag that would say so and never reads it

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/feed.js:104` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/config.js marks two vaults `legacy: true` with chain strings that read "withdraw only" — d69c933f (XMR-old, 5 decimals) and bfd444b7 (ZEC-old). No new units of either can ever be minted: app/feed.js:49 `vaults()` excludes them from the deposit picker and from every quote chip, by design. 2. app/feed.js:104 computes `s.quoteLegacy = !!(v && v.legacy)` for every market whose quote is a legacy vault, and app/feed.js:303 computes `s.baseLegacy` for a market whose base asset is one. 3. `grep -rn 'quoteLegacy|baseLegacy' app/*.js` returns those two assignment lines and nothing else. Neither flag is read by H.risks (home.js:17), by U.warnings (ui.js:106), by the token page, by the market row, or by the trade sheet. 4. The consequence on the live deployment: three markets quoted in XMR-old are listed as ordinary markets. A token page renders "PRICE · XMR-OLD" as a normal quote. The Markets table lists "BRW / XMR-…" and two "XMR-old / DERO" pools with split-pair warnings — which correctly warn about split liquidity and say nothing about the quote being a dead asset. 5. A user who sells into one of these markets is paid in units of a vault that can never mint again; a user who wants to buy must first acquire units that cannot be obtained. The Wrap tab does not list the vault (feed.js:49), so the only route to the burn exit is the Portfolio screen, and only when the wallet already holds a balance (screens.js:59).
- **Preconditions** A deployment with a superseded vault and at least one market quoted in it. Both hold on the live simulator; a mainnet vault migration would recreate it.
- **Impact** A trader can be paid in, or price against, an asset the product has retired, with no notice at the point of trade. The exit exists (the code comment at feed.js:47 is explicit that a holder must always have it) but is not discoverable from any screen that shows the market. This is a §31 failure state that has been half-built: the detection is there, the disclosure is not.
- **Exploitability** No attacker needed, but it is trivially abusable — a market maker can quote an attractive price in a retired wrapped asset knowing the counterparty has no way to acquire more of it and no obvious way to get out.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -rn 'quoteLegacy\|baseLegacy' app/*.js   → app/feed.js:104 (assignment), app/feed.js:303 (assignment). No read sites.    .sim/ui-audit-ux2/markets.json — market f8405ccf… has quote d69c933f… (XMR-old), pair "BRW / XMR-old".   .sim/ui-audit-ux2/c3-token-wrapped.png and token-wrapped.txt — the token page for it renders "BRW · Browser Coin · pool · Verified pool · PRICE · XMR-OLD 0.0001207 … Thin market: about 2.06 XMR-old of liquidity." The only warnings shown are "Ticker shared" and "Thin market". Nothing about the quote.   .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-markets.png — rows 21, 22 and 23 are XMR-old markets carrying only split-pair and ticker warnings.   sed -n '39,50p' app/feed.js — vaults() excl
- **Fix** Wire the flags that already exist. app/home.js H.risks (line 17) is the single function that feeds the token page, the market row and the trade sheet, and app/ui.js U.warnings feeds the row marks — both already have the shape.  In H.risks, immediately after the `impersonates` case (which is correctly ranked first) and before `split`:    if (s.quoteLegacy) out.push({ k: "retired", t: "This market is priced in " + D.qsym(s) + ", a superseded vault: no more of it can ever be minted. You can only get it by buying it from someone, and the only thing you can do with it is burn it back out on the chain it came from — from the Portfolio screen, which is the only place that vault still appears." });   if (s.baseLegacy) out.push({ k: "retired", t: "The asset this market trades is " + s.ticker + " from a superseded vault: no more of it can ever be minted." });  and add a matching short mark ("Retired quote") to U.warnings so the market row carries it. home.js has 7.3 KB of DOC headroom and ui.js 7.5 KB.  No contract change, no config change, no decision — the flags, the ranking mechanism and the render path all exist.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs: navigate to the token page of a market whose `quoteLegacy` is true (find one via `window.Veil.data.assets`) and assert the rendered risk list contains "superseded". Fails today. Pair it with a tests/js assertion that every property assigned in feed.js decorate() is read somewhere in app/ — that is the class check, and it is what would have caught this.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\home.js, app\ui.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. The legacy-vault concept is deliberate and documented at feed.js:47-49; the gap is that the derived flags were never wired to a warning.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real and cleanly demonstrated. `grep -rn 'quoteLegacy|baseLegacy' app/*.js` returns exactly two lines, both assignments (feed.js:104, feed.js:303) and no read anywhere. I read app/home.js:17-32 (H.risks) end to end: impersonation, split, alloc, tax, thin — no legacy branch. The rendered consequence  || REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Reproduced, and the live surface is slightly worse than reported. grep confirms `quoteLegacy` (feed.js:104) and `baseLegacy` (feed.js:303) are assigned and never read anywhere in app/. Reading window.Veil.data.assets on the live deployment I find four affected markets, not three: one with quoteLegac

### FEE-04 — The wrapped-asset fee gap is wider than BL-90: the bot's own ClaimFees sweeps the asset leg of DERO-quoted markets into the treasury wallet, unrecorded and unshareable, and a stray asset sent to Deposit is stranded forever

- **Where** `tools/treasurybot/main.go:133` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** pool.bas ClaimFees (247-262) sends BOTH legs in one call: SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(t, fd, q) for the quote and SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(t, fa, LOAD("asset")) for the asset; launch.bas ClaimFees (246-263) does the same with SCID(). Only fd is counted, by `fc`; there is no counter for fa anywhere. The bot skips a market only when its QUOTE is not DERO (main.go:133), so a wXMR/DERO pool passes the filter, the bot sends ClaimFees, and the sell-side protocol fee — denominated in wXMR — lands in the treasury wallet. journal.claimMarket records only fd (main.go:160) and depositShare (journal.go:190) takes `share` bps of that. The wXMR is off the books entirely. founders.bas cannot take it in any case: Deposit reads DEROVALUE() (line 139).
- **Preconditions** Any pool.bas market whose asset is a vault SCID and whose quote is DERO — which the app's own pool form cannot create (forms.js:154 pins the DERO quote for both sides) but which any direct install can, and which the factory registers for 0.1 DERO with no dedup or content check.
- **Impact** Protocol fees earned in XMR/ZEC reach the treasury and can never reach a Founders Pass holder, and for this sub-case they are not even disclosed: `fc` proves what was claimed in DERO, nothing proves what was claimed in the asset. The app's Public accounting card (nfts.js:96) shows `dep` and `paid` only, so a holder auditing the promise cannot see the asset leg at all. Separately, an asset attached to Deposit alongside DERO is accepted and permanently stranded — founders.bas has no entrypoint that can send anything but DERO and its own SCID.
- **Exploitability** No attacker required; it is the design. A hostile creator can amplify it by installing wrapped-asset markets that route real protocol fees into an asset the pass structurally cannot distribute.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersCannotTakeAWrappedFee. Output:       founders_attack_test.go:159: founders.bas now holds 1000000000000 units of a wrapped asset with no entrypoint that can ever send them   --- PASS (asset-only Deposit is refused; DERO+asset is accepted and the asset stays in the contract balance through a subsequent Distribute). The ClaimFees two-leg behaviour is read from contracts/pool.bas:247-262 and contracts/launch.bas:246-263 and the bot's single-leg accounting from tools/treasurybot/main.go:133-160 plus chain.go:20,33 — that half is reasoned from code, not executed against a live wrapped market.
- **Fix** Three parts, only the first safe now. (1) Disclosure, local: tools/treasurybot must print the asset leg it is about to sweep (read `fa` and `asset`/`qt` alongside `fd` in chain.go and log "claiming N units of <asset> that cannot reach holders"), and app/nfts.js's accounting card must say that only DERO deposits are shown and that fees earned in any other asset reach the treasury and no holder — wording that agrees with app/notice.js:59. (2) Accounting, contract change: per-asset reward pools. Deposit(asset String) reading ASSETVALUE(HEXDECODE(asset)), keys acc_<a>/rpool_<a>/und_<a>/dep_<a>/paid_<a>/era_<a>/ea_<a>_<e> and per holder hd_<a>_<addr>/he_<a>_<addr>, Claim(asset)/PayBatch(asset,from,n), plus a bounded asset registry (an, as_<i>, ai_<a>, cap 8) so enumeration and dust-asset spam stay bounded. hu_/hst unchanged — the pass itself is one asset. Cost: storage gas is affordable (PayBatch(0,15) charges 620 of the 20,000 cap, measured; per-asset key suffixes add roughly 190 bytes of key material); the constraint is compute — PayBatch(15) is already 6,814,600 of 10,000,000, so the n cap must drop from 15 to about 12. Install gas today is 10,797 of 20,000; +1,500-2,500 source bytes puts it near 13,000, still under the cap (launch.bas is 15,257 bytes / 15,868 gas). This is operator decision D-3. (3) Not buildable at all: making the promise enforceable on chain. The DVM has no send-to-contract primitive (SEND_*_TO_ADDRESS needs a registered account and an SCID is not one), so no market can pay founders.bas directly and the bot is structurally required. `share` is a stored number nothing enforces — that must be said, not engineered away.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersCannotTakeAWrappedFee pins the contract's DERO-only nature. Add a treasurybot test over the existing `chain` fake: a DERO-quoted market with fa > 0 must be reported in the owed-but-not-sweepable list rather than claimed silently.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: tools/treasurybot/main.go, tools/treasurybot/chain.go, app/nfts.js, contracts/founders.bas, app/templates/founders.bas, app/config.js, docs/BACKLOG.md
- **Already known as** BL-90 (A) and BL-64 (B). The backlog UNDERSTATES both: BL-90 is scoped to markets quoted in a wrapped asset, which is exactly the case the bot already refuses (main.go:133-138). The case it does not refuse — a DERO-quoted market whose ASSET is wrapped — is in no backlog item, has no on-chain counter, and silently moves a wrapped protocol fee to the treasury wallet on the bot's own instruction.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real, and the finder UNDERSTATES its own precondition while overstating its novelty. Confirmed mechanism: contracts/pool.bas:238 accrues the sell-side protocol share `pf` into `fa` denominated in the ASSET; ClaimFees (pool.bas:247-262) sends both legs and increments `fc` by `fd` only, so there is no

### FEE-05 — The Founders Pass screen contradicts app/notice.js on what the share actually is — a hard-rule violation

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/nfts.js:95` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** app/notice.js is the authoritative plaque and says (line 30) "<share>% of the DERO fees the treasury claims", (line 59) "<share>% of claimed DERO fees to Pass holders; wrapped-asset fees to none", and (line 69) "It runs the bot that deposits the pass share, and nothing in a contract makes it." Three other surfaces say something materially stronger and unconditional: app/nfts.js:32 "<share>% of every protocol fee, split between registered holders"; app/nfts.js:95 "The promise is stored in the contract and cannot be changed: <share>% of the protocol fees claimed from every market is deposited here"; app/home.js:167 "<share>% of fees". The nfts.js:95 sentence is the worst: "stored in the contract and cannot be changed" is true of the NUMBER and false of the DEPOSIT, and it sits directly above the accounting card as the assurance.
- **Preconditions** None. Every visitor to the NFTs tab and the Home hero sees it.
- **Impact** A prospective buyer of a 20-edition pass at the mint price is told the pass entitles them to a fixed share of every protocol fee. In fact: fees earned in XMR or ZEC reach no holder at all (FEE-04); DERO fees reach holders only while an off-chain bot the operator runs keeps depositing (nothing on chain forces it, and no DVM mechanism can — FEE-04(3)); and NFA protoFee never passes through the bot at all, since it walks only pool_/launch_ (main.go:124). This is a mis-sold instrument and it breaks the directive's hard rule that the app must never contradict app/notice.js.
- **Exploitability** Not an exploit; a disclosure defect on the one screen where money changes hands for the instrument.
- **Reproduced** yes — Read directly:   grep -n "protocol fee|every market|% of fee" app/nfts.js app/home.js app/notice.js   app/nfts.js:32:  "...% of every protocol fee, split between registered holders"   app/nfts.js:95:  "The promise is stored in the contract and cannot be changed: ...% of the protocol fees claimed from every market is deposited here..."   app/home.js:167: "...% of fees ..."   app/notice.js:30: "...% of the DERO fees the treasury claims"   app/notice.js:59: "...of claimed DERO fees to Pass holders; wrapped-asset fees to none" And the code behind it: contracts/founders.bas:139 (LET d = DEROVALUE()), tools/treasurybot/main.go:124 (pool_ and launch_ only), main.go:133 (wrapped quotes skipped).
- **Fix** Rewrite the three sentences to the notice's own words. nfts.js:32 -> "<share>% of the DERO fees the treasury claims, split between registered holders". nfts.js:95 -> "<share>% is the share stored in the contract; it cannot be changed. Nothing in a contract makes the deposit — the treasury's bot does, and only from fees claimed in DERO. Fees earned in a wrapped asset reach the treasury and no holder. Deposits, payouts and the registry are all on chain, so anyone can check what was deposited against what was paid." home.js:167 -> "<share>% of claimed DERO fees". Purely local text, no contract or config change, and there is room: app/nfts.js is 6,628 of the 18,432-byte DOC budget, app/home.js 11,068.
- **Regression test** Add an assertion to tools/uitest/ui.mjs on the NFTs screen: the rendered text must not contain "every protocol fee" or "every market", and must contain "DERO" within the share sentence. A grep-based test over app/*.js in tests/js would be cheaper and catch it before a render.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfts.js, app/home.js, tools/uitest/ui.mjs
- **Already known as** REQ·A8-08, CONFIRMED-open in docs/audit/DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md. The follow-up's P7-03/A8-01 fix corrected notice.js and left these three sentences. Still open; the backlog does not carry it as a BL number.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real, verified verbatim, and correctly self-declared as a DUPLICATE of REQ·A8-08 (P2, CONFIRMED-open). All three surfaces still say what the finder says they say, and app/notice.js has already been corrected to the narrower truth, so the app does contradict its own designated final word — a stated h

### FEE-06 — The legacy template hash list lets a Founders Pass with materially different distribution rules display as Verified — and that is exactly what is deployed

- **Where** `app/config.js:22` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** app/config.js templates.founders carries sha256 49009d2c... plus three `legacy` hashes. app/feed.js verify() (161-165) accepts any of them and app/founders.js loadFounders (line 12) then renders the contract as a fully verified Founders Pass with the identical accounting copy. The legacy hashes are not a formatting difference: I pulled the code of the deployed pass (config.js:10, aee67463...) off the simulator daemon and its distribute() reads `IF hst == 0 THEN GOTO 90` where the current contracts/founders.bas:246 reads `IF hst < LOAD("minhst") THEN GOTO 90`. The deployed instance has NO anti-sweep floor whatsoever, and no `minhst` key at all.
- **Preconditions** A pass installed from any hash on the legacy list. The current DMZ deployment is one.
- **Impact** The C9 guard the operator added to stop the pre-registration sweep is absent from the contract users actually see, and the app gives it the same Verified badge and the same "the promise is stored in the contract" copy as the fixed one. A user cannot tell from the app which distribution rules their pass obeys. On this deployment FEE-01 applies at every maxsupply, not just at 20. Beyond the pass, the same pattern applies to every legacy hash in config.js: the badge asserts "this is our contract" and silently means "this is one of four different contracts".
- **Exploitability** Passive today (dep = 0, paid = 0 on the live pass, so nothing has been lost). It becomes live the moment the treasury deposits into a legacy pass.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -n 'founders' app/config.js     10:  founders: "aee674633edab4c12fe5007765629f06852d1e379847da5553de6a1a4928dde2",     22:    founders: { file: "templates/founders.bas", sha256: "49009d2c...", legacy: ["8afd70fb...", "c096e0c2...", "fd0e1e28..."] },   curl -s -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' http://127.0.0.1:20000/json_rpc -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":"1","method":"DERO.GetSC","params":{"scid":"aee674633edab4c12fe5007765629f06852d1e379847da5553de6a1a4928dde2","code":true,"variables":true}}'   -> variables: maxsupply 500, reserve 50, minted 54, share 5000, mfee 250, hst 4, hn 1, dep 0, paid 0, acc 0, era 0 — and NO minhst key.   -> returned code (13,312 bytes, zero occurrences 
- **Fix** A policy decision, not a patch. Either (a) drop the legacy list for founders in deploy/networks.json and redeploy the pass from the current template — which delists the existing simulator pass, acceptable because dep = 0 and paid = 0 so no holder is owed anything; or (b) if legacy hashes must stay, app/feed.js must return the matched hash and the UI must mark a legacy contract distinctly ("an earlier build of this contract — its reward rules differ") instead of the plain Verified badge. Both touch app/config.js, which only the final validator may regenerate via `genconfig -dist -network simulator`. In every case, legacy hashes must never be carried into the mainnet block for a contract that holds or distributes money.
- **Regression test** Add to tests/templates_test.go: for each kind, assert that every hash on the legacy list parses to a contract whose entrypoint set AND whose distribute/settle bodies are byte-identical to the current template, or that the kind has no legacy list at all. That would have caught this at the moment the C9 fix shipped.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: deploy/networks.json, app/config.js, app/feed.js, app/nfts.js, tests/templates_test.go
- **Already known as** New. BL-1/BL-2 cover template verification generally; nothing covers the semantics of the legacy list. The mappers recorded that the live pass has no minhst key (legacy template) but did not connect it to the verification badge.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real — I reproduced it independently against the live simulator daemon rather than taking the finder's word. The deployed pass is a pre-C9 build with NO anti-sweep floor at all, and app/feed.js:152 folds every legacy hash into the same `kind`, so verify() returns 'founders' and app/founders.js rende

### FEE-07 — Just-in-time registration takes a full pro-rata share of a deposit after one call of holding

- **Where** `contracts/founders.bas:85` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** Register (85-99) settles, snapshots hd_ at the current acc and raises hst, with no holding period recorded anywhere. Withdraw (114-121) settles and returns the units. Any deposit that lands between the two is split pro rata with no time weighting. tools/treasurybot deposits on a fixed -interval (default 60s) and every step is public — the market's fd, the journal's claim, and the pass's dep counter — so the timing is mechanical rather than a race.
- **Preconditions** Passes in a wallet (mint, or the marketplace), and hst already at or above minhst so the deposit is distributed rather than parked (the parked case is FEE-02, which is worse).
- **Impact** Dilutes every long-term holder by exactly the sniper's stake, on every deposit. Reproduced: a wallet registered 10 units one call before a 2,000,000 deposit, took 1,000,000 (half), and withdrew the passes again in the same call that paid it; the holder registered for 5,000 blocks got the other half.
- **Exploitability** Two transactions per deposit, no capital at risk beyond the passes, entirely public timing.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersJustInTimeRegistrationTakesAFullShare. Output:       founders_attack_test.go:86: sniper held for one call and took 1000000 of the 2000000 deposited; the 5000-block holder gets the rest   --- PASS The test asserts sniper's Withdraw pays exactly 1000000 DERO and returns all 10 passes.
- **Fix** Add a holding period to settle(). Store hl_<addr> = BLOCK_HEIGHT() in Register (and reset it whenever hu_ increases), and in settle() pay nothing while BLOCK_HEIGHT() < hl_ + lock, advancing hd_/he_ as now so the unpaid amount stays in rpool for the other holders. `lock` must be an install parameter (immutable, like share) so it is auditable, and at least a few multiples of the bot's deposit interval to be meaningful. This is a distribution-policy change on a hashed template: operator decision plus redeploy. A cheaper partial mitigation with no contract change is an unpredictable deposit cadence — but the deposit is visible in the mempool either way, so it is not a fix.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersJustInTimeRegistrationTakesAFullShare — invert to assert the sniper receives 0 once a lock exists, and add a case proving a holder past the lock is unaffected and that the skipped amount stays claimable by the others rather than being burnt.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/founders.bas, app/templates/founders.bas, app/config.js, tools/simdeploy/main.go, tests/founders_attack_test.go
- **Already known as** PROD·P7-11, open in docs/audit/FINDINGS.md and re-verified by the mappers. The backlog states it fairly; this adds the measurement.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real and reproduced, and correctly declared as PROD·P7-11, which docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:1970 rates P3 and DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md:349 marks CONFIRMED-open. Register (founders.bas:85-99) records no holding period anywhere and Withdraw (114-121) settles and returns the units, so any deposit between 

### FEE-12 — Mint has no per-address cap and the checked-in deployment still mints 500 editions with 50 reserved, so the directive's "exactly 20" exists nowhere and would concentrate the whole fee flow in two wallets

- **Where** `contracts/founders.bas:65` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** Mint (58-74) bounds n only by `maxsupply - minted` and by exact payment; there is no per-address cap, no per-block cap and no allow-list. `start` is a single block height, so at that height one transaction takes everything left. The installer separately receives `reserve` editions free at line 52, bounded only by reserve <= maxsupply/5 (line 18). At the directive's 20 editions that is 4 free to the installer and 16 takeable in one call.
- **Preconditions** Enough DERO for n x price and being first in the block at `start`. Both are ordinary MEV conditions on a public chain.
- **Impact** The instrument that is supposed to distribute the exchange's fees to a set of founders can end up wholly in two hands — the installer's 4 and one buyer's 16 — with no dispersion mechanism at all. Combined with FEE-01 (minhst 1 at 20 editions) the same wallet is also the natural first registrant. Separately, the checked-in mainnet path contradicts the target: tools/simdeploy/main.go:48,50 default to -pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50 and deploy/README.md:233 repeats them in the mainnet command, so following the runbook produces 500 editions, not 20.
- **Exploitability** One transaction at a publicly known block height.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersOneWalletTakesEveryEditionAtStart. Output:       founders_attack_test.go:179: one wallet holds 16 of 20 editions; the installer holds the other 4   --- PASS (asserts minted == 20 after a single Mint(16)). Deployment mismatch:   grep -n "pass-max|pass-reserve" tools/simdeploy/main.go deploy/README.md     tools/simdeploy/main.go:48: passMax = flag.Uint64("pass-max", 500, "editions")     tools/simdeploy/main.go:50: passReserve = flag.Uint64("pass-reserve", 50, ...)     deploy/README.md:233:  -pass-price 500000 -pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50 -pass-share 5000 -pass-mfee 250
- **Fix** Two parts. (1) Operator decision (D-9-adjacent): write the mainnet founders parameters down — maxsupply, reserve, price, start, share, mfee — and change tools/simdeploy/main.go's defaults and deploy/README.md:73,233 to match, since founders.bas has no setter and no UpdateCode so every one of them is frozen at install (verified: `grep -rn UPDATE_SC_CODE contracts/` returns nothing, and every STORE of maxsupply/price/share/mfee/treasury/minhst is inside InitializePrivate behind the initd guard). (2) If dispersion matters, a per-address mint cap needs a contract change: store mn_<addr> and refuse when mn_ + n exceeds a cap parameter. That is a hashed-template change and a redeploy.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersOneWalletTakesEveryEditionAtStart, inverted to expect a refusal once a cap exists. Add a deploy test that the mainnet block of deploy/networks.json, once populated, carries the agreed maxsupply — today it is empty, so the runbook is the only record and it is wrong.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/founders.bas, tools/simdeploy/main.go, deploy/README.md, app/templates/founders.bas, app/config.js, tests/founders_attack_test.go
- **Already known as** The 500/50 mismatch is a premise gap the mappers recorded; the per-address concentration is not in any backlog item. D-9 covers freezing the deploy parameters generally.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Two claims bundled; the facts are right, the framing is not. (a) 'Mint has no per-address cap' — true (contracts/founders.bas:65 bounds n only by maxsupply - minted and exact payment) but this is not a defect. A fixed-supply, fixed-price public mint with no per-wallet cap is ordinary; nobody is over

### NFA-2 — The app tells the owner a held bid can only be refunded, never that it is a standing claim on their asset — and hides it entirely once they relist

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/nfas.js:152` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** app/nfas.js:152 renders the held bid with the note "A bid the contract still holds from an auction closed without settling. It is refunded when the owner's next listing takes a bid or is cancelled; nothing else releases it." That last clause is false in the one direction that matters: an empty Bid() on any ENDED listing releases it — by handing the asset to the stale bidder at the stale price (NFA-1, reproduced). Worse, that row is inside `if (st === "none")`, so the moment the owner starts a new listing the held bid disappears from the screen; bidCount is reset to 0 by resetVars, so app/nfas.js:155 shows 'Top bid: none yet' on a live auction that already carries a settled outcome. And at app/nfas.js:139, for an ended listing with bidCount 0, the owner is offered 'Close · unit back to you' with the note 'Nothing sold; closing returns the unit to your wallet' — while any wallet can front-run that Close with an empty Bid() and take the asset.
- **Preconditions** An NFA that has ever had a bid stranded (NFA-1 step 2). The owner reads the app rather than the chain.
- **Impact** An owner who could defend themselves — by cancelling inside the 300 s buffer of their next listing, which runs outbidReturns (nfa.bas:252) and clears the record — is told there is nothing to defend against, and is then shown a screen with no trace of the claim. The app's own words are what makes NFA-1 practical rather than theoretical.
- **Exploitability** n/a — this is the disclosure half of NFA-1. Reading only.
- **Reproduced** yes — Read app/nfas.js:139, :152 and the state() helper at :22. Cross-checked against the DVM behaviour in tests/nfa_r4_test.go: after the stale strand, Start resets bidCount to 0 (asserted in TestNFAStaleBidSettlesANewOwnersListing, `if c.U(e.nfa, "bidCount") != 0 { t.Fatal }` — passes), while currBidAmt survives (asserted, 2,000,000). Reasoned for the rendering itself; I did not take the UI lock (see notPerformed).
- **Fix** Local and testable in app/nfas.js, no contract change and no operator decision. (1) Correct the note at :152 to state what a held bid actually is: 'Until it is cleared, any wallet can settle your next listing to this bidder at this amount after that listing ends. Clear it by starting a listing and cancelling inside the first 300 seconds.' (2) Move the held-bid row out of the `st === "none"` branch so it renders on a live and an ended listing too — it is read from currBidAmt/currBidAddr, which loadNFA already fetches (app/feed.js NFA_NUM/NFA_STR). (3) Add currBidAmt and currBidAddr to NFA_PROBE (app/feed.js:24) so a change in them alone refreshes the page. (4) At app/nfas.js:139, when currBidAmt > 0 and bidCount == 0, replace 'Nothing sold; closing returns the unit to your wallet' with a warning that a held bid stands and anyone may settle to it first.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js that loads app/nfas.js's listing()/position() against a fixture record with bidCount 0n and currBidAmt > 0n and asserts the warning text is present in both the live and the ended state. app/nfas.js is an IIFE over window like ui.js; tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs (written) shows the shim that loads such a file under node --test.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfas.js, app/feed.js, tests/js/nfas.test.mjs
- **Already known as** New. BL-68 asks for contract work; nothing in the backlog covers the copy or the disclosure gap, and app/notice.js says nothing about it either.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FOUND-1 — At the directive's own mainnet size of 20 editions the Founders Pass anti-sweep floor degenerates to one pass, and the first single registrant takes 100% of everything deposited before any holder existed

- **Where** `contracts/founders.bas:49` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** founders.bas:49 sets minhst = 1 + maxsupply / 50. That is the C9 guard: distribute() (founders.bas:246) parks a deposit in `und` until at least minhst units are registered, so that a lone early registrant cannot take the whole pool. At maxsupply 500 (the deployed and documented size) minhst is 11 — 2.2% of supply. At the 20 editions the directive requires for mainnet, integer division gives 20/50 = 0, so minhst is 1: a single pass clears the gate. The installer holds up to reserve = maxsupply/5 = 4 editions for free (founders.bas:18, :52), so the installer is also the party best placed to be that registrant. Sequence: treasury bot Deposits before anyone has registered (the normal state of a fresh deployment); any holder of one pass calls Register then Claim; distribute() releases all of `und` against hst = 1 and settle() pays it out in full. The same holder may Withdraw immediately after.
- **Preconditions** maxsupply set to 20 at install — which is what the directive requires and which is immutable afterwards. Any DERO deposited to the pass before the first Register.
- **Impact** One pass out of twenty (5% of supply) collects 100% of the pre-registration pool. The larger point is that the mainnet configuration the product requires has never been installed or tested: tools/simdeploy/main.go:48-50 defaults to -pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50 and deploy/README.md:73 and :233 print the mainnet command line with those same 500/50 values, so the shipped runbook does not produce the pass the directive describes.
- **Exploitability** No attack needed — it is the default outcome of the ordinary deployment order (install, bot starts depositing, holders trickle in). Deliberate exploitation is one Register plus one Claim.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/founders_supply_test.go. Run:    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'FoundersSupply|FoundersMinhst' -v  Output: === RUN   TestFoundersSupplyCannotExceedMaxsupplyAt20     maxsupply 20 -> minhst = 1: the C9 anti-sweep floor is one single pass --- PASS === RUN   TestFoundersMinhstAt20LetsOneUnitSweepEverything     one pass of 20 (5%) claimed 100% of the 900000 deposited before any holder existed; the same holder can Withdraw immediately after --- PASS  The second test deposits 900,000 with hst 0 (asserted und == 900000, rpool == 0), mints and registers ONE pass, and asserts Claim sends the full 900,000. checkFounders (the existing invariant helper) passes afterwards.  Also
- **Fix** Two parts, and only the second is safe now. (a) Contract: minhst should be a floor with a minimum that survives a small supply — `1 + maxsupply / 50` becomes something like `(maxsupply + 4) / 5` (20% of supply, so 4 of 20 and 100 of 500), or minhst should be an install parameter the deployer sets and the app displays. That is a template change, needs a new hash in app/config.js, and needs the operator to choose the fraction — NOT safe now. (b) Deployment and docs, safe now: deploy/README.md:73 and :233 must carry the real mainnet numbers (-pass-max 20 -pass-reserve <=4), and tools/simdeploy should refuse a mainnet install whose maxsupply/50 is 0 unless the operator passes an explicit acknowledgement, so the degenerate floor cannot be reached by accident. Either way the operator must also decide whether the treasury bot may deposit before the pass has holders; deferring the first deposit until hst >= a stated threshold removes the whole case without touching the contract.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_supply_test.go TestFoundersMinhstAt20LetsOneUnitSweepEverything (written, passing). On a fix it should assert the opposite: that a single registered pass of 20 cannot release `und`, and that the pool is released only at the chosen floor. TestFoundersSupplyCannotExceedMaxsupplyAt20 already asserts minhst's value directly and will fail loudly if the formula changes.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/founders.bas, app/templates/founders.bas, app/config.js, deploy/README.md, tools/simdeploy/main.go, tests/founders_supply_test.go, docs/BACKLOG.md
- **Already known as** The arithmetic consequence is noted as a premise gap by the architecture readers, but no BL number covers it and docs/BACKLOG.md does not mention minhst at all. The related PROD·P7-11 (just-in-time registration) is open and is a different, weaker case: it needs a deposit to front-run, whereas this one sweeps everything already waiting.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### NFA-3 — No NFA protocol fee reaches Founders Pass holders in ANY quote, including DERO — docs/NFA.md §6.6 says the DERO case works
