- **Where** `docs/NFA.md:331` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a false statement about where money goes, on a launch requirement. Two independent reasons the claim cannot hold. (1) contracts/nfa.bas:349 pays protoFee straight to the treasury ADDRESS inside processDEROFinalPayment, in the same call that moves the unit. There is no fd accumulator and no ClaimFees entrypoint on nfa.bas at all, so there is nothing for a sweeper to claim. (2) tools/treasurybot/main.go:124 walks exactly two registry prefixes — `{"pool_", "pc"}, {"launch_", "lc"}` — and never nfa_ or nft_. `grep -n "nfa_" tools/treasurybot/*.go` returns nothing. docs/NFA.md:331 nonetheless states: "On a DERO-quoted NFA the share is DERO and the treasury bot sweeps it to founders.bas Deposit() for Pass holders." docs/NFA_BUILD.md:42 repeats it as 'how NFA sales feed the Founders Pass'.
- **Preconditions** None. It is the shipped state.
- **Impact** A creator or a pass holder reading the design note is told NFA sales fund the pass. They do not, in any asset. This is the kind of claim the directive's requirement (3) and app/notice.js exist to prevent, and app/notice.js is correct where the design note is not — so the two documents of record contradict each other on a fee promise.
- **Exploitability** n/a — documentation defect. The money is not stolen; it reaches the treasury exactly as nfa.bas:349 says, and stops there.
- **Reproduced** yes — grep -n "ClaimFees\|\"fd\"\|fc" contracts/nfa.bas -> no output (no accumulator, no claim path). grep -n "nfa_" tools/treasurybot/main.go tools/treasurybot/chain.go -> no output. sed -n '124p' tools/treasurybot/main.go -> `for _, p := range []struct{ prefix, count string }{{"pool_", "pc"}, {"launch_", "lc"}} {` sed -n '328,336p' docs/NFA.md -> the §6.6 claim quoted above. Reasoned from those reads; no transaction needed.
- **Fix** Safe now, and doc-only: correct docs/NFA.md §6.6 and docs/NFA_BUILD.md §1 to say that an NFA's protoFee is paid directly to the treasury address at settlement, in the quote, and reaches Founders Pass holders in no asset today, because nfa.bas keeps no claimable fee balance and tools/treasurybot walks only pool_ and launch_. Widen BL-64 from 'in a wrapped quote' to 'in any quote' and raise it, since the directive makes the fee path a launch requirement. The app is already honest here (app/nfas.js:39 says 'Protocol fee ... -> treasury'), so no app change is needed for truthfulness — but if the operator wants the fee to reach holders, that is a contract change (an fd accumulator plus ClaimFees on nfa.bas) and an operator decision, not a doc fix.
- **Regression test** A Go test in tools/treasurybot that asserts the set of registry prefixes the sweeper walks, so that any doc claiming a third one is caught: `TestSweeperWalksOnlyPoolAndLaunch`. Pair it with a docs assertion — the repo already has tests/templates_test.go as the model for checking a document against the tree.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: docs/NFA.md, docs/NFA_BUILD.md, docs/BACKLOG.md, tools/treasurybot/journal_test.go
- **Already known as** docs/BACKLOG.md BL-64 (B) — and it understates. BL-64 is scoped to 'the Founders Pass share of NFA sales in a wrapped quote' and says protoFee in wXMR 'reaches the treasury, not the pass holders'. It implies the DERO case is fine. It is not: nothing sweeps NFA fees in any asset.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### APP-1 — NFAs are the only listed asset that never gets the impersonation and look-alike check, so an NFA named after a wrapped asset or a real collection wears the plain green "Verified" badge

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/feed.js:314` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** app/feed.js decorate() (line 307) runs the impersonation and homoglyph guards inside `for (const s of this.assets.values())` at line 314 — this.assets holds pools, launches and nfts. this.nfas is a separate Map (app/feed.js:354 assigns it alongside), and decorate() never reads it. So s.impersonates and s.lookalike are undefined on every NFA record. app/ui.js verified() (line 90) branches on s.impersonates first, then treasuryUnverified, wrappedBase, pool/assetVerified, unlisted — an NFA matches none of them and falls through to line 104: a plain green shield reading "Verified", title "Contract code matches the shipped template and pays the protocol treasury". Meanwhile app/feed.js:280 does set s.ticker = s.name.slice(0,3) on every NFA, so the input the fold needs is already there and simply unused. An attacker installs an NFA from the shipped template with nameHdr "wXMR" or "Wrapped Monero" or "DMZ Founders Pass", or a Cyrillic look-alike of an existing collection's name, registers it for the 0.1 DERO regfee (contracts/factory.bas RegisterNFA, no dedup, no name check), and it renders on the NFTs tab and its own page with the app's strongest trust signal and no name caveat. A pool with the identical name is caught by the same codebase.
- **Preconditions** regfee (0.1 DERO) and one install. The NFA must use the shipped template and the deployment's treasury and protoFee — which the attacker wants anyway, because that is what gets it listed (app/feed.js:276-279).
- **Impact** The one guard DMZ built against name impersonation is not applied to the asset class where a name is the entire product. A buyer sees 'Verified' next to a name that says wrapped Monero, and the title text tells them the treasury was checked — true, and beside the point.
- **Exploitability** One transaction plus a registration fee. No race, no privilege.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs (registered in tests/js/index.js). Run:    node --test tests/js   # tests 17 / # pass 17 / # fail 0  The case 'the look-alike fold that markets get is never applied to NFAs' loads app/ui.js under a DOM shim and asserts U.tickerKey of the Cyrillic АВЕ === "ABE" and U.hasHomoglyph of the same === true (the fold can see it), then slices app/feed.js from `decorate()` to `async refresh(` and asserts /this\.nfas/ does NOT appear in that body while /hasHomoglyph|impersonation/ does. It passes today, and it is written to fail the moment decorate() starts reading this.nfas.  Also read directly: app/feed.js:307-333 (decorate body, this.assets only) and app/ui.js:90-104
- **Fix** Local, testable, no contract change and no operator decision. In app/feed.js decorate(), after the market loop, run the same three lines over this.nfas: set s.lookalike = V.ui.hasHomoglyph(s.name), s.impersonates = this.impersonation({ticker: s.name, name: s.name, scid: s.scid}), and group NFAs by V.ui.tickerKey(s.name) for dupTicker so two NFAs whose names read alike are both marked. impersonation() (app/feed.js:80) already takes a record with .ticker/.name/.scid and returns the wrapped symbol being impersonated, so no new logic is needed. app/ui.js verified() and warnings() then produce the right badge with no change at all. Watch the TELA cap: feed.js is 16,144 of 18,432 bytes, so keep it to one loop.
- **Regression test** tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs (written) inverts cleanly: change the decorate-body assertion to require /this\.nfas/, and add a case that builds two fake NFA records (one named "wXMR", one Cyrillic) through decorate() and asserts impersonates/lookalike are set and U.verified() returns the 'Code checked · name is not' branch.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/feed.js, tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs
- **Already known as** Partly BL-63 (B), which covers collection-name ownership only ('collection is a free string at install; two creators can share one'). It does not cover the NFA's own name, and it does not cover the badge. PROD·P7-09 covers social display names, a different surface. The gap between the guard that exists and the records it is run over is new.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-3 — The joint Monero wallets are opened by file name and never checked against st.Joint, so Bob's gate counts coins that are not at the joint address

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:510` (dimension: Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix)
- **Attack path** walletName (swap.go:464-471) is `"xmrswap-" + htlc[:8] + "-" + Lock.ID + "-" + kind`. Every input is public: the htlc SCID is on the DERO chain and is what both parties type into -htlc, and the lock id is the contract's `n` at the moment Bob locks.  openOrGenerate (swap.go:475-481) is `if OpenWallet(name) == nil { return nil }` — a pre-existing wallet file of that name is accepted whatever keys it holds, and GenerateFromKeys, which is the only thing that checks the address, is never reached. confirmedAtJoint (swap.go:509-547), which IS the gate, opens walletName("view") on every pass and then reads Incoming() and UnlockedBalance() from it without ever asking Address(ctx) whether the wallet it just opened is st.Joint. Contrast ownWallet (swap.go:485-502), which does exactly that check for the party's own wallet — the check exists in the same file, it is simply not applied to the joint wallets.  Failure sequence: a wallet file named xmrswap-<scid8>-<id>-view already exists in the wallet-rpc's --wallet-dir holding some other address with a balance. Bob's dero_locked → openOrGenerate says OK; watching_xmr → confirmedAtJoint returns that wallet's confirmed balance; if it is >= AmountXmr, Bob releases k_b (swap.go:730-741) although the joint address holds nothing. Alice Claims the DERO. Bob then sweeps a wallet that is not the joint one. The benign variant is the mirror image: the decoy has no balance, the gate never opens, and Bob refunds at t1 having paid fees and hours for nothing.
- **Preconditions** A wallet file of that exact name already in the wallet-rpc's wallet-dir with different keys: a restored wallet-dir backup, a re-installed htlc contract that restarts lock ids at 0 while the first 32 bits of the SCID collide, or a wallet-dir writable by anyone other than the companion. The name is fully predictable in advance from public data, so an attacker with any write path to that directory chooses the moment; without such a path this is a robustness gap rather than a live exploit, which is why I rate it MEDIUM and not HIGH.
- **Impact** Bob's DERO is released for XMR that was never locked (the gate opened on the wrong wallet), or the swap deadlocks until t1. Invariant 2 — "the gate waits for this party's own view-only wallet" — is enforced on the wallet's NAME, not on the address it holds.
- **Exploitability** The exploit itself is one file placed in a directory; the difficulty is entirely in getting write access to that directory or in engineering the name collision. No protocol interaction is required and the victim's own code does the rest.
- **Reproduced** yes — Tests at ...\scratchpad\xmrswap_adversarial_test.go (TestAdvGateReadsWhateverWalletHasTheName, TestAdvOpenOrGenerateAcceptsAStaleWallet); run inside tools/xmrswap/, then removed.  $ cd tools && go test -count=1 -run TestAdv -v ./xmrswap === RUN   TestAdvGateReadsWhateverWalletHasTheName     joint address           53vEYsYZayYJxy5izMU1ZfWm7mYhCu8EVaCEdiXgsgV9MA8AhygbnWTbqxUGaLxfiFGCayAmYbpyLWJxXHiuPEG3JHHeeHF     decoy wallet "xmrswap-abababab-0-view" holds 54yckzaDmivApojLrs1YNd5rLGujXaixnMXJZ7LyMqpLKZff1zyN1VFEgaAVGZqynmQT2xN39FwgKDYSkeMoS9TeFdvR3iZ     confirmedAtJoint reports 1000030000000 piconero; the joint address holds 0     BUG REPRODUCED: the gate would open on 1000030000000 piconer
- **Fix** Add `func (s *Swap) openJoint(ctx context.Context, name string) error` that calls OpenWallet (or GenerateFromKeys) and then requires `s.xmr.Address(ctx) == s.st.Joint`, and route confirmedAtJoint (swap.go:510), openOrGenerate (swap.go:475-481) and sweep (swap.go:558) through it. Also fold st.SwapID — already random per swap (state.go:28) — into walletName, so a name can never be inherited from an unrelated run. Local, one file, fully testable against the fake.
- **Regression test** The two tests above with their assertions inverted: confirmedAtJoint must return an error rather than a balance when the wallet under that name is not st.Joint, and openOrGenerate must refuse a pre-existing wallet whose address differs.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/swap.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md and not in any prior audit — DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md, FINDINGS.md and DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md contain no xmrswap finding at all. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Both halves reproduce exactly, with my own tests. confirmedAtJoint (swap.go:509-547) opens walletName("view") and never asks Address(ctx) whether that wallet is st.Joint — the identical check exists twelve lines above in ownWallet (swap.go:495-500) and is simply not applied here. openOrGenerate (swa

### XMR-4 — Nothing checks which Monero network the wallet-rpc is on, nor that it was started with --wallet-dir and without --restricted-rpc; all three are discovered only after Bob's DERO is irreversibly locked

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:430` (dimension: Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix)
- **Attack path** cfg.Net is used in exactly two places (grep over swap.go returns four sites, none a check): line 430, to build the joint address with a network prefix, and lines 611 / 972-977, to compare the two parties' DECLARED strings. ParseAddress is never called on st.MyXmr, so the wallet's own primary address — which encodes its network in the prefix (keys.go: 18 mainnet / 24 stagenet / 53 testnet) — is stored and compared as an opaque string only. cmd/xmrswap/main.go probes the wallet with Height() (line 116), which answers on any network.  Separately, all the wallet-management calls the companion makes are refused outright unless monero-wallet-rpc was started with --wallet-dir and without --restricted-rpc: on_open_wallet (wallet_rpc_server.cpp:3692-3705) and on_generate_from_keys (3904-3917) both begin `if (m_restricted) { "Command unavailable in restricted mode." }` then `if (m_wallet_dir.empty()) { WALLET_RPC_ERROR_CODE_NO_WALLET_DIR, "No wallet dir configured" }`. Nothing in docs/XMR_SWAP.md, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md, the CLI usage block (cmd/xmrswap/main.go:1-10) or the flag help mentions --wallet-dir; the only place in this repository that gets it right is the vendored reference implementation at research/xmr-btc-swap/docs/asb/README.md:10-11.  Order of events for Bob: setup (own wallet read, no network check) → verified_pi_a → dero_locking, DERO LOCKED AND IRREVERSIBLE UNTIL t1 → dero_locked → openOrGenerate(view) → GenerateFromKeys → refused (wrong network prefix, or NO_WALLET_DIR, or restricted mode). Bob is stuck in dero_locked retrying every 5 s until t1 (default T1Delta = T1Min+20 = 420 blocks, roughly two hours at 18 s), then refunds and pays the Refund fee. For Alice: her first joint contact is Transfer to a wrong-prefix joint address → the wallet answers -2 "Invalid destination address" → xmr_locking sets transferTried BEFORE the call (swap.go:1077), so the next pass takes the LockUnknown branch (swap.go:1064-1070) and moves to xmr_locked with no XMR locked and no error surfaced; the swap silently degrades to Bob's refund.
- **Preconditions** An operator typo in -net, or a monero-wallet-rpc started the ordinary way (--wallet-file, no --wallet-dir) or with --restricted-rpc. No adversary.
- **Impact** Bob's capital is locked for the full t1 window and the Lock and Refund fees are burnt for a mistake detectable in one RPC call before anything is locked. Alice's failure is worse in kind: a DEFINITIVE wallet refusal is recorded as "fate unknown" and reported as if she had locked, which is the wrong side of the conservative/liberal line for a message whose only purpose is to say whether coins moved.
- **Exploitability** Not adversarial — a configuration trap. Its value to an attacker is indirect: a counterparty who knows the victim will get stuck until t1 can plan around a two-hour window of certain inaction.
- **Reproduced** yes — Test at ...\scratchpad\xmrswap_adversarial_test.go (TestAdvNetworkMismatchSurvivesSetup); run inside tools/xmrswap/, then removed.  $ cd tools && go test -count=1 -run TestAdvNetworkMismatch -v ./xmrswap === RUN   TestAdvNetworkMismatchSurvivesSetup     cfg.Net = mainnet; the open wallet's own address is on stagenet (53gQZZhe9BT1)     BUG REPRODUCED: setup accepted a stagenet wallet under -net mainnet; nothing decodes the wallet's own address --- FAIL: TestAdvNetworkMismatchSurvivesSetup (0.01s)  $ grep -n 'cfg.Net' tools/xmrswap/swap.go 430:	return Address(p, v, s.cfg.Net) 611:		st.Terms = Terms{... Net: s.cfg.Net.String() ...} 972:		want := Terms{... Net: s.cfg.Net.String()} 977:		case ...
- **Fix** In cmd/xmrswap/main.go, after wallet.Height(ctx) and before any Swap is built: (1) `a, _ := wallet.Address(ctx); pa, err := xmrswap.ParseAddress(a); if err != nil || pa.Net != network { log.Fatalf(...) }` — one call, and ParseAddress already exists in keys.go; (2) probe the wallet-management capability once, e.g. OpenWallet(ctx, "xmrswap-probe-does-not-exist"), and refuse to start when the error is NO_WALLET_DIR or "Command unavailable in restricted mode" (an ordinary "file not found" means the wallet-dir is configured); (3) document `monero-wallet-rpc --stagenet --wallet-dir <dir> --rpc-bind-port 38082` in docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md §3.D and in the CLI usage block. Separately, in swap.go's xmr_locking, distinguish a definitive *RPCError (-2 wrong address, -13 no wallet open, -29 watch-only, -17/-37 not enough money) from a transport failure: a definitive refusal means nothing was sent, so the phase should stay xmr_locking and surface, not become LockUnknown. Local, no contract change.
- **Regression test** tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap: a unit test over the new startup check asserting a Stagenet-prefixed address under -net mainnet is refused before any Swap is constructed; plus a swap_test case with a Wallet whose Transfer returns &RPCError{Code:-2}, asserting Alice stays in xmr_locking with LockUnknown false and the error surfaced.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. BL-11 ("no stagenet run") is the nearest entry and does not cover it. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM/reproduced: Real as a bundle of three configuration traps, MEDIUM is fair for the bundle. (a) Network: reproduced — ownWallet accepted a genuine stagenet address under cfg.Net=Mainnet, and grep confirms cfg.Net is read at only three sites (swap.go:430 to BUILD the joint address, 611 and 972-977 to compare DECLA

### XMR-5 — The hot-key posture is worse than the docs imply: both parties run spend-capable Monero wallets for the whole swap, the joint spend key lands in a passwordless wallet file that is never closed, and the wallet-rpc password is passed in argv

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/monero.go:356` (dimension: Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix)
- **Attack path** Is view-only + offline signing practical here? No, and the code says so: - Alice must run a monero-wallet-rpc holding her spend key, unlocked, to make the lock transfer (monero.go:334, swap.go:1078). There is no do_not_relay / unsigned-txset path on the Wallet interface (monero.go:27-49), so cold signing is not expressible at all. - Bob's WATCH is genuinely view-only (GenerateFromKeys with spendKey "" at swap.go:682), which is right. The sweep is not: swap.go:791-796 forms s_a+s_b and hands it to generate_from_keys (monero.go:356-379) with `"password": ""` and `"autosave_current": true`. On a real server that writes an unencrypted wallet file holding the joint spend key into --wallet-dir. - CloseWallet is declared (monero.go:38) and implemented (monero.go:385) but called from nowhere in swap.go — `grep -n CloseWallet tools/xmrswap/swap.go` returns no matches. The joint spend wallet is left OPEN in the wallet-rpc process after the swap and its file is left on disk. Nothing deletes it. - The state file holds every secret in plaintext hex (state.go:20-60: Own.Secret, Gate, View, and after a Claim/Refund also Revealed — i.e. after the peer's reveal it contains BOTH halves of the joint spend key). It is 0600 via writeAtomic (state.go:164-180), but state_test.go:63 skips the mode assertion when OS=Windows_NT, which is the operator's platform, so on Windows the mode is neither enforced nor tested. writeAtomic also opens path+".tmp" with O_CREATE and no O_EXCL, so a pre-existing temp file keeps its own wider mode and a symlink at that path is followed. - The --rpc-login password is reachable only as -monero-pass (cmd/xmrswap/main.go:43); there is no environment fallback (grep for Getenv across tools/xmrswap/ and its cmd/ returns only test helpers), unlike tools/netwallet's VEIL_WALLET_PASSWORD. It therefore sits in argv, readable by any local process. - monero.go's digest auth is MD5 over plain HTTP (monero.go:222-275), and cmd/xmrswap/main.go:82-88 only WARNS for a non-loopback -monero, so an operator who ignores the warning ships MD5-digest credentials over the wire.  The worst combination is with XMR-2: when the sweep ends sweep_unknown the XMR is still at the joint address AND its spend key exists unencrypted in two files plus one open wallet-rpc process.
- **Preconditions** Ordinary operation. The exposure widens on Windows (no mode enforcement, and the state_test assertion is skipped there), on a shared host (argv), and if -monero is not loopback.
- **Impact** A key that can move the swap's XMR is at rest, unencrypted, on the user's disk for as long as the operator leaves the files there — with no ceremony, no wipe and no close. This is the answer to §20/§22: the architecture is not "view-only plus offline signing"; it is two hot wallets and a plaintext key file per swap, for a window the protocol sizes at about eight hours.
- **Exploitability** No remote exploit — this is exposure, not a hole. Anything that can read the operator's disk or process list during or after a swap (malware, a backup, a shared machine, a second local account) takes the joint spend key; on Windows the file mode is not even a speed bump.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from code, with the load-bearing call sites verified by grep rather than by eye: $ grep -n CloseWallet tools/xmrswap/swap.go            → (no matches; only monero.go:38 and monero.go:385) $ grep -n Getenv tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go tools/xmrswap/*.go tools/xmrswap/proof_test.go:197 ... state_test.go:63 ... swap_test.go:466,483   (test helpers only) monero.go:362 is the literal `"password": ""`; state_test.go:63 is the literal `&& os.Getenv("OS") != "Windows_NT"` skip; state.go:166 is `os.OpenFile(tmp, os.O_WRONLY|os.O_CREATE|os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)` with no O_EXCL. Not run: I have no monero-wallet-rpc to observe the file generate_from_keys leaves behind, and I created no key mater
- **Fix** (1) Give the joint wallets a password: 32 random bytes kept in the state file beside the key that is already there, passed to generate_from_keys and open_wallet — it costs nothing and stops a stolen wallet file being usable on its own. (2) Call CloseWallet on the `swept` transition, and add an `xmrswap wipe -state <file>` subcommand that overwrites the secrets once Sweep.Txs are confirmed. (3) Add -monero-pass-env (or read XMRSWAP_MONERO_PASSWORD) so the password need not be in argv, as tools/netwallet already does. (4) In writeAtomic use O_EXCL and an explicit Chmod 0600 so an existing file or a symlink cannot widen it. (5) State plainly in docs/XMR_SWAP.md §10 that both parties run a hot spend-capable Monero wallet for the whole window and that the joint key is written to disk in the clear. All local; only (2) needs a judgement about when a wipe is safe, so ship (1), (3), (4) and (5) now and sequence (2).
- **Regression test** tools/xmrswap: a test asserting GenerateFromKeys is never called with an empty password; a test asserting CloseWallet is called on the `swept` transition; a writeAtomic test that pre-creates path+".tmp" mode 0666 and asserts the written file is 0600; and extend state_test.go so the Windows branch asserts the file's DACL rather than skipping silently.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/monero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/state.go, tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, docs/XMR_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** Partly known — the architecture map lists "Secrets on disk" and the Windows 0600 no-op. Not carried by any BL number. The passwordless never-closed joint wallet, the argv password, the writeAtomic mode/symlink gap, and the conclusion that no view-only mode exists for either party are new.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/MEDIUM: Real as a design fact; the finder is honest that it is reasoned, not run, and I did not run it either (I created no key material and have no monero-wallet-rpc). Every load-bearing sub-claim I checked holds: monero.go:362 is the literal \"password\": \"\" for generate_from_keys and monero.go:386 the 

### ZEC-5 — ParseV5 panics instead of erroring on an oversize length: an integer overflow in the bounds check

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `3175401`.
- **Where** `tools/zecswap/sighash.go:293` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** reader.bytes guards with `if n < 0 || r.p+n > len(r.b)`. n comes from `int(r.compact())` on a length field read out of a transaction the node handed us (ParseV5 lines 251, 259, and orchard.go's Proofs read). For a CompactSize of 0x7fffffffffffffff, n is positive so the first clause misses, and r.p+n overflows int64 to a negative number so the second clause misses too; the slice expression r.b[r.p : r.p+n] then panics. The watcher this package is meant to sit under reads raw transactions from a chain anyone can write to (the maker's claim, in particular, is a transaction chosen by the counterparty), so a crafted or corrupt transaction kills the process rather than being rejected.
- **Preconditions** The parser is fed any byte string with a length prefix above roughly 2^63 − len(raw). No key or funds are involved.
- **Impact** Denial of service on the taker's or the maker's watcher at exactly the moment it must act (reading the claim to extract s, or scanning for the funding). With a companion that has no supervisor, that is a missed cltv or a missed T_dero — the loss paths of ZEC-7.
- **Exploitability** Cheap for anyone who can get bytes in front of the parser; also reachable by accident from a truncated RPC response.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd tools && go test -count=1 -run TestParseV5HugeCompactSize ./zecswap   --- FAIL: TestParseV5HugeCompactSize (0.00s)       fuzz_precision_test.go:46: ParseV5 panicked instead of erroring: runtime error: slice bounds out of range [:-9223372036854775743] My own first probe produced the same stack, rooted at sighash.go:297 inside (*reader).bytes called from ParseV5 at sighash.go:251.
- **Fix** In reader.bytes, compare against the remaining length instead of adding: `if n < 0 || n > len(r.b)-r.p { r.err = …; return nil }`. Do the same for every `int(r.compact())` call site (sighash.go:251, 259 and orchard.go's Proofs) or, better, add reader.varBytes() that reads the count and the payload together with that guard. While there, bound Tx.ExpiryHeight to ZIP 203's maximum of 499,999,999 on parse and on build.
- **Regression test** tools/zecswap/fuzz_precision_test.go TestParseV5HugeCompactSize and FuzzParseV5NeverPanics (already in the tree from a concurrent pass; both RED until the fix).
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/sighash.go, tools/zecswap/orchard.go
- **Already known as** New to my pass; a concurrent auditor's tools/zecswap/fuzz_precision_test.go (TestParseV5HugeCompactSize, FuzzParseV5NeverPanics) landed in the tree during this session and covers the same defect, so the regression test already exists. Not in any backlog entry.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-7 — The whole safety margin of the ZEC market lives in a watcher and broadcast layer that does not exist, and the claim branch never expires

- **Where** `docs/ZEC_SWAP.md:196` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** tools/zecswap today is script bytes, addresses, ECDSA signing and ZIP 225/244 digests over a Tx a caller must construct by hand. Every safety property docs/ZEC_SWAP.md asserts is assigned to the companion: the 8-block DERO stability wait (§3 step 4), the field-by-field check of the lock, the C_zec = 10 confirmation wait before claiming (§5, and Z5's mitigation), the T_dero − now ≥ 1,200 precondition (§5), the P_lock deadline, the per-offer secret and reused-H refusal (Z8), the dust/fee floor (Z10), the "refuse to claim a script that does not match" rule (§3 exits). None of them is code. Separately, the redeem script's IF branch carries no timelock, so after cltv both branches are live: a maker who walked away can claim the taker's ZEC at any later moment. Since T_dero (8 h) is far past cltv (2 h), a taker who has not refunded within those six hours faces a maker who has already Refunded the DERO and can now take the ZEC for nothing.
- **Preconditions** Anyone using the package as it stands, or a taker whose refund is late for any reason — including ZEC-4 (no valid branch id), ZEC-5 (a crashed watcher) or simply being offline.
- **Impact** Loss of the taker's ZEC in the late-refund case; in every other case the market cannot be performed at all. This is the reason the market must not be exposed in the app (there is no ZEC screen today, which is correct).
- **Exploitability** Not an attack path so much as the absence of the defences the note counts on; the late-refund case is a free option for the maker that costs one transaction.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from the code, not exploited — no Zcash node exists here. Evidence of absence: `grep -rn "getblockchaininfo\|sendrawtransaction\|getaddressutxos\|http" tools/zecswap` returns nothing outside comments; the CLI (tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go:22-32) has exactly two subcommands, `script` and `address`; `grep -rniE "zcash|zswap|board\.bas" app/*.js` finds no swap screen, only the wrapped-asset vault. The claim branch's lack of a deadline is visible in the redeem script bytes: 63 a8 20 <H> 88 76 a9 14 <pkh> 88 ac — no OP_CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY before OP_ELSE.
- **Fix** Not a patch — a build, and these are its exact requirements. NODE: read the tip height, `consensus.nextblock` from getblockchaininfo, getaddressutxos for the P2SH address (zebrad implements all three; zcashd reached end of support on 2026-07-18 at height 3,417,100 and will not support NU6.3, so name zebrad first in §7 and drop zcashd), getrawtransaction to read the maker's claim, sendrawtransaction to broadcast. FUNDING (taker): pay exactly wantAmount to the P2SH address, fee per ZIP 317 (marginal_fee 5,000 zat, grace_actions 2, so at least 10,000 zat), refuse to fund unless (i) h_<id> is exactly the lower-case hex of the bytes in the script (ZEC-2), (ii) the DERO lock is ≥ 8 blocks deep and every field matches the board row, (iii) T_dero − now ≥ 1,200 DERO blocks, (iv) CheckCLTV passes (ZEC-3), (v) H has not appeared in another board row (Z8). CLAIM (maker): build the spend with ClaimScriptSig, sign under the tip's nextblock branch id, nSequence 0xffffffff is fine, nLockTime 0, nExpiryHeight 0 or a value above the current tip, and refuse to broadcast within a margin of cltv so the taker still has its DERO window. REFUND (taker): nLockTime ≥ cltv AND nSequence on the input ≠ 0xffffffff, or CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY fails (BIP 65: "the nSequence field of the txin is 0xffffffff"); the transaction becomes minable at height cltv+1; nExpiryHeight MUST be 0 or strictly greater than cltv, because ZIP 203 makes a transaction invalid in any block above nExpiryHeight while nLockTime keeps it out of every block at or below cltv — an expiry below cltv is an unbroadcastable refund. The refund must be attempted at cltv and retried until it confirms; a companion that stops before that loses the ZEC to the maker's never-expiring claim branch. STATE: durable intent before every broadcast, a status command that says what is lost if it stops now, and a resume that reads both chains — the discipline tools/xmrswap already has. Finally, add the claim-branch-never-expires fact and the six-hour late-refund window to docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §9 as a new row; it is not in Z1-Z16.
- **Regression test** When the builder lands: a table test that a refund transaction with nSequence 0xffffffff, or nLockTime < cltv, or 0 < nExpiryHeight ≤ cltv is refused before signing; and a testnet run recording the three txids (BL-42).
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: tools/zecswap/, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** BL-40b ("the watcher, the ZIP 317 fee and broadcast remain") and BL-42 (testnet). The backlog understates the scope: there is also no transaction builder, no funding path, no node client, no branch-id read, no durable state or resume, no status command, and none of the §5 policy checks — and it does not record that the claim branch has no deadline, which is what turns a missed refund into a loss rather than a delay.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-8 — The design note has no transparent-pool risk section, while a dated draft ZIP would cut off the supply of transparent ZEC

- **Where** `docs/ZEC_SWAP.md:1` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** Not an attack: a protocol-direction risk the note is silent about. The market is transparent-only by construction (§1). A draft ZIP under discussion on the Zcash forum, "Disabling Addition of New Value to the Transparent Pool", would disable creating transactions with transparent outputs and non-transparent inputs, with a proposed activation of 28 October 2026. Transparent-to-transparent spends and existing P2SH HTLC scripts keep working, so the mechanism does not break — but a Zcash holder whose ZEC is shielded (the shielded pool passed $1bn in August 2026) could no longer move it to a t-address, so the population able to take an offer collapses to holders of already-transparent ZEC. Separately, the note names zcashd first among the nodes a party runs (§7); zcashd reached end of support on 2026-07-18 at height 3,417,100 and will not support NU6.3.
- **Preconditions** None in the code; the risk is that DMZ commits its first mainnet market to a pool the ecosystem is deliberately draining.
- **Impact** Not a loss of funds — a launch decision. If the note's §11 sequencing ("the first thing DMZ can honestly put on mainnet for a Zcash holder") is acted on without answering this, the market may have no addressable users within weeks of shipping.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only. Sources read: https://forum.zcashcommunity.com/t/draft-zip-disabling-addition-of-new-value-to-the-transparent-pool/46599 — the proposal would "disable any facilities to create transparent transactions that have both one or more outputs to Transparent addresses, and one or more inputs from non-Transparent addresses", proposed activation 28 October 2026, status draft/under discussion, transparent-to-transparent unaffected. https://z.cash/support/zcashd-deprecation/ — "zcashd is deprecated and will not support NU6.3", end of support at block height 3,417,100. I also answered the first half of BL-47 while here: ZIP 255 (NU6.1), ZIP 257 (NU6.2) and ZIP 258 (NU6.3) make no change to
- **Fix** Documentation and an operator decision, not code. Add a section to docs/ZEC_SWAP.md stating: which pools DMZ supports (transparent only) and why; that shielded ZEC must be unshielded first and what that reveals; that a draft ZIP with a proposed October 2026 activation would make that unshielding impossible, with the forum link; and the branch-id/upgrade cadence the companion must track. Update §7 to name zebrad as the node and record zcashd's end of support. Answer BL-47's first half in the backlog with the ZIP 255/257/258 citations above. Then put the same sentence in front of the taker on the accept screen when BL-43 is built. Whether to ship a transparent-only market at all in that light is the operator's call.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest assertion, once the ZEC screen exists (BL-43), that the accept screen carries the transparent-only sentence verbatim; nothing testable in Go.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: docs/ZEC_SWAP.md, docs/BACKLOG.md
- **Already known as** BL-47 (R): "Confirm CLTV and P2SH remain in the transparent consensus rules on the Zcash roadmap; if transparent pools are ever deprecated, the market has no leg." The backlog understates it by filing it as research with no date: there is a named draft with a proposed activation of 28 October 2026, and BL-44's planned "unshield to trade here" guidance is the exact thing it would forbid. The design note itself — the document that decides this market goes to mainnet first — never mentions it, and §12 Sources does not list a single ZIP by number.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### DVM-2 — nfa.bas Start stores the clamped charity share before it validates the charity address, and the refusal path commits: one refused Start poisons the NFA so every later settlement reverts and any bid taken meanwhile is locked in the contract

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`. Start stores the clamped share only after the address check (`contracts/nfa.bas` note 7). zzsplitpay S2 and S3.

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:139` (dimension: DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17))
- **Attack path** Start() line 137 (`155 IF charityDonatePerc > tempPercCount`) sends an over-large share down the clamp path: line 138 clamps it, line 139 (`170 STORE("charityDonatePerc", ...)`) persists the clamped value, and only then does line 140 (`175 IF IS_ADDRESS_VALID(ADDRESS_RAW(charityDonateAddr))`) check the address. On failure it goes to 400, which returns the unit and RETURNS 0 — and RETURN 0 commits, so the store at line 139 survives a call that did nothing else. init() never stores charityDonateAddr (contracts/nfa.bas:71 stores charityDonatePerc 0 and nothing else), and resetVars stores it as the empty string (line 366). The contract is now in a state that Start can never clear, because line 143 (`190 IF charityDonatePerc > 0`) tests the ARGUMENT, not the stored value: every later listing keeps the poisoned share while its own arguments say 0. At settlement, processDEROFinalPayment line 342 sees the share > 0 and line 345 does LOAD("charityDonateAddr") — a missing key on a fresh NFA (DVM panic, full revert) or the empty string after any resetVars (SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS panics at vendor/derohe/dvm/dvm_functions.go:406, full revert).
- **Preconditions** One Start() call carrying charityDonatePerc greater than the room the fees leave — (10000 - protoFee - 100*artificerFee - 100*royalty)/100, e.g. 95 on a 5% royalty / 50 bps NFA — together with a charityDonateAddr that does not decode. app/nfas.js clamps the share client-side (nfas.js:88, 101), so this is NOT reachable through the shipped DMZ app; it is reachable from any other wallet, from a hand-built SCDATA call, from Engram, or from any third-party ART-NFA-MS1 tool, all of which the standard exists to allow.
- **Impact** The NFA becomes unsellable: BuyItNow, CloseListing and the settling empty Bid all revert (buyers and bidders lose only gas). If the owner runs an auction in the meantime, the winning bid is held by the contract and cannot be released — CloseListing reverts, CancelListing is past its 300-second window, and the only escape is the already-known stranded-bid path (a stranger's empty BuyItNow on the expired auction), which clears the poison via resetVars but leaves the bid stranded until the owner relists and someone else bids. So a state a SELLER creates locks a THIRD PARTY's funds, and the contract's own header claim of an exit for every state does not hold for it.
- **Exploitability** Self-inflicted by the lister, but it is the shape a mistyped address takes rather than a deliberate act, it persists silently across every later listing whose own arguments say charity 0, and nothing in the app or on any explorer shows the stored share until a settlement reverts. A malicious seller could do it deliberately to take a bid hostage, but gains nothing beyond griefing.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ cd <repo> $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestPoisonedCharityShare|TestAResetLeavesAnEmptyCharity' -v  === RUN   TestPoisonedCharityShareMakesTheNFAUnsellableAndLocksABid     poisoned: charityDonatePerc=94 with no charityDonateAddr, from a Start that was refused     CloseListing by the owner refused by the DVM: Recovered in function KEY NOT FOUND ...     empty Bid by the winner refused by the DVM: ...     empty Bid by a stranger refused by the DVM: ...     STUCK: the NFA cannot be sold and alice's 5000000 units of the quote are locked in the contract; the only exit left is the BuyItNow-on-an-expired-auction path, which strands the bid instead     resetV
- **Fix** Contract change, so not safe now (redeploy + new template hash, same chain of files as DVM-1; existing NFAs cannot be repaired). In contracts/nfa.bas Start, delete the store at line 139 and move it after the address check, so the clamp path matches the ordering the non-clamp path already uses at lines 144-146: `175 IF IS_ADDRESS_VALID(...) == 1 THEN GOTO 180 ELSE GOTO 400` / `180 STORE("charityDonatePerc", charityDonatePerc)` / `185 STORE("charityDonateAddr", ADDRESS_RAW(charityDonateAddr))`. Belt and braces, make the refusal path a real refusal: line 154's `400` branch should RETURN 1 rather than RETURN 0, so nothing a rejected Start touched can commit — the DVM refunds the attached unit on a revert at ring 2, which is the ring nfa.bas requires anyway. Safe now, and worth doing regardless: app/nfas.js already clamps the share client-side; keep that clamp and add a line to the sheet naming the stored share when it is non-zero, so a poisoned NFA is visible before someone bids on it.
- **Regression test** tests/zzsplitpay_test.go TestPoisonedCharityShareMakesTheNFAUnsellableAndLocksABid and TestAResetLeavesAnEmptyCharityAddressBehindTheShare, both written and passing. After the fix, invert the first assertion: the refused Start must leave charityDonatePerc at 0, and the later auction must settle normally. Add a case asserting that no path can produce charityDonatePerc > 0 with EXISTS("charityDonateAddr") false or LOAD("charityDonateAddr") == "".
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/templates/nfa.bas, app/config.js, deploy/networks.json, app/nfas.js, tests/zzsplitpay_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md (BL-60..BL-74 cover NFA copy, fuzz, R4 stranding and indexers, not this) and not in any prior audit. Inherited verbatim from upstream ART-NFA-MS1 — I fetched https://raw.githubusercontent.com/civilware/artificer-nfa-standard/main/ART-NFA-MS1/ART-NFA-MS1.bas and its lines 165-180 are byte-for-byte the same store-then-validate order, and its init() likewise never stores charityDonateAddr. DMZ rewrote only the bound at Start 150-165 (nfa.bas header line 28-29) and kept the ordering defect.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-4 — Every execution sheet signs a min_out derived from a snapshot of unbounded age; the slippage setting guards nothing

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/token.js:141-252 (tradeSheet), app/sheets.js:66-100 (liquidity), app/nfas.js:61-84 (buy/bid):244` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** feed.js loadContract() builds a NEW market object whenever the probe signature changes (feed.js:344, next.set(id, s)), and refresh() replaces this.assets wholesale. The token PAGE re-draws on D.onChange (token.js:133) but no sheet does: T.tradeSheet captures `s` in its closure and never calls D.assets.get again. go() computes `min = A.minOut(q.out, slip)` from that captured record, and app.trade's only floor is `minOut >= A.minOut(q.out, MAX_SLIP)` - a check against the same stale q. There is no age display, no re-quote, no staleness banner and no timeout: a sheet left open across a large price move signs a min_out anchored to a price that no longer exists. Because SC call arguments are visible in the DERO mempool, the gap between the fresh fair output and the stale min_out is free extraction for a sandwicher.
- **Preconditions** A sheet open across a material reserve move - a user reading the cost rows, switching tabs, or being interrupted. No attacker action is needed to create the gap; a mempool watcher is needed to take it.
- **Impact** Loss bounded only by how far the market moved while the sheet was open, not by the slippage the user chose. Quantified with the app's own mirror on a simulator-sized pool: a single opposing trade turned a 1% slippage setting into an 81.9% giveaway. The same shape applies to sheets.js AddLiquidity/RemoveLiquidity (min_lp / min_dero / min_asset) and to nfas.js buySheet, which pays a captured s.startPrice - nfa.bas treats an over-price BuyItNow as the sale and splits the whole amount, so a relist at a lower price silently takes the excess.
- **Exploitability** Requires a mempool-watching adversary, which the app's own copy already assumes exists (token.js:230, "anyone can compute which address made this call"). The window is entirely under the victim's control and is routinely minutes.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ node <scratch>/stale.mjs   # imports the real app/amm.js via tests/js/harness.mjs shown out  t0: 1809913 min_out signed: 1791813   (1% below the t0 quote - the default chip) true  out  t1: 9921112    (after one opposing sell of 30,000,000 into rd=1e7 ra=2e7) extractable by a sandwicher: 8129299 units = 81.9% of the fill  Static proof that no sheet re-reads state: $ grep -n "onChange" app/token.js app/sheets.js app/nfas.js app/token.js:133   (the PAGE) app/nfas.js:210    (the PAGE) $ awk 'NR>=136 && NR<=270' app/token.js | grep -n "D.assets.get\|onChange"   -> no output
- **Fix** In app/token.js go() (and the equivalents in sheets.js and nfas.js), re-read the record - `const cur = D.assets.get(s.scid)` - recompute the quote, and refuse if the fresh output differs from the one on screen by more than the chosen slippage, re-rendering the sheet with the new numbers instead of signing. Cheap alternative that also fixes the display: subscribe each sheet to D.onChange the way the pages do, re-running quote() and repainting the cost rows, with the confirm button disabled for one tick after a change. Local, testable, no contract change.
- **Regression test** tests/js - drive the amm mirror through open-quote -> reserve move -> confirm and assert the signed min_out is derived from the post-move reserves, i.e. (fresh_out - min_out) / fresh_out <= slip.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/token.js, app/sheets.js, app/nfas.js, app/app.js
- **Already known as** New in this shape. The FOLLOWUP treats slippage as adequate; it is, only against movement between signing and inclusion, not against movement between rendering and signing.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-5 — The final gas estimate names the payer to the node before the wallet ever prompts, contradicting the app's own rule

- **Where** `app/app.js:134:134` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** app.tx builds the estimate as `Object.assign({transfers, sc_rpc: estRPC, ringsize: ring}, ring === 2 ? { signer: W.address } : {}, base)` and sends it to DERO.GetGasEstimate before W.transfer. sc_rpc carries the entrypoint and every argument. So for every ring-2 write - every self-paid trade, add/remove liquidity, stake/unstake/claim, follow, chat post, founders mint/register/claim/buy, NFA bid/buy/start, vault Register and Burn - the node learns the exact address, the exact contract, the entrypoint and the amounts at the moment of intent, and learns it even when the user then cancels in the wallet. app/sheets.js:36-49 does the opposite for the preview and says why in its own comment ("asking 'what would this cost' must not tell the node who is about to act"), using a random signer; app.tx then undoes it on the call that matters.
- **Preconditions** A node the user does not run, or one that is compromised or logging. The app permits a remote wss:// node (rpc.js:99-111) and adopts the wallet's GetDaemon endpoint with no confirmation step (app.js:28).
- **Impact** An observer at the node builds a complete intent log keyed to the DERO address: what each address was about to do, when, for how much, and whether they went through with it. That is strictly more than the chain reveals - cancelled transactions never reach the chain at all. It is worst on vault Burn, where the estimate carries the DERO address and the foreign destination string together before either is public. It does not affect the ring-16 private trade path, which correctly omits the signer.
- **Exploitability** Passive; no attacker capability beyond running or watching the node the user already trusts for reads.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from the source, not executed against a logging node. $ sed -n '134p' app/app.js   ... Object.assign({ transfers, sc_rpc: estRPC, ringsize: ring }, ring === 2 ? { signer: W.address } : {}, base) ... $ sed -n '46,48p' app/sheets.js   // ... the signer is a random address and never the connected one, because asking "what would this cost" must not tell the node who is about to act.   const r = await V.node.gasEstimate({ scid: opts.scid, ringsize: 2, signer: gasAddr, ... }); To observe it: run a logging proxy in front of 127.0.0.1:20000, point Settings at it, and confirm any ring-2 action - the GetGasEstimate frame carries `signer: dero1...` before the wallet prompt appears.
- **Fix** Use the same cached random address sheets.js already derives (V.node.randomAddress()) as the estimate's `signer` in app.tx, and fall back to the connected address only if the node refuses the call - the estimator runs the contract at BLOCK_HEIGHT() = 0 anyway, so it is not simulating the real execution for height-gated entrypoints regardless. Where an entrypoint genuinely prices differently per signer, say so in the gas row rather than paying for it silently. Local and testable.
- **Regression test** tests/js - stub V.node.call, run app.tx with ring 2, and assert the GetGasEstimate params contain no address equal to W.address.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/app.js, app/sheets.js, app/forms.js
- **Already known as** Listed as a risk by this audit's frontend reader; no BL number. Not in FINDINGS.md.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-6 — The published app is mutable forever by one key, and no tool in the tree can reach the immutable state

- **Where** `tools/telainstall/main.go:249,259 + vendor/tela/TELA-INDEX-1/TELA-INDEX-1.bas:19-35,52-55:249` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** TELA-INDEX-1's init() stores `owner = address()`, and address() returns ADDRESS_STRING(SIGNER()) at ring 2 and the literal "anon" above it (lines 19-35). UpdateCode refuses only when owner == "anon" (line 52). tools/telainstall installs the INDEX at ring size 2 and has no flag to do otherwise; deploy/README.md's rollback procedure depends on keeping that key. Whoever holds it can publish a new bundle at any time: config.js decides the factory, the founders and chat SCIDs, the vault list with its symbols and decimals, feeBps/pfeeBps, and the template hashes the whole verification rests on; index.html carries the CSP in a meta tag, so a republish rewrites the policy too. Nothing the user can check on chain distinguishes "the key was destroyed" from "the key is waiting".
- **Preconditions** Loss, theft or retention of the deployer wallet. No other capability.
- **Impact** Complete compromise of every user: a substituted config.js re-points every SCID and every accepted template hash, and app.tx's allow-list is built from that same file, so the attacker's contracts become the only "verified listings". The wallet still asks the user to confirm, but it confirms the transaction the substituted page built. This is the frontend counterpart of the directive's liveness rule ("the operator revokes their own access after deployment"): for the INDEX that state is reachable in the contract (owner == "anon") and unreachable with the shipped tooling.
- **Exploitability** Requires the key. The point of the finding is that the key is required to survive under the current runbook, and that the app cannot prove otherwise.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from source; no mainnet INDEX exists to test against. $ grep -n 'Ringsize\|ringsize' tools/telainstall/main.go 7://  [-index=<scid>]   update this INDEX instead of installing a new one (ringsize 2 owner only) $ sed -n '19,35p;52,55p' vendor/tela/TELA-INDEX-1/TELA-INDEX-1.bas 30 STORE("owner", address()) ... 30 IF IS_ADDRESS_VALID(s) THEN GOTO 50 / 40 RETURN "anon" / 50 RETURN ADDRESS_STRING(s) 10 IF LOAD("owner") == "anon" THEN GOTO 20   (UpdateCode refuses) To confirm the immutable path on the simulator: install an INDEX answering ringsize 16, then DERO.GetSC that scid and read `owner` - expect "anon", and UpdateCode then returns 1 for everyone. NOT PERFORMED (needs a wallet).
- **Fix** Give tools/telainstall a `-final` flag that installs the INDEX above ring size 2 so `owner` is "anon", and make the mainnet runbook use it for the last publish; keep the ring-2 form for testnet and for staged mainnet publishes. Add an app-side disclosure that reads the INDEX's own `owner` and `commit` keys and prints them on the notice, so a user can see for themselves whether the app they are running can still be replaced. Operator decision, because it permanently forecloses rollback - that is exactly the trade the directive asks for and it is not mine to make.
- **Regression test** A tools test that installs an INDEX at ring > 2 on the simulator and asserts DERO.GetSC returns owner == "anon" and that UpdateCode from the installer returns 1.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: tools/telainstall/main.go, deploy/README.md, app/notice.js
- **Already known as** Disclosed in app/notice.js:70. Adjacent to BL-1/BL-147/BL-241 (revocation), which describe a burn-address route that does not exist. The backlog understates it: it treats revocation as a factory-ownership question and does not record that the INDEX is the larger trust anchor, nor that no tooling path installs it at ring > 2.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-05 — A tmode-2 launch that never graduates locks every atom of tax its buyers paid, with no entrypoint on the contract able to release it

- **Where** `contracts/launch.bas:283` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** Every phase-0 buy and sell accrues tx = bps(amount, tax) into tacc (launch.bas buyto/sellto), for any tmode != 0. The three exits from tacc are: ClaimTax, which refuses unless tmode == 1 (launch.bas:270); Buyback, which refuses unless tmode == 2 AND phase == 1 (launch.bas:283); and dist(), reached only through Distribute/Stake/Unstake/Claim, which refuse unless tmode == 3 (launch.bas:371). A tmode-2 launch in phase 0 therefore satisfies none of them. Phase 1 is entered only by a buy that fills the curve to `grad` (launch.bas:141-145); there is no time-based graduation, no partial graduation and no abandonment path. If the token never sells out, tacc grows with every trade and stays in the contract for ever.
- **Preconditions** A launch created with tmode 2 (buyback-and-lock) and tax > 0 — both offered in app/forms.js — that does not reach its graduation target. Up to 10% (tax <= 1000 bps) of the quote leg of every trade is affected.
- **Impact** Permanent lock of trader money in the market's quote asset, which on a wrapped-quote launch is wXMR or wZEC. Reproduced: five 1,000,000,000-unit buys on a 1e12-supply / 1e11-grad launch left 500,000,000 quote units in tacc, unreachable by Buyback, ClaimTax, Distribute, Claim or ClaimFees, from any caller and at any later height. It is also a direct hit on the directive's 'nobody can extract more than was deposited' from the other side: money goes in and can never come out.
- **Exploitability** No attack needed; it is the default outcome of a supported and app-offered configuration, and flopped launches are the common case. A creator could pick tmode 2 deliberately to make the tax look 'locked for the community' while knowing it is simply destroyed.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestLaunchTmode2TaxIsLockedForeverIfTheTokenNeverGraduates. $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'LaunchTmode2Tax' -v === RUN   TestLaunchTmode2TaxIsLockedForeverIfTheTokenNeverGraduates     launch.bas: 15257 bytes, install storage gas 15870, compute 1426000; C=800000000000 Lq=200000000000 vd0=33333333333 vt0=1066666666664     LOCKED: 500000000 of the quote asset sits in tacc at phase 0 with tmode 2. Buyback needs phase 1, ClaimTax needs tmode 1, dist() needs tmode 3. Only a full graduation can ever release it. --- PASS The test calls Buyback (from two different addresses), ClaimTax, Distribute, Claim and ClaimFees, then repeats Buyback at rin
- **Fix** Contract, needs a redeploy policy (launch.bas is a hashed shipped template; changing it rotates app/config.js templates.launch and the legacy list): let Buyback operate on the curve in phase 0 as well — buying from the bonding curve and adding the tokens to `locked` is well defined before graduation and is the same economic act — or, if buying on the curve is unwanted, make tmode-2 tax accrue only in phase 1 and leave the phase-0 quote leg untaxed. Safe now without a redeploy: app/forms.js should refuse tmode 2, or state before the create button that tax collected before graduation is unrecoverable if the token does not graduate, and app/token.js should show phase-0 tacc on a tmode-2 market as 'locked until graduation, permanently if it never graduates' rather than as a pending buyback.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go TestLaunchTmode2TaxIsLockedForeverIfTheTokenNeverGraduates — invert once fixed (Buyback in phase 0 must move tacc and grow `locked`), and keep a case asserting the 20-block spacing and the 1%-of-reserve cap still apply on the curve.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/launch.bas, reference/launch.go, app/forms.js, app/token.js, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** docs/BACKLOG.md PROTO·BC-6 ('tmode 2 tax accrued in phase 0 is unreachable until graduation', open). The backlog UNDERSTATES it by saying 'until graduation': for a bonding curve that never fills — the normal outcome — there is no 'until'. The funds are locked permanently and no party, privileged or not, has a call that moves them.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-06 — A sealed vault has no wind-down: no signer, honest or not, can ever recover a bond, and a slashed signer who bonds again loses that DERO to nobody

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:226` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** Unbond (vault.bas:226) refuses whenever LOAD("sealed") != 0 and an isg_ key EXISTS for the caller. Slash (vault.bas:556) sets isg_ to 0 rather than DELETEing it, so a slashed signer still satisfies EXISTS and is barred from Unbond for ever; AddSigner2 (vault.bas:180) refuses them for the same reason, so they can never be reinstated and no future Slash can move the balance either. Meanwhile Bond (vault.bas:200-217) accepts DERO from anyone including a slashed signer, storing it under bond_<addr> and (correctly) not counting it in bondtot. The result is a bond_ balance that no entrypoint in the contract can ever pay out. For an active signer the picture is only slightly better: the sole path that moves a bond after Seal is Slash, which sends it to the TREASURY (vault.bas:565), never back to the signer. There is no dissolution, retirement or expiry entrypoint anywhere in the file.
- **Preconditions** Any sealed vault. For the sharper case, a signer who has been slashed and later calls Bond() — which the contract invites, since Bond is how a candidate becomes eligible for AddSigner2.
- **Impact** Every DERO a signer bonds is locked for the life of the contract, and the contract has no end. An honest signer who wants to retire, or who bonded into a vault the operator later abandons, has no way out; the deposit is either frozen indefinitely or forfeited to the treasury. This is a state that needs a human (a k-quorum slash, which pays someone else) to leave, and for the slashed-re-bond case it is a state nobody can leave. It is also a live recruitment problem: nobody rational bonds into a vault whose bond is unconditionally unrecoverable.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack; a structural property. Reproduced twice.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestASlashedSignerWhoBondsAgainCanNeverRecoverThatDero and TestAfterSealAnHonestSignerBondCanNeverComeBack. $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'SlashedSignerWhoBonds|AfterSealAnHonestSigner' -v === RUN   TestASlashedSignerWhoBondsAgainCanNeverRecoverThatDero     STUCK: 7777 DERO recorded under bond_<slashed signer>, not counted in bondtot, and no entrypoint (Unbond, Slash, AddSigner2, ClaimFees) can ever move it. --- PASS === RUN   TestAfterSealAnHonestSignerBondCanNeverComeBack     STUCK: bondtot 3000 -> 2000; every DERO a signer bonds is either locked for the life of the vault or forfeited to the treasury. There is no wind-down entrypoint.
- **Fix** Two parts, both needing a redeploy policy for a live vault. Cheap and safe in the contract: make Bond refuse an address that has an isg_ key with value 0 (a slashed signer), so the trap cannot be entered — one added condition at vault.bas:204. Structural, an operator decision: decide whether a sealed vault should have a wind-down at all — e.g. a k-of-n Retire(addr) returning an active signer's bond once sup is 0 and no withdrawal is open, and a k-of-n Dissolve once sup is 0 that releases every remaining bond. Safe now with no contract change: deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md and the missing deploy/federation.md must tell every prospective signer that the bond is unconditionally unrecoverable after Seal, and app/wrap.js's trust panel should say the same rather than only 'slashable by a quorum of the others'.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go carries both. Once Bond refuses slashed signers, TestASlashedSignerWhoBondsAgainCanNeverRecoverThatDero inverts to asserting the Bond is refused.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, deploy/federation.md, app/wrap.js, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** Adjacent to docs/BACKLOG.md BL-87/BL-89 (signer recruitment) and PROTO·BC-4 (slashed bonds go to the treasury, not victims). Neither records that the bond has NO return path at all after Seal, nor the slashed-signer re-bond trap. Not in any prior audit.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-07 — An htlc.bas lock past t2 can be moved only by the named taker, so an abandoned swap freezes the maker's DERO for ever

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`. In `htlc.bas` v2 anyone may send Punish from `t2`. It carries no secret and pays only `t_<id>`, so the sender chooses when an abandoned lock closes, never who is paid (`contracts/htlc.bas:122-141`). The study's alternative, a maker window from `t2 + G2`, was not needed: this form gives the taker's Punish no deadline and still leaves no lock frozen.

- **Where** `contracts/htlc.bas:116` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** Refund (contracts/htlc.bas:94-117) requires t1 <= BLOCK_HEIGHT() < t2 and SIGNER() == m_<id>. Punish (htlc.bas:120-136) requires BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= t2 and SIGNER() == t_<id>. There is no third window and no permissionless sweep. Once the height passes t2 with st still 0, the only signature that can move v_<id> is the taker's. If the taker's key is lost, the taker abandons the swap, or the locked amount is below the taker's gas and attention threshold, the value stays in the contract for ever. The same corner is reached deterministically when the maker cannot satisfy Refund at all: htlc.bas checks only that hb is 64 characters, not that it is lowercase hex of a hash the maker can preimage, so a maker who locks against a malformed or unknown hb has Refund permanently closed and Punish becomes the only exit — again the taker's alone.
- **Preconditions** An htlc.bas lock whose maker misses the [t1, t2) window, or whose hb was never a hash the maker holds a preimage for, and whose taker then does not call Punish. The default policy widths (T1_MIN 400, T2_MIN +400 in tools/xmrswap) are companion policy, not contract rules; htlc.bas accepts t1 = now+2, t2 = t1+1.
- **Impact** Permanent lock of the maker's DERO (or one asset) with no permissionless exit and no privileged party to appeal to. Reproduced with 100,000 DERO still held at st 0 ten million blocks past t2. htlc.bas is not deployed on any network today (no SCID in deploy/networks.json or app/config.js), so this is a pre-launch finding rather than live exposure — which makes it the cheapest one on this list to fix.
- **Exploitability** No attacker needed; it is the intersection of two ordinary liveness failures. A taker who wants to hurt a maker at zero cost simply never calls Punish — the maker's money is destroyed and the taker keeps their XMR, which is strictly better for a taker whose XMR leg was never locked.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go — TestHtlcPastT2NeedsTheTakerOrTheFundsNeverMove. $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'HtlcPastT2' -v === RUN   TestHtlcPastT2NeedsTheTakerOrTheFundsNeverMove     STUCK: 100000 DERO held at height 10000026, st=0, and only the taker own signature can ever move it --- PASS Asserted: past t2, Punish by the maker and by a stranger both fail; Refund by the maker and by a stranger both fail; ten million blocks later the maker still cannot refund; st_0 is 0 and the contract still holds 100,000.
- **Fix** Contract change, so it needs a redeploy policy — but htlc.bas is undeployed, which makes now the cheapest moment. Add a final window: from t2 + G (G a generous grace, e.g. the same width as t2 - t1) let the MAKER take the funds back with no secret, so the punish period is a bounded penalty rather than an unbounded destruction. That keeps Punish's incentive intact (the taker has a whole grace period in which only they can act) while guaranteeing every lock resolves without any specific human. Separately, tighten Lock to reject an hb that is not 64 lowercase hex characters (and the same in swap.bas:132), so a maker cannot lock against a hash that makes their own Refund impossible. Safe now with no contract change: tools/xmrswap must enforce a minimum t2 - t1 and must schedule the Punish call rather than relying on the operator being at the keyboard (BL-15), and docs/XMR_SWAP.md §2 should stop describing Punish as certain.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go TestHtlcPastT2NeedsTheTakerOrTheFundsNeverMove — invert once a final maker window exists: at t2 + G the maker's Refund must succeed and the contract must be empty. Add a Lock-refusal case for a non-hex hb to tests/htlc_test.go's refusal table.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/htlc.bas, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, docs/XMR_SWAP.md, tests/htlc_test.go, tests/zzadversarial_liveness_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md; docs/XMR_SWAP.md §2 frames Punish as 'the price of doing nothing' for Bob, which assumes Alice always collects. BL-15 (no sleep-until scheduling) and BL-16 (last-block race) touch the neighbourhood but not this. The XMR-swap architecture reader noted the maker's liveness burden but not that the funds have NO exit if the taker never acts.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-08 — factory.bas can never reach an ownerless state, so the operator's promised revocation is unattainable and SetTreasury remains a permanent kill switch over every listing

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`, with SEC-K-02: after `Renounce` the factory is ownerless and SetTreasury refuses everyone. The runbook's Renounce step and `tools/verifydeploy`'s check of it are in the same pass.

- **Where** `contracts/factory.bas:45` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** factory.bas has exactly nine entrypoints (Initialize, SetTreasury, SetRegFee, TransferOwnership, ClaimOwnership, RegisterPool/Launch/NFT/NFA) plus the internal reg(); grep confirms no UPDATE_SC_CODE anywhere in contracts/. TransferOwnership (factory.bas:45-52) only stores tmpowner; ClaimOwnership (factory.bas:55-62) requires SIGNER() == LOAD("tmpowner"), so the transfer completes only when a key that can sign accepts it. A burn address cannot sign, so a transfer to one leaves tmpowner dangling and `owner` unchanged — the original key keeps SetTreasury and SetRegFee for ever. There is no renounce entrypoint and no state in which `owner` is absent or unusable. Separately, SetTreasury (factory.bas:27-36) rewrites the tstr the app compares against: app/feed.js:224 lists a market only when the market's install-time (immutable) treasury equals the factory's current one, so one owner call silently delists every pool, launch, NFT and NFA that already exists, with no state transition that can undo it for those markets — their treasury is immutable, so they can never match a new tstr again.
- **Preconditions** Any mainnet factory deployment. The exposure lasts as long as the owner key exists anywhere — and its non-existence is unverifiable on chain, which is the point.
- **Impact** The directive's central deployment premise — 'the operator revokes their own access after deployment: any state that needs a human to leave it is a defect' — is unattainable for factory.bas. Revocation can only mean destroying a key, which no user can check. Until then, one key can redirect every future registration fee and can permanently delist the entire market surface of the app; a thief of that key can do the same. No contract state, and no user action, can leave it.
- **Exploitability** Requires the owner key. The point is not that it is hard to reach but that no configuration of the deployed system ever removes it.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from code, not executed — there is no state to reproduce, only an absence. Confirmed by reading: $ grep -n '^Function' contracts/factory.bas lists all nine entrypoints with no renounce; $ grep -rn 'UPDATE_SC_CODE' contracts/ returns nothing; factory.bas:57 is IF SIGNER() != LOAD("tmpowner") THEN GOTO 900. The delisting consequence is read from app/feed.js:224 and stated in app/notice.js:385.
- **Fix** Operator decision plus a contract change, so not safe now. Either (a) add a Renounce() entrypoint that the owner calls once and that DELETEs `owner` and permanently disables SetTreasury and SetRegFee — the smallest change that makes the promise true and checkable, since anyone can then read the absent key; or (b) drop SetTreasury and SetRegFee entirely and make the treasury and regfee immutable at Initialize, matching every other contract in the tree, which removes the owner concept from factory.bas altogether. Whichever is chosen, app/feed.js must stop pinning market verification to the factory's LIVE tstr — pin it to a treasury list shipped in app/config.js (the loader already has the fallback branch at feed.js:137-141) so a treasury change cannot delist existing markets. Safe now with no code change: correct docs/BACKLOG.md BL-241/BL-147 and DECISIONS.md D-10, which describe a burn-address revocation the code cannot perform, and make deploy/README.md's mainnet runbook state what the surviving key can do.
- **Regression test** A DVM test in tests/registry_test.go asserting that after the intended revocation ceremony SetTreasury and SetRegFee are refused from every address including the deployer — a test that cannot be written today because no such state exists, which is itself the finding.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/factory.bas, app/feed.js, app/config.js, docs/BACKLOG.md, DECISIONS.md, deploy/README.md, tests/registry_test.go
- **Already known as** docs/BACKLOG.md BL-241 and D-10 describe a 'no-owner state'; BL-93/BL-147 describe a two-step transfer to a burn address. Both UNDERSTATE it: the code cannot reach either. app/notice.js:66-67 is the only place in the repository that states the truth ('There is no renounce, only a transfer to another key'), so the notice and the backlog contradict each other and the backlog is the wrong one.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-04 — Two companions on one state file each send the Monero lock; the ten-minute lock file never refreshes its own mtime

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go:188` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** takeLock (main.go:188-205) writes the pid once and never touches the file again, while the Step loop runs for the length of the swap (hours at mainnet policy). Any lock older than ten minutes is treated as a dead process's litter and taken over, so a second `xmrswap alice …` on the same -state file starts cleanly at minute eleven. The in-process one-shot guards do not span processes: `resumedPhase` and `transferTried` are per-Swap fields (swap.go:110-115), so two processes that each enter xmr_locking in-process — i.e. both loaded the state at saw_dero_lock or earlier — both see `resumedPhase != "xmr_locking"` and `transferTried == false` and each calls Wallet.Transfer (swap.go:1073-1096).
- **Preconditions** The operator starts a second companion on the same state file more than ten minutes after the first (a second terminal, a supervisor that respawns, a scripted retry). Both must reach xmr_locking in-process.
- **Impact** The taker pays the joint address twice. The maker's sweep_all takes everything at the joint address, so she loses 2x AmountXmr for 1x AmountDero. Reproduced at 2,000,000,000,000 piconero against a swap priced at 1,000,000,000,000.
- **Exploitability** Operator error, not a counterparty attack — but the guard exists precisely to prevent it and does not, for the whole duration of every real swap.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo>/tools && go test ./xmrswap -run TestTwoCompanionsOnOneStateFileBothLockXmr -v  === RUN   TestTwoCompanionsOnOneStateFileBothLockXmr     adversary_test.go:118: two companions on one state file paid the joint address 2 times, 2000000000000 piconero in total (one swap's price is 1000000000000) --- PASS  (An earlier run of the same test with the harness's own transfer hook still armed failed with "TEST INVARIANT: transfer from phase xmr_locked" — the second process paying against a state file the first had already advanced.)
- **Fix** Two changes. In main.go: refresh the lock file's mtime on every pass of the Step loop and raise the staleness threshold to a small multiple of -interval, so a live companion never looks dead. In swap.go: make the one-shot cross-process by re-reading the state from disk immediately before the transfer and refusing if its phase is no longer "xmr_locking" — the same durable check the sweep already relies on. Advisory file locking (O_EXCL plus a heartbeat) is the minimum; the mtime refresh alone closes the reproduced case.
- **Regression test** TestTwoCompanionsOnOneStateFileBothLockXmr inverted: two Swaps on one state path must produce exactly one Transfer. Add a unit test on takeLock that a lock refreshed within the interval is never taken over.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/adversary_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. BL-15 ("companion liveness") is about a companion being absent, not about two being present. state.go's header claims the file is 'the record that must exist before any irreversible step… a restart finds the intent and finishes it instead of doing it again' — true of a restart, false of a second live process.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-05 — A swap resumed against a different -dero wallet is accepted, and the lock can then never be refunded
