- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:128` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** LoadState (state.go:125-149) is strict about role and contract only. newSwap (swap.go:128-160) validates that cfg.MyDero parses, never that it equals st.MyDero. A companion restarted with -dero pointing at another wallet RPC is accepted silently. Bob's lock names the first wallet as m_<id>, so Refund from the second is refused by htlc.bas:26 for the whole [t1, t2) window and the taker Punishes at t2. Alice's case is the mirror: her Claim is refused by htlc.bas:25 until the cutoff passes and she loses the trade. Worse, if the mismatch is present before the lock, dero.Lock's FindLock keys on the new wallet's address (dero.go:582-589) and will not find the first wallet's lock, so it locks again from the second wallet — the maker then holds two locks and can refund neither with a single wallet.
- **Preconditions** An operator restart with the wrong -dero endpoint: two wallets on one machine, a copied command line, a wallet RPC that came up on a different port and was re-pointed.
- **Impact** The maker loses the whole locked amount to Punish at t2 with no error the operator can act on beyond a repeated 'refused' line. Fund loss, self-inflicted but silently permitted by a companion that checks the same thing carefully on the Monero side.
- **Exploitability** Operator error only; no attacker path. Listed because the directive names 'a swap resumed with different flags' and the guard for the equivalent Monero case already exists.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo>/tools && go test ./xmrswap -run TestAResumeAgainstADifferentDeroWalletIsAccepted -v  === RUN   TestAResumeAgainstADifferentDeroWalletIsAccepted     adversary_test.go:390: accepted: state says myDero deto1qyx6dat…, the flags say deto1qy05unf…; the lock's maker is the first and only it can Refund (phase watching_xmr)     adversary_test.go:403: at t2 the lock is still open: the resumed companion never refunded --- PASS
- **Fix** In newSwap, after LoadState: `if st.MyDero != "" && st.MyDero != cfg.MyDero { return nil, fmt.Errorf("state %s belongs to DERO address %s, the wallet at -dero is %s", …) }` — the same shape as state.go's role and contract checks and as ownWallet's Monero check. Four lines.
- **Regression test** TestAResumeAgainstADifferentDeroWalletIsAccepted inverted: NewBob must return an error naming both addresses.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/adversary_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. The asymmetry is visible in the code: ownWallet (swap.go:509-530) refuses to act when the open Monero wallet is not st.MyXmr; nothing does the same for the DERO wallet.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-06 — The Claim re-send policy ignores ClaimMargin, so a re-send can land inside the band Invariant 9 exists to keep it out of

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/dero.go:683` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** claimCutoff (swap.go:1226-1231) stops Alice sending a Claim once `h+1 >= t1 - 8 - ClaimMargin`, i.e. she stops at h = t1-17 with the default margin of 8. canResend (dero.go:683-691) permits a Claim re-send while `h + 8 + 1 < t1`, i.e. up to h = t1-10. So inside exit()'s retry loop a Claim is re-submitted at heights the outer policy has already declared unsafe, with zero blocks of slack for mining delay. If that re-send is mined in the last eight blocks before t1 and its block is then replaced, the re-mined Claim is refused at or after t1: the lock reopens with s_a already read by the maker's node, and a dishonest maker can then both Refund (recovering his DERO and revealing s_b) and sweep the joint XMR with s_a + s_b — review R2's exact scenario, whose whole defence is this margin.
- **Preconditions** A Claim that does not mine inside DeroRPC.Timeout (3 min) near the cutoff, plus a DERO reorganisation shallower than STABLE_LIMIT = 8, plus a maker willing to take both legs. The honest maker's own stable-height wait (swap.go:770-790) protects him, not her.
- **Impact** The taker can lose both legs: her XMR to the joint key she can no longer complete alone, and the DERO she thought she had claimed. The window is narrow but it is exactly the window the margin was introduced to remove.
- **Exploitability** Low — needs a reorg inside the eight-block band and a dishonest counterparty watching his own node's mempool. Reasoned from code, not reproduced: the fakes model no reorganisation.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only. Read tools/xmrswap/swap.go:1226-1231 against tools/xmrswap/dero.go:683-691: claimCutoff subtracts DefaultStableBlocks + margin, canResend subtracts DefaultStableBlocks + 1. With the default margin of 8 the re-send band is [t1-17, t1-10). To reproduce, fakes_test.go's fakeChain needs a reorg hook that un-mines the top k blocks; there is none today.
- **Fix** Give canResend the margin: pass the companion's ClaimMargin into DeroRPC (a field beside FixedFee) and make the Claim case `h + DefaultStableBlocks + 1 + margin < l.T1`, so exit()'s retry loop stops exactly where claimCutoff does. Then correct the closing paragraph of docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md §4, which states the unmargined rule.
- **Regression test** A dero_test.go table over canResend("Claim", h, l) asserting it turns false at the same height claimCutoff(l.T1, margin) does, for margins 0, 8 and 16.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/dero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/dero_test.go, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md
- **Already known as** Adjacent to BL-16 ("the refund-window race (R2/R11)"), which asks for the last-block race to be measured; it does not record that the implementation's own two policies disagree. docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md §4's closing paragraph states the weaker rule ("re-sent with a higher fee while now + 8 + 1 < t1"), so the doc blesses the inconsistency with Invariants 9 and 11 above it.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-07 — A definitive Monero wallet refusal is recorded as 'fate unknown', so the taker burns the whole t1 window against a lock she never funded and ends with a misleading outcome

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:1073` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** swap.go:1073-1096 calls Wallet.Transfer once and treats every error identically: `s.transferTried = true` is set before the call, so the next Step takes the LockUnknown branch, logs 'the Monero transfer's fate is unknown; not sending it again' and saves xmr_locked. walletRPC.Transfer (monero.go:333-347) returns the raw RPC error, so a definitive refusal — insufficient unlocked balance, an unparseable destination, a wallet that is not the open one — is indistinguishable from a lost answer. The taker then waits out the whole t1 window for a gate that can never come (the maker's view wallet shows nothing), the maker refunds at t1, and afterLockMoved (swap.go:1243-1251) sees LockUnknown true and routes her to refund_seen → sweep_ready → SweepAll on an empty joint wallet, which errors and terminates the swap as 'sweep_unknown: the sweep of the joint wallet failed…; the spend key is in the state file' — a message about a key and a sweep when in fact nothing was ever sent.
- **Preconditions** Any wallet-rpc error on the one Transfer call. On a real monero-wallet-rpc this is the common case, not the rare one.
- **Impact** No fund loss, but hours of both parties' capital immobilised for nothing (at mainnet policy the maker's DERO is locked for the full 400 blocks), and a terminal outcome line that tells the operator the wrong story about where their money is. Liveness and honesty, both of which the directive treats as defects.
- **Exploitability** No attacker; it is the default behaviour on any wallet hiccup.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only. Trace swap.go:1073-1096 (`s.transferTried = true` precedes the call; every error path falls into the LockUnknown branch on the next Step) with monero.go:333-347 (the error is returned verbatim, with no code inspection) and swap.go:1243-1251 and 552-576 (the sweep of an empty wallet ends in sweep_unknown). Cannot be shown against a real wallet here: no monero-wallet-rpc exists in this environment.
- **Fix** Classify the error before deciding. walletRPC.call already decodes the JSON-RPC error object; expose its code and treat the definitive ones (-2 invalid address, -17/-37 balance, -13 no wallet open) as 'nothing was sent': abort the swap immediately with an outcome that says so, so the maker's refund window is the only thing spent. Keep the LockUnknown branch for transport errors and timeouts only. Also stop routing a never-funded taker through refund_seen: afterLockMoved should only reach it when a transfer actually left.
- **Regression test** A swap_test case with a Wallet whose Transfer returns an rpc -37: Alice must finish in a 'nothing was locked' outcome on the same Step, with zero transfers and without ever entering sweep_ready.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/monero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/swap_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. BL-11 asks for the Monero backend against a real node but does not name this failure; it is the failure most likely to appear on the first stagenet run, because monero-wallet-rpc's -37 (not enough unlocked money) and -17 (invalid destination) are ordinary answers, not anomalies.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-08 — Secrets at rest: 0600 is a no-op on Windows, and the joint spend wallet is created with an empty password, autosaved and never closed or deleted

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/state.go:165` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** State.Save writes the file with mode 0600 (state.go:165) — meaningless on NTFS, where Go ignores the mode bits — and the file holds this party's key share, the maker's gate secret k_b and the shared view key as plaintext hex (state.go:20-70). Separately, openOrGenerate (swap.go:472-482) calls GenerateFromKeys with `"password": ""` and `"autosave_current": true` (monero.go:355-366), so a wallet file whose spend key is s_a + s_b — the key over the swapped Monero — is written unprotected into monero-wallet-rpc's wallet-dir. `grep -rn CloseWallet tools/xmrswap/` shows the method is used only by monero_test.go: no phase of either state machine ever closes or removes the joint wallets, so they stay open and on disk after the swap ends.
- **Preconditions** Any other local process or user account able to read the operator's files, a backup, a synced folder, or a shared machine.
- **Impact** Reading the state file during a swap gives k_b (which, held together with the taker's role, opens the DERO leg) and the view key (the amount and timing of the Monero lock). Reading the joint wallet file after a swap gives the spend key over that swap's Monero output; if the sweep failed — the sweep_unknown terminal state is reachable and documented — the coins are still there. This is the swap's analogue of the platform rule 'never store private keys in the browser'.
- **Exploitability** No network path; local only. Straightforward wherever the machine is shared or backed up.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned, with direct evidence: tools/xmrswap/state_test.go:62 skips the mode assertion when runtime is Windows; tools/xmrswap/monero.go:355-366 sets password "" and autosave_current true; `grep -rn "CloseWallet" tools/xmrswap/` returns monero.go:38, monero.go:385 and three monero_test.go lines only — no call site in swap.go.
- **Fix** (1) On Windows set an explicit ACL on the state file and the used-secrets register (golang.org/x/sys/windows ACL, or at minimum refuse to run when the file is world-readable and say so). (2) Give the joint wallets a random password held only in the state file, and call CloseWallet after the sweep terminal states (swept, refunded-and-swept) and delete the wallet files once Sweep.Txs is recorded. (3) Zero Own.Secret and Gate in the state file once the swap is Done and the sweep has succeeded — the register only needs their hashes.
- **Regression test** Extend state_test.go so the Windows branch asserts an ACL rather than skipping. Add a swap_test assertion that after a swept outcome the fake wallet has received a CloseWallet and holds no wallet named walletName("spend").
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/state.go, tools/xmrswap/monero.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/state_test.go
- **Already known as** Partly BL-22 (C) ("secret hygiene tests… TestStateFileIsPrivate checks permissions") — the backlog understates it: state_test.go:62 skips the permission check on Windows, which is the operator's own platform, and BL-22 says nothing about the joint wallet file, its empty password, or the fact that CloseWallet is defined (monero.go:385) and never called by swap.go.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### INT-01 — app/founders.js under-reports every pending Founders Pass reward once the reward accumulator has rolled an era, and hides the Claim button entirely for some holders

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app\founders.js:31` (dimension: Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43))
- **Attack path** Not an attacker path — an accounting defect in the reader, triggered by ordinary use. contracts/founders.bas settle() (lines 263-288) computes a holder's pending reward as the sum over every era the holder missed: pend = SUM_e muldiv(hu, ea_<e> - hd, 2^32) then muldiv(hu, acc - hd, 2^32), driven by he_<addr> and the contract's era counter. app/founders.js:31 reads only hu_ and hd_ and computes a single term, pend = hu*(acc-hd)/2^32, with a guard `f.acc > hd` that silences the negative it would otherwise produce. distribute() (founders.bas:32-35) closes an era whenever acc + inc would wrap 2^64, storing the pre-wrap accumulator in ea_<era> and restarting acc at 0. From the first roll onward, acc is no longer comparable to a hd_ recorded in an earlier era, so the app's single term is wrong for every holder: it is 0 for anyone whose hd_ is at or above the restarted acc, and it silently drops one whole era's worth for everyone else.
- **Preconditions** One era roll, i.e. cumulative deposits of about hst * 2^32 atomic DERO = about 42,950 DERO per registered unit (founders.bas:30, inc = floor(pool*2^32/hst)). With the mainnet target of 20 editions and, say, 4 registered units that is roughly 172,000 DERO of lifetime deposits into the pass. The Founders Pass is the destination of `share` bps (5000 on the shipped deployment) of every DERO protocol fee, so this is a when, not an if.
- **Impact** A registered holder is told the pass owes them nothing when it owes them a real balance, and the Claim button is not offered. Funds are not lost or stealable: rpool still holds the DERO, PayBatch (which tools/treasurybot pushes every sweep) still pays correctly, and Register/Withdraw settle correctly on the contract side. The error is one-directional — the page can only under-report, never over-report — so it cannot cause an over-payment. The damage is that the pass's public accounting card lies to the people it pays, and a holder who trusts it concludes the promise is not being kept.
- **Exploitability** No adversary needed; it happens on its own. A hostile party gains nothing directly, but the divergence is a ready-made basis for a 'the pass never paid me' claim against the operator.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests\zzfoundersapp_test.go, which replays the same two-deposit era roll as tests/saturation_test.go, computes exactly what app/founders.js:31 would print from exactly the keys it reads, and compares it with what a real Claim pays on the DVM.  $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash <isolated copy of tests/run.sh> -run FoundersAppPending -v === RUN   TestFoundersAppPendingAfterEraRoll     zzfoundersapp_test.go:51: after the roll: era=1 acc=12884901888000000000 ea_0=12884901888000000000 rpool=9000000000 hst=2     zzfoundersapp_test.go:78: a (claimed once, hd = the pre-roll accumulator): app/founders.js would show 0, the contract paid 3000000000     zzfoundersapp_test.go
- **Fix** Make myFounders walk the eras the way settle() does. Read he_<addr> alongside hu_/hd_ (myKeys already takes a prefix list, so add "he_"), read `era` (already loaded into f) and fetch ea_<e> for e from he_ to era-1, then sum muldiv(hu, ea_<e> - hd, 2^32) with hd reset to 0 after the first term, and finish with muldiv(hu, acc - hd, 2^32) — capping the total at f.rpool exactly as the contract does. Drop the `f.acc > hd` guard, which today converts a wrong result into a silently zero one. Purely a reader change: no contract, no deployment, no operator decision.
- **Regression test** tests\zzfoundersapp_test.go (added, currently failing by design) measures the app formula against a real Claim after an era roll and fails on any disagreement. Once myFounders is fixed, mirror the corrected formula in a tests/js case seeded with the same era=1 / acc / ea_0 / hd_ values that test prints, so the JS is pinned to the contract without needing the DVM.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\founders.js
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md under any BL number. The DVM side (era rollover) is tested and correct — tests/saturation_test.go TestFoundersAccSaturation. The app side is untested: tests/js has no founders test. The 2026-09-08 follow-up audit does not mention it.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### INT-02 — reference/launch.go caps the graduation target at 1e12 while contracts/launch.bas accepts 1e15, so the entire wrapped-quote range — 1 to 1000 XMR — is outside every differential and fuzz test of the curve

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `reference\launch.go:30` (dimension: Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43))
- **Attack path** reference/ is described in its own package comment as "the executable specification of every formula in the Veil contracts", and tests/launch_test.go compares the contract to it at every step: newLaunchEnv (launch_test.go:47-50) calls ref.NewLaunch and t.Fatalf's whenever contract-ok and model-ok differ. NewLaunch refuses grad > 1000000000000 (1e12); contracts/launch.bas:15 refuses only grad > 1000000000000000 (1e15); app/forms.js:84 offers the contract's full range. The consequence is that no test can be written over grad in (1e12, 1e15] — the harness aborts before the first trade — so the bonding curve's arithmetic has never been differentially checked anywhere in that band. On a twelve-decimal wrapped quote 1e12 base units is exactly 1.000000 XMR, so every launch quoted in wXMR that graduates above one XMR, and every wZEC launch above 10,000 ZEC at eight decimals, sits in the untested band. A secondary effect: tests/launch_test.go:262 asserts that grad = 1000000000000001 ("one above the ceiling, which spans DERO, XMR and ZEC base units") is refused — but the model refuses everything above 1e12, so that case passes without ever testing the ceiling it names.
- **Preconditions** None. The gap exists today in the shipped tree; anyone creating a wXMR launch through app/forms.js with a graduation target above 1 XMR is running curve arithmetic that no test covers.
- **Impact** A false verification claim on exactly the market the product exists for. The repository's own gate — "every product of two possibly-large numbers goes through muldiv and the models are differentially fuzzed" — does not hold over the range the XMR/ZEC launches use. I hand-checked the two products that could overflow there and they do not (see repro), so this is a coverage defect rather than a proven arithmetic bug; but it means nobody has evidence, and the one test that claims to probe the ceiling does not reach it.
- **Exploitability** Nothing to exploit directly. The exposure is that a genuine defect in that band would ship green.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests\zzextreme_test.go.  $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash <isolated copy of tests/run.sh> -run 'GradCeiling|LaunchCurveAtContractCeiling' -v === RUN   TestLaunchGradCeilingIsUnmodelled     grad=1000000000001: contract installs=true, reference models it=false     grad=10000000000000: contract installs=true, reference models it=false     grad=100000000000000: contract installs=true, reference models it=false     grad=1000000000000000: contract installs=true, reference models it=false --- FAIL: TestLaunchGradCeilingIsUnmodelled  And, to separate the coverage gap from an actual maths bug, TestLaunchCurveAtContractCeiling installs at the contract's real ceiling with
- **Fix** Change reference/launch.go:30 to `grad > 1000000000000000`, matching contracts/launch.bas:15 and app/forms.js:84, then extend tests/launch_test.go's parameter table and the launch fuzz to draw grad from the full range (including 1e12+1, 1e14 and 1e15 with the maximum supply) so the change buys real coverage rather than just removing a wall. Widening a model can only reveal disagreements, never hide them, so the edit is safe on its own — but it is worthless until the fuzz seeds cover the new band, and it will make tests/launch_test.go:262's ceiling case meaningful for the first time.
- **Regression test** tests\zzextreme_test.go TestLaunchGradCeilingIsUnmodelled (added, failing by design) asserts that anything the contract installs, the reference models. Keep it: it is a general guard against the contract and its specification drifting apart on any bound, not just this one.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: reference\launch.go, tests\launch_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md and not in any prior audit. The eight-reader architecture map states the contract bound correctly ("launch.bas grad bound [10^6, 10^15]") and does not notice that the executable specification disagrees.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-04 — An unattested vault index bars that DERO address from ever registering again — a permanent state only the federation can leave, and a per-user censorship power with no on-chain remedy

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:275` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** Register (vault.bas:264-284) refuses when the caller's newest index is unattested: line 28, `IF EXISTS("da_" + LOAD("ul_" + s + "_" + (c - 1))) == 0 THEN GOTO 900`. There is no timeout, no abandon entrypoint and no way for the user to cancel their own index. The only writer of da_<j> is Attest, which needs k signer votes AND is refused while halted (line 293). So (a) a quorum can silently exclude one named address from the vault for ever by never attesting its newest index, and (b) any halt that lands between a Register and its Attest leaves every registrant in that window barred until the federation acts.
- **Preconditions** Either k colluding signers declining one address, or an ordinary halt/outage overlapping a Register. The user has already paid regfee.
- **Impact** Directly contradicts the file header's "no state needs a human to leave it" (vault.bas:4-9) and the directive's liveness-by-construction rule: this state is unreachable without the federation. Practical harm is bounded — the user can register from a fresh DERO address — but the app's deposit walkthrough dead-ends on step 1 and re-offers Register, which the contract refuses, costing a reverted-transaction fee each time; and a targeted user has no way to tell exclusion from an outage.
- **Exploitability** Costless for a quorum (do nothing). Reached accidentally by any halt, which is the vault's only emergency control.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestAnUnattestedIndexBarsThatAddressForEver.    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestAnUnattestedIndexBarsThatAddressForEver' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestAnUnattestedIndexBarsThatAddressForEver       zz_adversary_priv_test.go:113: ADV-2 CONFIRMED: an address whose newest index is unattested can never Register again until k signers attest it; no timeout exists   --- PASS: TestAnUnattestedIndexBarsThatAddressForEver (0.04s)  The test registers, halts (2 of 3), shows Attest is refused while halted, lets the 20,000-block lease lapse, then shows Register still refused at +20,001 and again at +1,020,001 blocks, and finally clears only when k signe
- **Fix** Contract (redeploy decision): give the rule an expiry — allow a fresh Register when BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= (a stored register height) + a fixed window, e.g. reuse the 20000-block Reclaim constant, so an abandoned index frees the address by itself while still bounding attestation spam. App (safe now): in wrap.js, when the connected address has an unattested newest index, say so and do not offer the Register button — today the walkthrough offers a call the contract will refuse.
- **Regression test** tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestAnUnattestedIndexBarsThatAddressForEver; invert the +1,020,001-block assertion once an expiry exists.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, app/wrap.js, tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. The vault architecture map has the opposite concern (Register spam forcing Attest work); nobody recorded that the anti-spam rule is an unbounded permanent lock in the other direction.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-05 — Vault fee units need the treasury key for ever and never leave sup, so the revocation the directive demands permanently inflates the supply the federation must back

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:487` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** Released (vault.bas:420-424) does `sup -= w_v` and `feepool += w_f`, so the wfee stays inside sup. ClaimFees (487-498) is gated on `SIGNER() == LOAD("treasury")` at ring 2 and moves feepool units to the treasury wallet without touching sup. sup only ever falls through Burn -> Released, which requires v >= minburn (line 382). With the deployment parameters (wfee 1e8, minburn 1e10, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md) the treasury needs 100 withdrawals' fees before it can redeem once, and the residue below minburn can never be burned at all.
- **Preconditions** The vault sealed and in use. Made permanent by destroying the treasury key — which the directive's liveness rule requires the operator to do.
- **Impact** Every wfee ever charged is a standing claim on the foreign reserve that the vault invariant (reserve >= sup - inflight) forces the federation to keep backing. If the treasury key is destroyed as planned, that claim can never be settled by anyone: signers must hold XMR/ZEC against units nobody can redeem, and the cap headroom those units consume never comes back. It ratchets one way for the life of the vault. There is no alternative claimant, no burn-to-nowhere path and no way to write the fee off.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack. It is the arithmetic consequence of following the deployment plan.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestFeeUnitsStayInSupAndNeedTheTreasuryKeyForEver.    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestFeeUnitsStayInSupAndNeedTheTreasuryKeyForEver' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestFeeUnitsStayInSupAndNeedTheTreasuryKeyForEver       zz_adversary_priv_test.go:158: ADV-3 CONFIRMED: sup stayed 3510 across ClaimFees; 10 fee units sit with the treasury below minburn, so they can never leave sup   --- PASS: TestFeeUnitsStayInSupAndNeedTheTreasuryKeyForEver (0.05s)  The test also proves the gate: ClaimFees is refused for both signers, the owner, the burner and a stranger; only the treasury succeeds. And a Burn of the claimed 10 units is refused because 10 < minbu
- **Fix** Contract (redeploy decision): either decrement sup inside Released for the fee (treating the fee as never having been a claim, and paying the treasury out of band), or let the treasury burn feepool with no minburn floor via a dedicated entrypoint. Docs/app (safe now): deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md and app/wrap.js should state that wfee units remain counted in outstanding claims until the treasury both claims AND burns them, and deploy/README.md's revocation section must record that the vault treasury key is one that cannot be destroyed without stranding those units.
- **Regression test** tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestFeeUnitsStayInSupAndNeedTheTreasuryKeyForEver.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, deploy/README.md, app/wrap.js, tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. The fee architecture map notes "a lost treasury key makes feepool an unredeemable permanent claim" as an aside; nobody recorded that ClaimFees does not reduce sup, that the claimed units are typically below minburn, or that this collides head-on with the required key destruction.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-06 — SetRegFee is unbounded: one factory-owner call freezes the registry for every market type, with no ceiling, no timelock and no way back but the same key

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired in part** in `47cdd4c`. After `Renounce` nobody can call SetRegFee, so the fee in force at that moment is fixed for good. Before `Renounce` it is still unbounded. The runbook sets it, reads it back and renounces in one session (`MAINNET_DEPLOYMENT.md`).

- **Where** `contracts/factory.bas:38` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** SetRegFee (factory.bas:38-43) is owner-only with no bound. reg() (factory.bas:101-108) refuses when DEROVALUE() < regfee. Setting regfee to 2^64-1 makes RegisterPool, RegisterLaunch, RegisterNFT and RegisterNFA unpayable for ever. Combined with SetTreasury — which repoints `tstr`, the only value feed.js compares each market's treasury against — one key both freezes new listings and delists every existing one from the app.
- **Preconditions** The factory owner key (retained by the operator, or stolen; there is no renounce).
- **Impact** A permanent listing freeze for the whole platform, and a delisting of everything already listed, from one key that the plan says will have been given up. No contract-level pause, cap or timelock stands in the way, and no user or creator can route around it while the app trusts one factory.
- **Exploitability** One transaction. Recoverable only by the same key.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go:TestOwnerCanFreezeTheRegistryWithOneCall.    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestOwnerCanFreezeTheRegistryWithOneCall' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestOwnerCanFreezeTheRegistryWithOneCall       zz_adversary_owner_test.go:71: ADV-6 CONFIRMED: regfee is now 18446744073709551615; every Register* entrypoint is dead and only the owner key can lift it   --- PASS: TestOwnerCanFreezeTheRegistryWithOneCall (0.00s)  A pool registers while regfee is 0; after SetRegFee(2^64-1) both RegisterPool and RegisterNFA are refused and pc stays 1.
- **Fix** Contract (redeploy decision): bound regfee at install (e.g. `IF regfee > <ceiling> THEN GOTO 900` in both Initialize and SetRegFee) so the fee can be tuned but never used as a freeze. App/docs (safe now): app/notice.js's factory-owner entry should name the freeze explicitly alongside the treasury repoint it already names, and Settings should display the live regfee it already reads.
- **Regression test** tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go:TestOwnerCanFreezeTheRegistryWithOneCall; invert once a ceiling exists.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/factory.bas, app/notice.js, tests/zz_adversary_owner_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. BL-93/D-9 treat regfee as a value to be frozen at deploy; nobody recorded that it is an unbounded kill switch while the owner key lives (and it lives for ever, see SEC-K-02).
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-07 — The supply-chain gate is permanently red on a false positive: binaries.lock recorded a source hash for telainstall that never matched the committed source, so 'verifyvendor green' can only be reached by a blind re-pin

- **Where** `deploy/binaries.lock:16` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** deploy/README.md:205 makes "tools/verifyvendor green" a mainnet go/no-go gate. It is red today with 75 findings, two of which are `tools/simrpc source changed since its binary was pinned` and `tools/telainstall source changed since its binary was pinned`. simrpc genuinely changed (commit 6ad2109 added address.go after the lock commit). telainstall did NOT: tools/telainstall/main.go is blob c7c1a0a7 at both HEAD and 60ab2e6, the very commit that wrote binaries.lock — so the recorded source hash 7e245179… was already wrong when it was written. The only way to clear the gate is `verifyvendor -write-binaries`, which re-pins whatever happens to be on disk, hashes and all. Two further gaps: dirHash (tools/verifyvendor/main.go:45-69) hashes only non-test .go files, so tools/telainstall/build.sh and tools/xswdwallet/build.sh — the recipes that decide where the tool is built and against which module — are unpinned; and the binary loop (:186) filters on the `.exe` suffix, so vendor/bin/derod.exe~ and vendor/bin/netwallet.exe~ are neither hashed nor reported as unlocked.
- **Preconditions** None — it is the shipped state of the repository.
- **Impact** A gate that has never been green and cannot be made green honestly is a gate nobody reads, and the sanctioned way to clear it re-pins tampering silently. The tool it wrongly accuses — telainstall — is the one that opens the deployer wallet file and installs/updates the TELA INDEX (see SEC-K-03), so this is exactly the artefact whose provenance most needs to be checkable. The unpinned build.sh means the build recipe for the wallet-opening tools is outside the integrity check entirely.
- **Exploitability** No attacker needed for the false positive. The realistic exploit is social: an operator under time pressure runs -write-binaries to clear a gate they know is spurious, and re-pins a modified tree with it.
- **Reproduced** yes —   cd tools && go run ./verifyvendor -root ..     ... FINDING: tools/simrpc source changed since its binary was pinned: rebuild and -write-binaries     ... FINDING: tools/telainstall source changed since its binary was pinned: rebuild and -write-binaries     binaries: 11 checked against ..\deploy\binaries.lock (5 source trees)     75 finding(s)   exit status 1   sha256sum vendor/bin/*.exe          -> all 11 match deploy/binaries.lock exactly   git log -1 --format=%h -- deploy/binaries.lock   -> 60ab2e6   git rev-parse HEAD:tools/telainstall/main.go     -> c7c1a0a7ed1dd91ddebafe94d4f2e4db6787f674   git rev-parse 60ab2e6:tools/telainstall/main.go  -> c7c1a0a7ed1dd91ddebafe94d4f2e4db6787f674   (
- **Fix** Safe now, three edits. (1) Rebuild telainstall and simrpc from the committed source and re-run `go run ./verifyvendor -root .. -write-binaries` from a clean `git status`, then commit binaries.lock — and record in deploy/README.md that -write-binaries is only ever run on a clean tree. (2) Extend dirHash to include build.sh (any file, not just .go) so the build recipe is pinned. (3) Change the binary loop to hash every regular file in vendor/bin (or at minimum report any file not in the lock, whatever its extension) so .exe~ leftovers cannot sit unaccounted; delete derod.exe~ and netwallet.exe~. Also delete the 133 untracked files under vendor/derohe/veilprobe/ and vendor/derohe/veil2/ that make up the other 73 findings — they are prior audit scratch trees.
- **Regression test** A test in tests/run.sh (or a tools/verifyvendor self-test) that runs verifyvendor on a clean checkout and fails if the exit status is non-zero; plus a unit test for dirHash asserting that editing build.sh changes the hash.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: deploy/binaries.lock, tools/verifyvendor/main.go, deploy/README.md, vendor/bin/derod.exe~, vendor/bin/netwallet.exe~
- **Already known as** BL-164 and BL-206 cover vendor bootstrap and upstream provenance. Neither records that the lock is self-inconsistent for telainstall, nor that dirHash ignores build.sh, nor that verifyvendor skips non-.exe files in vendor/bin.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-08 — simrpc sends no HTTP authentication, but netwallet forces --rpc-login on mainnet: the documented mainnet deploy, treasury bot and vault signer cannot authenticate to the wallet they are told to use

- **Where** `tools/simrpc/simrpc.go:41` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tools/netwallet/main.go:73-76 exits on mainnet unless -rpc-login user:password (or VEIL_RPC_LOGIN) is given, and passes it to the wallet RPC server. vendor/derohe/walletapi/rpcserver/rpc_websocket_server.go:104-118 then returns HTTP 401 to any request without matching BasicAuth. tools/simrpc — the only RPC client simdeploy, treasurybot and vaultsigner use — issues bare `http.Post("http://"+host+"/json_rpc", ...)` at simrpc.go:41 and :270, with no Authorization header and no credential flag anywhere in the package. deploy/README.md:213-214 and :232 tell the operator to start netwallet with -rpc-login and then point simdeploy/treasurybot/vaultsigner at that port.
- **Preconditions** A mainnet deployment following deploy/README.md. Also affects any operator who hardens a testnet wallet with -rpc-login.
- **Impact** Every write path to mainnet — contract install, treasury sweeps, federation signing — stops at a 401 the tools do not interpret (simrpc's error policy is log.Fatal). The mainnet runbook has never been executed end to end, which is why this has not surfaced. Secondary, and the reason -rpc-login exists: on simulator and testnet the wallet RPC runs with no authentication at all (`if rpcserver.user == "" { return false }`), so any local process can drive the deployer wallet — the wallet that owns the testnet factory and the TELA INDEX — for the duration of a deploy.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack; a blocked operation. The no-auth testnet exposure needs local code execution.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from code, not run — reproducing the 401 needs a mainnet daemon and a funded wallet.   grep -n 'http.Post' tools/simrpc/simrpc.go   -> :41 and :270, no Header.Set anywhere in the file   grep -rn 'Authorization' tools/simrpc/        -> (no match)   sed -n '68,78p' tools/netwallet/main.go       -> mainnet exits without -rpc-login   sed -n '103,118p' vendor/derohe/walletapi/rpcserver/rpc_websocket_server.go     -> `if rpcserver.user == "" { return false }` then 401 on missing/incorrect BasicAuth   grep -n 'rpc-login' deploy/README.md          -> :213, :214, :221 (mainnet runbook) To reproduce without funds: start `netwallet -network testnet -rpc-login u:p -rpc-bind 127.0.0.1:40406` aga
- **Fix** Safe now: give tools/simrpc a credential — accept `user:pass@host` in the -wallet/-w0 flag (or a -rpc-login flag / VEIL_RPC_LOGIN env), parse it once in NewClient, and set `req.SetBasicAuth` on both request paths (simrpc.go:41 and :270), using http.NewRequest instead of http.Post. Keep the credential out of argv by preferring the env var, as netwallet already does. Then correct deploy/README.md's mainnet section to show the credential flowing to each tool.
- **Regression test** A Go test in tools/simrpc that stands up an httptest server demanding BasicAuth and asserts the client authenticates (and that a wrong credential produces a clear error rather than a JSON decode failure).
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/simrpc/simrpc.go, deploy/README.md, tools/simdeploy/main.go, tools/treasurybot/main.go, tools/vaultsigner/main.go
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. docs/PLATFORM_BACKLOG.md:215 comes closest ("netwallet already accepts -rpc-login. Add it to the client and require it on mainnet") but does not say the mainnet runbook is unrunnable today.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-09 — The Monero swap companion leaves the joint spend key in an empty-password wallet file it never closes, contradicting its own comment; the 0600 mode it relies on is not enforced on the operator's platform

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `9064f82`.
- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/monero.go:351` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** After a swap settles, swap.go:478 (Alice, recovery) and swap.go:795 (Bob, sweep) call GenerateFromKeys with the joint spend key s_a+s_b. monero.go:351-366 creates that wallet with `"password": ""`, autosave_current true, and its own comment claims the file "is closed after the sweep; a password the companion would have to store buys nothing". CloseWallet is defined (monero.go:38, :385) and exercised only in monero_test.go — `grep -rn CloseWallet tools/ | grep -v _test.go` returns the definition and nothing else. So the wallet stays open in the wallet-rpc and the unencrypted file stays in its wallet-dir indefinitely. Separately, the companion's own state file holds s_a or s_b, k_b and the private view key v as plaintext hex; state.go:167 writes it 0600, and state_test.go:62-63 skips the permission assertion when OS == Windows_NT — the operator's platform.
- **Preconditions** Any completed or refunded swap. Read access to the user's disk or to the wallet-rpc, or another local account on a Windows machine.
- **Impact** The key that spends the joint Monero output survives the swap in cleartext in two places, and the code comment states the opposite, so a reviewer and an operator will both believe it was cleaned up. On Windows neither file has meaningful access control. The joint output has normally been swept by then, so the residual value at risk is usually zero — but the state file's s_a/s_b and v also deanonymise the swap and, for any swap that ends in sweep_unknown (the failure mode the xmrswap architecture map flags as likely against a real wallet-rpc), the key still controls funds.
- **Exploitability** Local read access. No network path.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -rn 'CloseWallet' tools/ | grep -v _test.go     tools/xmrswap/monero.go:38:  CloseWallet(ctx context.Context) error      <- interface     tools/xmrswap/monero.go:385: func (w *walletRPC) CloseWallet(...)        <- implementation     (no call site outside tests)   sed -n '351,366p' tools/xmrswap/monero.go   -> "password": "", and the comment claiming it is closed   grep -n 'GenerateFromKeys\|spendKey(' tools/xmrswap/swap.go -> :434 :478 :791 :1179   grep -n 'Windows' tools/xmrswap/state_test.go     :63  if m := fi.Mode().Perm(); m&0o077 != 0 && os.Getenv("OS") != "Windows_NT" {
- **Fix** Safe now. (1) Call CloseWallet in a defer around the sweep in swap.go (both the Bob sweep path and Alice's recovery path) and, once Sweep.Txs is recorded, delete the generated wallet file and its .keys sibling; if the companion cannot delete it, say so in the terminal state rather than in a comment. (2) Give the generated wallet a random password held only in memory for the life of the call, or correct monero.go:351-352 to state plainly that the file persists unencrypted. (3) On Windows, set an ACL on the state file (or at minimum print a one-line warning at startup that the state file is unprotected on this platform) instead of skipping the assertion.
- **Regression test** Extend tools/xmrswap/swap_test.go's crash fuzz with an assertion that the FakeWallet has recorded a close (and, if deletion is implemented, that no spend wallet remains) after every terminal outcome; keep state_test.go's permission check but make the Windows branch assert the warning was emitted rather than skipping.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/monero.go, tools/xmrswap/state.go, tools/xmrswap/state_test.go, tools/xmrswap/swap_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. BL-11 covers the missing stagenet run; nothing covers secrets at rest for the companion.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-02 — The NFA anti-snipe extension has no cap: two addresses hold an auction — and the owner's asset — open indefinitely for transaction fees, and on a 12-decimal quote the escalation is free

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:170` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** generateEndBlock (nfa.bas:277-283) caps the INITIAL duration at 604,800 s and is called only from Start (line 240). Bid lines 170-180 then unconditionally set endBlockTime = BLOCK_TIMESTAMP() + 900 whenever a bid lands within 900 s of the end, with no reference to that cap and no counter. The price a bid must meet is currBidPrice = findLesserIncrease(prev) = prev + min(10%, staticBidIncr=10000 atomic units) (nfa.bas:372-379). Every outbid is refunded inside the same call by outbidReturns (nfa.bas:319-329), so two cooperating addresses alternate and neither has capital locked beyond one bid. The owner has no exit while it runs: CloseListing needs checkActive == 111 (ended) and CancelListing needs startBlockTime + 300 >= now.
- **Preconditions** A live NFA auction. Two addresses under one party. One transaction per ~15 minutes, plus the escalating top bid, which only one of the two holds at a time.
- **Impact** The owner's NFA is locked in the contract for as long as the griefer keeps paying gas — indefinitely, with no owner exit and no time limit — and the honest bidders' price is walked up. On a wXMR quote (12 decimals) staticBidIncr = 10,000 atomic units is 0.00000001 XMR, so the escalation cost over a week is a rounding error; the whole attack costs transaction fees. On a DERO quote (5 decimals) 10,000 units is 0.1 DERO, so the top bid climbs ~9.6 DERO a day — still cheap, and it is the griefer's own money that is refunded on each outbid.
- **Exploitability** Two wallets and a cron job. No capital at risk beyond one bid at a time; the refund is atomic in the outbidding call.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestNFAAntiSnipeExtensionHasNoCap and ran:    $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestNFAAntiSnipe' -v   === RUN   TestNFAAntiSnipeExtensionHasNoCap       zzadv_market_test.go:78: nfa.bas install (wrapped=true): 13859 bytes of code, storage gas 14860, compute gas 1051600       zzadv_market_test.go:107: auction held open for 632900 s (7.3 days) by 700 alternating bids; the price to meet rose from 1000000 to 7990000, i.e. 6990000 units of the quote in total   --- PASS: TestNFAAntiSnipeExtensionHasNoCap (1.26s)  The test Starts a 1-hour auction, then 700 times sets the clock to endBlockTime-1, reads currBidPrice, bids exactly it from alternating addr
- **Fix** Operator decision (NFA.md R4 already frames it): keep the standard's behaviour and say so on the listing page — the end must read "extends while bids arrive", never as a date — or diverge and cap the extension, e.g. in Bid line 180 store min(BLOCK_TIMESTAMP()+900, LOAD("startBlockTime") + 604800 + 900). Separately (R11), scale staticBidIncr by the quote's decimals at install so the minimum outbid means something on a wrapped quote; a wrapped quote is already a DMZ extension, so that changes nothing for DERO-quoted assets. Either way this is a contract change and a redeploy of the shipped nfa template.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestNFAAntiSnipeExtensionHasNoCap. If the cap ships, invert it: assert the 701st extension is refused and that the auction ends at startBlockTime + 604800 + 900.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/templates/nfa.bas, app/config.js, app/nfas.js, tests/zzadv_market_test.go
- **Already known as** NFA.md §7 R4 (first half) and R11, both marked OPEN; no BL number. The backlog does not carry it at all — BL-67 covers only the one-bid-per-timestamp refund. NFA.md's analysis is accurate; what it does not do is put a measured number on the case it calls "expensive".
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-03 — The trade sheet's market snapshot is captured once when the sheet opens and never refreshed, so the displayed quote and the signed min_out are anchored to reserves that can be arbitrarily old

- **Where** `app/token.js:141` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** T.tradeSheet(s, side) (token.js:141) captures the market record `s` by reference. quote() (token.js:196) reads s.rd / s.ra through A.launchBuy / A.poolBuy and is called only from input.oninput, the slippage chips, destIn.oninput, the presets, the impact acknowledgement, the private-mode toggle and once at open. There is no L.onBlock and no D.onChange subscription inside the sheet (verified: grep over token.js:141-253 for onBlock/onChange/setInterval returns nothing; the only onBlock in app/ is app.js:78, which schedules a data refresh, not a re-quote). D.refresh replaces the record object wholesale (feed.js loadContract returns a fresh object literal; refresh does `this.assets = next`), so once the market's probe signature changes the sheet's `s` is a detached copy of the reserves as they were when the sheet opened — it can never update again. go() (token.js:220) then computes `const min = A.minOut(q.out, slip)` from that frozen q and hands it to app.trade, whose only guard is minOut >= A.minOut(q.out, MAX_SLIP) with MAX_SLIP = 1000 bps (app.js:9, 201) — measured against the same frozen q. Meanwhile every input to a sandwich is public before inclusion: SCDATA is a plain serialized rpc.Arguments in the transaction body (vendor/derohe/transaction/transaction.go:87, 475; blockchain/transaction_execute.go:301), so min_out, publish and the private-mode dest are readable in the mempool, and the amount is a plaintext uvarint BurnValue (transaction.go:92,117; transaction_execute.go:290). Ring signatures hide who pays, not what the call is or how much it carries.
- **Preconditions** A user opens the trade sheet, types an amount, and signs some blocks later — reading the cost rows, toggling publish, confirming in the wallet, waiting for inclusion (app.tx waits up to 180 x 1.5 s). DERO blocks are 18 s. No attacker is needed for the wrong number on screen; an attacker is needed to convert it into a loss.
- **Impact** Two things. (1) The "You receive (estimated)", price impact, "Minimum received" and the price line are silently stale for the whole life of the sheet, with no age and no staleness marker — requirement 3 says every cost stands in front of the button, and this one is a number from an unstated past. (2) min_out is a floor computed from a stale reference, so the slippage protection is 0.5-5% of a price that may have moved much further. An observer who sees the call in the mempool (amount, min_out, market) can move the pool to exactly min_out and take the difference between the live price and that stale floor. The 10% MAX_SLIP cap does not bound this, because it is applied to the same stale q.
- **Exploitability** The staleness is unconditional and happens to every user. The extraction on top of it needs mempool observation and a front-running transaction in the same or an earlier block — ordinary AMM MEV, made materially more profitable by the stale anchor. Not exercised on the simulator (see notPerformed).
- **Reproduced** no — Code-traced, not executed. Evidence: (a) `awk 'NR>=141 && NR<=253' app/token.js | grep -n "onBlock|onChange|setInterval|L\."` returns nothing — the sheet has no live hook; (b) `grep -n onBlock app/*.js` returns only app.js:78 and live.js:9; (c) app/token.js:141 takes `s` as a parameter and app/token.js:220 uses the closed-over `q`; (d) app/feed.js loadContract builds a new object and refresh() assigns `this.assets = next`, so the captured record is orphaned on the first state change; (e) app/app.js:201 measures the guard against the same q. Mempool visibility confirmed by reading the vendored chain: vendor/derohe/transaction/transaction.go:87 (`SCDATA rpc.Arguments`), :475 (`tx.SCDATA.Marsha
- **Fix** Local and testable, app only. In T.tradeSheet keep the scid, not the record: `const cur = () => D.assets.get(scid) || s;` and read reserves through cur() inside quote(). Subscribe to D.onChange while the sheet is open (unsubscribe on close, the way the page router already does) and re-run quote() on each change. Recompute min_out inside go() from the fresh record rather than from the captured q, and refuse to send if the fresh quote differs from the displayed one by more than the chosen slippage — asking the user to look again instead of signing a number they did not see. Add the reference block to the cost block ("quoted at block N") so the sheet can never show an unstated age.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js that drives the quote path with a mutable market record: build s, compute q, mutate s.rd/s.ra, and assert the recomputed min_out changed — plus a UI-pass assertion (tools/uitest/ui.mjs) that the trade sheet's "Minimum received" row changes after a simulated block in which the pool moves.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/token.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** Not in BACKLOG.md, FINDINGS.md or DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md — I grepped all three for stale/re-quote/sandwich/front-run/mempool and found nothing on the trade path. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-04 — The trending rank that drives Home, the price tape and the Trade hub is dominated by a term that costs 0.00329 DERO a block to manufacture

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `099a4b1`.
- **Where** `app/stats.js:80` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** m.hot = f1.blocks * 4 + pub1.who * 3 + Math.min(50, Math.log1p(f1.vol / qscale(s)) * 8) (stats.js:80). The comment beside it states the anti-wash design: "distinct publishers count, not published trades (one account spamming posts gains nothing), and volume enters through a log so a single large reserve move does not own the ranking". Both guarded terms are bounded — pub1.who needs distinct addresses, and the volume term is hard-capped at 50. The unguarded term is f1.blocks, which flow() (stats.js:43-55) computes as the number of consecutive price points in the last hour where b.rd !== a.rd and lpMove is false — i.e. simply the count of blocks in which the reserves moved at all, regardless of size. It carries weight 4 and its only ceiling is the ~200 blocks an hour holds at 18 s. One dust swap per block therefore buys 4 points a block, up to 800 — more than the other two terms can contribute together (3 x distinct publishers + 50). D.trending() (feed.js:390) sorts by m.hot first and feeds Home's trending list, L.tape (home.js:189), and the Trade hub's "Markets, ordered by activity" list (home.js:195, which sorts D.active() by m.hot directly).
- **Preconditions** Any listed pool or launch the attacker controls, seeded with enough liquidity that a minimum-size swap produces a non-zero output. One transaction per block for an hour, and thereafter to hold the position.
- **Impact** The exchange's front page ranking is purchasable. A market with no genuine interest reaches hot = 800 and outranks a real market that trades in, say, twenty blocks an hour (hot ~ 80 + 50 = 130). The same score sets the "HOT" badge at hot > 12 (home.js:12 via H.badges), which four active blocks in an hour buys. Home's trending list, the scrolling price tape and the Trade hub — the three surfaces whose buttons are Buy and Sell — can all be topped by dust. The split-pair guard does not help: ranked() only excludes the thin half of a duplicate pair, and a fresh launch is not a duplicate of anything.
- **Exploitability** Measured cost below. It needs no privileged access, no victim, and no timing.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestPoolTheCheapestReserveMoveCostsOnlyGas and ran:    $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestPoolTheCheapest' -v   === RUN   TestPoolTheCheapestReserveMoveCostsOnlyGas       zzadv_market_test.go:222: smallest accepted buy 3 atomic DERO (0.00003 DERO), storage gas 129, network fee gas+200 = 329 atomic (0.00329 DERO); an hour of 18 s blocks is ~200 such blocks, i.e. hot = 800 for about 0.66 DERO   --- PASS: TestPoolTheCheapestReserveMoveCostsOnlyGas (0.01s)  The test seeds a pool.bas at the shipped 50/5000 bps, then walks d from 1 upward until SwapDeroForAsset succeeds — 3 atomic DERO is the smallest buy the contract accepts at that depth — and re
- **Fix** Local and testable, app only, in app/stats.js:80. Cap the blocks term the way the volume term is already capped, and make it earn its weight: e.g. `Math.min(40, f1.blocks) * 4` plus a size floor in flow() — count a block only when the reserve move exceeds some fraction of the reserve (say one basis point of depth), so a dust trade is not an "active block". The size floor is the important half: it is what makes the term proportional to something an attacker must actually pay. Keep the existing comment honest by naming the third guard beside the two it already claims.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js that builds a synthetic points series of 200 consecutive blocks each moving rd by 1 atomic unit on a 1e8 reserve and asserts enrich().hot stays below the score of a market with five blocks of 1% moves. Plus the DVM test above, kept, so the measured cost of one manufactured block is on record.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/stats.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** Not in BACKLOG.md or the prior audits. DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT DEFECT 11 / D3-11 touches the `hot > 12` threshold as a magic number, not the manipulability of the score. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-05 — A market quoted in an asset the deployment does not name is listed "Verified", silently priced at a placeholder of 5 decimals, and the trade sheet tells the user its quote is a federation-backed wrapped asset

- **Where** `app/feed.js:106` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** launch.bas InitializePrivate checks only `IF STRLEN(quote) != 32 THEN GOTO 900` (contracts/launch.bas, line 30 of the function); pool.bas the same (pool.bas:18-19). Nothing on chain records a quote's decimals. feed.js verify() (feed.js:161-165) hashes only the contract CODE against the shipped template, and loadContract's only other gates are the factory treasury (feed.js:224) and fee/pfee equal to cfg (feed.js:228-229) — all three of which an attacker satisfies by installing the shipped launch template with the factory's own treasury and the shipped fee, then paying regfee (0.1 DERO) to the permissionless RegisterLaunch. nameQuote (feed.js:101-113) then finds no record in cfg.vaults and falls through to `s.quoteSym = s.quote.slice(0,6)` and `s.quoteDec = 5`, setting s.quoteDecUnknown = true — which nothing outside nfas.js reads. ui.js verified() (ui.js:90-105) has no tier for an unnamed quote and returns the plain green "Verified"; ui.js warnings() has no pill for it; home.js H.risks has no row for it. token.js:229 then renders, on the truthiness of s.quote alone: "This market settles in wrapped <first 6 hex of the SCID>." followed by "Wrapped units are backed one for one by a k-of-n federation that can fail" and a button "Get wrapped <6 hex>" that routes to the Wrap screen.
- **Preconditions** An attacker installs launch.bas (or pool.bas) directly — from a wallet RPC, tools/simdeploy or any DVM client — with quote set to any 32-byte SCID that is not in app/config.js vaults[], plus the factory's treasury and cfg fee/pfee, and pays regfee to RegisterLaunch/RegisterPool. The app's own launch form only offers DERO and the configured vaults, so this requires a tool, not the app.
- **Impact** Three at once. (1) A false backing claim: the app states, in a warning box above the amount field, that an arbitrary token is a wrapped asset backed one for one by a federation, and offers a route to a Wrap screen that holds nothing for it — the hard rule about never claiming trust properties the assumptions do not justify, broken by the app's own copy. (2) Every quote-denominated number on the market is wrong by 10^(real decimals - 5): stats.js qunit(s) (stats.js:35) scales liq, vol24 and the candle volumes by 10^quoteDec, so a market holding 0.000001 of a 12-decimal quote reports liquidity of 20 and escapes m.thin (stats.js:82) and H.risks' thin row (home.js:32) entirely — the under-warning direction. markets.js:160 prints that inflated figure in the Liquidity column and sorts by it. (3) The green "Verified" badge, whose tooltip says "Contract code matches the shipped template and pays the protocol treasury", is technically true and reads as a blessing of a market whose unit of account the app cannot name.
- **Exploitability** One install plus 0.1 DERO of regfee. No race, no victim interaction beyond opening the market.
- **Reproduced** no — The contract precondition is reproduced; the app misrepresentation is code-traced, not rendered. Added tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestLaunchInstallsAndTradesInAQuoteNothingNames and ran:    $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestLaunchInstallsAndTrades' -v   === RUN   TestLaunchInstallsAndTradesInAQuoteNothingNames       zzadv_market_test.go:187: a launch quoted in 777582e20f8e3bb2… (an asset no deployment record names) installed and traded; the contract stores no decimals for it   --- PASS: TestLaunchInstallsAndTradesInAQuoteNothingNames (0.01s)  The test installs a stand-in asset, mints it to a buyer, installs the shipped launch.bas with quote set to that SCID, asserts the qt 
- **Fix** Local and testable, app only, three parts. (1) token.js:229 must branch on whether the quote has a deployment record, not on s.quote being truthy: only a market whose quote is in cfg.vaults may be described as a wrapped asset or offered the Wrap route; anything else gets a red box saying the quote is a contract the app cannot name, with its full SCID. (2) ui.js verified() gains a tier above the plain badge — "Code checked · quote unknown" — and ui.js warnings() a pill, both driven by s.quoteDecUnknown, so it reaches every row that already renders U.warnings; add the matching H.risks line so it appears inside the trade sheet. (3) Where a number is scaled by a placeholder, say so or do not print it: nfas.js:16 already has the pattern ("units of X (scale unknown)") — apply it to stats.js's liq/vol24, markets.js:160 and the trade sheet's amount rows, and make m.thin fail closed (thin = true when quoteDecUnknown) rather than silently pass.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js over a fixture market with a quote SCID absent from cfg.vaults, asserting: quoteDecUnknown is true; enrich().thin is true; and the verified/warnings helpers emit the unknown-quote tier. Plus a UI-pass step (tools/uitest/ui.mjs) that opens the trade sheet on such a market and asserts the text "wrapped" does NOT appear and the full quote SCID does.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/token.js, app/ui.js, app/home.js, app/stats.js, app/markets.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** Adjacent to BL-102 (multi-quote tokens) and BL-104 (a wrapped graduation target once installed at the wrong scale), but neither covers an UNNAMED quote. The placeholder itself is deliberate and flagged in code — feed.js:110 computes s.quoteDecUnknown and feed.js:304 computes s.decUnknown — but `grep -rn "quoteDecUnknown|decUnknown" app/*.js` shows the only consumer anywhere in the app is nfas.js:16 and :119. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-06 — No market surface reads the state of the vault behind a wrapped quote: a pool or launch priced in wXMR trades identically whether the federation is live, halted, or has lost its bond ratio

- **Where** `app/token.js:229` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** The wrapped-quote disclosure the trade sheet renders (token.js:229-233) is built entirely from app/config.js: `qv = D.allVaults().find(v => v.scid === s.quote)` gives the symbol, the chain string and nothing else. No market surface calls D.loadVault or reads the vault contract's halted / sealed / cap / sup / bondtot keys. The only place in the whole app that reads a vault's live state for a market is nfas.js:165 — `s.quote && D.vault && D.vault.scid === s.quote && D.vault.halted > BigInt(D.height || 0)` — and D.vault is set only by wrap.js:47 inside loadVault, which runs only when the user visits the Wrap screen (`grep -rn "D.vault\b|\.vault =" app/*.js` gives nfas.js:165, notice.js:20, wrap.js:47/116/117/495/501/512 and nothing else). So even the NFA halt warning silently fails to render unless the user happened to open the Wrap screen for that exact vault earlier in the session.
- **Preconditions** A market quoted in a wrapped asset — which the product model makes the headline case — and a vault whose federation has halted (a k-vote 20,000-block lease, vault.bas:503-521, renewable indefinitely), or whose cap/capratio has broken after a Slash, or whose reserve has drifted short. None of these stop the wrapped units moving on DERO, so every pool, launch and NFA quoted in that asset keeps quoting and settling.
- **Impact** The one fact that determines what the quote asset is worth — whether new units can still be minted and existing ones redeemed — is invisible at the moment of trade. A trader buys a token "for 1.5 XMR" while the XMR vault has been halted for a week and the wrapped units cannot be redeemed; the sheet says only that wrapped units "are backed one for one by a k-of-n federation that can fail", in the present tense, with no reading of whether it has. The same silence covers what an LP holds: a wrapped-quote AMM's entire quote reserve is a federation claim, and no market screen ever states its condition.
- **Exploitability** No attacker required — this is a fail-open disclosure gap. It becomes an attack surface when a federation halts (which k signers can do at will and renew forever) and markets keep pricing against a frozen reserve.
- **Reproduced** no — Code-traced and grepped, not rendered. `grep -rn "halted|vaultHalt" app/*.js` outside wrap.js returns exactly two lines, both in nfas.js (:165 and :172). `grep -rn "D.vault\b|\.vault =" app/*.js` returns nfas.js:165, notice.js:20 and wrap.js:47/116/117/495/501/512 — D.vault is populated only by wrap.js:47 inside loadVault, and loadVault is called only from wrap.js:495 on the Wrap route. app/token.js:229-233, app/markets.js and app/home.js contain no vault read of any kind. I could not exercise a halt on the simulator: halting requires k signer votes and my wallets are read-only (see notPerformed).
- **Fix** Not safe to land blind, for two reasons that are the operator's to settle. (1) Byte budget: feed.js is 16,144 of the 18,432 TELA cap and token.js 13,076 — adding a per-vault read plus the disclosure has to be measured against the cap on the file it lands in. (2) Read cost: doing it properly means loading every configured vault's small key set (kind, v, halted, sealed, sup, cap, bondtot, capratio) once per refresh rather than once per Wrap visit, which is a per-refresh RPC decision the same size as PERF·F6. The shape of the fix: move loadVault's key read into feed.js's refresh as a `D.vaultState` map keyed by SCID; have nameQuote attach the live state to every market with a wrapped quote; render one line in the trade sheet's existing wrapped-quote warning box ("This vault is halted until block N: no new deposits are being minted") and one pill on the market row; and change nfas.js:165 to read the map rather than the single D.vault, so its warning stops depending on which screen the user visited first.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js asserting nameQuote attaches vault state for every market whose quote is a configured vault, and that a halted vault produces the disclosure regardless of which screen loaded first. Plus a simulator step in tools/simvault's e2e: halt the XMR vault, run the UI pass, and assert the trade sheet on a wXMR-quoted market names the halt.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: app/feed.js, app/token.js, app/nfas.js, app/markets.js, app/wrap.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** Not carried as a market-side item anywhere. UX·F5 in FINDINGS.md is the adjacent finding on the Wrap screen's own "Open" flag. The wrap-screen half is well handled; the market half is unhandled. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-07 — board.bas: any address freezes any open offer for up to ~208 days with one transaction, and the maker cannot take it back

- **Status (2026-09-11)** **repaired** in `47cdd4c`. In `board.bas` v3, Accept records `ad_<id>` = the accept height + `aw` (200), and the maker may Cancel an accepted offer from then (`contracts/board.bas:34, 96`). One Accept holds an offer for 200 blocks, not until its expiry. `TestBoardV3AcceptanceDeadline`, and the final live `simswap` run on the finished contracts, which cancelled offer 0 at height 688, its deadline, against an expiry of 744 (tx `43082bc9…`). What remains is a griefer who re-accepts at one fee per 200 blocks: GAP-026.

- **Where** `contracts/board.bas:55` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** Accept (board.bas:55-70) requires only a valid signer, st_<id> == 0, height < e_<id>, STRLEN(refundPkh) == 40, cltv != 0, and signer != maker. The refundPkh is any 40-character string and the cltv any non-zero number — neither is verified against anything, and the taker never has to fund the counter-chain script. Accept then sets st_<id> = 1 permanently: there is no un-accept, no timeout on the acceptance, and no second Accept (line 23 refuses st != 0). Cancel (board.bas:74-86) lets the maker retire an OPEN offer at any time, but line 26 refuses an accepted one until BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= e_<id> — and Post (line 26) allows expiry up to h + 1,000,000 blocks, which at 18 s is ~208 days.
- **Preconditions** An open offer on the board. One ring-2 transaction with no value attached, from any address that is not the maker.
- **Impact** The transparent-Zcash market's only discovery surface can be emptied at will. Every open offer is bound to a griefer who will never fund, the honest taker is locked out (a second Accept is refused), and the maker cannot retire the row until its own expiry — which, for a maker who chose a long window so takers had time, is up to seven months. The maker's recourse is to Post again, at which point the griefer Accepts again: the exchange is one griefing transaction per honest offer. Nothing of value is at risk (the board holds none), but the market is denied. The DMZ liveness rule — any state that needs a human to leave it is a defect — is satisfied in the letter (the row dies at expiry) and defeated in practice.
- **Exploitability** A script and a wallet. Cost is one transaction fee per offer; the measured floor for an SC call on this chain is 329 atomic units of fee (see ADV-PRICE-04's measurement of gasstorage + 200), so the whole board costs pennies to hold shut.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestBoardAcceptByAnyoneFreezesAnOfferUntilExpiry and ran:    $ tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestBoardAcceptByAnyone' -v   === RUN   TestBoardAcceptByAnyoneFreezesAnOfferUntilExpiry       zzadv_market_test.go:154: one Accept transaction froze the offer for 1000000 blocks (~208 days at 18 s)   --- PASS: TestBoardAcceptByAnyoneFreezesAnOfferUntilExpiry (0.00s)  The test installs board.bas, has the maker Post one offer at the maximum expiry (height + 1,000,000), has a griefer Accept it with refundPkh = forty zeros and cltv = 1 and asserts the call succeeds and st_0 == 1; then asserts an honest taker's Accept is refused, and that the maker's Cance
- **Fix** Contract change and a mechanism decision the operator has to make, which is why it is not safe to land blind — ZEC_SWAP.md §5 already has the timelock policy this must sit beside. Two candidates: (a) an acceptance deadline — store the accept height in board.bas and let the maker Cancel (or let anyone re-open the row to st = 0) once the taker has not produced the counter-chain funding within a stated window, since the design already requires the maker to Lock in swap.bas within P_lock = 200 blocks; or (b) require the taker to attach a small bond at Accept, refundable when the swap settles or when the maker Locks, forfeit otherwise. Option (a) keeps the board value-free, which is its whole point, and is the smaller change. board.bas is not deployed on any network (no SCID in deploy/networks.json or app/config.js) and has no app screen, so changing it now costs nothing but a template hash. Separately, Post should require a minimum expiry distance so a row's freeze window is bounded whatever the maker types.
- **Regression test** tests/zzadv_market_test.go TestBoardAcceptByAnyoneFreezesAnOfferUntilExpiry, inverted when the fix lands: assert that after the acceptance deadline the maker can Cancel an accepted offer, or that a second Accept re-opens it, and that Post refuses an expiry closer than the minimum.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/board.bas, tests/board_test.go, tests/zzadv_market_test.go, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md, docs/BACKLOG.md
- **Already known as** BL-45 (B) is the nearest entry and it UNDERSTATES this: it asks whether a board entry needs a bond on top of gas — i.e. it treats spam as posting spam by the maker. The accept side, where one griefing transaction disables another party's row, is not in the backlog and is not in ZEC_SWAP.md §9's Z1-Z16 table. BL-46 (C) asks for expiry-by-construction, which is already there and is exactly what makes the freeze last as long as it does.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)


## LOW (43)

### PRIV-05 — vault.bas still carries a comment asserting the privacy property the function beneath it destroys — the app copy was corrected, the contract source was not

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:262` (dimension: Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares)
- **Attack path** Not an attack; a false statement in the artefact reviewers read as the source of truth. contracts/vault.bas:262 reads "// A FRESH index per deposit (nothing on chain links two deposits of one user), paid for with regfee." The very next function, Register (lines 264-284), stores `ul_<s>_<c> = j` and `uc_<s> = c+1` keyed by the caller's address — exactly a public index of all of that user's deposits — and `ua_<j> = s`, the reverse map. The claim is true only of the FOREIGN side (one address per deposit) and is written as if it held on DERO.
- **Preconditions** None.
- **Impact** contracts/*.bas is what auditors, signers and any future integrator read to decide what the system reveals. A reviewer who trusts this comment concludes the deposit path is unlinkable and stops reading. It also contradicts contracts/README.md's own key list and the app screen that describes the same function.
- **Exploitability** n/a — documentation.
- **Reproduced** yes — sed -n '260,284p' contracts/vault.bas shows the comment at line 262 immediately above `Function Register()`, whose lines 30-32 are `STORE("ua_" + j, s)`, `STORE("ul_" + s + "_" + c, j)`, `STORE("uc_" + s, c + 1)` with `s = SIGNER()` at line 20 (ring 2, so a real address). grep of app/wrap.js line 286 confirms the app now states the linkage correctly, so the two disagree in the same repository.
- **Fix** Replace the parenthetical with the truth: "// A FRESH index per deposit, so the FOREIGN chain sees a different address each time; on DERO the opposite holds — ua_<j>, ul_<addr>_<c> and uc_<addr> publish which address owns every index, so all of one user's deposits are linked here in the clear. Paid for with regfee." This is safe now and cannot change behaviour: reference.Canon (reference/canon.go:22-33) drops whole-line comments and blank lines before hashing or installing, so the canonical bytes, the install gas and every template hash are unchanged (TestCanonOnlyDropsCommentsAndBlanks covers exactly this).
- **Regression test** None is possible for a comment, and inventing one would be theatre. The durable fix is the PRIV-02 Go test that asserts ua_/ul_/uc_/da_ are readable plaintext after Register+Attest — a passing test that says out loud what the comment denied.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: contracts/vault.bas
- **Already known as** The app half of SEC·RT-04 (FINDINGS.md:568-582, which cites app/wrap.js:275 'Registering again gives you a fresh index, so two deposits of yours are not linked on chain') has been FIXED — wrap.js:286 now says the opposite and says it well. The contract half was left behind and no finding tracks it.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: True as stated and it is a pure documentation fact, so reading is the reproduction. contracts/vault.bas:262 asserts "A FRESH index per deposit (nothing on chain links two deposits of one user)" and the function immediately below it stores ul_<s>_<c> and uc_<s> keyed by the caller's own address — pre || REAL/INFO/reproduced: The comment is exactly as quoted and it is false about the DERO side. But LOW overstates a whole-line comment with no behaviour, no attack path and no user-facing text; INFO is the honest rating. Two things the finder got right and I confirmed: the app half of SEC·RT-04 really has been fixed (app/wr

### PRIV-06 — "Clear local trade history, watchlist and cache" leaves the chat mute list on the device because it is the one storage key that does not use the veil. prefix

- **Where** `app/screens.js:277` (dimension: Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares)
- **Attack path** app/screens.js:277 clears storage with `if (k.startsWith("veil.") && k !== "veil.settings") localStorage.removeItem(k)`. app/chat.js:24 stores the mute list under `const MUTE = "dmz.chat.mute"`.
