- **Exploitability** Not attacker-controlled; it is the shipped configuration meeting the shipped cap. It degrades the credibility of the one screen that must be believed.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ grep -n 'U.clean(a.chain' app/wrap.js 203:      const a = w.asset || {}, name = U.clean(a.name || "the foreign asset", 40), chain = U.clean(a.chain || "its own chain", 40); $ node -e 'console.log("Monero - simulated on this network (12 decimals, as Monero)".length)'  -> 58 Screenshot .sim/ui-audit-frontend/02-wrap.png: "MONERO - SIMULATED ON THIS NETWORK (12 D" appears in the hero, the settlement row, the custody row and steps 2-5.
- **Fix** Either raise the cap to the length the config actually ships (and cap the config field in tools/genconfig so the two cannot drift), or truncate on a word boundary with an ellipsis. Better: shorten the shipped `chain` strings to a name, and put the decimals note where the decimals are shown.
- **Regression test** A UI-pass assertion that no rendered string on #wrap ends mid-word, or simply that config.vaults[].chain.length <= the cap wrap.js applies.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/wrap.js, app/config.js, tools/genconfig/main.go
- **Already known as** New. Cosmetic, but on the one screen where a mistake is irreversible.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### FE-11 — Nothing prevents the app being framed: no frame-ancestors, no X-Frame-Options, and TELA sends no headers

- **Where** `app/index.html:6 + tools/serve/main.go:1-18 + vendor/tela (plain http.FileServer):6` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** frame-ancestors cannot be expressed in a meta CSP, and neither tools/serve nor TELA's own server sets any response header (tools/serve/main.go is a bare http.FileServer, and its own comment says "TELA hosts serve exactly like this: plain files on localhost, no headers of note"). Any page can therefore iframe the app at its localhost origin and overlay it. The iframe reports its own origin in the XSWD ApplicationData url (xswd.js:48), so the wallet's connect prompt names DMZ truthfully while the framing page drives the clicks.
- **Preconditions** The victim visits an attacker page while a TELA host is serving DMZ on a known port (the range starts at 8082 and is small).
- **Impact** Bounded by the wallet's per-transaction confirmation, which shows the real transaction - so this is not a path to a silent transfer. What it does reach is every control that changes state without a wallet prompt: the Settings image toggle (IP disclosure), the slippage chips, "Post this trade", the private-mode destination field, and the watchlist. It can also steer a user into a wallet prompt they did not intend to open.
- **Exploitability** Needs the victim on an attacker page with DMZ concurrently served on a guessable port; every money-moving step is still gated by the wallet's own dialog.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned; not staged. $ grep -c 'frame-ancestors' app/index.html    -> 0 $ grep -n 'Header()\|X-Frame' tools/serve/main.go   -> no matches $ sed -n '1,18p' tools/serve/main.go   # http.ListenAndServe(addr, http.FileServer(...)) To stage: serve any page containing <iframe src="http://127.0.0.1:8082/index.html"> and confirm it renders and responds to clicks.
- **Fix** Not fully fixable in a meta CSP - say so honestly on the notice rather than implying otherwise. Practical mitigations local to the app: a framebuster in app.js (`if (window.top !== window.self)` render a refusal), and moving the promptless controls (Settings toggles, the private-mode destination) behind a click that requires focus. If TELA hosting is ever fronted by a server the operator controls, set frame-ancestors 'none' there.
- **Regression test** A UI-pass step that loads the app inside an iframe and asserts it refuses to render.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/app.js, app/index.html, app/notice.js
- **Already known as** New. Noted as a risk by the frontend reader; no BL number.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-09 — A mistyped signer address before Seal can never be removed, so the vault can never be sealed and the deployment must be abandoned

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:153` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** AddSigner (vault.bas:153-170) appends to sg_<n>, sets isg_ and increments n, with no matching removal entrypoint anywhere in the file and no way to decrement n. Seal (vault.bas:250-254) walks every i in [0, n) and refuses unless bond_<sg_i> exists and is at least minbond. If the owner adds an address that nobody controls — a typo, a wrong network's address, a signer who then declines — that slot can never hold a bond and Seal is refused for ever. AddSigner also refuses to re-add an existing isg_ (line 160), so the address cannot be replaced by a correct one either.
- **Preconditions** One AddSigner call with a wrong address before Seal.
- **Impact** The vault is bricked before it ever holds value: it can never be sealed, and an unsealed vault refuses Register, Mint and Burn, so it can hold nothing. No user funds are at risk and the bonds already posted are recoverable — Unbond is open to anyone pre-Seal (vault.bas:226 only bars signers once sealed=1), which the code comment at 219-220 explains as exactly this escape. The cost is a wasted install (~16,392 storage gas) and a redeploy: a state that needs a human, whose only remedy is outside the contract.
- **Exploitability** Owner error only; AddSigner is owner-gated pre-Seal.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from code, not executed. vault.bas has no RemoveSigner (confirmed by reading all 571 lines); Seal's loop at lines 250-254 has no skip; AddSigner line 160 refuses an address whose isg_ already exists.
- **Fix** Either add an owner-only RemoveSigner usable while sealed == 0 (small and local, but a contract change and therefore a redeploy for any existing vault), or accept it and make deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md and the missing deploy/federation.md require that every signer address be verified — round-tripped through ADDRESS_RAW and confirmed by the signer posting their own bond — before AddSigner is called, with the ordering 'candidate bonds first, owner adds second' as the checklist rule. The documentation half is safe now.
- **Regression test** A DVM case in tests/vault_test.go: AddSigner with an address that never bonds, then assert Seal is refused and that no entrypoint can remove the slot.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, deploy/federation.md, tests/vault_test.go
- **Already known as** Not recorded anywhere. deploy/README.md's federation section and the missing deploy/federation.md do not mention it.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### LIV-10 — Vault fee units can only ever be claimed by the treasury key, and stay counted in sup for ever if it is lost

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:487` (dimension: DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION))
- **Attack path** ClaimFees (vault.bas:487-498) requires SIGNER() == LOAD("treasury") at ring 2. `treasury` is written once at InitializePrivate (vault.bas:76) and has no setter — no owner path, no k-of-n vote, nothing. Every wfee a Released withdrawal leaves behind accumulates in feepool (vault.bas:424) and remains counted in sup, so the foreign reserve stays committed against it. No tool calls it: tools/treasurybot walks only the factory's pool_ and launch_ lists and never touches a vault, so this is a manual step by a wallet the operator must keep for the life of the vault.
- **Preconditions** A sealed vault that has settled at least one withdrawal. Loss or retirement of the treasury key.
- **Impact** The fee units become permanently unredeemable and permanently encumber the federation's reserve. Bounded (wfee per withdrawal, and wfee < minburn by vault.bas:61) and harmful to the operator rather than to users, but it is a standing requirement for a live human key that directly contradicts the directive's revocation goal — the operator cannot walk away from a vault without abandoning its fees and leaving the reserve over-committed.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack. A permanent operational dependency.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned from code, not executed. vault.bas:491 is the treasury check; grep over the contract shows `treasury` is stored only at line 76; grep over tools/ shows no caller of a vault ClaimFees.
- **Fix** Contract change, needs a redeploy policy: either let the k-of-n quorum sweep feepool to the treasury (making it a signer duty rather than an operator duty), or have Released reduce sup by the full v so the fee units stop encumbering the reserve when they cannot be claimed, or add a k-of-n SetTreasury for this one purpose. Safe now with no code change: automate the claim in tools/treasurybot (add vaults to its sweep, which it already knows how to skip for wrapped quotes) and state the standing key requirement in app/notice.js and deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md.
- **Regression test** A DVM case asserting that whatever mechanism is chosen (quorum sweep, or sup reduced by v) leaves no unclaimable feepool after a Released, plus tests/vault_fuzz_test.go's conservation invariant updated to match.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: contracts/vault.bas, tools/treasurybot/main.go, app/notice.js, deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md, tests/vault_fuzz_test.go
- **Already known as** Noted in passing by the vault architecture reader ('a lost treasury key makes feepool an unredeemable permanent claim on the reserve'); not in docs/BACKLOG.md.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-09 — The maker's lock windows are decided once and never re-decided, so a lock delayed past its own t1 is refused for ever with no exit but a human deleting the state file

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:655` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** `case "verified_pi_a"` (swap.go:652-657) fixes T1 = h + T1Delta and T2 = T1 + T2Delta at the height it ran, saves them, and moves to dero_locking. dero_locking (swap.go:658-679) reuses st.Lock.T1/T2 on every attempt for the life of the state file; nothing re-decides them. htlc.bas:33 refuses a Lock whose `t1 <= h + 1`, so once the chain has advanced past the recorded t1 — a wallet or daemon outage longer than T1Delta blocks, ~2 hours at mainnet policy — every attempt is refused at the estimate, the outer loop retries every -interval for ever, and the companion has no phase that gives up or re-decides.
- **Preconditions** An outage between the durable T1/T2 decision and a successful Lock that exceeds T1Delta blocks. Nothing is locked, so no funds are at stake — only the swap.
- **Impact** An unattended companion spins for ever on a swap that can never start; the only way out is a person deleting the state file, which the directive names as a defect ('any state that needs a human to leave it').
- **Exploitability** No attacker; an availability/liveness defect.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only. swap.go:652-657 writes LockRecord{IntentHeight, T1, T2} once; swap.go:658-679 never reads the current height except for the LockSettleBlocks guard. htlc.bas:33 `IF t1 <= h + 1 || t2 <= t1 || t2 > h + 1000000 THEN GOTO 900`.
- **Fix** In dero_locking, before submitting, compare the current height to st.Lock.T1: if `h + 1 >= st.Lock.T1 - <a small floor>` and no lock exists yet on the chain for these hashes, either re-decide T1/T2 from the current height (durably; the lock has not gone out, so there is nothing to re-announce) or finish the swap as 'abandoned: the window expired before the lock could be sent'. Both are safe because no value has moved.
- **Regression test** A swap_test case that advances the chain past T1 while dero_locking keeps erroring, asserting the companion either re-decides the windows or reaches a terminal phase within a bounded number of Steps.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/swap_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-10 — A call the contract refused is retried at the same fee for ever, with no cap and no distinction from an out-of-gas revert

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/dero.go:621` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** exit() (dero.go:621-679) doubles the fee only on errNotMined. A call that mines but reverts — the DVM's 'Insufficient Storage Gas' panic, or any state the contract will never accept — reads back with the wrong st_ and is reported as 'was included but the contract refused it'. swap.go's refund_window/claiming/punish_window return that error, cmd/xmrswap/main.go logs it, sleeps -interval and calls Step again, which re-sends at the same fee. Refund and Punish run on a fixed fee (-fee, default 3000) because the estimator runs the contract at height 0 and refuses height-gated calls, so there is no estimate to correct it either.
- **Preconditions** Either a systematic refusal (see XMR-05) or a fixed fee below the call's real storage gas. Today's headroom looks comfortable — htlc.bas installs at 3,598 storage gas for 3,561 bytes, so a Refund's ~90 bytes of SCDATA plus two stores is far under 3,000 — but that is a measurement nobody has taken for the exit calls, and the asset variant of Lock (BL-20) is untested.
- **Impact** An unbounded fee burn plus the silent loss of the window the call was for: at mainnet policy a maker who cannot refund inside [t1, t2) loses the whole locked amount to Punish. It is the mechanism by which XMR-05 becomes fund loss.
- **Exploitability** No attacker path on its own; it is an amplifier of every other refusal.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only. dero.go:643-676: the fee doubles only inside the errNotMined branch; the terminal read-back at dero.go:672-676 returns an error the caller retries from scratch. Measuring the real storage gas of Refund and Punish needs the DVM harness: `tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run Htlc -v` reports install gas only (3,598, seen this session), so tests/htlc_test.go would have to be extended to print per-call storage gas.
- **Fix** Distinguish the two refusals: after a mined-but-refused exit, re-read the lock and decide — if the state changed, report it and let the caller route; if the state is unchanged and the call's own preconditions still hold, treat it as an under-fee and escalate the fee once (bounded, with a cap and a logged reason) before giving up with an error that names the fee. Cap the outer retries per window and print the remaining blocks. Separately, measure Refund/Punish storage gas in tests/htlc_test.go and set -fee's default from that number rather than by hand.
- **Regression test** A dero_test case where the daemon mines the call but leaves st_ unchanged: exit() must not resubmit more than the capped number of times and must return an error naming the fee.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/dero.go, tools/xmrswap/dero_test.go, tests/htlc_test.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. BL-17 (B) asks for fee disclosure, not for escalation behaviour.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-11 — One channel directory serves exactly one swap: peer names are fixed and messages are consumed by kind, so two concurrent swaps destroy each other's setup

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/channel.go:299` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** DirChannel addresses peers by the fixed names 'bob' and 'alice' (channel.go:299-306) with no swap id in the path, and Recv takes the oldest file and deletes it. swap.go's recv (swap.go:246-273) drops any message whose kind is not the one the phase wants — permanently, since the file is already gone. So a second concurrent swap in the same directory has its offer or dero_locked consumed by the first swap's companion and discarded, or vice versa. If the crossed message is a dero_locked, the taker adopts the other swap's lock id, CheckLock refuses it (different ha) and she finishes 'refused' while the maker's DERO stays locked until his own refund at t1.
- **Preconditions** Two swaps run from one channel directory — the natural mistake, since -channel is a plain directory and the CLI says nothing about it.
- **Impact** No fund loss: every refusal happens before the taker's XMR moves, and the maker's DERO returns at t1. Cost is the maker's locked window and both parties' fees. Also a griefing surface for anyone with write access to the directory, which is unauthenticated by design.
- **Exploitability** Operator error, or anyone who can write the shared directory. Fails closed on money in every path traced.
- **Reproduced** no — Reasoned only, from channel.go:299-427 (fixed peer names, oldest-file-wins, delete on read) and swap.go:246-273 (the 'dropping %s in phase %s' log happens after the file has been consumed).
- **Fix** Namespace the channel by swap: put st.SwapID (or the contract scid plus the lock's hashes) in the directory path, and refuse to start when the directory already holds another swap's messages. Cheaper still: do not delete a message whose kind the phase did not want — rename it aside as .later and re-scan, so an out-of-order delivery is held rather than destroyed.
- **Regression test** A channel_test with two swaps writing into one directory, asserting each companion sees only its own messages; and a swap_test asserting a message of an unexpected kind is not lost.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/channel.go, tools/xmrswap/swap.go, tools/xmrswap/channel_test.go
- **Already known as** Adjacent to BL-10 (A) ("the rendezvous channel between strangers"), which asks for a real transport; it does not record that the current transport is unusable for two swaps at once even between the same pair.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### INT-03 — zecswap.ParseV5 panics with a slice-bounds error on a transaction whose compact-size length field is at or above 2^63: r.p + n overflows int and the bounds check passes

- **Where** `tools\zecswap\sighash.go:293` (dimension: Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43))
- **Attack path** reader.bytes guards with `if n < 0 || r.p+n > len(r.b)`. n comes from `int(r.compact())` at sighash.go:251 (scriptSig), :258 (scriptPubKey) and orchard.go:113 (Orchard proofs). compact() reads a 0xff prefix followed by a little-endian uint64 (sighash.go:328). A value of 2^63-1 converts to a positive int (MaxInt64), so `n < 0` is false; `r.p + n` then overflows int64 to a negative number, so `r.p+n > len(r.b)` is also false; execution reaches `r.b[r.p : r.p+n]` with a negative high bound and the runtime panics. Any 8-byte length between roughly 2^63 - len(raw) and 2^63-1 does it, and the same shape reaches orchard.go's Proofs read.
- **Preconditions** Something must hand ParseV5 raw bytes it did not build. Today nothing does: tools/zecswap has no node client, no watcher and no broadcast (BL-40b), so the only callers are the tests. The moment the ZEC-swap watcher of docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §3 exists, it will feed ParseV5 the raw hex of the funding transaction and of the maker's claim, both read off a chain anyone can write to, in order to recover the preimage from the claim's scriptSig.
- **Impact** An unrecoverable panic in the process that is watching a live swap. For the taker that process is the only thing that knows to broadcast the refund after CLTV; for the maker it is the only thing that reads the secret out of the claim. A crash at the wrong moment costs the swap, and a crash loop costs every swap that companion is running. No fund is taken directly.
- **Exploitability** Cheap to trigger once a watcher exists — a single junk transaction paying the watched script address, or a transaction the watcher happens to parse. Costs one Zcash fee. It needs the watcher to be built first, which is why this is LOW today and not higher.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tools\zecswap\fuzz_precision_test.go with a minimised case (TestParseV5HugeCompactSize) that builds a well-formed v5 header, one input, and a scriptSig length of 0xff + 2^63-1:  $ cd tools && go test ./zecswap -run TestParseV5HugeCompactSize -v === RUN   TestParseV5HugeCompactSize     fuzz_precision_test.go:46: ParseV5 panicked instead of erroring: runtime error: slice bounds out of range [:-9223372036854775743] --- FAIL: TestParseV5HugeCompactSize  The same file carries FuzzParseV5NeverPanics; a 60-second coverage-guided run found no other panic: $ go test ./zecswap -run FuzzParseV5NeverPanics -fuzz FuzzParseV5NeverPanics -fuzztime 60s fuzz: elapsed: 1m0s, 
- **Fix** Do the bound in uint64 rather than int, in reader.bytes: `if n < 0 || uint64(r.p)+uint64(n) > uint64(len(r.b))`. Or, better, give reader a `bytesN(n uint64)` and refuse anything above len(r.b) before converting. Fix orchard.go:113 the same way. Purely local to the parser; no protocol or interface change.
- **Regression test** tools\zecswap\fuzz_precision_test.go: TestParseV5HugeCompactSize (deterministic, currently failing by design) plus FuzzParseV5NeverPanics as a corpus-building guard. Add the minimised bytes to testdata/fuzz/FuzzParseV5NeverPanics/ so it becomes part of every plain `go test` run.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools\zecswap\sighash.go, tools\zecswap\orchard.go
- **Already known as** Inside BL-40b's unbuilt scope ("the watcher, the ZIP 317 fee and broadcast remain"), but the backlog does not name the parser's robustness. Not in any prior audit.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### INT-04 — zecswap.ScriptNum panics on math.MinInt64, the value a companion produces by converting board.bas's unbounded cl_<id> to int64

- **Where** `tools\zecswap\script.go:140` (dimension: Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43))
- **Attack path** ScriptNum negates a negative input with `n = -n` (script.go:139). For math.MinInt64 the negation wraps back to math.MinInt64, which is still negative, so the `for n > 0` loop never runs and `out` stays nil; line 147 then indexes `out[len(out)-1]` on an empty slice and panics. The path that reaches it: contracts/board.bas:62 accepts any cltv Uint64 except zero (`IF STRLEN(refundPkh) != 40 || cltv == 0 THEN GOTO 900`) — no upper bound, no range check. The ZEC-swap companion of docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §3 must read cl_<id> off the board to build the maker's redeem script; the obvious conversion, int64(cl), turns 0x8000000000000000 into MinInt64.
- **Preconditions** A companion that calls ScriptNum with a board-supplied value as int64. The shipped CLI does not: tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go:54 refuses cltv above 0x7fffffff, and HTLC.CLTV is uint32, so RedeemScript's ScriptNum(int64(h.CLTV)) is always positive. The companion is unbuilt (BL-40b), so this is latent.
- **Impact** A crash in the maker's script builder, triggerable for one board Accept fee (one ring-2 DERO transaction) by any taker. Denial of service on the offer, not a loss of funds.
- **Exploitability** Trivial once the companion exists: Accept any open offer with cltv = 9223372036854775808. The board's Accept is permissionless and the maker cannot Cancel an accepted offer until it expires (board.bas:82), so the offer is frozen as well as unbuildable.
- **Reproduced** yes — Added tools\zecswap\fuzz_precision_test.go FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics, seeded with the int64 edges:  $ cd tools && go test ./zecswap -run FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics -fuzz FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics -fuzztime 60s failure while testing seed corpus entry: FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics/seed#8 --- FAIL: FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics     fuzz_precision_test.go:83: ScriptNum(-9223372036854775808) panicked: runtime error: index out of range [-1]  I also confirmed the shipped CLI cannot reach it: main.go:54 rejects *cltv above 0x7fffffff, and I exercised the boundary by hand (see INT-05's repro).
- **Fix** Handle the one value that cannot be negated: after `neg := n < 0`, take the magnitude as `m := uint64(n); if neg { m = -uint64(n) }` and build from m, which is correct for MinInt64. Alternatively change the signature to ScriptNum(n uint64) since every caller in this package passes a height, and let the caller refuse a value that does not fit. Either is local to script.go with no wire-format change (the encoding of every currently reachable value is unchanged).
- **Regression test** tools\zecswap\fuzz_precision_test.go FuzzScriptNumNeverPanics (added, failing on its seed corpus by design) — it also asserts minimality of the encoding, so it will keep catching the neighbouring class. Separately, when the companion is written, it must bound cl_<id> before converting: add that check with its own test.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools\zecswap\script.go
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md. Another auditor's concurrently-added tools/zecswap/hazards_test.go covers a different ScriptNum defect (non-minimal encoding of 1..16, `push(ScriptNum(1)) = 0101, want 51`); that is theirs, not this.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### INT-05 — Nothing bounds the swap board's CLTV below BIP 65's height/timestamp threshold, and the CLI calls a Unix timestamp a height: a refund branch can be spendable the moment it is funded

- **Where** `tools\zecswap\cmd\zecswap\main.go:54` (dimension: Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43))
- **Attack path** BIP 65 lists among CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY's failure conditions: "the lock-time type (height vs. timestamp) of the top stack item and the nLockTime field are not the same" — the two are separated by LOCKTIME_THRESHOLD, the standard 500,000,000 (values below it are block heights, values at or above it are Unix times). contracts/board.bas:62 accepts any non-zero Uint64 as cltv. tools/zecswap/cmd/zecswap/main.go:54 accepts 1 .. 0x7fffffff (2,147,483,647), a range that straddles the threshold, and the CLI's own flag help calls it "the Zcash height after which the taker may refund" while its output line reads "nLockTime <n> (the refund transaction's, at least)". A cltv such as 1,600,000,000 — which reads like a plausible large number and is a real Unix time from 2020 — produces a script whose ELSE branch is a timestamp lock already in the past, so the refund is spendable immediately and consistently (both the stack item and nLockTime are timestamps, so the type check passes and the time check passes). A maker who checks "is this cltv far enough ahead of the current Zcash height" is comparing a timestamp against a height and gets a meaningless answer. Separately, HTLC.CLTV is uint32 while board's cl_ is Uint64, so a companion doing uint32(cl) truncates any value above 2^32 to a different, silently wrong lock.
- **Preconditions** A taker who sets cltv wrongly by accident or on purpose, and a maker whose tooling builds the script from the board without a threshold check. The companion that would do this is unbuilt (BL-40b), and board.bas is not deployed on any network (no SCID in deploy/networks.json or app/config.js), so this is latent.
- **Impact** The Zcash leg's only timelock loses its meaning. The taker can pull their ZEC back at will after funding, so the maker's DERO can be locked for the full t_dero window against an escrow that was never really escrowed — the maker loses time and the DERO's opportunity cost, and gets it back at t_dero. The reverse mistake, a cltv just under the threshold when a timestamp was meant, locks the taker's ZEC for the remaining ~478 million blocks. No direct theft, because the secret still gates the claim branch.
- **Exploitability** One board Accept (one ring-2 DERO transaction) sets it. It is at least as likely to happen by mistake as by design, since neither the contract nor the CLI says which units cltv is in.
- **Reproduced** yes — $ cd tools && go run ./zecswap/cmd/zecswap script -hash 00..01 -claim cd*20 -refund ef*20 -cltv 1600000000 -net testnet redeem script 63a8200000...00018876a914cdcd...cd88ac670400105e5fb17576a914efef...ef88ac68 (95 bytes) scriptPubKey  a9148a9f6b85cf3d8452e9f376e2d7b2a70270b5264787 address       t2KBnCipd3aKoaoTrf2z7mR6LJQzChnAwZV (testnet) nLockTime     1600000000 (the refund transaction's, at least)  The pushed script number is 5f5e1000 little-endian = 1,600,000,000, at or above LOCKTIME_THRESHOLD, so BIP 65 reads it as a Unix time (13 September 2020) rather than a height; the CLI accepts it, calls it a height, and says nothing. For contrast, -cltv 499999999 produces ff64cd1d, the largest v
- **Fix** Two halves. (a) In the tool, refuse cltv >= 500000000 with the reason ("BIP 65 reads that as a Unix time, not a height") and, when the companion is written, apply the same check plus a minimum distance from the current Zcash tip and a refusal of anything above 2^32-1 before the uint32 conversion — main.go:54 and the companion's board reader, local and testable. (b) On chain, add an upper bound to board.bas Accept (cltv < 500000000, and a minimum distance from a plausible tip if the operator wants one). board.bas is not deployed anywhere yet, so the contract half is still free — but it is a contract edit and belongs to whoever owns the deployment sequence, not to this pass.
- **Regression test** A table test in tools/zecswap asserting that the script builder refuses 500000000 and above and accepts 499999999, and that a value above 2^32-1 is refused rather than truncated. On the contract side, a case in tests/board_test.go asserting Accept refuses cltv = 500000000 once the bound exists.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: tools\zecswap\cmd\zecswap\main.go, tools\zecswap\script.go, contracts\board.bas
- **Already known as** Not in docs/BACKLOG.md; the eight-reader map lists a neighbouring LOW ("neither the CLI nor board.bas Accept bounds cltv to a plausible height") without measuring it or naming the BIP 65 type rule. BL-46 covers the timelock policy in the abstract, not this.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-10 — SetViewKey accepts any string, and the app turns any non-empty value into 'anyone can check the reserve'

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:136` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** SetViewKey (vault.bas:136-150) checks only `STRLEN(key)` in [1, 400] and k signer votes. There is no hex check, no length-64 check, no curve check — the DVM could not verify a Monero or Zcash key anyway. app/wrap.js:337-339 renders any non-empty `viewkey` as a green trust line stating the reserve is checkable.
- **Preconditions** k colluding signers, or an honest quorum that publishes the wrong string.
- **Impact** The one property that would let outsiders audit the reserve without trusting the federation becomes a checkbox the federation ticks itself. A user reading the trust panel concludes the backing is verifiable when nothing has been published that can verify it.
- **Exploitability** k votes, gas only.
- **Reproduced** yes — Wrote tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestSetViewKeyAcceptsAnyString.    tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'TestSetViewKeyAcceptsAnyString' -v  Output:   === RUN   TestSetViewKeyAcceptsAnyString       zz_adversary_priv_test.go:175: ADV-4 CONFIRMED: viewkey is now "not a viewing key at all -- just words" — no hex, length or curve check exists   --- PASS: TestSetViewKeyAcceptsAnyString (0.02s)
- **Fix** App, safe now: wrap.js should render the viewing key itself (mono, copyable) with the address it is claimed to view, and word the line as "the federation has published this string as the viewing key — check it against the reserve yourself", never as an assertion that anyone has. Contract, redeploy decision: require STRLEN(key) == 64 and hex-decodability (HEXDECODE panics on non-hex, which reverts) as a cheap shape check for the Monero case.
- **Regression test** A JS test that feeds wrap.js's trust panel a junk viewkey and asserts the rendered text does not claim verifiability; keep tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go:TestSetViewKeyAcceptsAnyString for the contract side.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/wrap.js, contracts/vault.bas, tests/zz_adversary_priv_test.go
- **Already known as** Named as a risk in the vault architecture map; no BL number. Not previously demonstrated.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-11 — The operator's OS account name and home directory are in 37 lines of the published public repository, and the autonomous-agent operating prompt is published with it

- **Where** `docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:13` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tools/publish.sh squashed everything before commit 60ab2e6 into one clean root commit precisely because "earlier commits carried machine-specific paths". The 71 commits pushed since carry them again. github.com/NoahMoney/DMZ therefore publishes `C:\Users\<user>\...`, `/home/<user>/...` and `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/...`, plus docs/LOOP_PROMPT.md — the operator's autonomous R&D cycle prompt, including the absolute repo path and the instruction to leave the repo "green and pushed at the end of every cycle".
- **Preconditions** None. The repository is public.
- **Impact** Deanonymisation of the operator by OS account name, and disclosure of the local layout and tooling of the machine that will hold the mainnet deployer, treasury and TELA INDEX keys. Minor on its own; it is targeting information for the key-holder identified in SEC-K-02 and SEC-K-03. No key material is exposed.
- **Exploitability** Reading a public repository.
- **Reproduced** yes —   git grep -cIE '/home/<user>|/mnt/c/Users/<user>|C:\\\\Users\\\\<user>|Users/<user>|Users\\\\<user>' origin/main -- . ':!vendor'     docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:20     research/crosschain/poc/proof-xmr/README.md:4     docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md:3     tools/simhtlc/main.go:2     docs/LOOP_PROMPT.md:2     tools/xmrswap/proof_test.go:1  tools/xmrswap/proof.go:1  tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go:1     research/crosschain/poc/proof-xmr/record.sh:1     docs/audit/DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:1  docs/NFA_BUILD.md:1   ... | wc -l  -> 37 lines total One of them (docs/audit/DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md:334) even publishes an AppData scratchpad path.
- **Fix** Safe now: replace every occurrence with a repo-relative path or a placeholder — `$REPO`, `<repo>`, `tools/...`, `<prover binary>` — in all 11 files; make the prover path in tools/xmrswap/proof.go:25, cmd/xmrswap/main.go:46 and simhtlc/main.go a flag default of the bare binary name `proof-xmr`, with the WSL invocation shown only in a doc that uses `$REPO`. Decide separately whether docs/LOOP_PROMPT.md belongs in a public repository at all. Rewriting the published history is not worth it; fixing the tip stops the leak growing.
- **Regression test** Add a check to tests/run.sh (or a tiny Go test) that greps the tracked tree for `Users.<user>`, `/home/<user>` and `/mnt/c/` and fails the suite — the directive already makes this a hard rule, so it should be enforced by the suite rather than by review.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: docs/audit/FINDINGS.md, docs/audit/DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT.md, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md, docs/NFA_BUILD.md, docs/LOOP_PROMPT.md, tools/simhtlc/main.go, tools/xmrswap/proof.go, tools/xmrswap/proof_test.go, tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, research/crosschain/poc/proof-xmr/README.md, research/crosschain/poc/proof-xmr/record.sh, tests/run.sh
- **Already known as** The directive's hard rule "No machine-specific paths in any repository file" and the xmrswap architecture map's risk list name six files. The real count in the published tree is 37 lines across 11 files — the map understates it by roughly half and misses docs/audit/FINDINGS.md, which alone carries 20.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-12 — Five unsigned Windows executables — including a 9.16 MB build of the federation signer daemon — are permanently in the public repository's history, and stale signer state is still tracked at the tip

- **Where** `testnet/signer-30003.json:1` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tools/publish.sh's clean-root discipline applied only to commits before 60ab2e6. Commits 5622e78, c872260, d68094d and 5cb8d1b — all reachable from origin/main and origin/wip/2026-09-09-swap-nfa — add tools/vaultsigner.exe (9,164,288 bytes), tools/simdeploy.exe, tools/simvault.exe, tools/treasurybot.exe and tools/simchat.exe. They were later deleted from the tip but remain fetchable. Meanwhile testnet/signer-30003.json and testnet/signer-30004.json are still tracked: identical files holding vault b3efec69…, one release intent in state "voted" to a fake Monero destination, and liveAt {0: 103}. .gitignore covers tools/vaultsigner-*.json but not testnet/.
- **Preconditions** None. Public repository.
- **Impact** No key material is exposed — I read both signer files and swept all 71 published commits for mnemonics, PEM keys, ghp_/github_pat_ tokens and password literals and found none. The concern is provenance: the repository ships, from its own history, unsigned Windows binaries of the deployer and the federation signer, built on the operator's machine, covered by no lock file and hashed nowhere — the exact class of artefact deploy/binaries.lock and tools/verifyvendor exist to prevent. The tracked signer state is stale operational data that suggests a live federation where none exists.
- **Exploitability** A third party who trusts the repository and runs a binary out of its history.
- **Reproduced** yes —   git log --all --pretty=format: --name-only --diff-filter=A | sort -u | grep -E 'tools/.*\.exe'     tools/simchat.exe tools/simdeploy.exe tools/simvault.exe tools/treasurybot.exe tools/vaultsigner.exe   git log origin/wip/2026-09-09-swap-nfa --oneline --diff-filter=A -- 'tools/*.exe'     d68094d  5cb8d1b  c872260  5622e78        <- all four are in the PUSHED history   git cat-file -s $(git rev-parse 5622e78:tools/vaultsigner.exe)   -> 9164288   git rev-list --count origin/main -> 71 ; git rev-list --max-parents=0 origin/main -> 60ab2e6 (same root as the wip branch)   cat testnet/signer-30003.json  -> vault b3efec69…, intent 1 state "voted", dest "4AxmrSecondDestination…", liveAt {"0":103}  
- **Fix** Safe now: delete testnet/ from the tree and add `testnet/` to .gitignore next to the existing `tools/vaultsigner-*.json` line. `tools/*.exe` is already in .gitignore but the files predate it, so also add a check that no tracked file has a .exe extension. Rewriting public history to purge the binaries is a judgement call for the operator; at minimum the README should state that no executable in this repository's history is a supported artefact and that vendor/bin is rebuilt from source per deploy/README.md.
- **Regression test** A check in tests/run.sh that `git ls-files` contains no `.exe` and no `testnet/` path, so a tracked binary or a signer state file fails the suite.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: testnet/signer-30003.json, testnet/signer-30004.json, .gitignore, tests/run.sh, README.md
- **Already known as** The deployment architecture map notes "a 9 MB tools/vaultsigner.exe in public history" and the tracked testnet/signer-*.json. Neither was confirmed against the pushed branch, and the other four binaries were not identified.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-13 — Settings tells the user 'There is no operator once this is deployed' while notice.js lists five surviving key-holders, and overstates what the CSP blocks

- **Where** `app/screens.js:266` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** app/screens.js:266 renders `can(false, "There is no operator once this is deployed: no support desk, no review, no refund.")` in the Security & privacy list. app/notice.js:64-74 — the page the directive makes authoritative and which says so itself ("If any other screen in this app appears to contradict it, this page is the one that is true") — names five parties who still hold a key: the factory owner, the treasury, the app publisher, the federation and the node. Separately, screens.js:262 claims "A Content Security Policy blocks any script that is not part of this build and any connection except your node, your wallet and image hosts"; the actual policy at app/index.html:6 has `connect-src 'self' ws://127.0.0.1:* ws://localhost:* wss: http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:*` — `wss:` with no host restriction, so any wss endpoint on the internet is permitted by the policy.
- **Preconditions** None. Shipped copy.
- **Impact** A user who reads Settings rather than the notice concludes no key survives deployment — the opposite of the truth established in SEC-K-02 and SEC-K-03 — and believes the CSP would contain an injected script's exfiltration when it would not. Violates the directive's hard rule never to contradict app/notice.js.
- **Exploitability** n/a — a copy defect, not an attack.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '255,268p' app/screens.js   -> the two can() lines quoted above   sed -n '58,78p' app/notice.js      -> "Who still holds a key": the factory owner / the treasury / the app publisher / the federation / your node, then "If any other screen in this app appears to contradict it, this page is the one that is true."   sed -n '6p' app/index.html         -> connect-src ... wss: ... (no host restriction)
- **Fix** Safe now, two one-line edits. Replace the operator line with something true and specific — e.g. `can(false, "No support desk, no review, no refund. Some keys do survive: see 'A message from the dev' for who holds them and what they can do.")` with a link to the notice route. Reword the CSP line to what the policy actually does: "Only this build's scripts run. Network access is limited to your node, your wallet, and — if you turn images on — image hosts; the policy itself also permits any wss:// endpoint, so the restriction that matters is which node you name above."
- **Regression test** Extend tools/uitest/ui.mjs's notice assertions to also open Settings and fail if the Security list contains the string "There is no operator", and to assert the Settings copy links to the notice route.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/screens.js, tools/uitest/ui.mjs
- **Already known as** The browser architecture map flags three nfts.js/home.js fee sentences as contradicting notice.js (A8-08, open). These two Settings lines are not among them.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-14 — xswdwallet auto-approves every wallet request and listens on every network interface; its guard checks only the daemon, never the listener or the wallet file

- **Where** `tools/xswdwallet/guard.go:17` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tools/xswdwallet/main.go:55-63 starts the civilware XSWD server with an appHandler that returns true for every application and a requestHandler that returns AlwaysAllow for every method — including `transfer`. vendor/derohe-civilware/walletapi/xswd/xswd.go:204 constructs `&http.Server{Addr: fmt.Sprintf(":%d", port)}`, i.e. 0.0.0.0:44326, and :267 serves it. guardTestOnly (guard.go:17-46) pins only the DAEMON to loopback and requires DERO.GetInfo to report testnet; it says nothing about the listener, and main.go:35 then forces `--testnet`/`--simulator` into globals regardless of what the opened wallet file is. So while the tool runs, any host that can reach port 44326 can spend from the opened wallet without a prompt.
- **Preconditions** The operator runs xswdwallet (the documented way to drive the UI test pass) on a machine reachable by anyone else — a shared LAN, a hotspot, a VM with a bridged adapter.
- **Impact** On the simulator this is play money. It matters because the same tool is the documented headless wallet for UI runs, and deploy/README.md:225 has to warn in prose ("Never point xswdwallet at any of them") about the testnet deployer wallet — which owns the testnet factory and the TELA INDEX. Nothing in code enforces that warning; the guard would happily open it.
- **Exploitability** Network reachability during a test run. No credential needed.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '55,63p' tools/xswdwallet/main.go        -> appHandler returns true; requestHandler returns xswd.AlwaysAllow   grep -n 'http.Server\|ListenAndServe' vendor/derohe-civilware/walletapi/xswd/xswd.go     :204  server := &http.Server{Addr: fmt.Sprintf(":%d", port), Handler: mux}     :267  xswd.server.ListenAndServe()   sed -n '17,46p' tools/xswdwallet/guard.go       -> daemon-only check; no listener check, no wallet-file check   sed -n '83,87p;225p' deploy/README.md           -> the prose warning Not run: I did not start the tool or bind the port.
- **Fix** Safe now: bind the XSWD listener to loopback. The vendored server hard-codes `:port`, so either pass a pre-bound loopback listener (add a NewXSWDServerWithListener to the fork's untracked cmd/xswdwallet shim, or wrap it) or — simplest and dependency-free — have guardTestOnly refuse to start unless the process can first bind 127.0.0.1:44326 itself, and print the actual bind address. Additionally, have the guard refuse any wallet file path that resolves inside .testnet/ or .mainnet/, turning deploy/README.md:225's prose warning into a check.
- **Regression test** A Go test in tools/xswdwallet that calls the guard with a .mainnet/ and a .testnet/ wallet path and asserts it exits; and a startup assertion (covered by the UI pass) that the listener's address is a loopback address.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xswdwallet/guard.go, tools/xswdwallet/main.go, deploy/README.md
- **Already known as** Documented in deploy/README.md:83-87 and in the tool's own header. Not previously traced to the bind address in the vendored server.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-15 — publish.sh passes the GitHub token on the curl command line, where every local process can read it

- **Where** `tools/publish.sh:15` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tools/publish.sh reads the stored git credential into $TOKEN and then invokes `curl -s -H "Authorization: token $TOKEN" …` three times (lines 15, 20, 24). Shell expansion puts the literal token into curl's argv, which is readable by any other process running as the same user, and which lands in shell history if the command is ever retyped.
- **Preconditions** Running the publish script while another local process is watching the process table.
- **Impact** Disclosure of a GitHub credential that can create repositories and push to github.com/NoahMoney. It is the account that publishes the app's source of record, so a stolen token lets an attacker alter what reviewers read. It does not touch any on-chain key.
- **Exploitability** Local, and only during the seconds the script runs. Standard hygiene defect rather than a live hole.
- **Reproduced** yes —   sed -n '11,27p' tools/publish.sh     CRED=$(printf 'protocol=https\nhost=github.com\n\n' | git credential fill)     TOKEN=$(printf '%s\n' "$CRED" | sed -n 's/^password=//p' | head -1)     LOGIN=$(curl -s -H "Authorization: token $TOKEN" https://api.github.com/user | ...)     CODE=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' -H "Authorization: token $TOKEN" "https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO")     R=$(curl -s -H "Authorization: token $TOKEN" ... https://api.github.com/user/repos) Not run — I did not execute publish.sh (it pushes).
- **Fix** Safe now: pass the header on stdin instead of argv, e.g. `curl -s --config - <<<"header = \"Authorization: token $TOKEN\"" …` or write the header to a `mktemp` file with 0600 and use `-H @file`, removed with a trap. Then correct the script header from "the token is never printed" to "the token never reaches stdout, a file or argv".
- **Regression test** A grep in tests/run.sh that fails if any tracked .sh file contains `"Authorization: ` on a curl command line with a variable expansion.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/publish.sh, tests/run.sh
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. The script's own header claims "the token is never printed", which is true of stdout and false of the process table.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### SEC-K-16 — TELA serves each app on the first free port from 8082 upward, so browser origin is not an app identity: DMZ's stored data and the origin the wallet displays can both belong to a different TELA app tomorrow

- **Where** `vendor/tela/tela.go:229` (dimension: Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29))
- **Attack path** tela.FindOpenPort (vendor/tela/tela.go:229-238) starts at DEFAULT_PORT_START = 8082 (:131) and takes the first port it can bind, incrementing. A served app's origin is therefore http://localhost:<whichever port was free>, assigned by order of opening, not by dURL or INDEX SCID. Consequences: (1) localStorage under that origin — veil.settings, veil.trades (up to 500 of the user's own trades with txids and amounts, feed.js:408-409), veil.watch, veil.m.<scid> — is readable and writable by any other TELA app that later occupies the same port; (2) a page on that origin can seed sessionStorage veil.endpointOk and localStorage veil.settings.daemon and then window.open DMZ, which inherits a copy of sessionStorage, defeating the remote-node confirmation gate at app.js:16-17,27; (3) the wallet's own defence is an Origin-vs-app.Url match (vendor/derohe-civilware/walletapi/xswd/xswd.go:445-458) plus the self-reported name — so a different TELA app on the recycled port presents byte-identical name/url to the approval prompt DMZ used.
- **Preconditions** The user opens more than one TELA app, in an order that recycles a port. No attacker code on DMZ's own bundle.
- **Impact** Trade-history disclosure to an unrelated app author, and a bypass of the one gate standing between the user and a hostile node (which SEC-K-03 and the app's own Settings copy identify as able to misreport every listing, price and code check). Bounded by requiring the user to open a hostile TELA app.
- **Exploitability** Requires the user to run a hostile or compromised second TELA app. No remote path.
- **Reproduced** yes —   grep -n 'DEFAULT_PORT_START\|func FindOpenPort' -A 10 vendor/tela/tela.go     :131 const DEFAULT_PORT_START = 8082     :229 func FindOpenPort() ... port := tela.port; for !found && port < max { net.Listen(":"+port) ... port++ }     :1155 link = fmt.Sprintf("http://localhost%s/%s", server.Addr+clone.ServePath, clone.Entrypoint)   sed -n '445,458p' vendor/derohe-civilware/walletapi/xswd/xswd.go   -> Origin must equal app.Url; nothing else identifies the app   grep -n 'veil.trades\|veil.endpointOk\|veil.settings' app/app.js app/feed.js     app/app.js:11,16,17,20 ; app/feed.js:408,409 Not run: I did not serve two TELA apps and recycle a port (see notPerformed).
- **Fix** App, safe now, partial: namespace every storage key by the deployment's factory SCID (e.g. `veil.<factory8>.trades`) so a different deployment or app on a recycled port reads nothing; and make veil.trades opt-in, since it is the only stored item with real disclosure value. Neither closes the endpoint-gate inheritance — for that, move the confirmation out of sessionStorage into an in-memory flag that a fresh page load always clears (an inherited sessionStorage copy then buys nothing). Docs/copy, safe now: the Settings 'This device' section should say that TELA serves apps on shared localhost ports, so another TELA app may share this storage. Upstream: the real fix is TELA serving each dURL on a stable, distinct origin — a note for civilware, not a change here.
- **Regression test** A JS test under tests/js that seeds sessionStorage veil.endpointOk with a remote wss URL, boots the app's connectNode logic against a stub, and asserts the remote endpoint is NOT used until confirmEndpoint is called in this page's own lifetime.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/app.js, app/feed.js, app/stats.js, app/screens.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** The browser architecture map raises same-origin storage sharing generically, and DMZ has already hardened against it (feed.js:168, app.js:14-15, xswd.js:10-11 all name the risk). The port-rotation mechanism that makes the origin non-app-specific is not recorded anywhere, and it defeats the XSWD origin check the wallet relies on.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-08 — Cross-quote thresholds and rankings are still value-blind: qscale exists as a hook and no vault populates it, so "thin" is 20 DERO on one market and 20 XMR on another and the hot ranking under-ranks every wrapped market

- **Where** `app/stats.js:41` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** qscale(s) (stats.js:41) returns s.quoteScale when finite and 1 otherwise. Nothing sets s.quoteScale: nameQuote (feed.js:101-113) copies sym, dec and legacy from the vault record and app/config.js:11-16 has no quoteScale on any of the four vault entries. So qscale is 1 for every market on every deployment. m.thin = m.liq < 20 * qscale(s) (stats.js:82) and H.risks' thin row (home.js:32, a bare `s.m.liq < 20`) therefore compare a count of whole quote units against 20, whatever the quote is. m.hot's volume term is Math.log1p(f1.vol / qscale(s)) * 8 (stats.js:80) with f1.vol also in whole quote units. markets.js:160 prints and markets.js:70 sorts m.vol24, m.liq and m.mcap as bare numbers across quotes.
- **Preconditions** Any deployment with both DERO-quoted and wrapped-quoted markets — i.e. the intended one.
- **Impact** Two directions. Over-warning: a wXMR market with 19 XMR of depth (thousands of dollars) is labelled "Thin market: about 19 XMR of liquidity. Small orders move the price a lot." on the token page and inside the trade sheet, so the pill that is supposed to mean something is on almost every XMR market and users learn to ignore it — which is the failure mode that matters, because a genuinely thin market then carries a warning nobody reads. Under-ranking: the hot score's volume term needs 517 whole quote units to saturate, so a busy Monero market scores below a quiet DERO one, on the very screens requirement 1 says must put XMR and ZEC first. Sorting Markets by Liquidity, Volume or FDV ranks a 1,000-DERO market above a 20-XMR one.
- **Exploitability** Not an attack; a systematic misreading. It amplifies ADV-PRICE-04: a DERO market's dust volume out-ranks a wrapped market's real volume.
- **Reproduced** no — Verified by grep, not executed. `grep -n "quoteScale|scale" app/config.js docs/BACKLOG.md` returns nothing in config.js and only an unrelated line 367 in the backlog; app/config.js:12-15 shows all four vault records and none carries quoteScale. app/stats.js:41, :80, :82 and app/home.js:32 read as quoted above. The cross-quote sum's fix confirmed at app/home.js:77 (flowsOf groups by D.qsym) and app/home.js:213-214 ("Flow 24h" prints the largest quote with the rest beneath it, never a total).
- **Fix** Not safe to land blind: the value of quoteScale is an operator decision — it is a claim about what one XMR is worth in the units the ranking treats as comparable, and DMZ deliberately has no oracle (BL-106). Two honest options, and the operator must pick: (a) put a static quoteScale in each vaults[] record with the disclosure that it is a fixed number set at publication, not a rate; or (b) make every threshold relative and drop cross-quote sorting — express "thin" as depth against the market's own 24h volume (FINDINGS.md already suggests exactly this), rank hot within each quote and interleave, and either split the Markets table's Liquidity/Volume/FDV sorts per quote or label them as within-quote only. Option (b) needs no number nobody can justify and is the one consistent with having no oracle.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js that builds two fixture markets with identical reserves at 5 and 12 decimals and asserts they receive the same thin verdict and the same hot rank — it fails today and passes under either fix.
- **Safe to fix now** no — needs a decision or a redeploy policy · files: app/stats.js, app/home.js, app/markets.js, app/config.js, tools/genconfig/main.go, deploy/networks.json, tests/js
- **Already known as** Known and open: DMZ_OPUS5_MASSIVE_AUDIT DEFECT 11 (merging D3-06, P7-08, D3-11 thresholds, RT-11/D3-05), FINDINGS.md around line 1702, and docs/TODO.md §1.3 ("cross-quote ranking is still wrong"). What has changed since is that stats.js now has a qscale() hook and comments explaining the intent — the behaviour has not changed, because app/config.js's vaults[] records carry scid/name/sym/chain/dec/conf/legacy and no quoteScale field. The genuine cross-quote SUM the audit named (home.js:179) HAS been fixed — home.js:77 flowsOf() now groups by quote and the Trade hub prints one figure per quote. So the backlog is accurate; this is a re-verification that the ranking half is untouched.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-09 — The Trade hub and the trade sheet's market switcher rank over active() rather than ranked(), so the thin half of a split pair can sit above the contract that holds the liquidity

- **Where** `app/home.js:195` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** feed.js:389 defines ranked() = active().filter(s => !s.splitWith || s.splitDeepest), and trending() and movers() use it, so Home's trending list, the price tape and Gainers are all correctly filtered. Two ranked surfaces bypass it: home.js:195 (`const list = D.active().sort((a, b) => b.m.hot - a.m.hot)`, rendered as the Trade hub's "Markets · Ordered by activity · pick a side to open the trade sheet") and token.js:224 (the "Choose a market" switcher inside the trade sheet, `D.active().sort((a, b) => b.m.hot - a.m.hot).slice(0, 30)`). Both are hot-ordered, and hot is manufacturable for pennies (ADV-PRICE-04), so a thin duplicate can be pushed to the top of both.
- **Preconditions** Two contracts trading the same base against the same quote — a second pool over a launch's token, or a second pool over the same wrapped asset. Anyone can create one: pool.bas accepts any asset SCID (F.pool in forms.js takes it as a text field) and the factory has no dedup (BL-105).
- **Impact** Bounded, because the row is marked: named() (home.js:62) and the switcher row both render H.mark(s), whose first warning for a non-deepest split member is "Split pair · thinner", and opening the sheet shows H.risks' full split paragraph naming the deeper contract's SCID. So the user is told — but on the one screen whose controls are Buy and Sell, the thin contract can sit at position 1 above the deep one, which is the ordering BL-100 exists to prevent.
- **Exploitability** Cheap: create the duplicate pool (install + 0.1 DERO regfee), seed it thinly, and buy hot with dust.
- **Reproduced** no — Code-traced, not rendered. `grep -n "D.active().sort" app/home.js app/token.js` returns exactly home.js:195 and token.js:224; feed.js:389-391 shows ranked() and its two users. The warning path confirmed at home.js:62 (named() includes H.mark), ui.js:110 (the "Split pair · thinner" pill is second in warnings(), after impersonation) and home.js:24-29 (H.risks' split row).
- **Fix** Local, one word each: use D.ranked() in place of D.active() at home.js:195 and token.js:224, exactly as feed.js:386-389's comment already says the rule is. The thin half stays reachable on Markets, marked, beside the contract that holds the liquidity — which is where feed.js says it belongs.
- **Regression test** A Node test in tests/js over two fixture markets sharing a pairKey with different depths, asserting that the list each of those two call sites builds contains only the deepest; plus a UI-pass assertion that the Trade hub's first row is never a market carrying the "Split pair · thinner" mark.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/home.js, app/token.js, tests/js
- **Already known as** BL-100 (A) "Split pairs everywhere … audit every surface (home, markets, trade sheet, token page, portfolio, share cards) so the thin half never ranks". These are two of the surfaces BL-100 asks for and they are still unfiltered. The split-pair machinery itself is correct and complete elsewhere.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### ADV-PRICE-10 — A graduated launch has no liquidity providers and no withdrawal path, but the trade sheet tells the trader the fee "stays in the pool, for its liquidity providers"

- **Where** `app/token.js:173` (dimension: price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5))
- **Attack path** token.js:173 branches only on `curve` (launch && phase == 0). For a graduated launch — phase 1, the constant-product branch — it prints "The rest of the trading fee stays in the pool, for its liquidity providers." launch.bas has no AddLiquidity and no RemoveLiquidity: its reserves are created at graduation (buyto lines 71-73 set rd = real and ra = Lq) and there is no entrypoint that pays them out. token.js:117 correctly refuses to offer the liquidity sheet for a launch (`if (!launch) actions.push(...)`), so the app knows there are no LPs; the cost-block sentence does not.
- **Preconditions** Any graduated launch. Every trade sheet opened on one shows it.
- **Impact** A statement about where the trader's money goes that is false in both halves: there are no liquidity providers on a launch AMM, and the fee that "stays in the pool" is locked forever — nobody, including the creator, can withdraw the reserves. The only value that ever leaves them is the creator's tax under tmode 1 (ClaimTax, launch.bas:266-277, up to 10% of the quote leg of every trade including post-graduation sells) and the Buyback path, which moves quote in and locks tokens. Under requirement 3 the sheet is meant to say where each cost goes; here it names a beneficiary that does not exist and implies a withdrawable pool that is not. It also matters for the question of who supplies liquidity and what happens when it disappears: on a launch AMM nobody supplies it and it cannot disappear — a genuinely good property that the copy hides.
- **Exploitability** No attacker. A wrong claim shown to every trader on every graduated launch.
- **Reproduced** no — Code-traced. app/token.js:173 renders the sentence on the non-curve branch; `curve` is defined at token.js:142 as `launch && s.phase === Z`. contracts/launch.bas has no AddLiquidity/RemoveLiquidity (its entrypoint list is the four swaps plus ClaimFees, ClaimTax, Buyback, Distribute, Stake, Unstake, Claim). app/token.js:117 gates the liquidity sheet on `!launch`, confirming the app's own model.
- **Fix** Local, one sentence in app/token.js:173. Split the non-curve branch by kind: for a pool keep the existing wording; for a graduated launch say what is true — "The rest of the trading fee is added to the market's reserves. A launch AMM has no liquidity providers and no withdrawal: the reserves stay in the contract permanently" — and, where the launch's tmode is 1, add that the creator's tax is the one path that takes value out of the quote leg (which H.risks already reports separately for tax > 500 bps; it applies at any non-zero tax).
- **Regression test** A UI-pass assertion (tools/uitest/ui.mjs) that the trade sheet on a phase-1 launch does not contain the string "liquidity providers", and does contain the permanence sentence; plus a Node test that the cost-block copy selector returns the launch wording for phase 1 and the pool wording for a pool.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/token.js, tools/uitest/ui.mjs
- **Already known as** Not in the backlog. Adjacent to BL-103 (LP position statement) and to PROD·P7-05 (the creator's ClaimTax drain of the locked AMM), neither of which names this sentence. New.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### DOC-1 — docs/NFA.md cites the wrong sha256 for the upstream standard, so the diff it asks a reviewer to reproduce cannot be reproduced

- **Where** `docs/NFA.md:6` (dimension: Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12)
- **Attack path** docs/NFA.md:5-6 and again at :560-561 give the upstream ART-NFA-MS1.bas sha256 as a5e4ad654acb92bdca8d7e951173d6dae2f4f75f55772b9bedaabbac8499b174. contracts/nfa.bas:6 gives 6c42bb7eae4c44d7b39c0aeb5f184338449b4225ea2a925e0eb539783d8115b1 (13,184 bytes, git blob 5aca6b91). I fetched the file and the contract header is right; the design note is wrong. It is not a line-ending variant: the CRLF form hashes to 365d75d5..., and git hash-object gives 5aca6b91 exactly as the contract claims.
- **Preconditions** A reviewer following docs/NFA.md, which is the page the repo offers as the read-before-you-diff page for this contract.
- **Impact** The provenance of the one template DMZ inherits wholesale from a third party cannot be checked against the document that claims to establish it, and a future upstream change would be indistinguishable from a transcription error.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes — curl -sL -o /tmp/ms1.bas https://raw.githubusercontent.com/civilware/artificer-nfa-standard/main/ART-NFA-MS1/ART-NFA-MS1.bas wc -c /tmp/ms1.bas      -> 13184 sha256sum /tmp/ms1.bas  -> 6c42bb7eae4c44d7b39c0aeb5f184338449b4225ea2a925e0eb539783d8115b1 git hash-object /tmp/ms1.bas -> 5aca6b91... sed 's/$/\r/' /tmp/ms1.bas | sha256sum -> 365d75d5...   (so a5e4ad65 is not the CRLF form either) grep -n "a5e4ad65" docs/NFA.md -> lines 6 and 561.
- **Fix** Doc-only, safe now: replace both occurrences with 6c42bb7eae4c44d7b39c0aeb5f184338449b4225ea2a925e0eb539783d8115b1 and note the git blob 5aca6b91 as contracts/nfa.bas:6 already does, so the two agree.
- **Regression test** Add the upstream file to tests/testdata with its hash and a Go test asserting the hash appears in both contracts/nfa.bas and docs/NFA.md — tests/templates_test.go is the existing model for checking a document against the tree. Without a pinned copy the test would need network access, which the suite does not have.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: docs/NFA.md
- **Already known as** New.

### FE-12 — Three calls skip the gas estimator with a hard-coded fee, and a reverted call is reported to the user as included

- **Where** `app/nfts.js:46 (fee 4000), app/nfts.js:99 (fee 20000), app/nft.js:49 (fee 2000); app/app.js:163-171:99` (dimension: TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35))
- **Attack path** Those three actions pass `fee` to app.tx, which skips the DERO.GetGasEstimate branch entirely (app.js:130-141). If the real storage gas exceeds the constant - PayBatch(15) already consumes 6.81M of the 10M compute budget per the DVM measurements, and any DVM gas repricing moves the storage side - the call is mined and panics with Insufficient Storage Gas, spending the fee. app.tx's "reverted" state is only computed for installs (app.js:163 checks `w.state === "reverted"`, which waitTx only ever returns when `install` is true). PayBatch and Distribute in nfts.js carry no `outcome` and no `probe`, so `known` stays false, `executed` stays true, and the user is told "Push rewards included in block N".
- **Preconditions** Storage gas above the constant, e.g. after a DVM repricing or on a contract with more holders than the constant was measured against.
- **Impact** The user pays and is told it worked. No loss beyond the fee, but the app's own careful outcome-probe design (app.js:164-171, "inclusion is not execution") is switched off for exactly the calls that most need it.
- **Exploitability** Not attacker-triggered; it is a latent misreport that arrives with a gas repricing or a larger holder set.
- **Reproduced** no — $ grep -n 'fee: 4000\|fee: 20000\|fee: 2000' app/nfts.js app/nft.js app/nfts.js:46 ... fee: 4000 ... app/nfts.js:99 ... entry: "PayBatch", args: [["from","U",0n],["n","U",15n]], fee: 20000, describe: "Push rewards"   # no probe, no outcome app/nft.js:49 ... fee: 2000 ... $ sed -n '163p;166,171p' app/app.js   # "reverted" is install-only; without a probe, executed defaults to true Reasoned only: I did not force an out-of-gas PayBatch on the simulator (needs a wallet).
- **Fix** Give each of the three a probe with a `check` (PayBatch: assert `paid` increased; Distribute: assert `rpool` or `und` moved; the Mint calls already have a `minted` probe and should drop the constant). Then try the estimator first and fall back to the constant only when the estimator refuses, as the install path already does.
- **Regression test** tests/js - stub V.node/W, run app.tx for a call with a fixed fee and an unchanged probe, and assert the toast is the "did not execute" one rather than "included".
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: app/nfts.js, app/nft.js, app/app.js
- **Already known as** New; adjacent to R-16 in RISKS.md, which is about the estimator failing on installs rather than about deliberately bypassing it.
- **Refuter verdicts** UNVERIFIED (the refuters for this dimension did not run)

### XMR-12 — Machine-specific paths in eight tracked repository files

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/proof.go:25` (dimension: The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44)
- **Attack path** Not an attack. `grep -rn "/home/<user>\|/mnt/c/Users/<user>" tools/ docs/` finds the operator's own WSL and Windows paths in: tools/xmrswap/proof.go:25, tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go:46, tools/xmrswap/proof_test.go:202, tools/simhtlc/main.go:7 and :48, tools/simhtlc/run.log:2, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md:174, :317 and :318. proof_test.go:202 is the one that executes: TestProofXMRBinary silently skips on any machine where that exact path does not answer keygen, so the only test of the real prover is green-by-absence everywhere but this laptop.
- **Preconditions** None.
- **Impact** A published repository names the author's home directory and drive layout, and the prover's only end-to-end test is a no-op for everyone else — a reviewer cannot tell a skip from a pass.
- **Exploitability** n/a.
- **Reproduced** yes — cd <repo> && grep -rn "/home/<user>\|/mnt/c/Users/<user>" tools/xmrswap/ tools/simhtlc/ docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md — eight hits, listed above.
- **Fix** Replace every occurrence with a placeholder ('<path to proof-xmr>' in docs and flag help; $XMRSWAP_PROVER or 'proof-xmr' on PATH in code). proof_test.go should skip only when XMRSWAP_PROVER is unset and 'proof-xmr' is not on PATH, and should say loudly that the real-prover test did not run.
- **Regression test** A repository-wide test (or a line in tests/run.sh) that greps tracked files for absolute user paths and fails.
- **Safe to fix now** yes · files: tools/xmrswap/proof.go, tools/xmrswap/proof_test.go, tools/xmrswap/cmd/xmrswap/main.go, tools/simhtlc/main.go, docs/XMR_SWAP_BUILD.md

## Added by refuters (16)

### MISS-PRIV-A — DERO's ring construction pins the sender and the receiver to opposite index parities, and the proof publishes which parity the sender is in — so private mode's anonymity set is 8, not the "one of 16" the app states in three places [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `app/screens.js:272`
- **Attack path** The wallet builds a ring by placing the sender at witness_index[0] and the receiver at witness_index[1], then shuffling — but under a constraint (vendor/derohe/walletapi/transaction_build.go:79-89): it re-shuffles until witness_index[0]%2 != witness_index[1]%2, i.e. until the sender and receiver land on opposite index parities. Which parity the sender landed on is not secret: vendor/derohe/cryptography/crypto/proof_generate.go:76 defines Proof.Parity() as a single predicate over the public proof element p.f.Element(0), and vendor/derohe/blockchain/blockchain/transaction_execute.go:430-437 uses exactly that bit to name the signer of a ring-2 call — `if (i%2 == 0) == parity`. Extract_signer only APPLIES the rule at RingSize == 2, but the bit itself is present in, and computable from, every transaction at every ring size. So for any ring-16 DERO transaction an observer deserialises the transaction, reads one boolean, and discards the eight ring members on the wrong parity. The payer is one of 8. No knowledge of the receiver is needed and knowing the receiver adds nothing, because the receiver is by construction in the class already discarded. DMZ's private mode (app/app.js:116, ring 16, used by app.trade whenever a destination is named, and by doReclaim) inherits this in full, and the app states the unreduced figure in three places.
- **Impact** The app's only privacy mechanism delivers half the anonymity it claims. app/screens.js:218 says "the payer is one of sixteen", app/screens.js:272 says "the payer is one of 16 accounts", app/token.js:240 says "the wallet hides the payer among 16 accounts" — the true figure is 8 in all three. On a product sold to Monero and Zcash holders, on the one screen where a user decides whether the privacy is worth the extra gas, a stated anonymity figure that is 2x the real one is a misrepresentation, not a rounding. It also silently doubles the effectiveness of any intersection attack layered on top: combined with PRIV-01's exact per-block trade sizes, an analyst correlating a known holder's balance movements against private trades needs half as many observations to converge. And because RING_PRIVACY.md §7 is the document the operator would hand a reviewer, the wrong figure is what a reviewer would check against.
- **Fix** Two parts, and unlike PRIV-07 the second one is cheap enough to do. (a) Copy, safe now: replace "one of 16 accounts" / "one of sixteen" / "among 16 accounts" with the true figure and its reason — "the payer is one of 8: DERO puts the sender and the receiver on opposite halves of the ring and the transaction's proof says which half the sender is in, so half the ring is excluded by anyone who looks." Same correction to the code comment at app/app.js:198 and to research/RING_PRIVACY.md §7. Do NOT ship PRIV-07's "15 in practice" wording — it replaces one wrong number with another. (b) Mechanism, and it is nearly free: send private mode at ring 32 instead of 16 (app/app.js:116, widening the ring === 16 test to an allow-list of 16/32/64). Ring 32 gives 16 real candidates, i.e. what the app has been claiming all along. research/RING_PRIVACY.md §6.4 already measured the cost on this simulator — 4,240 bytes against 3,336, and 23 s against 22 s to confirmation, with gas unchanged, since DERO's fee is size-based and the wallet draws decoys from the base tree which always has them. That is a one-line change plus a simulator run, not a contract change and not a redeploy. If the operator prefers not to widen the ring, the copy must say 8.

### PRIV-M1 — DERO's ring hides half of what the app says it hides: Proof.Parity() is public at every ring size, so private mode's anonymity set is 8 of 16, for any observer, not 16 and not 15 [HIGH]

- **Where** `app/screens.js:272`
- **Attack path** An observer fetches any DMZ private-mode transaction with DERO.GetTransaction. The daemon hands back a `ring` field listing all 16 member addresses in order, and `txs_as_hex`. The observer deserialises the payload and calls Proof.Parity() — a public method over bytes already in the proof (vendor/derohe/cryptography/crypto/proof_generate.go:76-83: it returns true iff f.Element(0) == 0). In proof generation the blinding scalars aa[0] and aa[m] are hard-set to zero (:515-521), witness_index is reverse(binary(Index[1]) ++ binary(Index[0])) so ba[0] is the least-significant bit of the SENDER's ring index (:523-533), and proof.f = b.Times(w).Add(a) (:797). Therefore f[0] = ba[0]·w, and f[0] == 0 exactly when the sender's ring index is even. The observer keeps the 8 ring members whose index parity matches and discards the other 8. Nothing about this depends on the ring size: blockchain/transaction_execute.go:424-443 applies the identical test and is capped at RingSize == 2 only by an `if` in that helper, not by the cryptography. The node-supplied burn destination that PRIV-07 blames is irrelevant — transaction_build.go:407-414 forces the sender and receiver into opposite parity classes, so the destination is always in the half parity already eliminated.
- **Impact** The app states a privacy figure that is wrong by a factor of two on the one feature sold as privacy. app/screens.js:216 'Private mode: ring 16 · pays a named address', app/screens.js:272 'the payer is one of 16 accounts' and app/token.js:240 'the wallet hides the payer among 16 accounts' are all overstatements; the true figure is one of 8. app/token.js:172's '16 · the payer is hidden' is half true — the CONTRACT genuinely never learns the payer (SIGNER() is 33 zero bytes above ring 2, which research/RING_PRIVACY.md §1 measured), but an OBSERVER's set is 8. For an audience choosing DMZ over a transparent venue specifically to be one of many, a stated anonymity set that is double the real one is the kind of claim the directive's hard rule about trust assumptions exists to prevent. It also silently halves the value of any future decision to raise the ring.
- **Fix** Copy first, safe now, and it is the half that must not wait, because the current text is a false number: change app/screens.js:216, app/screens.js:272 and app/token.js:240 from 'one of 16' / 'among 16 accounts' to 'one of 8 — the ring has 16 members, and a public bit in the proof tells any observer which half the payer is in'. Leave app/token.js:172's '16 · the payer is hidden' only if it is qualified in the same breath; the contract half of that sentence is true and worth keeping distinct from the observer half. Record the measurement in research/RING_PRIVACY.md — §3 and §7 both need it, and §7's 'one of N accounts instead of one' becomes 'one of N/2'. Add a BL number. What is NOT safe now and is an operator decision: nothing in this repository can fix the leak — it is DERO consensus-level proof structure, not DMZ code. The only lever DMZ has is the ring size, and it now costs twice what the size suggests: ring 32 buys an anonymity set of 16 at 4,240 bytes (research/ringprobe-sizes.log). Whether to raise the default from 16 to 32 is a size-versus-privacy call for the operator, and it should be taken with the corrected arithmetic in front of them.
