Do not apply PRIV-07's proposed wording, which would replace one wrong number with another.

### PRIV-M2 — The vault publishes every deposit's foreign-chain transaction id, output index, amount and height against the depositor's DERO address — in the clear, with no viewing key involved, and no screen says so [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `contracts/vault.bas:362`
- **Attack path** contracts/vault.bas Mint (:313-367) is called by k signers once a foreign deposit is seen. Line 26 builds `id = HEX(txid) + ":" + vout` and lines 62-65 store `mint_<id>` = 1, `mh_<id>` = block height, `mj_<id>` = j and `ma_<id>` = amount. The key ITSELF carries the foreign transaction id, so it is readable by anyone through DERO.GetSC with variables:true — no argument decoding needed. `mj_<id>` gives the deposit index j; `ua_<j>` (stored by Register at :264-284) gives the depositor's DERO address; `ul_<addr>_<c>` and `uc_<addr>` enumerate every index that address owns. One RPC call therefore yields, for every deposit ever made: (Monero or Zcash transaction id, output index, exact amount, DERO height, depositor's DERO wallet). On Monero that txid is looked up on any public explorer and gives the deposit transaction's decoy ring, key images and timing; on transparent Zcash it gives the funding address outright. From the DERO side, the same wallet is then the one that received the wrapped units (Mint line 66 sends them to ua_<j>) and traded them — and, per PRIV-01, its trades are attributable. Refunded (:411-427) does the same for deposits that were sent back: `rf_<id>` = the foreign refund txid.
- **Impact** An XMR or ZEC holder who wraps through DMZ has the specific foreign-chain transaction that funded their position published on DERO, bound to their named DERO wallet, forever. That is the strongest cross-chain link in the system and it is created by the ordinary happy path, not by an optional transparency feature. The wrap walkthrough at app/wrap.js:286 was recently corrected to disclose the DERO-side linkage well — 'the vault stores which DERO address owns every index and the foreign address attested to it, in the clear' — and stops exactly one clause short: it never says the foreign TRANSACTION is recorded too. The app already prints the foreign settlement txid for a WITHDRAWAL (wrap.js:452, deliberately, 'printed in full because…'), so the pattern is disclosed on the way out and silent on the way in. There is also a hard-rule dimension: 'document every trusted party' is satisfied, but a depositor cannot weigh the federation against the alternatives without knowing this is the cost of using it.
- **Fix** Copy, and it is safe now — one clause added to a sentence that is otherwise already good. Extend app/wrap.js:286 to end: '…so anyone reading the contract can link all of yours together — including the transaction id, output and exact amount of every deposit on ' + chain + ', which the vault records against your index when the signers mint. Treat a deposit as public on both chains.' Add the same fact to the deposit walkthrough step BEFORE the Register button, not after it, since Register is where the user commits the index to their address. deploy/VAULT_PARAMETERS.md and research/wrapper/README.md must both record it next to the reserve-proof discussion, because it is the reason a viewing key adds little (see my PRIV-02 verdict) and the reason a depositor's privacy cost is fixed by the design rather than by a federation policy choice. NOT safe now, and an operator decision: removing the leak means not storing the foreign txid on DERO at all — e.g. storing HEX(SHA256(txid + salt)) so the id still deduplicates a deposit but does not name it, with the salt held by the signers. That preserves Mint's one-deposit-one-id property (line 27's EXISTS check) and the double-mint defence, at the cost of making the reserve unauditable from DERO alone by anyone who does not hold the salt. It is a vault redeploy and it trades a real transparency property for a real privacy one; put both halves to the operator rather than choosing.

### FEE-M1 — DERO deposited below the anti-sweep floor has no exit at all: if the registry never reaches `minhst` the whole pool is frozen forever, with no owner, no expiry and no refund [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `contracts/founders.bas:260`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a construction defect. distribute() is the only writer of `und` (contracts/founders.bas:258 and :260; `grep -n '\"und\"' contracts/founders.bas` returns exactly lines 41, 243, 258, 260). Below the floor every deposit takes the GOTO 90 branch at :246 and is added to `und`. The ONLY path out is a later distribute() call taken when hst >= minhst. There is no refund entrypoint, no owner, no timelock, no expiry, and Distribute() (:149-154) succeeds and moves nothing. So if the registry never reaches minhst — passes lost, keys lost, holders simply never registering enough units, or a pass that is unpopular — every DERO ever deposited is permanently unreachable by anyone, including its depositor.
- **Impact** Permanent loss of the entire accumulated fee stream, to nobody. It is the exact shape the directive's liveness-by-construction rule forbids: a state the system cannot leave without a human — here, without enough third parties choosing to register — and unlike a lease or a timelock it never lapses. It also silently falsifies the accounting card, which shows the frozen amount under 'Waiting for the first registration' as if it were pending rather than potentially unrecoverable. Note the interaction with FEE-02: the same pool is either swept by a late whale or frozen forever; there is no outcome in which it reaches the holders who earned it.
- **Fix** Do not add a rescue path — add a bound. The minimal correct change is the one C9 already specified and did not ship: make the release LINEAR rather than gated, i.e. keep `IF hst == 0 THEN GOTO 90` (so distribute cannot divide by zero) and delete the `minhst` threshold, releasing to whoever is registered at the time. That closes this finding and FEE-02's frozen-backlog at once, at the cost of re-opening FEE-01's narrow first-registrant window — which is the correct trade, because that window has no victim (nobody is registered) whereas this one loses the money outright. Do NOT delete the `hst == 0` branch as FEE-01/FEE-02 propose: distribute() then PANICs (see my FEE-02 verdict). If the floor must stay, it needs an expiry — e.g. store the height of the first deposit into `und` and release to whoever is registered once BLOCK_HEIGHT() exceeds it by a fixed number of blocks, regardless of hst. Either is a distribution-policy change on a hashed template: operator decision, then contracts/founders.bas + app/templates/founders.bas + app/config.js templates.founders.sha256 + redeploy. Safe and useful NOW, with no contract change: app/nfts.js must stop calling the frozen amount 'Waiting for the first registration' and say what it is — '<und> DERO cannot be released until <minhst> passes are registered (<hst> are); there is no other way out of this pool.'

### FEE-M2 — The Founders Pass screen never discloses that up to 20% of the editions were minted free to the installer — a buyer sees only a `minted` count that silently includes them [LOW]

- **Where** `app/nfts.js:40`
- **Attack path** contracts/founders.bas:45 sets `minted = reserve` at install and :61 sends those editions to the signer for nothing (`SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(s, reserve, SCID())`), bounded only by reserve <= maxsupply/5 at :23. app/founders.js:13 already fetches `reserve` into `f`. app/nfts.js renders `stat("Minted", f.minted + " / " + f.maxsupply)` at :40 and '<maxsupply - minted> of <maxsupply> left at <price> each' at :38 — and `grep -n reserve app/nfts.js` returns nothing. So the one number a buyer uses to judge scarcity and their pro-rata share silently includes editions nobody paid for.
- **Impact** A buyer paying the mint price for a pro-rata claim on the fee stream cannot see from the screen that a fifth of that claim was given away, nor who holds it. It breaks the directive's third standing requirement (fees and costs obvious before acting) at the one place money changes hands for the instrument, and it compounds FEE-12: at 20 editions the installer's 4 free passes are 20% of the fee stream and also, at minhst 1, the natural first registrant.
- **Fix** One row and one clause, both local, no contract or config change, and the data is already loaded. app/nfts.js:40 — add `stat("Free to the creator", f.reserve + " / " + f.maxsupply)` to the stats block, or a kv row in accounting(); and change the note at :38 to '<maxsupply - minted> of <maxsupply> left at <price> each; <reserve> were minted to the creator at install and paid nothing'. app/nfts.js is 6628 of the 18432 DOC cap, so there is ample room.

### FEE-M3 — The Founders Pass is the only Verified contract in the app with no install-parameter check and no treasury check — the code hash alone is the whole badge [INFO]

- **Where** `app/founders.js:9`
- **Attack path** Every other verified kind gets a second check beyond the code hash. app/feed.js:224 rejects any market whose `treasury` is not one the factory records ('treasury differs from the factory'). app/feed.js:228-229 rejects a pool or launch whose `fee`/`pfee` differ from the protocol's, with the comment 'the template fixes the code, not its install parameters: a market with the right code and treasury but a fee of 0 … would look Verified without this check'. app/feed.js:279 rejects an NFA whose `protoFee` differs. app/founders.js:9-26 loadFounders calls this.verify(scid) for the code hash and then renders — it never checks `share`, `mfee`, `price`, `maxsupply` or `reserve` against anything, and never checks that the pass's immutable `treasury` (which receives every mint payment, founders.bas:71) is the factory's treasury.
- **Impact** The single contract that IS the fee promise carries the app's weakest verification. A mainnet install with share 0, an out-of-policy mfee, a reserve at the 20% cap, or a treasury pointing somewhere other than the factory's would render with the same Verified badge and the same 'the promise is stored in the contract and cannot be changed' copy. Combined with FEE-06's legacy list and FEE-12's undocumented mainnet parameters, the app has no check that would catch the exact deployment error FEE-12 warns about.
- **Fix** Add the same class of check loadFounders is missing, using values the app already has: after verify(), reject (or badge as unverified, matching feed.js's `untrusted` convention) a pass whose `treasury` is not in `this.registry.treasuries`, and — once FEE-12's operator decision writes the mainnet parameters into deploy/networks.json — whose `share`, `mfee` and `maxsupply` differ from the deployment's recorded values, which genconfig can emit into app/config.js beside the SCID. app/founders.js is 1840 of 18432 so the budget is not a constraint. The `share`/`maxsupply` half depends on FEE-12 landing first; the treasury half is safe and useful immediately.

### XMR-R1 — A transient failure to OPEN the joint spend wallet — after the sweep intent is durable and before SweepAll is ever called — is just as terminal as a failed sweep, and the outcome it writes states a falsehood [HIGH]

- **Where** `tools/xmrswap/swap.go:558`
- **Attack path** sweep() (swap.go:552-570) in `case "sweep_ready"` does three things in this order: save("sweeping") — the durable intent — then OpenWallet(walletName("spend")) — then SweepAll. If OpenWallet returns an error, sweep() returns it and the phase on disk is already "sweeping". The very next Step re-enters `case "sweep_ready", "sweeping"` (swap.go:800 for Bob, swap.go:1188 for Alice's refund recovery), sweep() takes `case "sweeping"` (swap.go:571) and immediately calls finish("sweep_unknown", "restarted during the sweep: it was attempted once and is not attempted again..."), setting Done=true forever.  Two things are wrong. (1) The `sweeping` branch exists to detect a RESTART — a process that woke up mid-sweep, where the payment genuinely may have gone out. It fires just as readily inside the SAME process that never got past open_wallet, because the branch tests the phase and never consults s.resumedPhase (which the same file uses for exactly this distinction at swap.go:658 for dero_locking and swap.go:1071 for xmr_locking). (2) The outcome recorded and shown to the operator — "restarted during the sweep: it was attempted once" — is false on both counts: there was no restart and no attempt. An operator reading it will believe a Monero transaction may be in flight when none was ever built.  Any ordinary transport error reaches this: the wallet-rpc restarted, a socket reset, the per-call timeout, a wallet-dir not configured (XMR-4), or a file lock while the wallet-rpc is autosaving the wallet generate_from_keys just wrote.
- **Impact** Bob has already lost his DERO to Alice's irreversible Claim; his XMR stays at the joint address and the companion is permanently Done. Alice's refund-recovery sweep is stranded the same way. Recovery is by hand from the plaintext joint spend key in the state file — the directive's LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION rule says any state that needs a human to leave it is a defect, and this one is reached by a dropped TCP connection.
- **Fix** Two changes in tools/xmrswap/swap.go, both local. (1) Move the durable intent to the last moment before the irreversible call: open the wallet FIRST, and save("sweeping") only immediately before SweepAll, so a failure to open leaves the phase at sweep_ready and the next pass retries — nothing was spent, so nothing needs protecting. (2) Make the `sweeping` branch mean what its comment says by gating it on s.resumedPhase == "sweeping" (the pattern already used at swap.go:658 and swap.go:1071); a `sweeping` phase reached inside this process without a SweepAll call is not an unknown and must not be reported as one. Correct the outcome string so it distinguishes "attempted, result unknown" from "never attempted". No contract change, no operator decision; testable entirely against the fake wallet.

### MISS-01 — #wrap/<any-scid> renders any v3 vault contract as the configured XMR vault — wrong asset, wrong decimals, wrong name, and the full deposit walkthrough — with no membership check on the route parameter [HIGH]

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:40`
- **Attack path** 1. app/wrap.js:196 accepts any 64-hex string as the route parameter with no membership test: `const picked = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(String(param || "")) ? param : null;` 2. app/wrap.js:33-42 loadVault(pick) sets `scid = pick` unconditionally and asks the node for it. Its only guards are `kind === "vault"` (line 39) and `w.v !== 3n` (line 42). Both are properties of the contract at that address, not of the app's configuration. 3. app/wrap.js:40 then resolves the asset record from `list.find((v) => v.scid === scid) || this.cfg.vaultAsset || {}`, where `list` is `vaults()` — which app/feed.js:49 filters to non-legacy configured vaults. Any scid not in that list falls through to `cfg.vaultAsset`, which app/config.js:18 fixes at name Monero / sym XMR / 12 decimals / 10 confirmations. 4. Every figure and every label on the screen is then drawn from the wrong record: the h1 ('Deposit & withdraw XMR'), the settlement row, the chain sentence, `amt()` scaling for Claims outstanding / Mint cap / Headroom, the minimum-deposit warnbox, the burn form's decimals, and the whole five-step walkthrough ending 'the vault mints you the same number of base units'. 5. Two ways to reach it. (a) Benign but live today: the two superseded vaults in app/config.js:14-15 are v3 vault contracts excluded from vaults(), so #wrap/d69c933f… renders a retired 5-decimal vault as the current 12-decimal XMR vault. (b) Hostile: contracts/vault.bas is the shipped template and its SHA-256 is published in app/config.js, so anyone can deploy it verbatim, Bond and Seal it with signers they control, and circulate <tela-host>/index.html#wrap/<their-scid>. The app renders their contract as the DMZ Monero vault — same title, same 'WRAPPED-ASSET VAULT · OPEN' hero, same 'Send Monero on Monero …' instruction, same 'What you are trusting' panel including 'The vault is sealed: the deployer has no power over it and nobody can add a signer without a quorum' — and walks the visitor through registering an index and sending Monero to an address the attacker's signers attest. The only thing on screen that differs is the contract id in the last row.
- **Impact** (a) Every monetary figure on the screen is misscaled by 10^7 and a retired asset is labelled with the live one's name and symbol. (b) The app's own vault screen — the most carefully written honesty surface in the product, and the one that carries the 'no operator, no override' guarantees — is lent, verbatim and unqualified, to a contract the build has never seen. Foreign-chain Monero sent to the resulting deposit address is unrecoverable by construction. This is precisely the impersonation the app spends feed.js:53-82 and ui.js:94 defending against for tokens, left open on the vault.
- **Fix** One line, in app/wrap.js:33-40, and it is the same rule the app already applies to every other contract it shows.    async loadVault(pick) {     const list = this.allVaults();                    // legacy included: a holder must keep the burn exit     const scid = (pick && list.some((v) => v.scid === pick)) ? pick : (this.vaults()[0] || {}).scid || this.cfg.vault;     …     const rec = list.find((v) => v.scid === scid);     if (!rec) return null;                            // never borrow another vault's identity  i.e. (1) resolve a picked scid only against allVaults(), so an unknown scid falls back to the default vault rather than borrowing its name — or, better, renders U.emptyState('This build does not ship a vault at that contract id'); and (2) delete the `|| this.cfg.vaultAsset || {}` fallback outright, since it can only ever attach the wrong record: for a configured vault the find() already succeeded, so the fallback fires exactly when it must not. `cfg.vaultAsset` is separately recorded as dead, contradictory data by ARCH·D1.  Then, for the legacy case specifically, render the superseded banner UX-08 asks for on the wrap screen too: this build knows d69c933f is legacy: true and should say so above the deposit steps rather than run them.  No contract change, no config change, no operator decision. app/wrap.js has 3,404 DOC bytes spare (15,028 of 18,432, measured).

### MISS-02 — The Founders Pass screen prints a 50-edition free deployer allocation as if it were demand: "Minted 54 / 500" and "446 of 500 left", with no line anywhere saying 50 were minted to the owner at install [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `app/nfts.js:40`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a disclosure gap on a paid instrument, and one the app already knows how to close for everyone except itself.  1. contracts/founders.bas:37 `45 STORE("minted", reserve)` and :52 `61 SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(s, reserve, SCID())` mint `reserve` editions to the installing owner at InitializePrivate, free, before anyone can buy one. founders.bas:18 caps it at `reserve > maxsupply / 5` — up to 20% of supply. 2. tools/simdeploy/main.go:50 defaults -pass-reserve to 50 of 500, and deploy/README.md:233 passes exactly that on the mainnet command line. 3. app/nfts.js:40 renders `stat("Minted", f.minted + " / " + f.maxsupply)` and nfts.js:38 renders `(f.maxsupply - f.minted) + " of " + f.maxsupply + " left at " + dero(f.price) + " each."`. Neither reads `reserve`, and `grep -rn 'reserve' app/*.js` finds no founders reserve anywhere in the app — the only hits are AMM pool reserves and chart reserves. 4. On the live deployment that reads as '54 / 500 minted' and '446 of 500 left'. 50 of those 54 are the deployer's; 4 were bought. A reader takes 54 as sales. 5. The app applies exactly this disclosure to every other creator: app/home.js:29 H.risks pushes 'The creator received X% of the supply at launch (…), sellable at any time' whenever a launch creator's allocation exceeds 1%. 10% of the Founders Pass sits well above that bar and gets no such line.
- **Impact** The pass is the product's only paid instrument and its value is a pro-rata share of deposits (founders.bas:241 distribute, PROD·P7-11). The deployer's 10% is therefore a permanent 10% claim on every future distribution, and it is also 50 editions that can be sold into the built-in marketplace at any time against buyers who believe 54 people bought in. On the same screen, 'Public accounting' presents the contract as fully legible ('Deposits, payouts and the registry are all on chain') while omitting the one allocation number that changes what a buyer is buying. It is the same misreading the app protects launch buyers from, on the instrument the operator sells.
- **Fix** app/nfts.js only; the field is already on chain and one extra key read away (the founders loader in app/feed.js already fetches the header keys — add "reserve" to its key list, or read it alongside maxsupply).  (1) nfts.js:38, split the two populations in the hero note:     (f.maxsupply - f.minted) + " of " + f.maxsupply + " left at " + dero(f.price) + " each. " + (f.reserve > Z ? f.reserve + " were minted to the deployer at install and never sold." : "")  (2) nfts.js:40, make the stat say what it counts:     stat("Minted", f.minted + " / " + f.maxsupply), stat("Sold", (f.minted - f.reserve) + " · " + f.reserve + " reserved")  (3) accounting() at nfts.js:95 gains one kv row: kv("Minted to the deployer at install", f.reserve + " of " + f.maxsupply + " (" + (Number(f.reserve)*10000/Number(f.maxsupply)/100).toFixed(1) + "%)"), with the sub-line "They share every deposit pro rata like any other registered pass, and can be listed on the marketplace at any time."  Copy plus one contract key, no contract change, no operator decision. app/nfts.js is 6,628 of 18,432 DOC bytes — 11.5 KB spare.

### MISS-03 — The mainnet runbook installs the Founders Pass at maxsupply 500 with a 50-edition reserve — both immutable, both wrong for a 20-pass mainnet, and the pair is arithmetically impossible together [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `deploy/README.md:233`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a launch defect in the checked-in deployment procedure.  1. deploy/README.md:233 is the mainnet simdeploy invocation and passes `-pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50`. deploy/README.md:73 repeats the same flag set as the recorded founders configuration. 2. contracts/founders.bas:32 `40 STORE("maxsupply", maxsupply)` and :34 `42 STORE("reserve", reserve)` are written once at InitializePrivate. The file header (line 6) states 'Immutable: no UpdateCode', and there is no setter for either — `grep -n '^Function' contracts/founders.bas` lists Mint, Register, Withdraw, Claim, Deposit, Distribute, PayBatch, List, Unlist, Buy and internals, and none of them writes maxsupply or reserve. 3. The operator's stated mainnet shape is exactly 20 passes. Running the runbook as written installs 500 — 25x the intended supply, permanently, diluting every holder's pro-rata share (founders.bas:241 distribute) by the same factor. 4. The two flags are also mutually impossible at the intended supply: founders.bas:18 refuses the install outright when `reserve > maxsupply / 5`. At maxsupply 20 the ceiling is 4, so `-pass-max 20 -pass-reserve 50` returns 1 and the contract does not install. An operator who corrects only the supply number discovers the second constraint as a failed mainnet transaction, having already paid the fee, with no note in the runbook explaining why. 5. Nothing in the mainnet go/no-go gate table (deploy/README.md:198-207) requires the pass parameters to be read back from the chain after install. The gate list covers the vault, the signer set, the wallets and the vendored binaries; the one instrument the project sells for money is not on it.
- **Impact** A permanent 25x oversupply of the instrument the operator sells, or a failed mainnet install, depending on which of the two wrong numbers is noticed first. Combined with MISS-02 (the reserve is invisible in the app) and UX-02 (the description is immutable and false), all three defects live in the same three lines of the same runbook and all three become uncorrectable at the same moment.
- **Fix** Three edits, all documentation and all pre-deployment.  (1) deploy/README.md:233 and :73 — replace `-pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50` with the intended mainnet numbers, and add the constraint inline so the pair cannot drift apart again: `-pass-max 20 -pass-reserve 4` (4 is the ceiling founders.bas:18 allows at 20; the operator picks anything from 0 to 4).  (2) deploy/README.md, a new line in the go/no-go gate table at :198-207: 'the Founders Pass name, description, maxsupply, reserve, share and mfee were passed explicitly on the command line and read back from the chain with DERO.GetSC after install — none of them can be changed afterwards'.  (3) Write the intended shape down where it can be checked. tools/simdeploy/main.go:47-50 should carry it in the flag help ('editions — IMMUTABLE; mainnet is 20') and, better, simdeploy should refuse `-network mainnet` unless -pass-max, -pass-reserve, -pass-descr and -pass-name were all passed explicitly, the way it already refuses mainnet without -yes (main.go:41). That last change turns three separate 'the default was inherited' defects — this one and UX-02 — into one impossible state.  None of it touches a contract. The specific numbers are the operator's; shipping 500 by default when the intent is 20 is not defensible either way.

### MISS-04 — Profile prints "Published vol" as a sum of trade amounts across every quote, scaled at DERO's five decimals — the cross-currency total standing requirement 2 forbids, and the finder listed requirement 2 as holding [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `app/screens.js:196`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a number that is not a quantity of anything, presented as a statistic about a person.  1. app/screens.js:196 `const vol = trades.reduce((a, t) => a + (t.side === 1 ? t.in : t.out), 0n);` over `D.tradesOf(addr)`, which spans every market on the deployment regardless of quote. 2. app/screens.js:210 `stat("Published vol", A.fmtDero(vol, 1))`, and app/amm.js:116 `const fmtDero = (v, f) => fmtUnits(v, 5, …)` — the sum is divided by 1e5 and labelled as though it were DERO. 3. A wrapped-XMR trade contributes 12-decimal base units to that sum; a DERO trade contributes 5-decimal base units; the app's own vault config (app/config.js:12-15) carries 12, 8, 5 and 5 decimals across four vaults. Adding them produces a figure with no denomination, and dividing by 1e5 makes a single 0.001 XMR trade read as 10,000,000 DERO of volume. 4. app/screens.js does this three sections below the Portfolio screen's own statement of the opposite rule — 'One line per asset, never a combined figure: wrapped XMR and wrapped ZEC are different coins and DMZ has no price oracle to convert between them' — and app/home.js:77 states the rule again in a comment: 'There is no rate between two quotes and there must not be one'. 5. It is not reachable in the finder's captures because the Profile screen renders the connect prompt with no wallet, which is why they concluded requirement 2 held. It renders for any address reached from the feed or from a Follow, wallet or not.
- **Impact** Standing requirement 2 — 'balances easy to see, never a total that mixes currencies' — is a launch requirement of the directive, and this is the one surviving place in the app that breaks it. The harm is not a loss but a reputational one of the kind §0 exists to prevent: the product's most-repeated discipline, enforced with comments in three files, is silently broken on the public identity screen where a trader's track record is displayed to strangers.
- **Fix** app/screens.js:196 and :210. The app already has the correct primitive — feed.js's per-quote bucketing and home.js:77's flowsOf — so mirror it rather than inventing anything:    const volByQuote = new Map();   for (const t of trades) { const s = D.assets.get(t.scid); if (!s) continue; const k = D.qsym(s); volByQuote.set(k, (volByQuote.get(k) || Z) + (t.side === 1 ? t.in : t.out)); }  and render one figure per quote in the stat, largest first, the way the Home hero and the Trade hub already do — or, if the stats4 grid cannot carry a list, drop the stat entirely and keep 'Published trades', which is a count and is denomination-free. Either is correct; the current figure is not a number about anything.  Copy plus a bucketing loop over data already loaded. No contract change, no config change, no operator decision. app/screens.js is 12,424 of 18,432 DOC bytes — 6.0 KB spare.

### MISS-05 — The product describes itself, once, to every new user, as "a social exchange that runs entirely on the DERO chain" — the one sentence everyone reads names DERO and never names Monero or Zcash [LOW]

- **Where** `app/app.js:265`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — the §0 requirement-1 failure at its highest-leverage point.  1. app/app.js:265, the first-run sheet: `h("h2", …, "Welcome to DMZ"), h("div", { class: "sub mt8" }, "A social exchange that runs entirely on the DERO chain.")`. 2. The three steps beneath it (app.js:266-268) are 'Your node, your wallet, nothing else', 'Trading is private by default' and 'Every listing is verified'. None of the four strings contains 'Monero', 'Zcash', 'XMR' or 'ZEC'. 3. app/index.html:10 `<title>DMZ</title>` — no description meta, so the sheet's subtitle is the only sentence in the build that says what the product is. 4. §0 says DERO is the cheap execution and hosting layer, NOT the primary trading currency, and that the product must read that way; standing requirement 1 is XMR/ZEC first and obvious on every screen. The one sentence the product uses to introduce itself names DERO as the chain it runs on and offers nothing else — which is accurate about the infrastructure and, as a description of the product, is the exact reading §0 was written to prevent. 5. It is read once, by everyone, before they can check anything — the same argument the finder makes for UX-11's title, applied to the line above it, which is the stronger target.
- **Impact** §47. The product's self-definition is the frame every later screen is read inside, and it defines DMZ as a DERO venue. Composed with UX-07 (the DERO-led hero, which is the next thing the user sees after dismissing this sheet) and UX-12 (a nav with no XMR or ZEC in it), the first five seconds say 'DERO exchange' three times before anything says Monero.
- **Fix** app/app.js:265, one string:    "A Monero and Zcash exchange. It runs on DERO, which is what makes it cheap and what pays for the page you are reading."  That states the product, keeps DERO in its §0 role as the execution and hosting layer rather than deleting it, and is shorter than the sentence it replaces. Optionally add 'XMR' and 'ZEC' to app/index.html:10's title. Copy only; app/app.js is 13,376 of 18,432 DOC bytes.

### MX-02 — The checked-in mainnet deployment command permanently mints a 500-edition Founders Pass with a 50-edition free reserve; the product's stated mainnet supply is exactly 20, and maxsupply has no setter [HIGH]

- **Where** `deploy/README.md:233`
- **Attack path** 1. deploy/README.md gives the mainnet simdeploy invocation twice — line 73 as the summary of founders flags and line 233 as the command to run — and both read `-pass-price 500000 -pass-max 500 -pass-reserve 50 -pass-share 5000 -pass-mfee 250`. 2. tools/simdeploy/main.go:85 passes maxsupply and reserve straight into founders.bas InitializePrivate. 3. contracts/founders.bas:5 guards on `EXISTS("initd")`, line 40 STOREs maxsupply, line 42 STOREs reserve, line 45 sets minted = reserve, line 57 sets minhst = 1 + maxsupply/50, line 61 SEND_ASSET_TO_ADDRESS(s, reserve, SCID()). I read the whole contract: there is no UpdateCode and no setter for any of them. Every one of those numbers is fixed for the life of the contract at the moment of install. 4. The directive states the mainnet Founders Pass is to have exactly 20 editions. I searched the repository for that figure — docs/, deploy/, contracts/ — and it is recorded nowhere. There is no gate, no comment, no runbook line and no test that would stop the 500 from shipping. 5. Line 23 of InitializePrivate caps reserve at maxsupply/5, so 50-of-500 is accepted: the deploying key receives 10% of the entire supply for free at install, and at 20 editions the same flag would be rejected outright (50 > 4) — meaning the runbook as written cannot even be run against the intended supply.
- **Impact** A permanent, uncorrectable divergence between the deployed instrument and the product as specified: 25x the intended supply, so 1/25th of the intended per-pass fee share, plus 50 free editions to the deployer. Unlike the description in UX-02, this cannot be softened by better copy — the fee split arithmetic in founders.bas distribute() runs off these numbers forever. It also silently invalidates every statement made to buyers about scarcity.
- **Fix** Change deploy/README.md:73 and :233 to the intended parameters and make them impossible to inherit: `-pass-max 20 -pass-reserve <n ≤ 4>` (the contract refuses reserve > maxsupply/5, so 50 is not merely wrong, it aborts the install at 20 editions — the runbook as written cannot produce the intended pass at all). Change the tool defaults at tools/simdeploy/main.go:47-49 to require the operator to state them on mainnet rather than defaulting: when -network mainnet, refuse to install founders.bas unless -pass-max, -pass-reserve, -pass-descr and -pass-name were all passed explicitly. Add a line to the mainnet go/no-go gate table at deploy/README.md:207 reading "the Founders Pass supply, reserve, name and description were passed explicitly and read back from the chain after install". No contract change; the contract is correct, the runbook is not.

### MX-03 — The app never offers a private burn, although the contract was written for one, the tx pipeline already implements ring 16, and vault.bas's own comment names the reason [MEDIUM]

- **Where** `app/wrap.js:483`
- **Attack path** Not an attack — a capability the product built, documented in its own contract comment, and never surfaced.  1. contracts/vault.bas:373-374, the author's comment on Burn: "refund is where a Veto returns the units, named rather than taken from SIGNER(): above ring 2 SIGNER() is blank, and a revert there cannot address its refund. Naming it also lets a burn stay private." The last sentence is the design intent, stated by the person who wrote it. 2. Burn's body contains no SIGNER() call, so nothing in it requires ring 2. 3. app/app.js:116 already implements the mechanism: `const ring = opts.ring === 16 ? 16 : 2` with the comment "16 only for entrypoints that pay a named address (SIGNER() is blank above 2)" — Burn is exactly such an entrypoint. app/app.js:208 uses it for private-mode trades, and app.js:132-134 already knows not to hand the node a signer on a ring-16 call. 4. app/wrap.js:483 sends Burn with no `ring` option, so every withdrawal in the product goes at ring 2, where — by the app's own statement at app/screens.js:272 — "anyone can compute which address paid a ringsize-2 call, not only the contract." 5. Result: on a Monero and Zcash exchange, the single transaction that joins a DERO identity to a foreign-chain destination is sent in the one mode the app elsewhere labels non-private, while the private mode ships and is offered for trades.
- **Impact** Every unwrap permanently publishes (payer's DERO address, foreign destination) as a linked pair — the payer via the ring-2 transaction, the destination via w_<j>_to — on the product whose proposition is that those two should not be linkable. The private alternative exists in the contract, in the tx pipeline, and in the app's vocabulary, and costs a checkbox. Rated MEDIUM rather than HIGH because the trade-off is genuine and not free: at ring 16 SIGNER() is blank, so a burn that reverts cannot refund itself from the signer — which is precisely why the contract takes `refund` as a named argument, so the trade-off is already solved on the contract side and the app need only expose it.
- **Fix** In app/wrap.js's burnForm, add a private-mode control matching the one the trade sheet already ships: a toggle "Withdraw privately (ring size 16)" that, when on, passes `ring: 16` to V.app.tx at wrap.js:483 and exposes the refund address as a required named field rather than defaulting to me(). State the trade-off in one line, because it is real: "At ring 16 the contract cannot see who signed, so a burn that reverts can only be refunded to the address you name here — name one you control. At ring 2 your wallet is published next to the destination, permanently." Default: leave ring 2 selected so no existing behaviour changes silently, and let the user choose. This is the same shape as app/app.js:208's `ring: dest ? 16 : 2` and reuses the whole pipeline; no contract change. Fix UX-04's false sentence at the same time — it is the sentence that tells the user this control could not exist.

### MX-01 — The first-run intro's third step says "Every listing is verified" — the app's whole badge system exists to avoid saying exactly that, and four of its five tiers say the opposite [LOW]

- **Where** `app/app.js:269`
- **Attack path** 1. app/app.js:269, step 3 of the intro shown on first run: title "Every listing is verified", detail "The app checks each contract's code against the shipped templates before it shows it. Warnings appear on thin or taxed markets before you confirm." 2. The detail is true. The title is the app's own reserved word used without any of the qualification the app spent five badge tiers building. app/ui.js:90-103 defines, in order: "Code checked · name is not" (an impersonator), "Code checked · treasury unread", "Wrapped asset · verified", "Pool verified · asset unknown", "Code verified · not listed" — four of the five are explicit statements that the listing is NOT verified in the sense the intro asserts. 3. app/token.js:35 sets `s.unlisted = true` for a hash-valid contract that is not in the DMZ registry and renders it anyway, with token.js:111 saying "Matches the shipped 'pool' template but is not in the DMZ registry." So the app shows listings nobody registered — and told the user on page one that every listing is verified. 4. On the live deployment the markets screen carries an impersonating token flagged "Not real XMR" and 21 "Ticker shared" marks. The intro's sentence was already false at the moment it was read.
- **Impact** The same failure mode as UX-11 and slightly worse in kind: UX-11 overstates a privacy property, this overstates the verification property that the rest of the app is unusually careful about. A user who reads "Every listing is verified" and then sees a green "Verified" shield on a market has been primed to read the shield as the blanket assurance the intro promised, which is exactly what the tier system was designed to prevent. It also undercuts the impersonation guard — the app's best safety feature — by telling the reader in advance that the check it performs is total.
- **Fix** app/app.js:269 — retitle to the check that is actually performed, which the detail already describes correctly:    step("3", "Every contract's code is checked; its name and its asset are not", "Before a market is shown, its code is hashed against the shipped templates and its treasury against the factory's. That says nothing about what it calls itself or what it trades: anyone can create a DERO asset with any name, and a market can be shown that nobody registered. Each listing carries a badge saying exactly what was checked, and warnings on thin, taxed or look-alike markets appear before you confirm.")  Copy only, in a file with headroom, and it is the operator's own vocabulary — every clause is lifted from app/ui.js's existing badge titles.

### MX-04 — simnfa writes a raw contract id into an NFA's permanent on-chain description, and the app renders it as prose: "priced in quote f23403b0 on the DMZ simulator" [LOW]

- **Where** `tools/simnfa/main.go:88`
- **Attack path** 1. tools/simnfa/main.go:86-88: `qname := "DERO"` and, when a quote SCID is given, `qname = "quote " + (*quote)[:8]` — a raw 8-hex prefix of a contract id used as if it were the asset's name. 2. main.go:113 splices it into the NFA's descrHdr at InitializePrivate: `"One unit, one owner; priced in "+qname+" on the DMZ simulator"`. 3. nfa.bas stores descrHdr at initialisation. Per the ART-NFA-MS1 shape the repo implements, Update rewrites iconURL, coverURL, fileURL, fileSignURL and tags — not descrHdr — so the description is fixed for the life of the asset. 4. app/nfas.js renders it on the NFA page directly under the price. The rendered result on the live simulator: "One unit, one owner; priced in quote f23403b0 on the DMZ simulator" — an eight-character hex fragment where the asset name belongs, on the page whose eyebrow two lines above correctly reads "NFA · PRICED IN XMR" because the app resolves the same SCID to a name perfectly well. 5. The tool has the name available: the quote SCID it was handed is a configured vault, and every other surface (the eyebrow, the On-chain table's "Priced in XMR · wrapped by the Monero vault") resolves it.
- **Impact** Small and bounded: it makes the XMR-priced NFA — the one asset class that carries the product's premise — read as debug output on the page where it is bought, and the string cannot be corrected after mint. Its real weight is as the second instance of a pattern that has already produced one launch-blocking defect (UX-02): a Go tool's string default becoming an immutable on-chain header that the app renders as prose. If NFA creation ever gains a non-simulator driver, this ships.
- **Fix** tools/simnfa/main.go:86-88 — resolve the quote to its configured symbol before building the string, and fall back to the word "a wrapped asset" rather than to a hex fragment: read app/config.js's vaults (or take a -quote-sym flag) and set `qname = sym` where known, `qname = "a wrapped asset"` otherwise. Never interpolate a contract id into a sentence. One function, no contract change, no new dependency.


## What held (156)

- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The ...To wrappers cannot publish a trade even if a foreign tool asks them to. contracts/pool.bas:144-152 and :191-199 call `buyto(s, min_out, 0)` / `sellto(s, min_out, 0)` with the publish argument hard-coded to zero, so a private trade can never write a t_<n> record naming the receiving address — the protection lives in the contract, not only in the app's `publish && !priv ? 1 : 0` at app/token.js:220. Verified by reading both wrappers and post() at pool.bas:281-288. Right place for it, and it held.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The trade sheet's own privacy copy is accurate and unusually careful. app/token.js:172 states the ring per mode ('2 · the payer is public' / '16 · the payer is hidden'); :174 explains each consequence in one sentence; :176 raises a warning box when the private-mode destination is the connected address ('the ring hides who paid, but the receiver is you, in clear'); :238 says publishing 'writes your address and this trade into the contract for everyone to see'; :242 explains a private trade is never posted because a public record would name the receiver. Every one of these checks out against the contracts. The intro (PRIV-01) is the outlier, not the norm.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The Settings ring-2 disclosure is correct and unhedged: app/screens.js:272 says 'anyone can compute which address paid a ringsize-2 call, not only the contract' — exactly what research/RING_PRIVACY.md §2 measured and what DERO.GetTransaction confirmed for me today. app/screens.js:215-218 repeats it as a two-row fact table. This is the sentence RING_PRIVACY.md §3 asked for, and it was written.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The vault deposit walkthrough now tells the truth about DERO-side linkage, reversing the app half of SEC·RT-04. app/wrap.js:286 states that registering again 'gives you a fresh index and a fresh deposit address, but not a fresh identity: the vault stores which DERO address owns every index and the foreign address attested to it, in the clear, so anyone reading the contract can link all of yours together', and :287 warns Register is ring 2 before the user pays for it. The old copy asserted the opposite. Only the contract comment was left stale (PRIV-05).
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The burn form discloses the destination correctly: app/wrap.js:388 — 'The destination you type is stored on DERO in the clear — anyone can read it. Use an address you are willing to publish.' That is precisely what vault.bas:41 stores in w_<j>_to. The false half of that paragraph is the ring sentence (PRIV-03), not this one.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] Chat's privacy copy matches chat.bas exactly. app/chat.js:178-179 says posting is a ring-2 call so 'this address is public in that transaction' and a message is 'permanent: nobody, including whoever deployed DMZ, can edit or remove it' — which is what chat.bas Post stores (rm_<room>_<j> = addr|height|text) and what the contract's lack of any delete path means. The same statement appears as a file-header comment at chat.js:5-6, so it is load-bearing rather than decorative.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The gas estimate correctly omits the signer at ring 16. app/app.js:132-134 branches `ring === 2 ? { signer: W.address } : {}` with a comment explaining that naming the payer to the node would undo the ring. The ring-16 half of the estimator is right; PRIV-04 concerns the ring-2 half and the read path.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] No key material reaches browser storage. Enumerating every write (`grep -rn "localStorage.setItem|sessionStorage.setItem" app/*.js`) gives eight keys: veil.settings, veil.endpointOk, veil.trades, veil.watch, veil.m.<scid>, the XSWD session app-id, and dmz.chat.mute. None is a seed, key or signature; the hard rule 'never store private keys in the browser' holds. The XSWD app id is a fresh random per session in sessionStorage (app/xswd.js:12-13), so it is not a cross-session tracker.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The renderer does not leak through hostile metadata. app/ui.js h() builds text nodes and setAttribute only (the sole innerHTML in the app is the chart tooltip, chart.js:132, numbers and locale strings); U.clean (ui.js:23) strips C0/C1 controls, bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width and joiner characters and the BOM; U.safeUrl (ui.js:34) admits https only, ≤300 chars, no whitespace or angle brackets. Creator-supplied images are off by default (app/app.js settings images:false) and the toggle states the cost in the user's own terms — 'Loading one tells that host your IP address. Sent without a referrer' (screens.js:258).
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The private-mode overshoot guard closes a real leak-and-loss path: app/token.js:202 disables the confirm button when `priv && q.refund > Z`, because a curve overshoot would send the refund to the named destination. The contract pays that refund to `dest` by design (launch.bas refundto), so refusing client-side is the correct conservative choice, and the button text says why ('Overshoots the curve: lower the amount').
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] The remote-node gate is real, not decorative: rpc.js endpointURL admits loopback ws:// or wss:// only, and a stored remote endpoint is not used until confirmed per session (app/screens.js:249 renders the warning box and an explicit 'Use it this session' button). The privacy problem is what a confirmed node then learns (PRIV-04), not that the app connects silently — though app/app.js:128 does accept the wallet-reported endpoint without that confirmation step, which another agent's map flags.
- [Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] app/notice.js contains no privacy claim at all — so nothing in it is contradicted by the code. Every privacy statement in the product lives in screens.js, token.js, wrap.js, chat.js and the intro. Worth stating plainly: the hard rule 'never contradict app/notice.js' is not breached by any finding here; PRIV-01's intro line contradicts the app's own Settings page and the repo's own research document instead.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] §32 forbidden-word sweep is clean, and clean in the strong sense — the words are absent because they were argued away, not avoided. `grep -rniE 'trustless|atomic|non-custodial|guaranteed|instant|unhackable' app/*.js app/*.html`: "trustless" appears twice and is negated both times (app/screens.js:68 "Backed, not trustless"; app/token.js:230 "backed one for one by a k-of-n federation that can fail, so they are not trustless"); "atomic" only ever means atomic *units*; "non-custodial", "guaranteed" and "unhackable" do not appear; "instant" appears once, in a code comment in feed.js. No claim in the app needed to be walked back on word grounds.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The trade sheet meets standing requirement 3 exactly. .sim/ui-audit-ux/05-trade-sheet.png: "WHAT THIS TRADE COSTS — Trading fee 0.50% 0.0025 DERO · of it, protocol treasury 100% 0.0025 DERO · Token tax 2% to stakers 0.01 DERO · Price impact 9.16% · Minimum received, or it reverts 28,133.548 BRW · Max slippage 0.5% 1% 3% 5% · Network fee (gas), in DERO estimating…". Every cost is in the asset actually charged, the protocol's share of the fee is broken out, and DERO gas is a separate labelled row — never folded into the trade's cost. A 9.16% impact is stated as a number before the button, and app.js:305 refuses any min_out below the 10% cap regardless of what the sheet computed.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Requirement 2 — never a total that mixes currencies — holds everywhere I could reach it, and the code says why. app/home.js:75-77 `flowsOf` buckets volume by quote symbol and the comment states the rule ("there is no rate between two quotes and there must not be one"); the Home hero prints one figure per quote on separate lines; app/app.js:88 refuses to sum holdings across quotes; the Portfolio screen prints per-asset rows under an explicit statement — rendered in .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-portfolio.png — "One line per asset, never a combined figure: wrapped XMR and wrapped ZEC are different coins and DMZ has no price oracle to convert between them." I looked for a mixed total on Home, Trade, Markets and Portfolio and did not find one.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The impersonation guard works end to end and outranks every other flag. .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-markets.png row 18: a token whose ticker is WXMR renders as "WXMR · ⚠ Not real XMR · Wrapped Moner…", and app/ui.js:94 gives it the qualified badge "Code checked · name is not" instead of the plain shield, with the title "That says nothing about the name: this is not XMR, and no vault holds anything for it." app/home.js:21 places that risk first in H.risks, above split-pair and thin-market, with the reasoning written in the comment. The uitest asserts it: "• impersonation: XMR and wXMR reserved to the vault; listed copies: WXMR→XMR".
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Split-pair disclosure is unusually good. .sim/ui-audit-ux/02-markets.png rows 21-22 each carry a band naming the *number* of contracts trading the pair, this contract's depth, the deepest contract's depth, and the deepest contract's full id — "⚠ 2 markets for XMR-old / DERO · liquidity is split · deepest of the 2, holding 2.15 DERO · next holds 1.09 DERO · b9c5d6fd…21093". app/home.js:24-29 computes it and the thin half is excluded from rankings (uitest: "split pairs: 1 group(s), thin halves never rank"). This is the kind of disclosure that costs the venue engagement and was shipped anyway.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] §31 failure states in the transaction pipeline are specific rather than "something went wrong", and they distinguish inclusion from execution — which almost nothing in this space does. app/app.js:163 "was included but did not execute: only the network fee was spent"; :170 the same with "your balance did not move"; :162 "is not in a block after 4.5 minutes: check the wallet history before sending it again"; :131 refuses and names the number when a node estimates a fee above 1 DERO; :68 handles a wallet that answers a transfer late by naming the txid and telling the user to check wallet history. app.js:168-169 verifies execution by re-reading a balance the caller declared must move, or a contract key that must change, and app.balanceAfter refuses to judge from a lagging wallet (returns null rather than "failed").
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The badge tier system in app/ui.js:90-104 is the best safety feature in the app. Five qualified tiers, each naming precisely what was and was not checked — "Code checked · treasury unread" when the factory's treasury could not be read this session; "Pool verified · asset unknown" when a pool's asset is not a DMZ launch; "Code verified · not listed" for a hash-valid contract nobody registered. The comments record why each tier was carved out. My UX-13 is a gap in this system, not an argument against it.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The halt state is modelled and explained honestly, including the part that is uncomfortable. app/wrap.js:258-263: "Registering and minting are refused while the signers report a problem, and no new payment starts. Burning still works and queues behind the usual delay; a withdrawal already paid still gets recorded. A halt is a lease: it lapses at block N (…) unless the signers renew it, so a federation that stops answering cannot leave it halted." That is an accurate reading of vault.bas's halt matrix (Register/Attest/Mint gated at lines 269/293/318; Burn/Released/Veto/Reclaim ungated) and it is the liveness-by-construction property stated plainly rather than claimed.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The minimum-deposit warning is exactly the pattern UX-01 asks for on the cap. app/wrap.js:270-272 publishes `mindep` before a wallet is connected and before any address exists, in a warnbox, with the consequence stated without hedging: "Anything smaller cannot be minted, and there is nobody who can return it. Check the amount before you send." The code comment explains the reasoning ("someone deciding whether to use this vault at all has to be able to see that first"). The team already knows how to write this class of disclosure; it just has not been applied to headroom.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] §48, the specific question asked: a Founders page is not warranted and the build does not have one. The pass lives at the top of an existing tab (app/nfts.js:131) plus one hairline row at the foot of Home's main column (app/home.js:164), which app/home.js:163 explicitly justifies — "secondary, and it says so: a hairline row at the foot of the column, no accent edge and no accent badge". There is no microsite, no dedicated tab, and no promotional treatment. My UX-12 concerns the tab labels one level above this, not the Founders integration, which is right.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] app/notice.js is the strongest document in the product and it does what the directive asks: it names the three keys that survive deployment (factory owner, treasury, TELA INDEX publisher) and the federation, states what each can do, and admits the thing the backlog still describes as achievable — "There is no renounce, only a transfer to another key" (line 67), against factory.bas:45-62 where TransferOwnership needs the new owner's signature and so can never complete to a burn address. Three of my findings are contradictions *of* notice.js; none is a defect *in* it.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Layout and performance hold under adversarial conditions. Zero horizontal overflow at 390px, 768px and 1024px across home, markets, wrap and notice; 7 tabs visible at phone width; the desktop shell engages at 1280px; enrich+redraw of 27 markets in 2.14 ms; exactly 2 onChange listeners after five navigations (i.e. the router's generation-tagged listeners are not leaking); no pageerror and no console error on any of the four cold deep-link routes I loaded (#wrap, a wrapped-quote #token, an XMR-priced #nfa, #nfts).
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Cold deep links paint correctly rather than flashing an empty or wrong state. Loading #wrap as the very first navigation of a fresh browser context reached the fully populated vault — "WRAPPED-ASSET VAULT OPEN … CLAIMS OUTSTANDING 0.5 XMR MINT CAP 100 XMR HEADROOM 99.5 XMR SIGNERS 2 of 3" — with no intermediate render claiming anything false (.sim/ui-audit-ux2/c1-wrap-firstpaint.png).
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The wrapped-quote trade sheet states the federation dependency at the point of the trade, not only on the Wrap screen. app/token.js:230: "You spend wrapped XMR, held on DERO as an asset — not XMR on Monero, and not DERO. Wrapped units are backed one for one by a k-of-n federation that can fail, so they are not trustless. The network fee is DERO either way." That is the sentence that has to exist, in the place it has to exist.
- [Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] The NFA creation form gets the quote ordering right where the rest of the app does not: app/forms.js:191-192 lists the wrapped vaults first with DERO appended last, and defaults to XMR. It also states the trade-off in the chip note (forms.js:231) — "buyers pay wrapped XMR, a claim on … held by a federation; DERO is only gas. Other DERO tools read it but cannot settle it." This is the shape UX-07 asks the launch form and the hero to adopt; it already exists in the codebase as a precedent.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] The accumulator arithmetic is exact and cannot over-credit. In distribute() (founders.bas:241-262) inc = floor(pool*2^32/hst) and covered = ceil(inc*hst/2^32); writing inc*hst = pool*2^32 - r with 0 <= r < hst <= 100000 < 2^32 gives covered = pool exactly, so `und` after a release is always 0 and rpool can never claim more DERO than the contract holds. The invariant contract DERO == rpool + und is asserted by checkFounders on every fuzz checkpoint and TestFoundersFuzz matched a math/big model exactly over 3000 random operations (1,141,479,417 atomic units paid across 6 holders).
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] No integer wrap anywhere in the fee paths. maxsupply <= 100000 and price <= 1e13 (founders.bas:18) bound Mint's cost = n*price at 1e18 < 2^64, and the same bound covers Buy's cost = n*lp_; bps(x,f) with f <= 1000 peaks at 1e17; the accumulator clamp at line 248 is hst*4294967295 <= 4.29e14; muldiv's hi >= c panic is unreachable at every call site because line 247 guards it. The 2^32 accumulator wrap is handled by the era mechanism, and settle()'s era walk (272-277) credits exactly (ea_e - hd) + sum(ea) + (acc - 0), which sums to the same total as an unwrapped accumulator — tests/saturation_test.go:TestFoundersAccSaturation exercises three rollovers.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] A holder cannot claim twice, and a fresh pass cannot claim history. settle() writes hd_ = acc and he_ = era (278-279) before the send, and Register (line 96) snapshots hd_ at the current accumulator after distributing. Reproduced: TestFoundersLateRegistrantEarnsNoHistory — a wallet registering 5 units immediately after a 1,000,000 deposit claims 0, the early holder claims the whole 1,000,000, and a second Claim by the early holder pays 0.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Registered passes cannot be transferred out from under a pending payout. Register requires the units to be physically sent in (ASSETVALUE(SCID()), line 82) and only Withdraw returns them (line 117), which settles first (line 115). There is no path that lets the same units be both registered (hst) and listed (lu_), so the header's "Contract units == hst + sum(lu_)" holds and the register/pay window cannot be gamed by a transfer.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Total supply is capped and immutable. `minted` starts at reserve (line 37), only ever grows (line 68), Mint refuses n > maxsupply - minted (line 65), and reserve <= maxsupply/5 (line 18). Every STORE of maxsupply/price/share/mfee/treasury/minhst is inside InitializePrivate behind the initd guard, and `grep -rn UPDATE_SC_CODE contracts/` returns nothing — so no path can raise the cap or redirect the pass's own treasury. Confirmed independently by another auditor's TestFoundersSupplyCannotExceedMaxsupplyAt20, which passes in my regression run.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] The internal helpers are not externally callable. founders.bas exposes distribute, settle, remove, bps, muldiv, muldivup, mulhi, divlu, nlz, shl, shr as Functions, but vendor/derohe/dvm/dvm.go:364-370 refuses any entrypoint whose first character is not an uppercase ASCII letter ("Invalid function name, First character must be Capital/Upper Case"). So nobody can call settle() with a forged address or remove() to corrupt the registry.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] PayBatch cannot be DoSed by many holders, and the fixed fees the app and the bot use are sufficient. hn <= hst <= minted <= maxsupply because every registered address holds at least one unit, so the number of batches is bounded by ceil(maxsupply/15) — two at the mainnet target of 20. Measured this session (TestFoundersFixedFeesCoverStorageGas): Mint(2) 84 storage gas against the app's pinned 4000; Register 164; PayBatch(0,15) 620 storage gas against the app's pinned 20000 and the bot's pinned 3000. The mappers' risk that these fixed fees could be exceeded is disproved. The real constraint is compute: PayBatch(15) is 6,814,600 of the 10,000,000 limit, so any DVM repricing (BL-148) needs the n cap lowered, and any per-asset rework must budget for it.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] The marketplace fee is correct and not gameable. Buy (211-235) requires exact payment, fee = bps(cost, mfee) is a floor with the remainder going to the seller, and the fee is routed to holders through the same distribute() as any deposit; a self-buy pays the fee to the holder set and so is never profitable. `la` is incremented once per List and decremented once per deletion behind an EXISTS guard, so it cannot underflow. The sends at 222-223 precede the stores at 224-225, which is safe only because distribute() has no failure exit and its muldiv calls cannot panic (guarded at 247) — TestNoExternalSendBeforeAFailureExit covers this class and passes.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Accumulated fees cannot be redirected once they exist. pool.bas and launch.bas ClaimFees always pay the treasury address recorded at that market's install and are permissionless, so no key can divert fees already accrued in fd/fa; founders.bas has no owner at all and its treasury (used only for Mint proceeds, line 71) is immutable. The redirect that IS available is prospective, not retrospective: the factory owner can SetTreasury (factory.bas:27-36) so that every newly installed market pays a different address, with no renounce path to prevent it — app/notice.js:66 states this correctly.
- [Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Rounding dust is small in absolute terms. It is bounded by roughly one atomic unit per holder per settle and does not compound across settles for a stable holder (the floor is taken over the cumulative acc delta); the fuzz reached 887 atomic units (0.00887 DERO) after 3000 operations with heavy register/withdraw churn, and my 7-holder case left 2. The dust matters only because tools/treasurybot uses rpool == 0 as its idle test (FEE-03), not as a loss.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE FOUNDER SUPPLY CAP HOLDS. maxsupply is written exactly once (contracts/founders.bas:32, STORE("maxsupply", maxsupply) inside the initd-guarded InitializePrivate at line 13) and there is no setter; `grep -rn UPDATE_SC_CODE contracts/` returns nothing in any contract. There are exactly two paths that send the pass's own asset into existence: the install reserve (founders.bas:52, bounded by `reserve > maxsupply / 5` at line 18, with `minted` set to `reserve` at line 37) and Mint (line 69, gated by `n == 0 || n > LOAD("maxsupply") - m` at line 65). Every other send of SCID() — Withdraw:117, Unlist:201, Buy:222 — returns units the contract physically received and accounts for under hst or lu_. TestFoundersSupplyCannotExceedMaxsupplyAt20 (tests/founders_supply_test.go, NEW) installs at maxsupply 20, mints to the cap, then refuses 12 further Mint shapes (n = 1, 2, 20, 2^64-1 crossed with DERO = 0, n*price, 1) and drives 16 steps of Register / Deposit / Distribute / PayBatch / Withdraw / List / Buy / Unlist plus four refused shapes, asserting after EVERY step that units-ever-sent-to-wallets plus hst plus the sum of every lu_ equals exactly 20 and that `minted` never exceeds it. PASS.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] NO INTERNAL FUNCTION IS REACHABLE AS AN ENTRYPOINT. This was worth checking hard, because vendor/derohe/blockchain/transaction_execute.go:373 takes the entrypoint verbatim from the transaction's SCDATA and vendor/derohe/dvm/sc.go:172 looks it up in sc_parsed.Functions with no allow-list — so the language, not the contract, has to protect the helpers. It does, by initial capital. TestNFAHelpersAreCallableEntrypoints and TestFoundersHelpersAreCallableEntrypoints (tests/nfa_entrypoint_test.go, NEW) call transferOwnership(newOwner=<attacker raw>), processDEROFinalPayment(saleAmt=<the contract's whole quote balance>), init(), resetVars(1), outbidReturns(), settle(s=<attacker>, a=<a holder's key>), distribute(x=2^40) and remove(a=<a holder>) directly, as strangers. Every one is refused with 'Invalid function name, First character must be Capital/Upper Case', the NFA owner key is unchanged, no quote leaves the contract, rpool+und stays equal to the contract's DERO, and the holder's registration survives. I then audited every `Function` declaration in founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas and factory.bas: every internal (distribute, settle, remove, bps, muldiv, muldivup, mulhi, divlu, nlz, shl, shr, init, transferOwnership, generateEndBlock, checkActive, processHighestBidder, outbidReturns, processDEROFinalPayment, resetVars, findLesserIncrease, reg) is lowercase, and every capitalised name is a genuine, guarded entrypoint.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] nfa.bas REFUSES A SECOND InitializePrivate AFTER INSTALL — no second unit. The architecture readers flagged this as the one initd-guard in the tree that no test asserted. Now asserted: TestNFAHelpersAreCallableEntrypoints calls InitializePrivate again with a full argument list and a different treasury; the EXISTS("owner") guard at nfa.bas:34 (and again at init line 57) refuses it with 'Discarded knowingly', and the one-unit invariant holds afterwards.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] A USER NFA CAN NEVER ACQUIRE A FOUNDERS PRIVILEGE. contracts/founders.bas Register:82 reads `ASSETVALUE(SCID())` — the founders contract's OWN asset — and refuses at line 83 when it is zero; List:183 does the same. An NFA unit is a different SCID, so ASSETVALUE returns 0 and the call is refused with a full refund. The DVM gives each call a fresh state.Assets populated only from that transaction's incoming_value (vendor/derohe/dvm/sc.go:206-212, `state.Assets[asset] += value` on an empty map), so ASSETVALUE cannot be confused with the contract's standing balance either. There is no cross-contract call in the DVM, so nothing else could bridge them.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE NFA SETTLEMENT SPLIT CANNOT WRAP OR UNDERFLOW. init line 87 enforces charity*100 + artificerFee*100 + royalty*100 + protoFee <= 10000 with charity 0 at install, and line 86 bounds royalty and artificerFee at 100 each so the *100 cannot wrap; Start:136 recomputes the room left as (10000 - protoFee - artificerFee*100 - royalty*100)/100 and CLAMPS the caller's charity to it rather than adding to it, so an absurd charityDonatePerc cannot carry the sum past the check; and BuyItNow:174 / Bid:208 refund anything above 1,844,674,407,370,955 = floor((2^64-1)/10000), so every product in processDEROFinalPayment stays under 2^64. Each of the four shares floors independently, so their sum is <= saleAmt and the owner's remainder at line 350 cannot underflow. The existing suite asserts the split to the unit on both a DERO and a wrapped quote (TestNFASale, TestNFAAmountBound); my stale-settlement test independently recomputed royalty and protoFee from the stale amount and matched what carol received.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE STRAY-DERO GUARD WORKS WHERE IT EXISTS. On a wrapped-quote NFA, DERO burned into Start, BuyItNow, Bid, CloseListing or CancelListing is refused with RETURN 1, which discards every store and transfer (vendor/derohe/dvm/sc.go:284-288) so the whole call is refunded at ring 2. Asserted in tests/nfa_stray_test.go: a Start carrying the unit plus 777 stray DERO is refused and leaves 0 DERO in the contract. (The two entrypoints that lack the guard are finding NFA-4.)
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE APP'S TEXT PIPELINE IS SOUND AGAINST HOSTILE METADATA. app/ui.js h() at line 7 builds every node with createElement/setAttribute and document.createTextNode — there is no innerHTML anywhere on the NFA path (the app's only innerHTML is the chart tooltip, which carries numbers). clean() at ui.js:23 strips C0/C1 controls, the Arabic letter mark, the Mongolian vowel separator, zero-width space/non-joiner/joiner, LRM/RLM, all four bidi embeddings and overrides, LS/PS, the word joiner and invisible operators, the four bidi isolates and the BOM, then bounds the length; app/feed.js:280 runs it over every NFA header with a per-field cap. safeUrl at ui.js:34 admits https only. tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs (NEW) refuses 13 hostile URLs (javascript:, JavaScript:, data:text/html, http:, protocol-relative //, vbscript:, file:, an embedded double quote, <svg onload=, a backslash, an embedded space, too short, 301 characters) and confirms clean() collapses a bidi-override + zero-width + BOM + isolate string to its visible letters. Images load only when the viewer opts in (settings.images, default false) and carry referrerpolicy="no-referrer"; links carry rel="noopener noreferrer".
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE WORD "peg" APPEARS NOWHERE. `grep -rni peg app/ contracts/ docs/NFA.md docs/NFA_BUILD.md` returns nothing.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] nft.bas SUPPLY IS BOUNDED. Mint refuses at `n >= LOAD("maxsupply")` (line 27 of the function) and writes mt_<addr> at line 37 which is never deleted, so one edition per address for life; exactly one unit is sent per mint (line 40) and maxsupply has no setter. The Sybil route — move the gating tokens to a fresh address and mint again — is real and is PROTO·BC-7 / BL-72, an open retirement decision, not a supply break.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE DMZ NFA IS THE STANDARD PLUS THE DECLARED CHANGES AND NOTHING ELSE, at the function level. I fetched the upstream ART-NFA-MS1.bas and its function set is identical to contracts/nfa.bas's — 17 functions, same names, same order. The stranded-bid branch of finding NFA-1 is upstream's own code (upstream BuyItNow 500/510/520/530/540 has no outbidReturns either), which is why NFA-1's fix is an operator decision about divergence rather than a straightforward bug fix.
- [Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] THE FOUNDERS REWARD ARITHMETIC IS CORRECT AND CANNOT DOUBLE-PAY. settle (founders.bas:263-288) writes hd_<a> = acc and he_<a> = era at lines 278-279 BEFORE the send at line 286, so a repeated call finds pend 0; distribute (241-262) clamps pool to hst*(2^32-1) at line 248 before muldiv so the accumulator cannot panic, rolls the era into ea_<era> at 252-254 when acc would wrap, and credits rpool only with covered = ceil(inc*hst/2^32) which is provably <= pool, so what stays in und is strictly less than one atomic unit per registered unit. sum(hu_) == hst is maintained by Register (95/98), Withdraw (116/121) and remove(). The existing TestFoundersFuzz and TestFoundersAccSaturation cover this against a big.Int model; my 16-step run re-checked the unit ledger and checkFounders() on top.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] monero.go's get_transfers window is correct, and I checked it against the source rather than the prose. Incoming() (monero.go:392-400) sends min_height = minHeight−1 because wallet2::get_payments filters `min_height < x.second.m_block_height` — verbatim from wallet2.cpp:7221, inclusive on max and EXCLUSIVE on min, exactly as the code comment claims. Omitting max_height is also safe: wallet_rpc_server_commands_defs.h declares it `KV_SERIALIZE_OPT(max_height, (uint64_t)CRYPTONOTE_MAX_BLOCK_NUMBER)`, so an absent field defaults to the maximum. Had that been a plain KV_SERIALIZE, every get_transfers call would have returned nothing and Bob's gate would never have opened. It is not.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The transfer-entry fields are read with the right meanings. wallet_rpc_server.cpp's fill_transfer_entry sets entry.unlock_time = pd.m_unlock_time (the RAW transaction field, not the reference page's 'number of blocks until spendable'), entry.locked = !is_transfer_unlocked(pd.m_unlock_time, pd.m_block_height), confirmations = blockchain height − entry height, amount = pd.m_amount. monero.go:404-421 maps exactly these. The amount it reads is the per-subaddress SUM wallet2 records for a transaction, so a lock split into several outputs to the joint address is counted once at its full value — neither doubled nor halved.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The gate's refusals hold, and they are tested. confirmedAtJoint (swap.go:509-547) skips any transfer with unlock_time != 0 whatever its depth, requires Confirmations >= cfg.Confirmations AND the wallet's own Unlocked verdict, then caps the total by get_balance.unlocked_balance — which is what excludes an output the scanner discarded as a duplicate one-time key. TestGateRefusesLockedTimeOutput and TestGateRefusesBurntOutput both PASS in my run, driven by fakewallet.go's SetUnlockTime and BurnNextTransfer. I traced every combination of burnt / honest / locked outputs: the cap can only make the gate stricter, never looser.
