- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The confirmation policy cannot be lowered by accident. cmd/xmrswap/main.go:69-78 refuses -confs below the default 20 unless it was typed on the command line, refuses it outright below 10 (CRYPTONOTE_DEFAULT_TX_SPENDABLE_AGE — below that nobody could spend the output the gate opened on), and prints the reorg warning whenever it is lowered at all.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] Digest authentication is implemented correctly against epee's server: the MD5/MD5-sess challenge pair, both qop forms, the nc counter, and a single re-challenge on a stale nonce (monero.go:151-275). TestWalletRPCDigestAuth PASSES against fakewallet.go's RequireAuth, which serves one WWW-Authenticate per algorithm as contrib/epee/src/http_auth.cpp does. The client also fails closed when the server asks for credentials and none were given (monero.go:167-169).
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] Amounts are piconero uint64 throughout with no float anywhere: Transfer marshals the Go uint64 straight into the JSON request (monero.go:334-338) and Incoming/UnlockedBalance decode into uint64 fields, so values above 2^53 are exact. keys.go refuses unreduced and zero scalars, non-canonical, torsion and identity points, and integrated addresses — so a joint address can never be integrated and s_a+s_b can never be zero (TestScalarReduction, TestParsePointRejectsTorsionAndGarbage, TestParseAddressNetworks all PASS).
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] Account and subaddress handling is consistent and minimal: every call names account_index 0 with no subaddr_indices, and the joint wallets are generated fresh so they have only a primary address. There is no path by which a payment to some other subaddress could be counted towards the gate.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The three exits are cryptographically exclusive and I could not construct a race for the joint output. On Claim only s_a reaches the chain, so only Bob (who holds s_b) can form s_a+s_b; on Refund only s_b reaches it, so only Alice can. There is no exit in which both parties hold both halves — which is the mistake that would turn a recovery into a race. Punish reveals nothing and destroys the XMR, correctly documented as such in docs/XMR_SWAP.md §2.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] No secret reaches a log line or a message of the wrong kind, under crash injection. TestCrashFuzz (PASS, 49–70 s) kills each side after every durable step of every exit and asserts s_a, s_b, k_b and v never appear in a log or in any message other than gate / alice_setup, that at most one Monero transfer and one sweep occur across every restart, and that the lock is never left open. That held across 40+ scenarios in my run.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The rendezvous directory is created 0700 and its message files 0600 with O_EXCL (channel.go:64, 92, 102), so the shared private view key and k_b are not left world-readable on a POSIX host.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] tools/vaultsigner fails closed on the REAL backends: -backend zcash|monero|solana exits with 'not implemented yet' rather than degrading to something that guesses. I reproduced that and it is the correct behaviour. The defect (XMR-1) is that the fake backend does not fail closed the same way.
- [Monero side and the hot-wallet question (directive §2, §4-XMR, §20, §22): tools/xmrswap/monero.go + fakewallet.go + swap.go's Monero paths, tools/vaultsigner's (unbuilt) Monero backend, and the XMR deposit/withdrawal test matrix] The vaultsigner Backend interface is well specified for the backend nobody has written: Verify() re-fetches a deposit at vote time, VerifyRelease() makes a signer confirm its own payment before voting Released, Finalized() separates confirmations from finality, and Health() is required to fail rather than guess (main.go:75-97). tools/vaultsigner/exactly_once_test.go and lapsed_mark_test.go PASS. Nothing about the interface needs changing to build the Monero backend — only the implementation is missing.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] The ZIP 244 digest tree is correct beyond the vectors, not just on them. transparent_sig_digest is built with the right personalization (ZTxIdTranspaHash) over hash_type, prevouts, amounts (ZTxTrAmountsHash), scriptpubkeys (ZTxTrScriptsHash), sequence, outputs and txin (Zcash___TxInHash) in that order, matching ZIP 244 as fetched; the amounts and scriptpubkeys digests cover every input, not just the signed one; the empty-Sapling digest is the personalized hash of the empty string; and the vectors that exercise it (4, 5, 6) have two transparent inputs each and a non-empty Orchard bundle, so the multi-input and Orchard paths are genuinely pinned — the architecture map's worry that only single-input transparent-only cases were covered is wrong. serialize(parse(tx)) is byte-equal to the wire form on every vector the package accepts.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] Signing holds up. Over 400 random digests, decred's ecdsa.Sign produced only strict-DER, low-S signatures with SIGHASH_ALL appended; ParseKey refuses a zero scalar and one at or above the curve order rather than reducing it; VerifySig refuses a signature against a different digest and refuses anything whose last byte is not SIGHASH_ALL. SigDigest refuses every hash type but SIGHASH_ALL, so there is no ANYONECANPAY, NONE or SINGLE surface at all — the right choice for an HTLC and the one that keeps the amounts and outputs committed.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] The in-tree BLAKE2b is right: RFC 7693's BLAKE2b-512("abc") vector passes, the block-boundary cases (empty, exactly 128 bytes, 129 bytes) are distinct and handled with the final flag where the spec requires it, the personalization occupies parameter-block bytes 48-63, and every ZIP 244 vector that passes is a second independent pin on it. Writing it in-tree rather than adding a dependency is justified as docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §7 says: a hash has no key and no timing surface.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] Transparent address handling is correct where it is implemented: the four version prefixes (mainnet t1 1cb8 / t3 1cbd, testnet tm 1d25 / t2 1cba) round-trip through Base58Check, ParseAddress recovers the network and kind, the mainnet form of the PoC's script hash carries the t3 prefix, and the CLI refuses a P2SH address where the script pays a key ("…is a script address; the swap script pays a key"). Shielded zs/zc/zt and lower-case unified addresses are refused by name with a reason, which is what §8 promises.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] The ZIP 317 arithmetic in docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §8 is right and I checked it against the ZIP: marginal_fee 5,000 zatoshi, grace_actions 2, so the floor is 10,000 zatoshi. Both swap spends land exactly on that floor rather than above it — the claim scriptSig is about 237 bytes (73 sig + 34 pubkey + 33 secret + 1 + 96 redeem) so the input is about 277 bytes and ceiling(277/150) = 2 logical actions, and the refund is about 244 bytes, also 2. There is no hidden fee cliff in this design.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] BL-47's first half is answerable and the answer is good news: ZIP 255 (NU6.1), ZIP 257 (NU6.2) and ZIP 258 (NU6.3) change nothing in transparent script semantics or in the ZIP 244 sighash algorithm — NU6.3 introduces the Ironwood shielded pool and restricts Orchard. CHECKLOCKTIMEVERIFY and P2SH are untouched, so the mechanism this market rests on is intact through the current mainnet epoch.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] The DERO leg's own defences hold under the DVM. swap.bas Claim is bound to the named taker (a stranger holding the right secret is refused, so Z11 is real), a wrong secret is refused, Refund before expiry is refused, the two exits are mutually exclusive through st_<id>, and the escrow is empty after either — TestSwapEscrow and TestSwapBoard, and my own additions, all exercise this on derohe's real dvm.Execute_sc_function. board.bas holds no value, so there is nothing on the board to drain.
- [Zcash side (directive §3, §4-ZEC, §21): tools/zecswap (script/sign/sighash/orchard/blake2 + CLI + vectors), contracts/swap.bas and board.bas as the DERO counterpart of the transparent-ZEC market, docs/ZEC_SWAP.md against the current Zcash protocol spec / ZIP 225 / 244 / 317 / 255 / 257 / 258 / 203 / 320 and BIP 65, and the requirements for the not-yet-built watcher and broadcast layer.] The dependency answer for secp256k1 is honest and the smaller primitive genuinely will not do: constant-time field and scalar arithmetic plus RFC 6979 nonces are exactly what a hand-rolled curve gets wrong invisibly, and the package is pure Go with no transitive dependency and no network. No new dependency was added by anything I found; every fix I propose is standard-library or in-tree.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] pool.bas arithmetic holds. Every product of two possibly-large numbers goes through muldiv/muldivup (128-bit, PANIC rather than wrap), and I checked each remaining line by hand: the credit guards at 63, 140 and 232 catch a wrapping deposit because the contract's own balance is already below 2^64 before the incoming value is added; out = muldiv(deff, ra, rd+deff) is strictly less than ra and out = muldiv(aeff, rd, ra+aeff) strictly less than rd, so `ra - out` and `rd - out` cannot underflow; pf <= fee <= d, so `ra + a - pf` cannot; bpsup cannot wrap on r+1 because r == 2^64-1 needs f == 10000 and fee/pfee are bounded at 100/10000 where the remainder is exactly 0.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] pool.bas has no donation path, so the classic first-depositor share-price inflation attack does not exist: rd and ra move only through the four entrypoints, value attached to ClaimFees lands outside rd/ra and can never be counted, and AddLiquidity reverts when the minted LP would be 0 (line 92). The LP counter is exact: circulating LP == lp - 1000 always, and RemoveLiquidity's `lp - l >= 1000` means you can never burn more than circulates. The dust donated on the non-binding side of AddLiquidity is bounded by one LP unit's worth, i.e. about one quote atomic unit.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] launch.bas curve invariants hold across a round trip, which is what stops a creator inflating supply with their premine. A phase-0 sell puts tokens back in the contract and decrements `sold`, so rd - real == vd0 and ra + sold == vt0 both survive, and the curve simply re-emits what it took in: maximum circulating stays alloc + cs + Lq == supply. The graduation branch is safe by construction too — ra - rem == vt0 - cs, and vt0 > cs for every admissible (supply >= 1e6, grad in [1e6, 1e15], cbps <= 500) because vd0 = grad/3 >= 333333 — so `muldivup(rem, rd, ra - rem)` can neither divide by zero nor wrap, and the overshoot refund is the last statement of the function (the BC-1 fix).
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] launch.bas dist()/settle() and founders.bas distribute()/settle() cannot brick or over-pay. The clamp `stot * 4294967295` / `hst * 4294967295` is only reachable when mulhi(pool, 2^32) >= hst, which forces hst < 2^32, so the product cannot wrap; `paid`/`covered` are ceil(inc*hst/2^32) <= the clamped pool <= the stored pool, so `tacc - paid` and `und + x - covered` never underflow; and pend = st*(acc-sd)/2^32 is bounded by the total ever distributed, so its muldiv cannot panic. hd_/he_ are written before the send in both, so no double claim.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] Every LOAD with a dynamic key in pool.bas, launch.bas, founders.bas, nft.bas, vault.bas, swap.bas, board.bas and htlc.bas is guarded by an EXISTS or by construction — I walked them individually (st_/sd_/se_/sea_ in launch; hu_/hd_/he_/ea_/h_/hi_/ls_/lp_/lu_ in founders; lk_/lh_/lm_ in nft; ua_/da_/dver_/bond_/sg_/mw_/w_*/fu_ in vault; the m_/t_/h_/a_/v_/e_/st_ families behind EXISTS("st_"+id) in swap, board and htlc). contracts/nfa.bas charityDonateAddr is the single exception in the fleet, and that is DVM-2.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] SIGNER() fails closed above ring 2 everywhere it authorises or pays. I checked each: launch ClaimTax (creator != 33 zero bytes), nft ClaimCreator, factory SetTreasury/SetRegFee/TransferOwnership/ClaimOwnership, every vault signer path through issigner (isg_ of 33 zero bytes never exists), vault ClaimFees (treasury), founders Mint/Register/Withdraw/Claim/List/Unlist/Buy and pool/launch's non-To swaps (IS_ADDRESS_VALID(zeros) == 0). The permissionless entrypoints that DO run at any ring — pool/launch/nft ClaimFees, launch Buyback/Distribute, founders Deposit/Distribute/PayBatch, vault Burn/Reclaim — all pay an address recorded earlier, never the caller.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] founders.bas cannot exceed its supply. maxsupply is written once at line 32 and has no setter; no contract in contracts/ contains UPDATE_SC_CODE (grepped); the only two paths that mint the pass are the install reserve at line 52, bounded by `reserve > maxsupply / 5` at line 18, and Mint at line 69, bounded by `n > maxsupply - minted` at line 65 with `n * price` unable to wrap (n <= 1e5, price <= 1e13). Register/Withdraw/List/Unlist/Buy only move units the contract physically received, and sum(hu_) == hst and contract units == hst + sum(lu_) hold statement by statement.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] vault.bas Slash's `bt * LOAD("capratio")` at line 567 cannot wrap: line 566 only reaches it when cap/capratio > bt, which forces bt*capratio <= cap - capratio < cap < 2^63. Burn's bigbps decomposition at line 388 is the same exact-bps form as pool's and cannot wrap either, and `v - f` is positive because InitializePrivate line 61 requires wfee < minburn <= v.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] Duplicate calls in one block are harmless where I looked: tick() writes one price point per active block and skips the second call; the vault's vote scopes all key on BLOCK_HEIGHT()/2000 so two votes in one block land in the same scope as intended; nfa.bas's one-bid-per-address-per-timestamp rule refunds the second bid rather than eating it; launch Buyback's 20-block bbh spacing and founders Mint are order-independent.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] A single unverified destination fails closed. When a call emits exactly one transfer and its destination is unregistered, SanityCheckExternalTransfers returns that Get's error and the whole call reverts with the ring-2 signer refunded — which is why vault.bas Veto, Reclaim, Mint, Register and Slash, factory reg, chat Post and every pool/launch/founders/nft/htlc/swap payout are safe from DVM-1. Re-ran the other finder's TestBurnRefundToAnUnregisteredAddressCanNeverBeReturned to confirm on the real DVM.
- [DERO contracts and DVM semantics — make it lose money (directive §6, §13, §17)] tests/sendorder_test.go's property still holds: no contract sends before a failure exit except the documented factory.bas reg allow-list. I re-derived it by hand for pool AddLiquidity and buyto/sellto, launch's graduation branch (stores at 62-67, sends at 68 and 76, no GOTO 900 after), founders Buy/Mint/Withdraw and nft Mint.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] No DOM injection sink outside one numeric tooltip. `grep -n "innerHTML|outerHTML|insertAdjacentHTML|document.write|eval(|new Function|createContextualFragment|srcdoc|javascript:" app/*.js` returns exactly one code hit: chart.js:132, whose interpolations are S.fmtPriceSub / S.fmtDeroShort / toFixed / a locale date - numbers only, no contract string reaches it. ui.js h() builds elements with createElement/setAttribute and appends only text nodes (ui.js:7-16), so every on-chain name, description, ticker, tag, chat message and NFA header is inert by construction.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The CSP is enforced and blocks inline script: the UI pass's first line is `CSP: inline script blocked - 1 violation report(s)`. script-src 'self', object-src 'none', base-uri 'none', form-action 'none', frame-src 'none'.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] Zero runtime dependencies in the app. The only absolute URLs in app/*.js are the SVG namespace and placeholder "https://..." strings; the only sockets are ws://localhost:44326/xswd and the node endpoint. The Go tools carry three direct dependencies, each justified in-tree and each doing something no smaller primitive covers: filippo.io/edwards25519 v1.1.1 (ed25519 scalar/point arithmetic for the Monero joint key - the stdlib exposes no group operations), golang.org/x/crypto v0.33.0 (sha3.LegacyKeccak256 for Monero addresses and ripemd160 for Zcash HASH160 - neither is in the stdlib), github.com/decred/dcrd/dcrec/secp256k1/v4 v4.3.0 (low-S DER signing on secp256k1 for Zcash transparent inputs - crypto/ecdsa has neither the curve nor low-S). x/sys is indirect.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The app never asks the wallet for anything but reads, a balance and a transfer. The complete set requested over XSWD is GetAddress, GetDaemon, GetBalance, GetHeight, transfer, Subscribe(new_topoheight), plus DERO.* proxied reads (xswd.js:65-66, 113-117). There is no seed, key-export, sign-message or spend-authority request anywhere.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] What the sheet shows is what the wallet signs, for the values under the app's control. app.tx builds `params` once (app.js:152) from the same opts the sheet passed; nothing between the click and W.transfer can alter dest, amount, min_out or the entrypoint. The only value the node chooses is the DERO burn destination (app.js:120-122), and it receives amount: 0 and is rejected unless it parses as a valid address. The remaining gap is temporal, not substitutional - that is FE-4.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The ring size is chosen correctly and disclosed on every sheet. Ring 2 is the default; ring 16 is used only for the ...To trade entrypoints and vault Reclaim, both of which pay a named address and never read SIGNER(); the gas estimate correctly omits `signer` at ring 16 (app.js:134). The trade sheet prints "Ring size 2 - the payer is public" or "16 - the payer is hidden" in the cost block before the button (visible in .sim/ui-audit-frontend/05-trade-sheet.png).
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] No prototype pollution from node JSON. Ran a hostile stringkeys payload containing "__proto__" and "constructor" through V.node.getSC(): Object.prototype is untouched (values are BigInt or string, so the __proto__ setter ignores them) and rpc.js's `vals` object is keyed only by app-supplied names. The veil.settings merge can have its own prototype swapped by a same-origin writer, but every setting the app reads is an own property of the defaults object and so shadows it, and daemon and slippage are re-validated on read (app.js:12-13).
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] Amount handling is exact integer end to end. parseUnits accepts only /^\d*\.?\d*$/ and builds a BigInt from padded digits (amm.js:117-121); jsonBig serialises BigInt as a bare integer literal so nothing above 2^53 passes through a Number on the way to the wallet (rpc.js:61-66); toBig refuses anything that is not a safe integer or a 1-20 digit decimal string (rpc.js:116-121). Floats appear only in display paths (priceOf, fmtCompact, impact, the chart) and priceOf now takes the market so the quote's decimals cannot be forgotten. The two existing amm tests covering 5/8/12-decimal quotes pass.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The template pipeline is intact for what it covers. sha256sum of all five app/templates/*.bas equals config.js templates[].sha256 exactly, and forms.js:15 re-hashes the fetched template before installing. The CSP's connect-src would block a template fetched from any host other than the app's own origin.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] Endpoint validation is sound and the remote-node gate works as designed. endpointURL (rpc.js:99-111) accepts host:port and ws:// only for 127.0.0.1/localhost and forces wss:// for anything else; it is applied to the stored setting, the wallet's GetDaemon and the shipped default alike, and re-applied on read. A stored non-loopback endpoint additionally needs a per-session sessionStorage confirmation, and sessionStorage is not inherited across the port boundaries TELA serves apps on, so the FE-7 storage attack cannot reach it.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The localStorage cache is not a trust path. Cached market records are shape-checked field by field (feed.js:180-190, including a canonAddress check on every trade's author), the newest VERIFY_N points and trades are re-read from the chain and byte-compared before the rest is used, and a mismatch drops the copy and re-reads the window (feed.js:250-259). Local trades are shape-checked on every read (validLocalTrade, feed.js:412-414).
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The deep-link path for an unlisted-but-verified contract fails closed: token.js renders it with a distinct "Code verified - not listed" badge, and app.tx's allow-list (app.js:117) refuses to build any transaction for it.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] Legacy pool templates are not a quoting hazard: the v1 pool at 39cdae3f... (config legacy) has the same bpsup/bps fee arithmetic as the current template and no `qt` key, which feed.js correctly reads as a DERO quote. The legacy problem is specific to launch (FE-1).
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] The trade sheet meets requirement 3 as written: trading fee, protocol share, token tax, curve refund, price impact, minimum received and the slippage cap are each stated in the market's own quote symbol, with the DERO network fee on a separate row estimated by the node against a random signer (sheets.js:36-49). No screen sums across quotes - app.loadHoldings deliberately keeps per-quote totals and app.js:96-98 records why.
- [TELA, frontend, wallet interaction, supply chain (directive §15, §16, §26, §35)] NFA metadata is handled defensively: fileURL and fileSignURL pass through U.safeUrl (https only, <=300 chars, no whitespace/quotes/angles/backslash) before becoming links, and are labelled "not verified against the hashes by this app"; images load only when the user opts in, with referrerpolicy no-referrer. The two known residuals - safeUrl accepting a userinfo prefix, and the look-alike fold never being applied to NFAs - are already documented and pinned by tests/js/sanitize.test.mjs and are not re-reported here.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] swap.bas liveness holds unconditionally. Refund (contracts/swap.bas:81-97) requires only BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= e_<id> and SIGNER() == m_<id>, with NO upper bound — unlike htlc.bas there is no closing edge, so every lock has a permanent exit for the maker, and the maker is by construction a registered ring-2 signer. A malformed or uppercase hash makes Claim impossible but leaves Refund untouched. Read in full; no state needs any party beyond the maker who put the value in.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] vault.bas Halt really is a lease and really never blocks the exit. Halt(1) stores BLOCK_HEIGHT()+20000 (vault.bas:515) and ishalted() (vault.bas:41) compares against the current height, so a federation that stops answering cannot leave the vault halted; Burn (vault.bas:375), Released, Paying, Veto and Reclaim contain no halt check at all. Asserted by tests/vault_statemachine_test.go TestHaltMatrix and TestAHaltIsALeaseAndNeverBlocksTheExit, both green in my full-suite run.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] An UNMARKED vault burn genuinely returns itself. Reclaim (vault.bas:475-485) reads no SIGNER(), accepts any ring size, pays only w_<j>_by and needs only BLOCK_HEIGHT() >= w_<j>_rel + 20000. tests/vault_statemachine_test.go TestAnUnsettledBurnReclaimsItselfUnlessMarked and TestReclaimIsPermissionlessAndRingAgnostic pass. LIV-03 is precisely that this property is destroyed by the Paying mark, not that it is absent.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] No contract in the tree can ever be rewritten: grep -rn 'UPDATE_SC_CODE' contracts/ returns nothing across all twelve files. There is no upgrade path, no proxy and no admin code-swap anywhere, so the immutability app/notice.js claims is real.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] founders.bas needs no privileged party to pay anyone. Deposit, Distribute and PayBatch (contracts/founders.bas:137, 149, 158) are callable by anyone at any ring size, and Claim/Withdraw are the holder's own; there is no owner key in the contract at all and maxsupply is written once at line 32 with no setter. Rewards can always be pushed to holders by a stranger, so no reward state waits on a human. (That nothing on chain forces the treasury to DEPOSIT is a different dimension and is honestly stated in app/notice.js.)
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] vault.bas Slash cannot overflow the cap it rewrites. Line 567 computes bt * capratio only on the branch where cap/capratio > bt (line 566), which bounds bt*capratio below cap, and cap is capped at 2^63-1 at install (line 58). I checked this specifically because a wrapped cap would be a silent mint-ceiling change; it cannot happen.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] vault.bas sup cannot underflow at Released. Burn requires v <= sup (vault.bas:384) and burned units physically leave circulation into the contract, so the sum of open claims can never exceed sup — I worked through the interleavings of two pending burns plus a Veto and a re-burn and found no ordering that lets Released subtract more than sup holds. tests/vault_fuzz_test.go's sup == minted - releasedNet invariant passes in the full run.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] The 1,000 LP units locked at first deposit (contracts/pool.bas:110, lp - l < 1000) and launch.bas's post-graduation reserves having no RemoveLiquidity are deliberate permanent locks, documented as such, and not defects — I looked for an unintended second lock and found none: RemoveLiquidity's two sends are its last statements and its arithmetic is muldiv-guarded.
- [DERO contracts — funds stuck forever, and states that need a human (directive §5, §29, LIVENESS BY CONSTRUCTION)] The whole DVM suite is green with my nine adversarial cases added: ok github.com/deroproject/derohe/veil/tests 64.778s. Nothing I wrote perturbs an existing assertion.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] The three exits are mutually exclusive and exhaustive on chain, and each leaves the contract empty. htlc.bas gates every exit on st_<id> == 0 and writes st_ before any send, and every SEND_* is after the last GOTO 900. Evidence: tools/withlock.sh dvm bash tests/run.sh -run 'Htlc|NoExternalSend' — TestHtlcClaim/Refund/Punish/LockRefusals/AssetRoundTrip and TestNoExternalSendBeforeAFailureExit all PASS; and the live simulator run left the contract holding 0 with st_0 = 1.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] 'After Punish the XMR stays under the joint key' is a CRYPTOGRAPHIC claim and it holds. Punish (htlc.bas:120-136) stores no secret; the taker holds s_a and never learned s_b, the maker holds s_b and never learned s_a, and the joint output needs s_a + s_b. Neither companion attempts a sweep from a punished outcome (both roles call finish("punished") with no route to sweep_ready). The claims for the other two exits are cryptographic too: Claim publishes s_a so the maker can form s_a + s_b, Refund publishes s_b so the taker can. What is NOT cryptographic: that the maker actually receives XMR after a Claim (that is the fake wallet's sweep — BL-11 and XMR-08 territory) and that either party is alive in its window (liveness — the maker from lock to gate and again in [t1, t2), the taker from the lock announcement to the claim cutoff).
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] Invariant 2 holds under crash: k_b is released only when the maker's OWN view-only wallet shows the agreed amount at the configured confirmations, unlocked, with unlock_time == 0, capped by the wallet's unlocked balance (swap.go confirmedAtJoint 509-547). Nothing the taker says enters the number — her xmr_locked message is consumed and explicitly not acted on (swap.go:695-702). The fuzz's checkSend hook re-reads the fake node at the moment of every gate send and fails the test otherwise; TestCrashFuzz -count=3 passed.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] Invariant 5 holds for the sweep and, within one process, for the Monero lock: every irreversible action is preceded by a durable phase save (fsync + rename, state.go:153-183), and a restart that finds a payment intent without a result never repeats it (sweep() 552-576, xmr_locking 1073-1080). The fuzz kills the process after every durable step of all three exits and asserts at most one transfer and one sweep. The gap is across processes, not across restarts — that is XMR-04.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] Invariant 13 holds: every generated secret is recorded in xmrswap-used.json by SHA-256 before it is used, the register is re-read and merged before each write, and a collision from another swap ends the swap as 'refused' (state.go:194-259, swap.go refuseIfReused). The fuzz includes a prover that repeats itself.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] Invariant 12 holds: every contract call is built at ring size 2 by the client itself, never inherited from a wallet default (dero.go submit(), "ringsize": 2), and TestDeroClientShapesAndExits asserts it on every request shape.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] The rendezvous channel cannot cause fund loss, which I traced rather than assumed. Every value that matters is re-derived from a proof or from the chain: a tampered offer fails the terms check (swap.go:970-983); a tampered setup fails verifyPeer against the proof (swap.go:389-410); a tampered dero_locked only names an id, and CheckLock then tests ha, hk, hb, taker, both windows, the amount and the asset against what this side already knows (swap.go:898-916); a tampered gate is checked against hk as the CHAIN holds it, not as the message claims, and is un-consumed if it fails (swap.go:1119-1126). A man in the middle who substitutes the maker's setup must fund a lock of the full agreed amount himself and ends up as the counterparty at the agreed price, not as a thief.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] The taker not checking l.Maker is safe, and I checked why rather than leaving it. Refund can only be sent by whoever the lock records as maker, the lock pays the taker regardless of who funded it, and every field that binds the lock to this swap's key material is checked. Nothing is gained by a third party funding a lock with these hashes.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] htlc.bas Lock's refusals are complete and fail closed: signer valid (ring 2 only), three 64-character hashes, now+1 < t1 < t2 <= now+1,000,000, a required and decodable taker, and exactly one of DERO or a single 32-byte asset with a non-zero value. HEXDECODE panics on non-hex, which reverts the call and changes nothing. TestHtlcLockRefusals enumerates them.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] The companion's own gate margin is consistent with the taker's claim cutoff: the maker releases k_b only while h + GateMargin < t1 with GateMargin defaulting to StableBlocks + ClaimMargin + 2, and the taker claims while h + 1 < t1 - 8 - ClaimMargin, so a gate that arrives leaves her at least one block. (The inconsistency is elsewhere, inside the re-send loop — XMR-06.)
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] LoadState is strict where it does check: a corrupt file, a file for another role and a file for another contract each stop the companion rather than being replayed (state.go:125-149). The gap is the DERO address, which it does not check — XMR-05.
- [The DERO↔XMR trustless swap as a state machine under failure (contracts/htlc.bas + tools/xmrswap + tools/simhtlc) — directive §5, §6, §24, §25, §44] Duplicated and out-of-order channel deliveries within one swap do not cause loss: recv drops kinds already in Consumed (swap.go:246-273), and the phases that read messages are ordered so that every kind a later phase could see has already been consumed. A gate re-sent for the whole [gate_sent, t1) window is noise, not a hazard, because k_b is already in the taker's hands. The dangerous case is two swaps in one directory — XMR-11 — and even there the taker refuses before her XMR moves.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] The DVM-BASIC arithmetic that actually runs on chain is exact. I installed the helper block of contracts/pool.bas verbatim (mulhi, nlz, divlu, muldiv, muldivup, bps, bpsup — the same block copied into launch.bas and founders.bas) and compared it with math/big through the real DVM over 21,824 triples: the full 23^3 edge table, 4,000 triples deliberately in the only regime that reaches divlu at all (a*b >= 2^64 with a quotient that still fits), and 4,000 random; plus nlz over all 128 power-of-two and mask shapes. Zero disagreements (tests/zzdvmarith_test.go, PASS in 48.9 s). This had never been tested directly before — reference/uint128.go was checked against math/big, and the .bas only reached through entrypoints — so divlu's two correction loops were previously unexercised. They are correct.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] muldiv fails closed rather than wrapping. `IF hi >= c THEN PANIC()` (pool.bas line 389) covers c == 0 as well as any result that would not fit in 64 bits, and muldivup's remainder test `a*b - q*c == 0` is exact under wrapping because the true remainder is below c. muldivup panics rather than wrapping when the ceiling would be 2^64 (line 403). Confirmed against math/big at every edge.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] bps and bpsUp cannot overflow for any f <= 10000, which is the only range any caller uses. (x/10000)*f + ((x%10000)*f)/10000 is bounded above by x itself, so it fits by construction; every call site is bounded at install — pool/launch fee <= 100 and pfee <= 10000 (pool.bas:40-50, launch.bas:29), launch tax <= 1000 (launch.bas:26), founders mfee <= 1000 and share <= 10000 (founders.bas:24), nft fee <= 100, nfa protoFee <= 10000 (nfa.bas:260), vault bigbps <= 10000 (vault.bas:26). Verified exact against math/big over 20,000 random x including MAX_UINT64.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] pool.bas's quote-balance overflow guard is exact, not approximate. A buy that fills rd + fd to exactly 18,446,744,073,709,551,615 is accepted; one atomic unit more is refused; MAX_UINT64 is refused (tests/zzextreme_test.go TestPoolQuoteBalanceNearWrap, PASS). Every reserve credit in pool.bas and launch.bas carries the same shape of guard, and each catches at most one wrap because the accounting counters plus the incoming value cannot exceed 2^65.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] app/amm.js is a faithful BigInt mirror of the two swap contracts. I traced launchBuy against launch.bas buyto line by line — including the graduating-overshoot branch (contract lines 44-59), the recomputation of fee and tax on `used` rather than on `d`, and the untaxed refund — and launchSell, poolBuy, poolSell, poolAdd and poolRemove against theirs, then replayed 21 vectors printed by the real DVM at 2^62-scale reserves, at the 1% fee ceiling, on a pool drained to rd=1, and at a 1.4e18 input: every accept/refuse decision and every `out` matched to the atomic unit (tests/js/amm.vectors.test.mjs, PASS). The one guard amm.js omits — the contract's `IF need > d` in AddLiquidity — is mathematically unreachable (when ld <= la, a >= ceil(ld*ra/lp) holds for integer a), so the omission changes nothing.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] No money crosses a float anywhere it matters. tests/run.sh gates on a grep for float32/float64 in reference/, tests/harness, treasurybot and vaultsigner. In the browser, exact values reach the app as decimal strings through DERO.GetSC's keysstring/valuesstring (vendor/derohe/cmd/derod/rpc/rpc_dero_getsc.go:174 formats them with %d) and are validated by feed.js:14's /^\d+$/ before BigInt; the JSON.parse round trip that would round anything above 2^53 only ever touches the `variables:true` whole-tree read, which is used solely for the social contract (feed.js:368). Wallet balances go through V.toBig (rpc.js:117-122), which refuses a Number that is not a safe integer rather than rounding it. Outgoing JSON is built by jsonBig (rpc.js:62-68), so a BigInt amount reaches the wallet as a bare integer literal. In Go, tools/simrpc decodes with UseNumber and ParseUint (simrpc.go:283-302), tools/xmrswap/dero.go the same (dero.go:155), and every Monero amount decodes into a typed uint64 struct field (monero.go:405-411, 437).
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] nfa.bas's settlement bound is exactly right and not a guess: 1,844,674,407,370,955 is floor((2^64-1)/10000), so protoFee * saleAmt at line 114 is the largest product and it cannot wrap; royalty, artificerFee and charity are each at most 100 and multiply by saleAmt/100, which is far smaller. The four shares each floor independently and their sum cannot exceed saleAmt because init line 500 and Start's clamp (lines 150-170) together keep (royalty + artificerFee + charity)*100 + protoFee <= 10000, so the owner's remainder at line 120 cannot underflow. Start's tempPercCount subtraction cannot underflow for the same reason.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] vault.bas's cap recomputation on Slash cannot wrap. Line 51 stores bt * capratio, but line 50 only reaches it when cap/capratio > bt, so bt <= floor(cap/capratio) - 1 and bt*capratio < cap <= 2^63-1. Burn's bigbps threshold (line 30) is the same exact bps decomposition as pool.bas's, with bigbps bounded at 10000 by init. Mint's supply check (line 41) tests sup + amount < sup as well as > cap. Register, Bond and Unbond all carry their own wrap guards.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] The two 2^32 reward accumulators are safe against both the wrap and the clamp. In founders.bas distribute (lines 24-25) and launch.bas dist (lines 23-24), the clamp `pool = hst * 4294967295` is only reached when mulhi(pool, 2^32) >= hst, i.e. when hst < 2^32, so the product cannot wrap; the era rollover then credits the full increment in the new era rather than the wrapped sum, which is why the arithmetic across a boundary is exact. tests/saturation_test.go proves both on chain across three eras. settle's `covered`/`paid` ceiling can never exceed the pool it came from, so tacc and und cannot underflow.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] CompactSize round-trips every uint64 exactly: 60 s of coverage-guided fuzzing, 6,654,518 executions, zero failures, and the encoder always consumes exactly the bytes it wrote.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] The app's slippage path cannot produce a negative or an unreasonable min_out. settings.slippage is validated as an integer in 1..1000 bps at app.js:12, minOut subtracts a ceiling that is bounded by the value itself, and app.trade refuses outright if min_out falls below 90% of the quote (app.js:201). Verified over 20,000 random values at every slippage the app offers.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] parseUnits and fmtUnits round trip losslessly at 0, 5, 8 and 12 decimals across 5,010 values including 0, 1, 2^53, 2^53+1, MAX_UINT64-1 and MAX_UINT64. The one lossy behaviour is deliberate and safe: parseUnits truncates fractional digits beyond the asset's decimals rather than refusing, always downward, and the truncated value is what the preview then shows.
- [Integer/precision and fuzzing (directive §17, §43)] launch.bas at the top of its supply range behaves. supply = 9,223,372,036,854,775,807 with cbps 500, tax 1000, fee 100 and pfee 10000 installs, sells the curve from a 1-unit dust buy through graduation, and switches to the AMM with reference/launch.go agreeing at every step — including the graduating buy, whose overshoot refund and recomputed fee the model matches exactly (tests/zzextreme_test.go TestLaunchExtremeCurve, PASS). vt0 = muldiv(grad + grad/3, c, grad) cannot panic at any permitted (supply, grad): its high word is 1.333*c/2^64, below 1 for every c the supply bound allows.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] No key material anywhere. I swept the working tree and all 71 commits of the published history for mnemonics (25-word DERO seed shapes), PEM private keys, ghp_/github_pat_/AKIA tokens and password literals: zero hits. Every match for 'private key' / 'secret key' is an identifier or a comment in tools/zecswap/sign.go, tools/xmrswap/keys.go and the docs. .gitignore correctly excludes .testnet/, .mainnet/, *.seed, *.db, *.db.bak, tools/vaultsigner-*.json and research/crosschain/poc/sim-*.json.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] Only one hard-coded non-simulator address exists in any contract: the Civilware artificer address dero1qy0khp9s9yw2h0eu20xmy9lth3zp5cacmx3rwt6k45l568d2mmcf6qgcsevzx at contracts/nfa.bas:60 and app/templates/nfa.bas:25, which is the upstream ART-NFA-MS1 standard's own value and is an open operator decision (D-4, default artificerFee 0). Every other dero1/deto1 string in the tree is a simulator or private-testnet wallet in a test, a log or a runbook.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] No DMZ contract can ever be updated. `grep -rn UPDATE_SC_CODE contracts/ app/templates/` returns nothing — the twelve DVM contracts have no code-update path. The only UPDATE_SC_CODE in the tree is upstream TELA-INDEX-1.bas:57, and TELA-DOC-1.bas has none, so every published app file is immutable once installed and only the INDEX pointer is mutable.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] Vault owner powers are genuinely dead after Seal, and this is verifiable on chain by reading one key. Every owner-gated path is guarded by `LOAD("sealed") != 0`: SetLimits at vault.bas:104, AddSigner at :157, Seal itself at :246. After Seal there is no path in the file that reads `owner` at all. This is the only privilege in the whole system with a real, checkable revocation.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] Every privileged check fails closed above ring size 2. SIGNER() is 33 zero bytes there, IS_ADDRESS_VALID rejects it and ADDRESS_STRING returns "". So `SIGNER() != LOAD("owner")` (factory.bas:29,39,47), `issigner(s)` (vault.bas:46-51 via isg_<raw>) and `s != LOAD("treasury")` (vault.bas:491) all refuse rather than pass. I attacked ClaimFees specifically and it held: ADV-3 shows it refused for both signers, the vault owner, the burner and an unrelated stranger, and succeeded only for the treasury.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] The vault's arithmetic around the privileged paths held under attack. Slash's `bt = bondtot - b` cannot underflow, because AddSigner (:33-34) and Bond (:32-34) only ever add an ACTIVE signer's bond to bondtot, and Unbond clamps (:27-29). Slash's `cap = bt * capratio` (:51) cannot overflow, because line 50 only reaches it when bt < cap/capratio, so the product is < cap < 2^63. Unbond after AddSigner leaves bond_ at 0, which Seal then catches at :32 (`bond < minbond` -> refuse), so a signer cannot seal a vault with a withdrawn bond.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] The vote helper is sound against the privilege attacks I tried. votek (vault.bas:13-35) closes a scope with <f>d_<kb> and moves a vote by decrementing the old counter (:30-33), so one signer cannot double-count; and because a RETURN 1 after votek reverts the whole call (the DVM discards non-zero returns, vendor/derohe/dvm/sc.go:271-283), a k-th vote that then fails Mint's cap/window checks leaves the scope open rather than burning it. AddSigner2 re-checks the candidate's bond at the k-th vote (:24-25), so a candidate cannot Unbond between the first and last vote and still be admitted; and a slashed key can never return, because :23 refuses any address for which isg_ merely EXISTS.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] The Halt lease behaves as documented: it is a 20,000-block lease (vault.bas:515) that lapses on its own, it never blocks Burn, Released, Veto, Paying or Reclaim, and Halt(0) is refused only when the vault is not halted (:508) so a lapsed halt needs no resume vote. I confirmed both the Attest block during a halt and the automatic lapse in ADV-2. A federation that simply stops answering cannot leave the vault halted.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] app/notice.js is accurate and complete on privileges. Its 'Who still holds a key' section (:64-74) names all five surviving parties — factory owner, treasury, app publisher, federation, node — states plainly that the factory has no renounce (:67) and that the INDEX is installed at ring 2 and republishable until the key is discarded (:70), and closes by asserting its own primacy over any contradicting screen. Nothing I found contradicts it except app/screens.js:266 and :262 (SEC-K-13).
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] tools/publish.sh handles the GitHub credential carefully in every respect but argv: it reads it via `git credential fill`, never writes it to disk, never echoes it, sends it only to api.github.com, and refuses to proceed unless the API login equals the owner argument (:18).
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] tools/xmrswap keeps a used-secrets register (state.go:194-259) that hashes every generated secret and refuses reuse across swaps (ErrSecretReused, Invariant 13), and keys.go refuses unreduced or zero ed25519 scalars — the two places where a key-handling slip would be silent and expensive.
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] The app never asks the wallet for anything privileged. app/xswd.js requests only GetAddress, GetDaemon, GetBalance, GetHeight, transfer, Subscribe and DERO.* proxy reads — never a seed, a key or a spend authority — and it uses a fresh per-session XSWD app id in sessionStorage with an explicit comment (xswd.js:10-11) that a persistent id would let a later page on the same origin impersonate the app. app.tx additionally refuses any contract outside the verified allow-list, any fee above 1 DERO, and any second transaction while one is in flight (app.js:114-144).
- [Secrets, keys, privileges, emergency controls (directive §27, §28, §29)] deploy/binaries.lock's eleven binary hashes all match the files in vendor/bin exactly; the only mismatches verifyvendor reports are source-tree hashes. And vendor.lock's two declared local changes to derohe — the private-genesis patch to cmd/derod/main.go and one deleted file with a name Windows cannot check out — are both accounted for and match, so the vendored consensus code is otherwise untouched.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] Where prices come from — confirmed, no oracle. Every price in the app is A.priceOf(market, point) = rd/10^quoteDec ÷ ra/10^dec, read from the contract's own reserve keys; the history is the p_<n> series each contract writes itself in tick(). There is no external feed, no TWAP, no admin price setter, and no cross-contract read (the DVM has none). `grep -rn "oracle|price feed" app/ contracts/` finds nothing. The manipulation cost of any price is exactly the depth of the one contract that quotes it, which is the honest property and is what BL-106 asks to be written down.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] pool.bas and launch.bas arithmetic under adversarial input. I re-derived every fee, output and reserve update by hand against the code. Fees round up (bpsup) and outputs round down (muldiv floor) in every path, so k never decreases; the protocol share pf = bps(fee, pfee) is floored and the remainder stays in the reserve, so the LP side can only gain from rounding. Overflow is guarded on every credit against the amount RECEIVED, not the amount used (pool.bas:63, 169, 232; launch.bas:41, 200), which is the correct guard given the node credits the full incoming value before the code runs. AddLiquidity mints l = min(ld, la) with the other side's need computed by muldivup and refuses l == 0 (pool.bas:500), so no zero-value LP mint and no rounding-theft from a later depositor; the ceil-after-floor at pool.bas:340/410 can never exceed the deposit (and is guarded anyway at 350/420). RemoveLiquidity keeps lp - l >= 1000 and its outputs are strictly below the reserves, so rd and ra can never reach zero once seeded — the `rd == 0 || ra == 0` guards in buyto/sellto are belt-and-braces.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] The bonding curve's sell path cannot drain more than was paid in, and its invariants hold. sellto phase 0 requires a <= sold and dout <= real (launch.bas:30, 33), and the invariants rd - real == vd0 and ra + sold == vt0 are preserved by every buy and sell. I verified the boundary case that worried me: a buyer who immediately sells back everything they bought recovers at most what they paid, because out <= deff·vt0/(vd0+deff) makes dout = floor(out·(vd0+deff)/vt0) <= deff — the floors always fall the pool's way.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] Graduation is priced continuously, which removes the obvious instant arbitrage. At sold == cs the curve's marginal price is (4·grad/3)/(c/3) = 5·grad/rest, and the AMM it graduates into opens at rd/ra = real/Lq = grad/(rest/5) = 5·grad/rest — identical. The last curve buyer cannot flip into the fresh AMM for a risk-free profit. The overshoot branch (launch.bas:45-59) recomputes fee and tax on the amount the curve actually consumed and refunds the rest untaxed, and the refund is the LAST send (line 76), after the graduation stores — the BC-1 fix, still correct.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] app/amm.js is a faithful mirror of the contract's overshoot branch. I diffed launchBuy (amm.js:50-71) line by line against launch.bas buyto lines 26-60 including the `used = muldivup(cost, 10000, 10000 - fee - tax) + 1`, the clamp to d, the recomputation of fee/tax on `used`, the `deff >= cost` re-check and the refund — they agree, including the sense of the `out >= rem` branch. So the number on the sheet is the number the contract computes, at the reserves the sheet was given (which is the separate defect ADV-PRICE-03).
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] min_out is enforced on chain in both contracts and on both legs — pool.bas:171 and :234, launch.bas:60 (including the graduating branch, where out is clamped to rem before the check), :203 and :207 — and the app refuses to sign anything below a 10% floor (app.js:9, :201). A front-run that pushes the fill below min_out reverts the whole call, and at ring 2 the attached value is returned. That is the right shape; the defect is the reference it is measured against.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] Buyback's sandwich resistance holds, and its comment is honest. Buyback (launch.bas:282-304) spends d = min(tacc, rd/100) at most once per 20 blocks. The profit of sandwiching a trade of size d with capital X is about d·X/rd; the round-trip fee at the shipped 50 bps is 1%·X; and the contract caps d/rd at exactly 1%. So the attack is break-even at the boundary and loses below it, which is precisely what the comment above the function claims. (It stops holding if a launch is installed with fee = 0, but the app refuses to list a market whose fee differs from cfg.feeBps, feed.js:228.)
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] Split-pair detection on the data layer is correct and complete. pairKey (feed.js:92) folds a launch to its own SCID and a pool to its asset, both against the quote, so a launch and a pool over its token are correctly one pair while the same token against two different quotes correctly is not. depth() (feed.js:97) uses `real` for a phase-0 curve and `rd` otherwise, so the virtual reserve vd0 never inflates a curve's apparent depth, and because the comparison is only ever made inside one pairKey both sides are the same asset at the same decimals — it never becomes the cross-quote comparison it would be wrong to make. ranked() (feed.js:389) drops the thin half, and trending(), movers(), Home and the price tape all use it. Every row that lists a market renders H.mark → U.warnings, whose second tier is "Split pair · thinner", and H.risks names the deepest contract's full SCID inside the trade sheet. The two ranked surfaces that bypass ranked() are ADV-PRICE-09; the machinery itself is right.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] The cross-quote SUM that the previous audit found (home.js:179, vol24 summed across every quote and printed as DERO) is fixed. flowsOf (home.js:77) now groups by D.qsym and the Trade hub's "Flow 24h" read-out prints the largest quote's figure with the rest beneath it (home.js:213-214), with the reason written in the comment. Requirement 2 — never a total that mixes currencies — is met there, and the Portfolio's refusal to invent a cross-quote rate is intact.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] Every cost the trade sheet states is in the asset actually charged, with DERO gas as its own row. token.js:159-176: the trading fee and the protocol share in feeSym (the quote on a buy or on the curve, the token on an AMM sell), the tax and the overshoot refund in the quote, the minimum received in the output asset, and V.sheets.gasRow labelled "Network fee (gas), in DERO" and priced by the node with DERO.GetGasEstimate. On a wrapped-quote market it also says in plain words that the network fee is DERO as well as the quote. That is requirement 3 done properly, and it should not be lost in any fix above.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] The stray-asset and wrong-quote guards on the swap paths hold. buyto/sellto in both contracts refuse a call that carries the wrong asset or stray DERO on a wrapped quote (pool.bas:161-162, 224-225; launch.bas lines 21-23 on both legs), AddLiquidity refuses stray LP (pool.bas:59), and RemoveLiquidity refuses stray quote or asset (pool.bas:116-117). app.trade branches the buy leg on s.quote so a wXMR market is paid in wXMR rather than DERO (app.js:214), which was the bug that used to revert every wrapped-quote buy.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] The app cannot seed a wrapped-quote pool and says so rather than sending a call that would revert. sheets.js:59 sets canAdd = !s.quote and sheets.js:108 explains that a deposit must arrive as two assets in one call, that this build can send only one asset alongside DERO, and that pool.bas would reject the stray DERO and take the network fee for nothing. Failing closed with the reason stated is the right behaviour; the consequence (wrapped-quote AMMs can only come from a launch graduation) belongs in the design notes, not in a revert.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] No liquidity can be pulled out from under a graduated launch. launch.bas has no RemoveLiquidity and no other path that pays out rd or ra; token.js:117 correctly declines to offer the liquidity sheet for a launch. So the answer to "what happens when the liquidity disappears" is, for a launch AMM, that it cannot — the only leakage is the creator's tax under tmode 1, which H.risks reports and PROD·P7-05 already tracks. (The sheet's wording about this is ADV-PRICE-10.)
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] The impersonation and reserved-name guards are strong where it matters most. reservedBy/reservedIn (feed.js:55-79) claim each vault's symbol, its name's first word and "w"+symbol as whole words with look-alikes folded, so a token calling itself WXMR or "Wrapped Monero" is marked "Not real XMR" on every row, in the trade sheet and on the token page — and ui.js:93 correctly demotes the green Verified badge to "Code checked · name is not" so the badge never reads as a blessing of the name. The whole-word rule (a token called Zecret is not flagged) is the right calibration.
- [price, quotes, liquidity, counterparty, front-running, griefing (directive §18, §19, §30, standing requirement 5)] ui.js's rendering path is hostile-input safe for market metadata. h() builds text nodes and setAttribute only; U.clean (ui.js:23) strips C0/C1 controls, bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width and joiner characters and the BOM, with a length cap; U.safeUrl (ui.js:34) admits only https URLs up to 300 characters; images load only when the viewer opts in, with referrerpolicy no-referrer. A creator-supplied ticker, name or tag cannot break out of the row it is drawn in.

## Not performed (59)

- **[Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] Confirming on a real Monero chain that a published federation view key plus da_<j> identifies a depositor's transaction (the mechanism half of PRIV-02).** — why: No Monero backend exists in the tree — tools/vaultsigner exits at main.go:470-476 with 'not implemented yet' for -backend monero, and tools/xmrswap's Monero side has only ever run against the in-process fake wallet (tools/simhtlc refuses to start without -fake-monero, main.go:69-71). The operator runs no stagenet node. Both simulator vaults have `viewkey` empty, so the state this finding is about does not exist anywhere yet. — how: Run monerod --stagenet and monero-wallet-rpc --stagenet --wallet-dir <dir>; create a federation wallet and note its private view key; derive subaddresses for indexes 0..2. On the simulator vault, Register three indexes from three different wallets, Attest each to the matching stagenet subaddress, and pay each subaddress from a separate stagenet wallet. Then, as an outsider holding only what DERO publishes: read `viewkey`, ua_<j> and da_<j> with DERO.GetSC; call monero-wallet-rpc generate_from_keys with the federation address, that view key and a restore height; call get_transfers {in:true}. Assert each returned transfer's subaddress_index and amount match a published da_<j>, and record the resulting (DERO address, Monero subaddress, amount, block) tuples. That output is the finding, made concrete.
- **[Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] Measuring the true anonymity set of a live ring-16 private trade — the check research/RING_PRIVACY.md §6 item 2 has had open since 2026-09-06 ('Three payers to one dest and an explicit Extract_signer check on each tx are still to run').** — why: Read-only wallet access: I was assigned no simulator wallet range and sent no transaction. Every ring-16 statement in the app ('the payer is one of 16 accounts', app/screens.js:272; app/token.js:240) therefore rests on the 2026-09-06 single-transaction probe plus reasoning, not on a measured set. — how: With three funded simulator wallets, make three private buys on one market to one named dest via tools/uitest/ui.mjs --wallet --dest=<addr> (the harness already supports this). For each resulting txid call DERO.GetTransaction decode_as_json; assert `signer` is absent and ring length is 16; dump the 16 member addresses; check whether the node-supplied burn destination (PRIV-07) is among them; and check whether the three transactions' rings overlap in a way that narrows the payer across them. Append the transcript to research/ringprobe.log and close §6 item 2.
- **[Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] A UI pass over the rendered screens to confirm the copy findings as a user meets them (PRIV-01, PRIV-02, PRIV-03).** — why: The UI harness takes the shared lock and runs one pass at a time, and my copy findings are all reproducible from the source strings and line numbers I quoted verbatim. Running it would have added screenshots, not evidence. — how: tools/withlock.sh ui env PW_DIR=<playwright> node tools/uitest/ui.mjs http://127.0.0.1:8082/index.html .sim/ui-privacy — then read the PNGs for the intro sheet (app.js:268), Settings 'What publishing reveals' (screens.js:272), the trade sheet ring block (token.js:172-175) and the wrap trust panel (wrap.js:336-346), and confirm each rendered sentence against the quotes in this report.
- **[Privacy leaks (directive §19, §22) — what each flow writes on which chain, who can link what, and how the app's copy compares] Auditing the privacy of the transparent-Zcash market (board.bas/swap.bas) and the DERO↔XMR swap (htlc.bas) as a user would meet them.** — why: There is no screen: nothing in app/ references board, swap or htlc, and app/app.js:117's allow-list would refuse a call to either SCID, so there is no user-facing copy to compare the leaks against (BL-13 open, BL-43 open). The contract-level leaks are real and already documented in docs/ZEC_SWAP.md §1 — board.bas publishes the maker's Zcash t-address (wa_<i>) and the taker's refund key hash (rp_<i>) on DERO at Accept, before any value moves, with both parties ring-2 signers; swap.bas Claim stores the preimage x_<id>, which joins the DERO swap to the Zcash spend — but with no screen there is nothing to test and nothing to contradict. — how: When BL-13 lands, re-run this dimension against the swap screen: assert that before the Accept button the copy names, in the user's words, the four linked identities (both DERO addresses via ring 2, the maker's t-address, the taker's pkh), the amount to the zatoshi, the hash H that joins the two chains, and any unshielding the taker performed to fund the P2SH — that is the BL-43 disclosure. Drive it with tools/simswap against the simulator and read the rendered screen.
- **[Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] A wallet-connected pass over the Wrap screen — Register, the published deposit address, "Your withdrawals" and the Reclaim button.** — why: I was assigned read-only wallets and may not send. This matters more than it normally would: a parallel reader reports a CONFIRMED live defect that app/wrap.js:54-121 requests keys as 'uc_' + the dero1 *string* while vault.bas:274-278 stores them under the raw 33-byte address, so none of the per-user rows can ever resolve. If that holds, the entire deposit walkthrough dead-ends at step 1 for every user and my UX-01 finding is downstream of a screen that does not work at all. I could not confirm or refute it without a wallet. — how: Start a wallet on the simulator with XSWD (vendor/bin/xswdwallet.exe --wallet .sim/<a funded wallet>.db --port 44326 --daemon 127.0.0.1:20000). Drive the app with Playwright to #wrap, connect over XSWD, approve Register (regfee is 0 on both simulator vaults, so this costs only gas), then assert the rendered DEPOSIT step 1 reads "Index 0" rather than offering Register again. Cross-check directly: curl DERO.GetSC on f23403b0… with variables:true and compare the raw uc_ key bytes against what app/wrap.js:54 asks for via keysstring.
- **[Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] A real Monero stagenet or Zcash testnet deposit and withdrawal driven through the rendered Wrap screen — the only way to see what the deposit walkthrough and the settlement rows actually say when foreign money moves.** — why: No Monero or Zcash backend exists: tools/vaultsigner supports only -backend sim (main.go:470-476 exits on monero/zcash/solana), and the operator runs no stagenet or testnet node. Every wrap screen I judged was rendered against a JSON file standing in for a chain — which is also why the config's `chain` strings say "simulated on this network" and why UX-06 is visible at all. — how: Run monerod --stagenet and one monero-wallet-rpc --stagenet --wallet-dir <dir> per signer; build the Monero backend behind the tools/vaultsigner Backend interface (Height/Finalized/Deposits/Verify/Address/ValidDest/Release/VerifyRelease/Reserve/Health, main.go:76-100) against it; install a fresh vault with tools/simvault; run three signers; then drive the app: Register, read the published da_<j> off the screen, send stagenet XMR to it, watch the walkthrough through 10 confirmations to Mint, then Burn to a stagenet address and read the settlement rows through Paying → Released. Capture the screen at each state — particularly the states nobody has ever rendered: a mint waiting on headroom, a burn a signer has marked, and a reclaim.
- **[Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Rendering the app as it would look on mainnet.** — why: deploy/networks.json's mainnet block is empty — no SCIDs and templates {} — so `genconfig -network mainnet` produces a bundle whose template map verifies nothing and whose factory is "". Only the final validator may run genconfig (it rewrites app/config.js), and I must not leave the repo's config pointed at a non-simulator network. — how: After mainnet SCIDs and template hashes exist in deploy/networks.json, run (cd tools && go run ./genconfig -dist -network mainnet), serve dist/mainnet/ on a spare port, and repeat the UI pass against it. Judge specifically: what the Home hero leads with when no market has traded, what the Founders Pass hero and Public accounting card say with the real -pass-descr and -pass-max 20, and whether the Wrap screen's `chain` strings still splice correctly at whatever the mainnet config names them.
- **[Real-world failure states, false confidence, product structure (§31, §32, §47, §48) — rendered-app audit] Testing the app against a hostile node — the trust boundary the Settings screen names as total.** — why: Out of scope for a read-only pass and it needs a second daemon to be stood up; also squarely the app-security reader's dimension rather than mine. I flag it because the screen I spent most of my time on (Wrap) prints a federation-supplied deposit address in full as the user's instruction to send irreversible Monero, and the app has no second source for it. — how: Run a proxy on 127.0.0.1 that forwards DERO.GetSC to the real daemon but rewrites the da_<j> value for one index, point Settings' node override at it, and confirm what the deposit walkthrough shows. Then judge whether step 2's copy ("Each signer derives the same address and votes for it") gives the user any way to notice.
- **[Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Timing the treasury bot's PayBatch bleed (FEE-03) against the live simulator, to turn the reasoned 86.4 DERO/day into a measured transaction count.** — why: My wallet allocation for this session is read-only, and the bot spends from the treasury wallet (127.0.0.1:30002). I proved the contract-side precondition (rpool never returns to zero) on the DVM instead, and read the gate and the fixed fee out of tools/treasurybot/main.go:173-184. — how: With an operator-authorised treasury wallet: install a fresh founders.bas from contracts/ with -pass-max 20, mint and register from two or three simulator wallets, deposit once so a distribution happens, then run `cd tools && go run ./treasurybot -wallet 127.0.0.1:30002 -config ../app/config.js -interval 10s` for five minutes with no market fees pending. Count the PayBatch transactions in the daemon's mempool and blocks and multiply by the 3000-atomic fixed fee at main.go:178. Expect ceil(hn/15) per interval, indefinitely.
- **[Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] A real wrapped-asset fee reaching (or failing to reach) pass holders end to end — a wXMR protoFee or pool fd claimed to the treasury and then not depositable (FEE-04).** — why: tools/vaultsigner has no Monero or Zcash backend (main.go:470-476 exits with "not implemented yet"); only the sim JSON backend exists, and no wrapped-quote market on the simulator has accrued fees. The DERO-quoted-market-with-wrapped-asset case additionally needs a pool installed with a vault SCID as its asset, which the app's own form cannot create. — how: On the simulator: install pool.bas directly (not through the app) with asset = the XMR vault SCID f23403b0... and quote = the zero hash; add liquidity and run a sell so `fa` accrues in wXMR; then run `go run ./treasurybot -once -dry-run` and observe that the market is NOT in the owed-but-not-sweepable list because its quote is DERO, and that a real run sends ClaimFees and moves wXMR to the treasury wallet with nothing added to the journal. For the vault leg proper this needs monerod --stagenet plus one monero-wallet-rpc per signer and a vaultsigner Monero backend that does not exist (BL-81/82).
- **[Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] A rendered UI pass over the Founders Pass screen to photograph the permanently-lit "Push rewards to holders" button (FEE-03), the no-op "Release waiting DERO" button (FEE-08), and the three contradicting sentences (FEE-05).** — why: I read the conditions and copy out of app/nfts.js, app/home.js, app/notice.js and app/founders.js and confirmed the contract behaviour behind them on the DVM; the UI harness takes the same single lock as the DVM suite and I judged the screenshot to add nothing the code did not already settle. It is still the honest way to confirm what a user sees. — how: tools/withlock.sh ui env PW_DIR=<playwright> node tools/uitest/ui.mjs http://127.0.0.1:8082/index.html .sim/ui-feeaudit — then read .sim/ui-feeaudit/*.png with the Read tool, specifically the NFTs tab. Note the live pass has dep = 0 and rpool = 0, so to see the lit button you must first deposit into it and push once, which needs a spending wallet.
- **[Fee architecture and distribution security (directive §7, §8, §14) — founders.bas line by line, the treasury bot, and every app surface that states a fee or a share] Confirming that a stray wrapped asset attached to Deposit at ring > 2 is kept by the chain rather than refunded (the FEE-04 stranding, worst case).** — why: The harness reverts only when a signer exists (tests/harness/chain.go:189-192), and the vendored derohe's ring>2 failure path (vendor/derohe/blockchain/transaction_execute.go:397-405) calls ErrorRevert with a zero signer where upstream calls ErrorDeposit. The two disagree, so the fate of the value must be measured on a real chain, not inferred. — how: On the simulator, send a Deposit call to the founders SCID at ring size 16 carrying only a vault asset (no DERO) from a wallet holding wrapped units, then read the contract's asset balance with DERO.GetSC and the sender's balance with the wallet's GetBalance. Repeat at ring 2 to show the refund. Needs a spending wallet with wrapped units.
- **[Founder NFTs, user NFAs, the NFA standard (founders.bas, nfa.bas, nft.bas, factory NFA registration, and the app's asset screens) — §9–§12] Indexer compatibility: confirm Gnomon / Engram / dReams index a DERO-quoted DMZ NFA as ART-NFA-MS1 v1.1.1, and do not mis-index a wXMR-quoted one (the three extra keys quote / protoFee / treasury, and an InitializePrivate that takes parameters where the standard has literals).** — why: Needs Gnomon running against the simulator; not in the tree and not something a read-only wallet assignment covers.
